## Chapter 0: Introduction

**¶1** ⟨p.1⟩
(1) [The first lesson is about] light [the Land of] which he has unveiled [to] his disciples [the] entire [treasure] of wisdom [and the] word in the beginning [living] concerning the separation [he taught] the heights [between] to the heights [from] and they spread out [and down,]il they should make war [upon the] kingdom [of the l]ight [l] in the heights [f]rom all the aeons [and it is separated] the light, the one that no [can be counted] in it.

> *¶1 #2 [moderate]:* Sets the polar region: ‘Land of Light’.
> *¶1 #3 [strong]:* Fits: ‘which he has unveiled to his disciples’.
> *¶1 #4 [strong]:* Fits: ‘the entire … of wisdom’.

> *¶1 #8 [moderate]:* Governs the following ‘concerning the separation…’.
> *¶1 #9 [strong]:* ‘concerning the separation between…’.
> *¶1 #10 [tentative]:* ‘the heights from the heights’ = separation of the two upper regions (Light above, Darkness below) in a schematic way.
> *¶1 #11 [tentative]:* Completes motion/extension: ‘they spread out … until they should make war’.
> *¶1 #12 [moderate]:* ‘make war upon (the) kingdom…’.

> *¶1 #14 [strong]:* Carries ‘…light…’ into next phrase.

> *¶1 #17 [moderate]:* Fits: ‘the light, the one that no … in it’ → uncountable/immeasurable purity.

**¶2** [And likewise concerning] the darkness, the world [of] their root [is] which poured forth [from below,] the one that the earth has [in it, the great] darkness [the] it is far from the light [and]

> *¶2 #1 [moderate]:* Parallel introduction to the second region.

> *¶2 #6 [moderate]:* ‘…the one that the earth has… the darkness…’.
> *¶2 #7 [moderate]:* Clause continuation.

**¶3** This is the first [great lesson] concerning the eternality; he [has spoken] to you, for each one of them exists [eternally,] they are divided from one another by their [own boundaries,] in an eternality since the [scripture] which I have written for you; this is, that [when] (4) the darkness has made war against the light, desiring that it would reign over an essence not its own. It spread a quarrel [Sin,] it disturbed [in the deep;] came up, it [need] need.

> *¶3 #1 [strong]:* ‘This is the first great lesson…’.
> *¶3 #2 [moderate]:* Impersonal didactic voice: the teaching ‘has spoken… to you’.
> *¶3 #3 [moderate]:* Pairs with ‘for each one of them exists…’.
> *¶3 #4 [moderate]:* Correspondential ‘bounds’ separating realms by nature, not by moral decree.
> *¶3 #5 [tentative]:* Refers to the written exposition just mentioned.
> *¶3 #6 [strong]:* ‘this is, that when…’.
> *¶3 #7 [moderate]:* Oldest substrate prefers ‘Sin’ as a hypostatized power initiating assault from Darkness.
> *¶3 #8 [tentative]:* Quarrel begins ‘in the deep’ (lower region) before rising.
> *¶3 #9 [moderate]:* Completes: ‘it … in this great need’. Context is distress/necessity prompting emanation of the saving power.

**¶4** The power came forth from [the Father, the one] is [st]st Man. He [put on] the armour of light [against] all [the enmity]. He crucified it in [his soul,] the armour [was mingled with it]

> *¶4 #1 [moderate]:* Source of the First Man as a power ‘came forth from the Father’.
> *¶4 #2 [strong]:* Completes ‘First Man’.
> *¶4 #3 [strong]:* Standard cosmogonic action: dons armour of light.
> *¶4 #4 [strong]:* ‘…armour of light against all the enmity’.

> *¶4 #6 [tentative]:* Crucifixion language here is correspondential: fixing the hostile mixture upon his living soul as battlefield.

**¶5** [Afterwards] he brought the sun [and the moon,] the light aeons [and] Living Spirit spread out the universe [and he made] the earth [and he separated] the light and the darkness in the beginning [the vessels] and the garments. He gave [to their places.] He set in order the [twelve signs] and the wheel of the course [of the year,] in the heights, that they would [their paths] of the darkness. He stood firm [f]ixed. He constructed the foundation [of the whole,] all of it was set up.

**¶6** Underneath [the earth are the lower regions] all of them; and also above he [three]e earths, they being conjoined. He enclosed the earth in [f]our mountains and three vessels [set] in its seas and its mountains [set in] order upon it. This universe stood firm [as] a bond for the evil powers [light]ght that they joined with the darkness [there.]

> *¶6 #1 [tentative]:* Sets vertical schema: beneath are lower regions ‘underneath … all of them’.
> *¶6 #2 [strong]:* Completes ‘three earths’.
> *¶6 #3 [strong]:* Single-letter constraint before ‘our mountains’.
> *¶6 #4 [moderate]:* ‘three vessels set in its seas…’.
> *¶6 #5 [strong]:* Accepted: ‘were set in order upon it’.
> *¶6 #6 [strong]:* ‘stood firm as a bond…’.
> *¶6 #7 [strong]:* Restores ‘light that they joined…’.
> *¶6 #8 [moderate]:* Closes the thought: joining occurred ‘there’ within the constructed bond/universe.

**¶7** Then the Third Ambassador! [He purified the light] by his image. He became [redeemer of] all things.

> *¶7 #1 [strong]:* Accepted: function of the Third Ambassador as the revealing image that separates/purifies the light.
> *¶7 #2 [strong]:* Accepted but understood cosmologically: ‘redeemer’ = one who draws out/separates the light-substance from mixture.

**¶8** (5) [This is the end,] they are in all the [universe,]niverse, and the fire blazes in it, and it is devoured [by it,] all [the members] of the light also, and he becomes purifier, and goes up to his essence. Conversely, the darkness which is in [it is cast] out, and it is gathered in to a bond [and the seals] unveil [the powers of the light] and they go in to him and become [as a pe]rfume [r]et up [st]d they reign for ever, and a single God comes to be [ov]er the totality, being above the totality. You find no opponent from this time on against the Father, the King of the Light and the [Holy Spirit.]

> *¶8 #1 [moderate]:* Introduces eschatological closure in impersonal didactic tone.
> *¶8 #2 [strong]:* Completes ‘in all the universe’.
> *¶8 #3 [tentative]:* Fire devours ‘by it’ (by the process) the remaining mixture.
> *¶8 #4 [moderate]:* ‘all the members of the light’ = dispersed light-parts gathered and ascending.
> *¶8 #5 [moderate]:* ‘Conversely, the darkness… it is cast out’.
> *¶8 #6 [tentative]:* ‘a bond … unveil …’ → the bond is closed/sealed; unveiling refers to the opening/manifestation of the pure forms before closure.
> *¶8 #7 [tentative]:* Those who ‘go in to him’ (the single God over totality) are the purified powers/light-substances.
> *¶8 #8 [moderate]:* Begins ‘as a perfume’: light as fragrant offering/subtle essence (correspondence of odor with perception/acceptance).
> *¶8 #9 [strong]:* Completes ‘perfume’.
> *¶8 #10 [moderate]:* Completes ‘and (they are) set up…’.
> *¶8 #11 [strong]:* Completes ‘over the totality’.
> *¶8 #12 [tentative]:* Triadic closure in Manichaean/Coptic idiom often adds ‘Holy Spirit’; here retained as a title for the upper vivifying power in the cosmology rather than a devotional insert.

**¶9** [These are the natures] which they occur in, from the beginning [until the separation,] they are mixed and joined with one another.

> *¶9 #1 [moderate]:* Summarizes the two roots/natures whose processes were described.
> *¶9 #2 [moderate]:* Sets temporal clause: from beginning until final separation they are mixed.

**¶12** (6) The first great lesson [is this,] which in the [twelve signs] that walks in it because of the twelve [months] in it, which in the cities and the dwelling places found in the [world] because of the doors that are opened in them [heavens] upon the earth, which in the four corners [these are the measures of the universe] and my pains. For also [are my labours] and my afflictions [my toils] to them [and the knowledge] suffices for the person [to become] wise person.

> *¶12 #1 [moderate]:* ‘The first great lesson is this…’.
> *¶12 #2 [moderate]:* Matches later ‘twelve … in it’ and earlier astral ordering.
> *¶12 #3 [moderate]:* ‘twelve months’ walking in it (the year).
> *¶12 #4 [moderate]:* ‘cities and dwelling places found in the world’.
> *¶12 #5 [tentative]:* Doors opened ‘in them’ = gates of the heavens/quarters regulating influx.
> *¶12 #6 [tentative]:* Correspondentially the list (12, cities, doors, 4 corners) are ‘measures’/orders by which the cosmos is held.
> *¶12 #7 [tentative]:* Fits pairing with ‘and my pains’.
> *¶12 #8 [tentative]:* Parallelism: labours/toils; pains/afflictions.
> *¶12 #9 [tentative]:* What is ‘given to them’ that ‘suffices’ is knowledge/gnosis, not exhortation.
> *¶12 #10 [moderate]:* ‘suffices for the person … to become (a) wise person’.

**Assessment:** Many gaps are too large to restore with certainty because the excerpt lacks the usual fixed formulae and parallel witnesses; still, the underlying correspondential structure (two eternal roots; assault; emanation of First Man; construction by Living Spirit; cosmic measures of 12 and 4; final separation by fire and sealing) constrains a fair number of fills. Confidence is strongest where syntax tightly demands particles/letters, moderate where standard cosmogonic actions recur, and tentative where exact nouns/adjectives could vary among recensions.

## Chapter 1: Concerning the Ad[vent] of the Apostle.

**¶3** Understand, O my beloved ones: All the apostles who are on occasion sent to the world resemble farmers; while their churches, which they choose, are like Parmuthi and Paophi. For, the way Parmuthi occurs not in all the months of the year; nor does Paophi [also occur] in all of them. Rather, the season [is appointed] for it alone, as they know [the order of] its vegetables [and the growth of] the time they are cropped; also the vegetables and the [of each] season; and they tend and ripen the pickings, and they [carry them up] to Paophi.

> *¶3 #1 [moderate]:* Paophi is being paralleled to Parmuthi: neither occurs (i.e., is suitable/appointed) in every month. Verb must match 'occurs' just used for Parmuthi.
> *¶3 #2 [moderate]:* The logic is seasonal appointment: a given month/season is 'appointed' for a given work (grain vs fruit). Old substrate speaks in terms of fixed order/season rather than exhortation.
> *¶3 #3 [moderate]:* Farmers know the ordered cycle: seasons and their correspondential labors. ‘order’ fits oldest-layer register (cosmic order).
> *¶3 #4 [tentative]:* They know (1) vegetables, (2) their growth, (3) cropping-time. Triadic agrarian knowledge mirrors correspondential science (state→form→effect).
> *¶3 #5 [tentative]:* Balances the phrase: “vegetables and the … season” → “vegetables and the (appointed) season of each.”
> *¶3 #6 [moderate]:* Transition “and they … to Paophi” requires a verb of bringing/carrying the produce into the Paophi stage (fruit month).

**¶4** Of course, now that farmer ⟨p.10⟩ [does not sow in] Parmuthi; in that, if he will come and sow some corn seed there [it will not become] cereal. And he shall be involved from the beginning [and receive] a corn seed and toil for it. Yet, when the summer comes each year, and his corn comes to the season of its harvest; then the farmer comes forth and harvests it. Even the gardener from the beginning [the gardener too,] shall toil for his fruits; and he tends them and he [waters them] and they ripen; and he comes forth and picks them from the tree.

> *¶4 #1 [moderate]:* Negative contrast: farmer and Parmuthi—he does not sow in Parmuthi; he harvests then. Fits the ensuing conditional ‘if he will come and sow…’ as an improper timing.
> *¶4 #2 [moderate]:* If sown in the wrong season, it will not become cereal. Matches agricultural correspondence: time/season as spiritual state prerequisite.
> *¶4 #3 [tentative]:* He is involved from the beginning: he takes/receives a seed and toils. ‘receive’ fits correspondential influx into ultimates.
> *¶4 #4 [moderate]:* Parallel construction: farmer / gardener. Requires resumptive phrase introducing gardener as a second image.
> *¶4 #5 [moderate]:* Gardener tends fruits; key action is watering before ripening/picking. Oldest layer stays concrete in the image.

**¶6** ⟨p.11⟩
Also the way [an apostle is sent] many years before [he comes to] the world, as he journeys below to the [depths,] makes the form of his church free, and [loosens the bonds] of the flesh, whose forms he had made free [from the bond]

> *¶6 #1 [moderate]:* Paragraph explains apostolic cycle: sent long before appearing; matches ¶8-¶9. The indefinite subject must be ‘apostle’.
> *¶6 #2 [strong]:* “many years before … the world” → before he comes to/into the world. Straightforward temporal descent motif.
> *¶6 #3 [moderate]:* Journey ‘below’ is into the depths (opposite the heights). Standard cosmological vertical axis.
> *¶6 #4 [moderate]:* Oldest substrate describes freeing forms from constraint; ‘bonds’ matches ‘flesh’ as binding/weight in ultimates.
> *¶6 #5 [moderate]:* Completes: “... and [from the bond] of the flesh” as the specific binding from which forms are freed.

**¶7** Like the farmer, for when he will [sow,] that moment he shall begin [to] tend [it until] it reaches Parmuthi also, he may harvest it [and not] toil for the fruits of Paophi [and not]

> *¶7 #1 [strong]:* Like farmer: first act is sowing; then he begins to tend until harvest month.
> *¶7 #2 [strong]:* “shall begin … tend …” requires infinitival link: begin to tend.
> *¶7 #3 [moderate]:* Object and duration needed: tend it until it reaches Parmuthi.
> *¶7 #4 [tentative]:* Contrast: harvest in Parmuthi, and not toil then for Paophi-fruits; each has its own season.
> *¶7 #5 [tentative]:* Parallel to #4: he may harvest (grain) and not toil (then) for fruits (Paophi).

**¶8** Again, this too is [how] the apostles [are sent] from the beginning of the moulding of humanity [in the heights], as I have told you, that when they [will appear] before every thing he shall [make its form] free above, first.

> *¶8 #2 [moderate]:* “this too is how the apostles …” requires ‘are sent’ or ‘work’; ‘are sent’ matches ¶3 opening and ¶12.
> *¶8 #3 [moderate]:* Before appearing ‘in flesh’, they act above: freeing forms in the heights (repeated in ¶6, ¶12).
> *¶8 #4 [tentative]:* Sequence: when they are sent/prepare, then they will appear/begin below. Sets up ¶9 descent into flesh.
> *¶8 #5 [moderate]:* “he shall … free above, first” → he shall make its form free above first (church-form). Echoes ¶6/¶12 phrasing.

**¶9** Yet, when it [will be] born in the flesh, he shall come down to it and at once [release] it in the [bond] which comes out from the [flesh; and it] is raised up to the greatness. He shall continue in the world at this time [until they will know] him, corresponding to the season when the world will come to his [measure,] when the season will mature he is raised up from the world [and] and he leaves his church {behind} and goes forth. Still, he [is not far from it,] and he helps and aids his church. Whoever comes out from their body after him, he shall become for them a support and a leader; and he goes on before them [in the way,], excepting the others who are set firmly in the flesh. He is the one who will become for them a helper and a guardian; he shall not take [part in its labour] in secret, until his church [shall attain] change it [to the other state,] when his church [has finished,] he was raised up after it, and went into the land of glory. And he rejoices and rests at the last [from the labour.]

> *¶9 #2 [moderate]:* At birth in flesh, apostle comes down and at once releases (loosens) it from the binding condition; matches ¶12 ‘releases his church’.
> *¶9 #3 [moderate]:* “in the … which comes out from the …” → in the bond which comes out from the flesh. Binding imagery is consistent.
> *¶9 #4 [tentative]:* What comes out from the bond is ‘the flesh’; then ‘it is raised up’. Keeps tight correspondence: from flesh to greatness (height).
> *¶9 #5 [tentative]:* He remains until the season appointed for him; phrasing of recognition/knowing aligns with correspondential ‘season’ as state of readiness.
> *¶9 #6 [moderate]:* “world will come to his …” is a limit/measure/term. Old substrate uses measure/order language.
> *¶9 #7 [strong]:* Connects clauses: when season matures, he is raised up … and he leaves his church.
> *¶9 #8 [moderate]:* Even after departure he still helps: he is not far from his church. Avoids later devotional tone while stating continuance of influence.
> *¶9 #9 [moderate]:* He ‘goes on before them …’ naturally takes ‘in the way’ (as guide/leader).
> *¶9 #10 [tentative]:* He shall not take part in secret: i.e., not take part in the hidden labor once raised, until church reaches its change/season. Keeps impersonal process-language.
> *¶9 #11 [moderate]:* “until his church … change it …” requires reaching/attaining the change (its appointed transformation).
> *¶9 #12 [moderate]:* The ‘change’ is into another state (degree). Old substrate emphasizes discrete degrees/state-change.
> *¶9 #13 [moderate]:* “when his church … he was raised up after it” → when his church has finished (its cycle), he is raised after it.
> *¶9 #14 [moderate]:* He rejoices and rests at the last from the labour—echoing farmer’s rest after harvest; keeps image coherence.

**¶10** Like the farmer who shall be glad of heart in [the day when] he was bringing in (the harvest). Or like the merchant who shall come up from a country with the doubling of his great cargo; and the riches of his trading.

> *¶10 #1 [moderate]:* Farmer glad of heart in the day when he brings in the harvest; fits idiom and parallel to merchant’s return.

**¶11** When the apostle will be raised up from the world, he and his church [rejoice,] for every apostle [labours] in the [world.] Since [a fruit] tree, that still is laden with its first fruits [when they are ripe;], and they are picked; they shall propagate other ones there, and they mature to the season to bring them forth. There is not any time the tree is bare of fruit! Rather, instead, those about to ripen as they pick them shall propagate other ones there. When they bear these [and set] them on the tree; they come up and are brought forth upon its branches. They shall pick from it those that mature and ripen. The farmer does not rest at any time from the labour and the pain. At what hour may he cease? When he can finish harvesting the corn in Parmuthi! [and set] them in the winter, the fruits of the summer shall [So also] grow up and be picked in the month of Paophi.

> *¶11 #1 [moderate]:* “he and his church …” naturally continues with ‘rejoice’ (then explanation of continuous fruiting/labour).
> *¶11 #2 [moderate]:* “for every apostle … in the …” → every apostle labours in the world. Basic substrate statement.
> *¶11 #3 [strong]:* Completes the location of labour: in the world (realm of effects).
> *¶11 #4 [strong]:* “Since … tree, that still is laden with its first fruits” → since a fruit tree… Standard phrase.
> *¶11 #5 [moderate]:* Explains picking: first fruits are picked when ripe; then new ones propagated. Keeps the internal cycle logic.
> *¶11 #6 [tentative]:* “When they bear these … them on the tree” → bear these and set them on the tree (i.e., establish new fruit).
> *¶11 #7 [moderate]:* Second verb needed with ‘them’: ‘set them on the tree’ completes syntax.
> *¶11 #8 [tentative]:* Transition from grain to fruits: “When he can finish… Parmuthi! … them in the winter…” needs a resumptive connector. ‘So also’ introduces next seasonal image.

**¶12** ⟨p.12⟩
The apostles are like this also. Now, when the apostle will be raised up to the heights, he and his church, and they depart from the world; at that instant another apostle shall be sent to it, to another church [which he shall make free] it [in the heights,]. Yet, first, he shall make the forms of his church free in the heights, as I have told you. When [it will be born in the flesh]; again, he too shall come down and appear [in the world;] and he releases his church and saves it from the flesh [of sin ...]

> *¶12 #1 [moderate]:* Another apostle sent to another church—relative clause: to another church which he shall make free (above).
> *¶12 #2 [moderate]:* Reasserts first action is above. Mirrors ¶6/¶8/¶12 refrain for correspondential order.
> *¶12 #3 [moderate]:* The repeated hinge: after making forms free above, then it is born in flesh; exact echo of ¶9’s “when it will be born in the flesh”.
> *¶12 #4 [strong]:* He comes down and appears in the world (realm of ultimates). Completes descent sequence.

**¶20** ⟨p.14⟩
Like a tree will be plucked, and the fruits on it taken away. And it remains behind without fruit.

**¶23** Yet, when the church assumed the flesh, the season arrived to redeem the souls; like the month of Parmuthi that cereal shall ripen in, to be harvested.

**¶24** He unveiled to me the hidden mystery, the one that is hidden from the worlds and the generations, the mystery of the depths and the heights. He unveiled to me the mystery of the light and the darkness; the mystery of the calamity of conflict, and the war, and the great [onslaught,], the battle that the darkness spread about. Afterwards, he unveiled to me also: How the light [seized] the darkness, through their mingling this universe was set up [and was bound in]

> *¶24 #1 [moderate]:* List: conflict, war, and the great …, the battle darkness spread. ‘onslaught’ fits as the intensification between war and battle without importing later moralizing.
> *¶24 #2 [moderate]:* “How the light … the darkness” in cosmogony: light is seized/captured by darkness (or darkness seized light). In Manichaean/Iranian structure, Darkness assaults and captures light-substance; ‘seized’ preserves impersonal mechanism.
> *¶24 #3 [moderate]:* “through their mingling this universe was set up” presupposes binding/entanglement of light in mixture. ‘was bound in’ matches bondage register used earlier (bonds/flesh).

**¶25** He opened my eyes also to the way that the ships were constructed; to enable the gods of light to be in them, to purify the light from creation. Conversely, the dregs and the effluent [are cast down] to the abyss. The mystery of the fashioning of Adam, the first man. He also informed me about the mystery of the tree of knowledge, which Adam ate from; his eyes saw.

> *¶25 #1 [strong]:* Dregs/effluent go to the abyss: they are cast down to the abyss. Directional correspondence (downward) is essential.

**Assessment:** Most gaps can be restored at the level of required correspondential mechanics: appointed seasons, descent/ascent, bonds of flesh, and cyclic labour/fruiting. Exact wording remains tentative in several places (especially ¶9–¶12 and parts of ¶11) because multiple Coptic-equivalent verbs could fit the same cosmological logic, but the underlying structure (order of seasons; freeing forms above → birth in flesh → release → ascent; mixture as binding) tightly constrains the semantic field.

## Chapter 2: The Second, concerning the Parable of the Tree.

**¶5** Great is [the error of those who speak] by his own mouth [and they utter] bad [good and] The sects [as though it were one thing] Yet, this that they explain about the two trees, as they reckon them, [are many and various] the trees [they do not know] in an explanation [are not grasped] because [that is true] tree, as it grows on this composite earth, [the good] date palm, because [it has portions that are taken and portions that are cast away], for not absolutely every part of the date palm is used!

> *¶5 #1 [moderate]:* Opening contrasts magnitude of error with subsequent correction; substrate register is impersonal diagnosis, not polemic.
> *¶5 #2 [moderate]:* Fits “by his own mouth” as idiom for self-proclaiming teaching.
> *¶5 #3 [moderate]:* Sets the needed polarity before “bad …”.
> *¶5 #4 [moderate]:* Prepares critique: they treat the two principles/trees as one mixed source.
> *¶5 #5 [moderate]:* Natural continuation: “The sects … Yet …”.
> *¶5 #6 [moderate]:* Transitions from sects to mistaken exegesis about the two trees.
> *¶5 #7 [moderate]:* “the trees … in an explanation …” needs a verb of comprehension.
> *¶5 #8 [moderate]:* Completes “in an explanation … because …”.
> *¶5 #9 [moderate]:* Required before “tree, as it grows on this composite earth,” introducing exemplar (date palm).
> *¶5 #10 [moderate]:* Matches the ensuing point: not every part is used; good in trunk/fruit, refuse in fibre/chaff in opposite sense.

**¶6** ⟨p.18⟩ [They liken] their child, as it strikes [its fruit and] while first one [the shoot] in them [sprouts] at the end there is one [is wholly sweet] it occurs in its fibres and its branches [there is sweetness] There is a bad dust in them [but] date palm [refuse] before the tree that [and in its bark] bad; because they give the decision [that clings] the one that they name [of the] the type [and they judge it] name it: tree [is] is sweet [wholly] writing [against] they shall establish [the tree of knowledge] man [which is] it is sweet [of this] seed of the [and they] and it is used for their nourishment [whose fruit] will explain [and its] may not join to the [according to their] mankind, as they are named [upon]

> *¶6 #1 [moderate]:* Begins the exemplum about the date palm by stating the comparison.
> *¶6 #2 [moderate]:* Sets up “their child, as it strikes …” i.e., fruit/seed causing propagation.
> *¶6 #3 [moderate]:* Date palm imagery: offshoot/child produced from the parent.
> *¶6 #4 [moderate]:* “as it strikes …” commonly “strikes root/sprouts”.
> *¶6 #5 [moderate]:* Contrasts later: within it there is also refuse/dust.
> *¶6 #6 [moderate]:* Needed before “in them … at the end there is one …”.
> *¶6 #7 [strong]:* Marks the turn from sweetness to mixed refuse within the same natural tree.
> *¶6 #8 [moderate]:* “at the end there is one … it occurs in its fibres and its branches” = useless/harmful parts.
> *¶6 #9 [moderate]:* Completes catalogue of non-edible parts together with fibres/branches.
> *¶6 #10 [moderate]:* Connects “bad dust … in them”.
> *¶6 #11 [moderate]:* Just the preposition/article to link “bad dust … date palm”.
> *¶6 #12 [moderate]:* Leads into “before the tree that … bad; because they give the decision …”.
> *¶6 #13 [strong]:* Grammatically needed: “the tree that … bad”.
> *¶6 #14 [moderate]:* They wrongly make a decision of absolute badness from partial refuse.
> *¶6 #15 [moderate]:* “give the decision …” typically “against” the object.
> *¶6 #16 [tentative]:* Old substrate: “knowledge” here is the mixed natural discernment they misread as the ‘tree’; avoids later Christianized title.
> *¶6 #17 [strong]:* Links “the one that they name … the type …”.
> *¶6 #18 [moderate]:* Type-of relation: the date palm as type of the good tree in the mixed world.
> *¶6 #19 [strong]:* Connects “name it: tree … is sweet”.
> *¶6 #20 [moderate]:* Needed to make sense: “tree … is sweet … seed … nourishment”.
> *¶6 #21 [moderate]:* Precedes “writing … they shall establish … man …”.
> *¶6 #22 [moderate]:* Ties “writing … establish” to sectarian textual proof.
> *¶6 #23 [moderate]:* “shall establish … man …” = impose upon mankind.

> *¶11 #1 [moderate]:* Transition into doctrinal disclosure (“how I will reveal to you …”).
> *¶11 #2 [moderate]:* Adverb fitting disclosure.
> *¶11 #4 [moderate]:* “the expound[er … ] the trees” expects “expounder of the teaching (about) the trees”.
> *¶11 #5 [moderate]:* Links to “the trees … the Father …”.
> *¶11 #6 [moderate]:* Substrate cosmology: Father of Light above all; avoids later ecclesial language.
> *¶11 #7 [moderate]:* Needed: “all of them, as he …”.
> *¶11 #8 [moderate]:* “in the … world” = upper world of Light.
> *¶11 #9 [moderate]:* Completes “world … this good tree …”.
> *¶11 #10 [moderate]:* Correspondence: tree = ordered degrees; rooted in aeons, manifested below.
> *¶11 #11 [moderate]:* Prepares: “the light of this good tree … the Pillar …”.

**¶12** ⟨p.20⟩ ... the intellectuals that are cloaked upon its body [and they are called] this good tree. They are the light elements that are mingled, being compounded in the totality.

> *¶12 #1 [moderate]:* Explains who the “intellectuals” are: the light-elements as ‘cloaked’ on body.

**¶14** Behold, this is the good tree [and its fruit is Life]

> *¶14 #1 [moderate]:* Concludes identification: good tree yields life/light.

**¶15** [Again] I reveal to you [also] it has five limbs. [They are consideration, counsel, insight, thought, mind.] Its consideration is the holy church. [Its counsel] is the Pillar of Glory, the Perfect Man. [Its insight is the Fir]st Man who dwells in the ship of living waters. Its thought is the Third Ambassador [who dwells in] the ship of living fire, that shines in [its rising] Also, the mind is the Father who dwells in greatness, who is perfect in the aeons of light.

> *¶15 #1 [moderate]:* Simple discourse marker starting the five-limbs exposition.
> *¶15 #2 [moderate]:* Balances “I reveal to you … it has five limbs.”
> *¶15 #3 [strong]:* Accepted as fitting the fivefold discrete degrees within the good principle.
> *¶15 #4 [strong]:* Accepted; matches pattern “Its X is …”.
> *¶15 #5 [strong]:* Accepted mid-word; leads to “st Man”.
> *¶15 #6 [strong]:* Accepted; standard locative formula for hypostases in their vessels/ships.
> *¶15 #7 [moderate]:* “living fire, that shines in …” naturally “in its rising” (sun/fire imagery of manifestation).

**¶17** It is consideration that shall [gather them] they shall be raised up [to counsel, which] is counsel; they shall be raised up to insight, which is the First Man who dwells in the ship of the night; from insight they shall rise up to thought, which is the Ambassador who dwells in the ship of the day. And he too, great glorious thought, shall bring them in to mind; which is the Father, the God of truth, the great Mind of all the aeons of glory.

> *¶17 #1 [moderate]:* Consideration (Church) gathers; then raises through degrees.
> *¶17 #2 [strong]:* Required staircase: consideration → counsel → insight → thought → mind.

> *¶18 #1 [moderate]:* “shall give good fruit, … is the entire life” needs “whose fruit”.
> *¶18 #2 [strong]:* Links fruit to “entire life and light”.
> *¶18 #3 [moderate]:* Life/light occur “in the aeons” eternally; substrate diction.
> *¶18 #4 [strong]:* Completes “ever, it is blessed …”.

**¶19** ⟨p.21⟩ [Again concerning the] bad [tree, the] happen in [things] from [the composite world] also [the bad tree] the way that it is [in contrast to] the good tree [and the smoke] of the fire [of the furnaces;] they are the ruling-power [of the five rulers] heaven and the wheel [of the] they are the five fleshes that walk [of the sphere;] fruit [walk in the]aching as of error, the mystery that [of]ich Satan appointed in the world.

> *¶19 #1 [moderate]:* New section on bad tree; mirrors earlier “Concerning …”.
> *¶19 #2 [moderate]:* Sets “bad … happen in …”.
> *¶19 #3 [moderate]:* “bad … happen in …” = bad things happen in the world.
> *¶19 #4 [moderate]:* Standard substrate location for mixture and suffering.
> *¶19 #5 [moderate]:* “from … also … the way that it is … the good tree” needs the subject ‘bad tree’.
> *¶19 #6 [moderate]:* Sets explicit opposition between trees.
> *¶19 #7 [moderate]:* Bad tree linked with fire in opposite sense: burning, smoke, torment.
> *¶19 #8 [moderate]:* Connects with later ¶22 “furnaces of fire … gehenna”.
> *¶19 #9 [moderate]:* Fivefold demonic powers correspond to five limbs in opposite sense.
> *¶19 #10 [moderate]:* Links to “heaven and the wheel …” (astral governance as binding power).
> *¶19 #11 [moderate]:* “heaven and the wheel” = sphere/zodiac as ruling mechanism in the lower order.
> *¶19 #12 [moderate]:* Restores phrase: “five fleshes that walk in the …”.
> *¶19 #13 [moderate]:* Leads to “teaching as of error”.

**¶20** However, the taste of these bad fruits is these evil people, the sects [which]ich are bound in law after law, they and their teachers [and ordinances,] the law of death; they taking taste for it, they being thirsty for it [are in] the souls of death.

> *¶20 #2 [moderate]:* “bound in law after law” naturally expands to ordinances.
> *¶20 #3 [moderate]:* “teachers … the law of death” = are in the law of death.

Its consideration is the law of death from which the sects take instruction. Its counsel is transmigration [understand.] in type after type. Also, its insight is the furnaces of fire [ge]henna; which is full of smoke. Thought [in the great] vessel. Its mind is the [in its] the lump, the last bond, the [lump,], they that Satan cast away [first bond of] the great [chains] and they fall [from the] the darkness [into] all [abyss] and they take away [down into] are swallowed in to the lump, and they are bound [of] the fruit of darkness, the one that they [the depths;] for ever and ever [their light,] his face [in] will arrive at that place; and they have no repentance therein.

> *¶22 #1 [strong]:* Restores discourse formula and keeps impersonal didactic tone.
> *¶22 #2 [moderate]:* “and you …” = “and you hear/understand”.
> *¶22 #3 [moderate]:* Completes introductory injunction before schema of five limbs.
> *¶22 #5 [tentative]:* “Thought … vessel” needs the locus: great vessel of darkness/astral container.
> *¶22 #6 [moderate]:* Connects to “vessel. Its mind is …”.
> *¶22 #7 [moderate]:* Mind of bad tree = lump/bond; term recurs immediately after.
> *¶22 #8 [tentative]:* Opposite of ascent: bonds that bind downward; “last bond” follows, so “first bond” balances.
> *¶22 #9 [moderate]:* Substrate imagery: bonds/chains as fixed states of captivity in Matter.
> *¶22 #10 [moderate]:* Leads to “they that Satan cast away …”.
> *¶22 #11 [moderate]:* Cast away into the great lump/bond.
> *¶22 #12 [moderate]:* “the great … and they fall … the darkness …” = great abyss.
> *¶22 #13 [moderate]:* Fall down into darkness.
> *¶22 #14 [strong]:* Grammatical link: “the darkness …”.
> *¶22 #15 [moderate]:* Standard lowest-region term in this mythic geography.
> *¶22 #16 [moderate]:* What is taken away/swallowed: the remaining light elements in souls.
> *¶22 #17 [strong]:* Bound in the lump/bond; preposition.

**¶23** [Behold,] this is the explanation of the bad tree, as I have explained to you, which you [have listened] to it [and have not] good fruit [joined] its fruit bad [but] in it, in its existent days [and has not ceased] in it.

> *¶23 #1 [moderate]:* Marks conclusion of schema.
> *¶23 #2 [moderate]:* “which you … to it” = you have listened to it.
> *¶23 #3 [tentative]:* Sets moral-intellectual contrast: hearing without separating/joining rightly.
> *¶23 #4 [tentative]:* “to it … good fruit” = not joined to good fruit / not taken the good.
> *¶23 #6 [moderate]:* “in it, in its existent days … in it” = the bad tree continually bears bad fruit; never ceases while it exists.

**¶24** Concerning this [good tree,] and my beloved ones [I say to you] them in truth, it being changed from [its nature] which lies in its good earth [life] and light; as death and darkness do not exist [within it.] Also, the bad tree is Matter [and the Darkness is beneath] it, I understand it alone among the [which]ich occurs in its bad earth [alone,] darkness and death.

> *¶24 #1 [moderate]:* “Concerning this …” refers back to the good tree definition.
> *¶24 #2 [moderate]:* Didactic connective, but kept non-devotional.
> *¶24 #3 [moderate]:* “changed from … which lies in its good earth” = its nature not changed; stable principle.
> *¶24 #4 [strong]:* Completes “... [ ... life] and light”.
> *¶24 #5 [moderate]:* “do not exist …” = within it (the good earth/aeon).
> *¶24 #6 [moderate]:* Balances: good tree = Life/Light above; bad tree = Matter below; discrete degrees.
> *¶24 #8 [moderate]:* Emphasizes singularity of Matter principle: “I understand it alone…”.

**¶25** ⟨p.23⟩ Blessed is every one [who understands] these two trees, and separates them one from another. He understands that they did not arise out of one another, nor did they come from one another. They did not come from one! The person who can separate them out may go up to the aeon of light, [as I have] revealed it to you.

> *¶25 #1 [moderate]:* Blessedness tied to discernment/separation (discrete degrees).

**¶26** And he sees [that the] bad fruit did not arise from it [good tree’s]; but whoever will not separate out [from the two roots] from one another, he falls to the land of darkness [in this mixture] until he arrives at the [lump.]

> *¶26 #1 [moderate]:* Begins clause: “And he sees … bad fruit …”.
> *¶26 #2 [moderate]:* Clarifies referent: bad fruit did not arise from the good tree.
> *¶26 #3 [moderate]:* Separation concerns roots/principles, not merely fruits; correspondentially necessary.
> *¶26 #4 [moderate]:* Context: composite world; those who don’t separate fall downward.
> *¶26 #5 [moderate]:* Matches ¶22: the final destination for the bound souls.

**¶27** Blessed is whoever can believe! He [separates Light from] darkness; which grows out of the [bad earth] for ever and ever.

> *¶27 #1 [moderate]:* Final beat: faith expressed as discernment/separation of principles.
> *¶27 #2 [moderate]:* Darkness grows out of its own ground (bad earth/Matter), not from the good.

**Assessment:** Most gaps can be constrained by the chapter’s two-tree correspondence: good vs bad principle; fivefold limbs in discrete ascent/descent; mixture on composite earth; final binding in the lump. Exact micro-phrasing (especially ¶6 and ¶22-23) remains uncertain without manuscript traces, but the restored wording keeps the oldest-layer impersonal cosmological register and maintains the structural correspondences.

## Chapter 3: The Interpretation of Happiness, Wisdom and Power; what they signify.

**¶4** [teach]each you of another happiness [which is in the place of Light] and another wi]sdom [which is in the place of Truth] and together another power [which is in the place of Power] So, now, listen that I may reveal to you [how] it is [with] these three: [ha]ppiness, wisdom and power.

> *¶4 #1 [strong]:* Completes the introductory promise formula; impersonal didactic register.
> *¶4 #2 [moderate]:* The triad is being re-instanced in successive cosmic ‘places’ (Father’s land; sun/ship; waters/ship; elements). The gap must introduce the first ‘place’ for the triad rather than moral ‘happiness’.
> *¶4 #3 [tentative]:* Pairs naturally with ‘happiness’/Father in ¶5 as ‘God of truth’; keeps the triadic correspondential mapping (Love/Truth/Power) consistent.
> *¶4 #4 [tentative]:* Prepares the reader for the repeated pattern: happiness (source), wisdom (pervading spirit), power (multiplicity of gods/angels).

**¶5** [happiness]ess of the glorious one is the Father, the God of [truth, who] is established in the great land of [light]. ⟨p.24⟩ His glorious wis[d]om is his Great Spirit that [is sent forth from him into the deep] below, which flows through all his aeons, and t[h]ey float therein. His great power is all the gods, the rich ones and the angels who were summoned from him as they [came forth] t]hey that are called aeons [and these are]

> *¶5 #5 [moderate]:* Wisdom here is the Great Spirit as a proceeding/pervading reality ‘below’ (lower discrete degree), flowing through aeons as their medium; thus a verb of procession fits the correspondential function.
> *¶5 #7 [moderate]:* Power is expressed as the multiplicity summoned from the One; ‘came forth’ matches emanational logic better than institutional ‘created’.
> *¶5 #8 [moderate]:* Bridges to the identification clause: those summoned are precisely ‘they that are called aeons’.

**¶6** [Again, there is a greatness] which is called] the sun [which is called the sun, the ship of] living fire, [and in it is established the Third Amba]ssador, the second greatne[dor, the second greatness. The] glorious [happiness] is the [Living] Spirit, [and the wisdom] is the Mother of Lif[ife; and great power] is [all] the gods, [the] rich o]nes and the angels who are within the ship.

> *¶6 #1 [moderate]:* Introduces the next instantiation of the triad in another ‘greatness’ (a standard cosmological step in this material).
> *¶6 #2 [strong]:* Restores the appositional naming: ‘the sun’ = ‘ship of living fire’.
> *¶6 #3 [moderate]:* The Third Ambassador is the indwelling mediator in the solar ship in this system; ‘established’ keeps the impersonal structural tone.
> *¶6 #4 [strong]:* Completes ‘Ambassador’ and the clause ‘the second greatness’.
> *¶6 #7 [strong]:* Maintains the triadic formula: happiness …, wisdom …, power …
> *¶6 #8 [strong]:* Completes ‘Life’ and transitions to the third member of the triad.

**¶7** Again, [h]appiness, wisdom and power exist in the s[ship of liv]ing waters [and they are ordered in it as in a dwelling] the happiness [is the Living Soul,] the Mind of the Father. Also, wisdom [is the Vir]gi]n of [Li]ght. And the power that is [i]n the ship is [all] the go[ds], the [ri]ch ones and the angels who are established i[n it].

> *¶7 #3 [tentative]:* The ‘ship of living waters’ functions as a containing order/realm; this phrase matches the repeated ‘within/established in it’ pattern without later pastoral coloring.
> *¶7 #4 [tentative]:* In the ‘waters’ level, ‘happiness’ is not the Father but a derived life-principle; ‘Living Soul’ coheres with the aqueous correspondence (life in ultimates) and with ‘Mind of the Father’ as inner ruling principle.

**¶8** Again, these three exist in the elements: happiness, wis[d]o]m and power. The happiness is [the Pillar of Glory], the Perfect Man. Wisdom is the [five sons of the] Living Spirit; and great powe[r is gathered from the fi]ve sons of the First Man [ve lights, who are encl]osed and compounded in the totality, that [is held together] while he supports the totality.

> *¶8 #2 [strong]:* Accepted: in the elements the ‘Pillar of Glory/Perfect Man’ is the stabilizing vertical correspondence (support in ultimates).
> *¶8 #3 [strong]:* Accepted: standard pentadic hypostases proceeding from the Living Spirit in this stratum.
> *¶8 #4 [moderate]:* In the elements, ‘power’ is described as a collection/aggregation of the five (a natural correspondence of ‘gathering’/compounding). This also prepares for ‘enclosed and compounded in the totality’.
> *¶8 #5 [moderate]:* The ‘five sons of the First Man’ are frequently expressed as ‘five lights’ in the elemental register; enclosure/mixture with the totality fits the ensuing ‘compounded’.
> *¶8 #6 [moderate]:* Needed before ‘while he supports the totality’: the Pillar’s function is cohesion/stability of the compounded elements.

**Assessment:** Most gaps can be restored at the level of structural correspondences because the chapter is a repeated triadic schema (happiness/wisdom/power) instantiated across discrete cosmic ‘containers’ (Father’s land; sun/ship of fire; ship of waters; elements). The tightest restorations are the formulaic linkers and titles (sun/ship; Third Ambassador; Mother of Life; Virgin of Light; Pillar of Glory; five sons). The weakest points are ¶4 #2–4 (introductory localization phrases) and ¶7 #3–4 (the specific ‘happiness’ term in the waters-ship), where multiple traditional designations could fit; these are therefore tentative.

---

## Chapter 4: Concerning the Four Great Days that have come forth from one another, together with the Four Nights

**¶2** The first great day is the Father, the God of truth, the fir[st-born, who dwells a]idst the aeons of his greatness, in his [l]iving kingdom. The twelve hours of this great day are the twelve great rich Gods of greatness. These, who are the first evocations that he evoked (to mirror) his greatness, he spread out to the four climes, three by three before his face.

> *¶2 #1 [moderate]:* Opening epithet must parallel later day-openers: a primal, pre-institutional designation of the Father as the first principle “dwelling” in the midst of the aeons. “first-born” fits the substrate idiom for the primal source without later ecclesial framing; “who dwells a-” anticipates “midst the aeons…”.

**¶3** The second day is the Third Ambassador, the one who dwells in the light ship. His twelve hours are the twelve virgins that he evoked in his greatness.

**¶4** [The third day is the Pillar of Glory, the great porter that is greater than] all [po]rters; the one that supports all [the universe], those above and those below. Its twelve hours are the five sons of the First Man; the five sons of the Living Spirit, who support all the weights of the universe; together with the summons and the obedience, which are counted to their ten brethren. These are the twelve light hours of the third day.

> *¶4 #1 [strong]:* Accepted as fitting the correspondential structure: the Pillar is the axial support/median that mediates above and below; “porter” is functionally exact in this layer (threshold-keeping, bearing, supporting).
> *¶4 #3 [moderate]:* The Pillar’s function in the oldest layer is cosmic support/axis: it ‘supports all’ in the totality, then specifies “those above and those below.” Hence “all the universe” is the demanded object of support.

**¶5** The fourth day is Jesus the Splendour who [dwells in] his church. His twelve hours are the twelve wisdoms, which are his [light] h[o]u[rs].

**¶7** ⟨p.26⟩ [This is the reckoning of the days and nights]: For just as four day[s ex]ist, s[o] too four nights exist.

> *¶7 #1 [moderate]:* The line introduces the formal correspondential parallel: as there are four days (light-orders), so four nights (dark-orders). A neutral, impersonal heading suits the substrate layer better than exhortation.

**¶8** The first night is the land of Darkness. It has twelve black [shadows] therein, which are its dark h[o]ur[rs]. The twelve shadows of the first night are the five elements of the land of Darkness, [which] poured forth from its five sense organs; and [the five kinds of smoke] which [are mingled and hidden] in its [fi]ve elements and its five [vessels]; the five [sp]irits that dwell in its elements [also]. These are the twelve shadows and spirits of the first night.

> *¶8 #3 [strong]:* Completes “hours” (h[o]ur[s]).
> *¶8 #5 [tentative]:* To reach twelve from the dark triads: (1) five elements + (2) five spirits in the elements already yields ten; the remaining two in this tradition are the ‘smoke/breath’ (dark exhalations) and the ‘fire’ of concupiscence—yet here ‘fire and lust’ are reserved for later nights. So the natural correspondential complement in the first night is the dark exhalative products of the sense-organs, commonly enumerated as ‘smokes’ in this register.
> *¶8 #6 [tentative]:* The clause describes how the added dark products operate within the five elements and their vessels: not yet individualized as ‘worlds’ but diffuse, mingled, latent.
> *¶8 #8 [moderate]:* The five sense-organs ‘pour forth’ into the five elements and their receptacles/vehicles; “vessels” matches correspondential anatomy: organs → elements → vessels as ultimates.
> *¶8 #10 [moderate]:* Smoothly completes: “the five spirits that dwell in its elements also.” The sentence then totals the twelve as ‘shadows and spirits’.

**¶9** The second night is Matter, the sculptress [of the five worlds] who has sculpted [the forms of males and females] and the entire ruling-power that is in the worl[ds of darkness]. She has fashioned it in five sense organs, five [male] and five female, two per world; together with the fire and the lust [that] dwell in men and women, inflaming [them] inward to one another. These are the twelve spirits of this second night.

> *¶9 #1 [strong]:* Matter as sculptress fashions the entire ruling-power distributed through ‘worlds of darkness’; the immediately following sentence counts “two per world,” so the missing phrase must introduce the five-world scheme.
> *¶9 #2 [moderate]:* What Matter ‘sculpts’ in this layer is primarily sexualized form as an instrument of bondage; the next line specifies “five sense organs, five male and five female,” so the missing object is the fashioned male/female forms.

**¶10** Indeed she, this Matter, the thought of death, ga[ve] the King of the realms of Darkness and his powers strength for the war and the fight, against the aeons of greatness. Yet, he was caught! They laid a snare for him through the Fi[rst M]an. [He] was ta[ken] t[the heights] through the powers [and the bonds were loosened from him.] was brought up from the land of death and s[et fastened,]ove and below in the [whole] universe; in the five par[t]s in the heavens above, as well as the five [parts] in the earths below; together with the heat and the cold that are the father and the mother, their fire and their l[u]st.

> *¶10 #5 [moderate]:* After “He was taken to … through the powers …” the direction is upward extraction (release from the land of death) preparatory to being ‘set … above and below’. “the heights” is the demanded opposite of “land of death,” without importing later institutional terms.
> *¶10 #6 [tentative]:* A correspondential necessity: capture in darkness is by bonds/mixture; deliverance is by loosening. This bridges “taken… through the powers” and “brought up from the land of death.”
> *¶10 #7 [moderate]:* The placement “above and below” is a fixing/fastening into the composite as a stabilizing measure; “set fastened” fits the cosmological engineering idiom of this layer.

**¶11** This is the second night that is begotten from the first night. They laid a snare for her through the First M[a]n. She was brought up by the hand of the Living Spirit, and set in this composite univers[e; and they balanced] he[r], above and below. The [upper side is the sense] organs of males; the lower side [is the sense organs of] fe[mal]es.

> *¶11 #2 [moderate]:* The sentence requires a completion after “universe” and before “her, above and below”: it must state the stabilizing/balancing function of setting Matter in the composite cosmos, matching the later “upper side… lower side…” antithesis.
> *¶11 #5 [strong]:* Parallelism demands the mirrored clause: upper = male sense-organs; lower = female sense-organs. The gap precedes “fe[mal]es,” so it must supply “is the sense organs of”.

**¶12** ⟨p.27⟩ The thir[d night is the five] worlds of flesh [... five] male, and five female; that were begotten from the powers [abo]ve. They have fallen upon the earth, being displayed in wh[at is d]ry and what is moist; together with the fire and the lust [that d]we[l]l in them, which drives them inward to one another.

> *¶12 #2 [minimal]:* Too little constraint to restore the intervening descriptive term(s) between “flesh” and “male”; only the numeric scheme is secure.

**¶13** [Mat]te[r] herself, the thought of death, is the [mother] of them all; as among them she is named 'the night'. [The worlds] of male and female fleshes, together with the fire and the l[ust], shall [make up] twelve hours of [this third] night. Again, [it] too, the third night that is in the worlds of fl[e]sh, was begotten from the second night; which the Living [Spir]it crucified in the uni[verse, and fixed]ove and below in heaven [in the five heights and the five depths].

> *¶13 #6 [moderate]:* The clause must express that the male/female flesh-worlds plus fire and lust *constitute* the twelve hours; “make up” is the simplest substrate phrasing for a structural total.
> *¶13 #7 [moderate]:* Completes “twelve hours of … night” with the identifying ordinal; avoids introducing later qualifiers.
> *¶13 #11 [moderate]:* The Living Spirit ‘crucifies’ = extends/fixes in the fourfold to hold the mixture in place; “and fixed” matches the next “above and below…”.
> *¶13 #12 [moderate]:* The text earlier used the same 5+5 architecture (heavens/earths). Here, after “above and below in heaven …” the substrate expects the reiterated fivefold vertical articulation.

**¶14** The fourth night is the law of Sin, which is the dark spirit who speaks in the twelve spirits, the twelve sects. They are the nakedne[ss]es, the twelve zodiacal signs of Matter. They are her thrones; she who is made public, as she sculpts and is displayed in the old man.

**¶15** And, also, the hours of this fourth night that is the old man, who reigns in the sects, are the twelve e[vi]l spirits; the [twelve measures] of the old man, which [are the boundaries that]fferentiate the day and night [in the realm] death.

> *¶15 #2 [moderate]:* The old man ‘reigns in the sects’ through twelve evil spirits; what follows defines them as something that differentiates day and night—i.e., measures/markers (zodiacal-temporal regulators) in the opposite sense.
> *¶15 #3 [moderate]:* The function is to divide/mark: boundaries differentiate day and night (light/dark alternations) in the realm of death. This fits the zodiacal-sign/throne imagery just prior.
> *¶15 #4 [moderate]:* Completes “differentiate the day and night … of death” as a cosmological locale, not a moral exhortation: “realm of death.”

**¶16** This is the fourth night [—four nights] that have come forth of one another. One has [its image in the next] just like the days of lig[ht; and] they manifested one another. So als[o in the darkness] they did arise out of one another.

> *¶16 #1 [moderate]:* Summative statement: the fourth night completes the tetrad; the next clause speaks of “that have come forth of one another,” so naming the set is required.
> *¶16 #2 [tentative]:* The line explains serial derivation: each night produces the next as an image/effect, corresponding to the way days of light ‘manifest’ one another by discrete degrees.
> *¶16 #3 [strong]:* Completes “light” and links to the final clause.
> *¶16 #4 [moderate]:* Balances the comparison: as light-days manifest one another, so also (in the opposite series) the nights arise out of one another in darkness.

**Assessment:** Most single-letter/word lacunae are tight and restored with high certainty. Larger gaps (notably ¶8 #5–6, ¶10 #5–7, ¶12 #2, ¶16 #2) are structurally constrained by the tetradic day/night architecture and the 5+5 cosmic partition, but exact wording remains uncertain; restorations there aim to preserve the oldest, impersonal cosmological register and the correspondential logic (axis/support; mixture/bondage; upward extraction; serial derivation by discrete degrees).

---

## Chapter 5: Concerning Four Hunters of Light and Four of Darkness.

**¶2** The first hunter is the First Man who was sent from the greatness. He threw himself down to the five storehouses of Darkness, he caught and siezed the enmity [and bound it fast] his net also [he spread] out over all the children of Darkness [and gathered them together]. His ship is his four sons who are swathed over his body. The sea is the land of Darkness [as in one shoal]; his net is [his mind] and his powers.

> *¶2 #1 [moderate]:* After the descent the First Man ‘catches’ the enmity; correspondentially the hunter’s first act is constriction/ligation of the hostile will so it can be handled by the net and sons (powers).
> *¶2 #2 [strong]:* A net is ‘cast/spread’ over a domain; the next clause already has ‘out over’.
> *¶2 #3 [moderate]:* The net over the ‘children of Darkness’ aims at a collective capture (bringing scattered powers into one hold).
> *¶2 #4 [tentative]:* Hunting-by-net over a sea commonly yields a ‘shoal/school’ image; correspondentially: many falsities/evils herded into a single constraint.
> *¶2 #5 [moderate]:* In the oldest register ‘net’ = the ordering faculty by which powers are arranged; thus ‘mind’ (the interior form that holds powers in a pattern) plus ‘his powers’.

**¶3** The second hunter is the Third Ambassador. This one, for by his light image, which he revealed to the depths below, he hunted after the entire light that is in all things; as it is established in them. His net is his light image, [with which he encircled] the whole universe and took it prisoner, to this likeness [And his] ship is his light ship. [The sea] is the universe [and its regions] which were hunted after by his net [and are displayed through] his [glorious] image.

> *¶3 #1 [moderate]:* The light-image functions as a net by surrounding/containing the mixed cosmos so the light within it can be taken ‘as established in them’.
> *¶3 #2 [strong]:* The syntax demands transition to the next item in the hunter-correspondence list (ship/sea/net).
> *¶3 #4 [moderate]:* ‘Sea = universe’ is then specified by its ‘regions’/spaces within which the hunted lights are lodged.
> *¶3 #5 [tentative]:* The hunted things are ‘taken prisoner’ and then ‘to this likeness’; they are made manifest by means of the image. This keeps the correspondential function: manifestation/drawing-out by form.

**¶5** The fourth hunter is the great counsel that [is the counsel of life] that lives in the circuit [of the worlds] ⟨p.29⟩ entire universe in it today. Yet, at the end, in the dissolution of the universe, this very counsel of life will gather itself in and sculpt its soul in the Last Statue. Its net is its Living Spirit, because with its Spirit it can hunt after the light and the life that is in all things; and build it upon its body. Its ship, in which it is established, is this light cloud whereby it itself travels in the five elements [of the elements], the great fire that will burn all the buildings of [this universe] in its net is the light and the life. It can rescue and free it from all bonds and fetters.

> *¶5 #1 [moderate]:* The fourth hunter is not a person but a cosmological function: the universal counsel that orders and finally gathers life into the Last Statue.
> *¶5 #2 [moderate]:* ‘Circuit of the worlds’ is the natural container-image for a universal counsel ‘living in the circuit’.
> *¶5 #3 [strong]:* The counsel ‘travels in the five elements’; the next clause explicitly names fire, so the missing genitive naturally identifies the elemental domain.
> *¶5 #4 [moderate]:* The fire burns ‘all the buildings of this universe’; oldest-layer register avoids moralizing and keeps the structural cosmogonic object.

**¶8** The first hunter is the King of they who belong to the Darkness, who hunted after the living soul with his net at the beginning of the worlds. His net is his fire and his lust that he has put upon the living soul, with which he has entangled it [in the beginning], through all his powers.

> *¶8 #1 [moderate]:* The entangling occurs ‘at the beginning of the worlds’; the lacuna resumes that temporal anchor before ‘through all his powers’.

**¶9** [The] second hunter is the evil counsel that lives in [the air] that hunts after the light [that is mixed] and [it draws] up from [from all] the earths to heaven. It binds them with its powers, which bring them to the heavenly worlds above. Its net is [its wind] whereby they are drawn up from the abyss [to the heights]hts.

> *¶9 #2 [tentative]:* The ‘evil counsel’ is placed in a middle region from which it can ‘draw up’ from earths to heaven; in this cosmology the aerial/mid-region is the seat of such upward-drawing mechanisms.
> *¶9 #3 [moderate]:* The target is ‘the light’ specifically insofar as it is ‘mixed’ in the lower compositions; this matches the earlier ‘light that is in all things’.
> *¶9 #4 [strong]:* Parallelism: ‘hunts… and draws up… from… to…’.
> *¶9 #5 [moderate]:* The source is plural ‘the earths’; the preposition phrase needs a universal qualifier before specifying the earths.
> *¶9 #6 [tentative]:* A net that ‘draws up from the abyss to the heights’ corresponds to a drawing force (wind/breath) rather than a static object; ‘wind’ fits the mid-region (air) logic and the verb ‘drawn up’.
> *¶9 #7 [strong]:* Completion of the broken word.

**¶10** The third hunter is lust [that] walks in every power of the flesh that walks [also] in the [members] the living souls [and binds] them in its bodies, which [are fashioned].

> *¶10 #1 [strong]:* Introduces defining clause: ‘lust that walks…’.
> *¶10 #2 [moderate]:* Balances the series of hunters on the Darkness-side: lust ‘also’ operates in every fleshly power.
> *¶10 #3 [moderate]:* ‘Powers of the flesh’ are realized in bodily ‘members’; lust ‘walks’ in them as in instruments.
> *¶10 #4 [moderate]:* The function of lust as hunter is to fasten/ensnare living souls into bodies.
> *¶10 #5 [tentative]:* The bodies are the constructed receptacles; ‘which are fashioned’ keeps the impersonal, structural register (not moral blame) and fits the fragment ‘which …’.

**Assessment:** Most gaps can be restored at the level of correspondential function (net = enclosing form; sea = domain; ship = conveyance/body; hunting = constriction and drawing-out). Exact wording is less tightly constrained where the Coptic Vorlage could have used several near-synonyms (bind/entangle/enclose; regions/spaces; wind/breath). Paragraphs 9–10 remain the least certain because the damaged clauses likely named specific middle-world mechanisms; restorations there follow the oldest-layer cosmological logic (aerial drawing-force; lust binding souls into fashioned bodies) rather than later moralizing phrasing.

---

## Chapter 6: Concerning the Five Storehouses that have poured forth from the Land of Darkness since the [Beginning]; the Five Rulers, the Five Spirits, the Five Bodies, the Five Tastes.

**¶2** Five storehouses have arisen since the beginning in the land of darkness! The five elements poured out of them. Also, from the five elements were fashioned the five trees! Again, from the five trees were fashioned the five genera of creatures in each world, male and female. And the five worlds themselves have five kings therein, and five spirits, five bodies, five tastes; in each world, they not resembling one another!

**¶3** The King of the world of Smoke [is the first ruler] who came up from the depth of darkness; this is he who is the head of all wickedness, and all malignity. The beginning of the spread of the war occured through him; all the battles, fights, quarrelling, dangers, destructions, fights, wrestling-contests! That is the one who first made arise danger and war, with his worlds and his powers. Afterwards, also, he waged war with the light. He pitched a battle with the exalted kingdom.

> *¶3 #1 [moderate]:* The sentence needs an appositive identifying the Smoke-king as the primordial leader emerging from the depth; in the substrate he functions as the first instigator of conflict and so is introduced as the first ruler among the dark worlds.

**¶4** ⟨p.31⟩
Now, regarding the King of Darkness, there are five shapes on him! His head is lion-faced; his hands and feet are demon- and devil-faced; his shoulders are eagle-faced; while his belly is dragon-faced; his tail is fish-faced. These five shapes, the marks of his five worlds, exist on the King of the realms of Darkness!

**¶5** Now, there are five other properties in him. The first is his darkness. The second is his putridity. The third is his ugliness. The fourth is his bitterness, his own soul. The fifth is his burning, which burns like an iron () as if poured out from fire.

**¶6** [There are] also three others in him! The first, that his body is hard and very tough, even as she has formed him [according to her image, hard and] cruel-hearted; namely Matter, who is the thought of death, [o]ne who sculpted him from the nature of the land of darkness. This is the manner of the body of the ruler of Smoke. He is harder than every iron, copper and steel and lead; as there is no cleaver at all, nor any iron implement, can [cleave] him and cut him. For Matter, his fashioner, has formed him [exceedingly] strong and hard.

> *¶6 #2 [moderate]:* The clause explains the bodily property (hard/tough) by reference to Matter as the forming matrix; the substrate speaks of formation ‘according to the image/nature’ of Matter, yielding hardness and cruelty.
> *¶6 #4 [strong]:* After ‘nor any iron implement, can … him and cut him’, the required verb is ‘cleave/sunder’.
> *¶6 #5 [moderate]:* Completes the intensifier before ‘strong and hard’, matching the parallel earlier ‘very tough’.

**¶8** ⟨p.32⟩
The third, that the King of the realms of Darkness knows the converse and language of his five worlds. He understands every thing he hears from their mouths, as they address one another; each one of them in his language. [Every] design they will consider against him, every snare they debate with one another to bring upon him, he knows them! He can also understand the gesturing they signal between one another. Yet, his powers and his rulers, who are subject to him, can not understand his wordy converse. While all these things are unveiled to him; still, their heart is not manifested to him. He knows not their mind nor their thought; he can not ponder their beginning and their end. Rather, he only knows and apprehends what is before his eyes.

**¶9** Also, another different thing is found in the King of the realms of Darkness! For when it pleases him to move, he spreads all his limbs out and walks. When it comes to mind, he with[d]raws his limbs and takes them in, and is rolled (?) to his companions; and he falls to the ground like a grape and a great iron ball! He terrifies by his cry, he is frightful. He frightens his powers with his sound; because when he speaks, being like thunder in the clouds, he resembles the [crashing] of the rocks [when they are split asunder] When he cries out and [roars] and calls [aloud] over his powers, they shall tremble and to[tter a]nd fall under foot; even as some birds would be [frightened before] the bird [at the hawk / or the eagle] and they fall down to the earth. Still, this thing only: he knows not what is far from him, he sees not who is at a distance, nor does he hear him. Rather, whoever is before his face he sees, hearing him and knowing him.

> *¶9 #2 [moderate]:* Thunder-like speech is then compared to the sound produced by stones; ‘crashing’ fits the acoustical correspondence (violent collision = outbreak of strife).
> *¶9 #3 [moderate]:* Completes the comparison: thunder resembles the crashing of rocks when they break; this supports the war/rupture correspondence.
> *¶9 #4 [moderate]:* Pairs naturally with ‘cries out’, marking escalating vocal terror; the substrate emphasizes sound as a power of domination.
> *¶9 #5 [moderate]:* ‘calls … over his powers’ needs an adverb of proclamation/command.
> *¶9 #7 [moderate]:* Bird-simile requires a passive fear-response before a stronger bird; matches ‘tremble and totter and fall’.
> *¶9 #8 [tentative]:* The comparison is to smaller birds falling when confronted by a raptor; hawk/eagle aligns with the dark-world bestial correspondences and the earlier eagle-mark among the shapes.

**¶10** These signs and these evil marks are found in the chief of the demons and fiends, the King of all the mountains of Darkness [who is the chief of them all,] to [whom] the land of darkness has given birth, begotten in its cruelty, in its wickedness and its wrath [from the beginning!]

> *¶10 #1 [moderate]:* The phrase must bridge ‘King of all the mountains of Darkness’ to an identifying epithet; the substrate typically uses a rank-formula (‘chief of them all’) rather than devotional titles.
> *¶10 #3 [moderate]:* The close of the description wants a temporal seal tying his birth to the primordial uprising of Darkness; ‘from the beginning’ matches ¶2–3 framing.

**¶11** ⟨p.33⟩
... more than all his fellow rulers, who are in all his worlds.

**¶12** Gold is the body of the King of the realms of Darkness. The body of all the powers who belong to the world of Smoke is gold. And also, the taste of its fruits is salty.

**¶13** In contrast, the King of the worlds of Fire is lion-faced, the foremost of all the beasts. Copper is his body. Again, the body of all the rulers who belong to Fire is copper. Their taste is the sour taste that is in every form.

**¶14** Again, the King of the worlds of Wind is eagle-face. His body is iron. Also, the body of all they who belong to the Wind is iron. Their taste is the sharp taste that is in every form.

**¶15** For his part, the King of the world of Water is fish-face. His body is silver. All the other rulers who belong to Water, silver is their body. Also, the taste of their fruit is the sweetness of water, the sweet taste that is in every (form).

**¶16** Again, [the Kin]g of the world of Darkness is a dragon. His [body is lead] and tin. All the other rulers [who belong to the world of] Darkness, their body is lead and tin. And also, the taste of their fruits is bitterness. Again, the spirit who reigns in them is the spirit who speaks till today in the soothsayers, giving oracles; in the givers of portents, every type; in they who are possessed; and the other spirits that give oracles, every type.

**Assessment:** Most gaps are small and could be restored by tight syntactic constraints (articles/letters, required verbs). The hardest place is ¶9 (multiple sound/bird similes), where the correspondential direction is clear (terror-sound like thunder/rocks; lesser powers like birds before a raptor) but exact zoological wording is not uniquely constrained; those restorations remain tentative.

---

## Chapter 7: The Seventh, concerning the Five Fathers

**¶2** Five fathers exist; they were summoned forth one of one. [Also], one did come out of another!

**¶3** The first Father is the Father of Greatness, the blessed one of glory, the [on]e who has no measure to his greatness; who also is the first only-begotten, the first eternal; who exists with fiv[e fa]thers for ever; the one who exists before every thing that has existed, and that will exist.

**¶4** Now he, the glorious Father, summoned from him three emanations. The [fi]rst is the Gre[at] Spirit, the first Mother, who came out of the Father. She appeared first. ⟨p.35⟩

**¶5** The seco[nd i]s the Belo[ved of] the Lights, the great glorious Beloved, [the one w]ho is honour[ed; w]ho came out of the Father. [He ma]nifested ou[t of h]im.

**¶6** The third father is the Thir[d Ambassador], the eldest of [a]]] the counsell[ors; who came out of] the first Father. He appeared.

> *¶6 #1 [strong]:* In the oldest substrate the third of the Father’s first triad is the primal Revealer/Envoy: the ‘Third Ambassador’ as mediating counsellor between the Light-realm and the mixed region. The title fits the triadic emanation pattern repeated in ¶8–12.

**¶7** This is the first Father, the first power, the one from whom the three great powers came out. This is the first Father, the first eternal, the root of all the lights; from whom the three emanations came out. They have humiliated the Darkness. They have brought its heart's desire to naught. They have given themselves the victory. They have also given the victory to their aeons.

**¶8** The second father, who came out of the first Father, is the Third Ambassador, the model of the King of lights. And again, he too summoned and sent out of him three powers.

**¶9** One is the Pillar of Glory, the Perfect Man; the one who bears up under all things; the great Pillar of blessing; the great porter, who is greater than all the porters.

**¶10** The second is glorious Jesus the Splendour; [the] one through whom shall be given life eternal.

**¶11** The third is the Virgin of Light, the glorious wisd[o]m; the one who takes away the heart of the [rul]ers and the powers by her image, as she fulfils the pleas[u]re of the greatness.

**¶12** The third father, who came out of the second father: he is glorious Jesus the Splendour. And, again, he too summoned three evocations after the pattern of the second father.

**¶14** The second power whom Jesus summo[n]ed is the gre[at Jud]ge, who gives judgement on all the souls of mankind, [his] dwelling being established in the atmosphere under [the sun / the moon] wheel [twelve] stars.

> *¶14 #4 [moderate]:* The Judge’s ‘dwelling in the atmosphere’ is fixed between the two great luminaries that govern the mixed region’s measures (time/season), i.e., sun and moon. In correspondential structure these are the two chief ‘wheels’ carrying and sorting Light-substance; thus the Judge is placed beneath them, before the starry zone.
> *¶14 #5 [moderate]:* The phrase ‘wheel … stars’ most tightly constrains to the standard cosmological count for the starry governance that follows the luminaries: the twelve (zodiacal) stars/signs as the ordered circuit above the atmosphere. This matches the substrate’s concern with measured order (number) rather than later pastoral imagery.

**¶16** The fourth father is the Light Mind, the one who chooses all the churches. And, again, he too summoned three powers after the pattern of Jesus.

**¶20** And also the fifth father is this Light Form; the one who shall appear to everyone who will go out from his body, corresponding to the pattern of the image to the apostle; and the thr[ee] great glorious angels who are come with her.

**¶22** These are the five fathers who have come out of one another. They have appeared and man[ifes]ted through one another!

**Assessment:** Most lacunae are straightforward letter-restorations constrained by the surrounding word-forms. The only substantial restorations are in ¶14, where the cosmological placement of the Great Judge in the atmospheric region demands the two luminary ‘wheels’ (sun and moon) and then the ordered starry circuit, best expressed by ‘twelve’ (zodiacal) stars/signs. These are moderate-confidence because alternative star-count traditions exist, but the inside→outside correspondential logic (luminaries as primary measures, then the fixed order of the stars) strongly narrows the options.

---

## Chapter 8: Concerning the Fourteen Vehicles that Jesus has boarded.

**¶3** The first vehicle is the light ship, since he received instructions from the Ambassador there.

**¶4** ⟨p.37⟩ The second carriage is the ship of the First Man, since his dwelling is established there.

**¶5** The third is the Pillar of Glory, the Perfect Man, since he shone forth there. He came below, he appeared in the world.

**¶6** The fourth is the summons that he clothed upon his body. The fifth is the obedience that he swathed upon the summons.

**¶7** The sixth is the living air, and since he assumed it too, he received anointment there. The seventh is the living wind that blows. The eighth is the light that illuminates. The ninth is the living water. The tenth is the living fire that is above them all.

**¶8** He was journeying in these ten carriages among all the orders and all the [aeons], those above and those below.

> *¶8 #1 [moderate]:* The list of ten 'carriages' is a cosmological map for the Soul’s traversal of discrete orders of being; the natural term that pairs with 'orders' (ranks) in this substrate register is 'aeons' (eternal domains). The closing apposition 'those above and those below' requires a comprehensive twofold division of the whole structure, which 'orders and aeons' supplies better than institutional or moral categories.

**Assessment:** Only one lacuna; the structure constrains it to a totalizing cosmological domain paired with 'orders' and divisible into 'above' and 'below'. 'Aeons' fits the oldest-layer register and the correspondential architecture, though exact Coptic equivalent cannot be verified from this excerpt alone.

---

## Chapter 9: The Explanation of the Peace, what it is; the Right Hand; the Kiss; the Salutation.

**¶2** You have questioned me about these five lessons. Now, they are displayed in the world as miniatures, being made little; and yet indeed they are great and honoured. I am the one who can reveal to you their mystery! So, these five signs are the mystery of the First Man. He came forth with them from the aeon of light. Also, when he finished his contest, he went up with these good signs. They received him into the aeons of light.

**¶3** ⟨p.38⟩ The first peace is the one that the gods and the angels in the land of light gave to the First Man; when he comes out against the enemy. The gods and the angels were walking with him; escorting him, giving to him their peace and power, and their blessing and fortification. This is the first peace that the gods and the angels gave to the First Man; as he comes forth from the aeon of light.

**¶4** The first right hand is the one that the Mother of Life gave to the First Man; when he comes out to the contest.

**¶5** The first kiss is this one with which the Mother of Life embraced the First Man; as he separates from her, coming down to the contest. Even all the gods and the angels who are in the aeons of light were making to embrace him! [And there embraced him] all of them also, and the church tutelaries (?), and the kinsfolk who belong to the household of his people. They were making to embrace him as [he] separates from them, accompanying him out, embracing him with the kiss of affection and love.

> *¶5 #1 [moderate]:* The list of those embracing the First Man continues: after the gods and angels, the next clause needs a verb to resume the action before enumerating further households/kin. A simple continuative phrase in the impersonal cosmological register fits best.

**¶6** ⟨p.39⟩ The first salutation is the salutation with which the First Man made obeisance; when he comes out [to the depths] below. He was bending his knees, making obeisance to the God of truth, and all the aeons [of light] who belong to the household of his people; entreating them for a power to escort him, as he comes forth.

> *¶6 #1 [strong]:* Fixed by sense: his obeisance occurs at the moment of descent toward the lower region.

**¶7** The first laying on of hands is the one that the Mother of Life bequeathed upon the head of the First Man. She armed him and made him mighty. She laid hands on him, and sent him to the battle. He descended. He completed his struggle with the great powers and the enmity.

**¶8** By the [se] five signs and these five myst[e]ri[es] the First Man came forth from the aeons of light against the enemy. He humbled him. He was victorious against him by them.

**¶9** Also, when he ascended from the war, he came in to the kingdom of the household of his people by these five mysteries!

**¶10** The peace with which [he ascen]ded is the peace that the summons gave to him, [w]hen it was sent forth from the [Fa]ther of Life. It gave him the peace in the world of darkness. Also, that peace with which he descended is th[e sam]e as the one with which he ascended from the struggle! Therefore, appropriate to the my[s]tery of that peace, which the summons gave the First Man, did this peace here and now come about. It was named and heard among the powers of the Father.

**¶11** Again, this second right hand is the one that the Living Spirit gave the First Man, when he [brou]ght him upward out from the struggle. Appropriate to [the] mystery [of t]hat right hand this right hand has come about; the one that occurs in the [midst of] mankind, as they give it to their companions, [it b]eing honoured and [in power]

> *¶11 #6 [moderate]:* The sentence explains why the human custom corresponds to the cosmic act: the right hand among humans is honoured as a sign of transmitted strength/authority, matching the Living Spirit’s raising of the First Man. A brief completion indicating the quality being honoured fits the substrate logic better than later ecclesial phrasing.

**¶12** Also, this second kiss is the one with which the [Father o]f Life and the Mother of the living embraced the First Man, when he ascended from the struggle. Again, this kiss occurs among mankind, as they make to embrace their companions with it; whether then [they] go away from home and be far from their friends, or else [if] they approach one another, according to the mystery of (the First Man).
