**¶13** ⟨p.40⟩ [The] second salutation with which the First Man made obeisance [at] time to the God of truth and [all] the blessed aeons and al[l] the aeons [in] whole [land] of light, whe[n] the Father of Life and the Mother of the living brought the Man upward from the abyss of the struggle. Appropriate to the mystery of the second salutation this salutation has come about; this one that occurs today in the midst of mankind, as they make obeisance to one another, doing an honour to their companions.

> *¶13 #2 [moderate]:* The clause needs a temporal marker: “with which the First Man made obeisance … at this time…”, paralleling ¶6 and matching the ascent-context given later in the sentence.
> *¶13 #3 [moderate]:* After “to the God of truth and … the blessed aeons”, the structure demands an inclusive term before “the blessed aeons”, yielding a complete triad: God of truth + (all) blessed aeons + all aeons/whole land of light.
> *¶13 #5 [moderate]:* Syntax: “and all the aeons [in] the whole land of light” is the natural completion, keeping the correspondential totality formula.

**¶14** The second laying on of hands is this: When the Living Spirit brought the First Man upward from the war, he saved him from all the surging waves. He brought him upward, he gave him ease in the great aeons of light, which belong to the household of his people. He set [him] firmly before the Father, the Lord of the totality. He received this great laying on of hands that he may become leader of his brethren in the new aeon. Again, appropriate to the mystery of the second laying on of hands, this laying on of hands has come about; the one that occurs in the midst of mankind as they lay hands on one another, the great giving authority to the lesser.

**¶15** These five mysteries and these five signs came about first in the divine. Mankind has been taught them; and they have instituted them in their midst [— yet] these mysteries were not at the beginning amongst the powers of darkness.

> *¶15 #1 [moderate]:* Contrast required: humans instituted these mysteries, yet they were not originally among the dark powers. “— yet” preserves the impersonal, non-pastoral register better than an explanatory ‘but then’.

**¶20** Once again: At the time of their coming forth, the Light Form shall come forth before them; and she redeems them from the darkness to the light [from the surging of the darkness.] This Light Form calms the person, with the kiss and her quiet, from fear of the demons w[ho de]stroy his body. By her asp[ec]t and her image [the] heart of the elect one, who is come forth from his bo[d]y, shall be calm for [him]. Afterwards, the angel who h[o]lds the victory prize extends to him the right hand. And it draws him out of the abyss of his body, and accepts him in with the [k]iss and love. That soul shall make obeisance to its red[eem]er, who is this Light Form. And also, at the inst[he is separated from the body,] he shall be perfected and increased according to [its rank / its measure] in the household of the living ones, with the go[ds] and the angel[s] and all the apostles and the chosen. [An]d he receives the crown [of] glory in the life for ever.

> *¶20 #1 [moderate]:* The Light Form’s role at departure is protective and pacifying; the missing phrase should name what she redeems them from beyond the simple ‘darkness to light’. ‘Surging’ echoes ¶14’s ‘surging waves’ and keeps the correspondential image of tumult in the lower nature.
> *¶20 #10 [moderate]:* The sequence describes post-mortem rites in order (appearance of Light Form → calming → right hand → drawing out → kiss → obeisance). The next needed hinge is the moment of full separation/perfection, before being ‘perfected and increased’ in the household of the living.
> *¶20 #11 [moderate]:* The increase ‘according to …’ in the household of the living demands correspondential criteria: degree of reception determines place. ‘Rank/measure’ expresses discrete degrees without importing later moralizing language.
> *¶20 #15 [moderate]:* The close requires “crown of … glory”. The simplest completion is the genitive link ‘of’, leaving the transmitted wording intact: ‘crown of glory in the life for ever.’

**Assessment:** Most gaps are minor letter-restorations fixed by the Coptic context. The substantial restorations were constrained by the correspondential structure: (1) the descent/ascent symmetry of the five signs, (2) the totality-formula for the land/aeons of Light, and (3) the psychopomp sequence in ¶20 (appearance → calming → right hand → extraction → kiss → obeisance → assignment by degree). A few places remain only moderately tight because the exact Coptic connective/verb could vary without changing the underlying cosmological sense.

---

## Chapter 10: Concerning the Interpretation of the Fourteen [Great Aeons] of Light, about which Sethel has spoken in [his Prayer]

**¶3** ⟨p.43⟩ Happen you know this, that the [powers] below abide in fourteen [great aeons of Light]: the five elements, the sons of the First Man, together with the call and the hearing. They anoint them, living in them. These shall make seven, five plus two.

> *¶3 #1 [moderate]:* The fourteen “below” are the supporting hypostases that sustain the cosmos; in the oldest register these are best named as abiding ‘powers’ (not moral agents). The list that follows (five elements + five sons + call + hearing) is a catalogue of operative powers.
> *¶3 #2 [strong]:* The context is the fourteenfold establishment of the cosmos by Light-realities; ‘of Light’ keeps the impersonal cosmological register and matches ¶5’s ‘aeons of light’.

**¶4** [These are] f[ive]ons of the Living Spirit who [bear] all [weight]ght of the universe; together with the [Li]vi[ng] Spirit [and the First]st Man, their fathers. These are the fourt[een pr]raises that he has proclaimed, the praise that is made of fourteen pra[ayers], by which the universe is established.

> *¶4 #1 [strong]:* This line resumes the enumeration from ¶3 and introduces the second heptad (sons of the Living Spirit etc.).
> *¶4 #3 [moderate]:* Underlying sense: they carry/hold up the cosmic load; ‘bear’ is the natural correspondent of spiritual support.
> *¶4 #7 [strong]:* Completes the pair of fathers (Living Spirit + First Man) in the standard cosmogonic ordering of this layer.

**¶5** [There are fourt]een aeons of light [as they stand ordered in the prayer] of Seth [h] ... ] about them in his prayer, for they are expl[ai]ned [in the land]nd that is not revealed. This land [in which their order is established]lished. They bore up under the enemy [for nothing equals]ls them. The hidden plenitude that [concealed within it] the Ambassador who came from the Father, the [revealed through] He came and stood firm in the midst of these [made manifest in]: he is called the good father of [all] the ae[ons and gods] who are holy and conjoined. He has become leader and a great king after the likeness of the first Father, the lord of all these cou[se]ls. This universe is held together by their will [and by their counsel it endures] to the end of all things.

> *¶5 #2 [moderate]:* The paragraph explains a fourteenfold structure ‘explained … in his prayer’; the gap most naturally links the aeons with their liturgical/cosmic ordering without introducing later devotional tone.
> *¶5 #3 [tentative]:* Fits ‘Seth h…’ → ‘Seth himself’/‘Seth has…’; the next lacuna supplies the continuation.
> *¶5 #5 [moderate]:* The phrase continues ‘… explained … the land that is not revealed’; ‘in the land’ keeps the cosmological ‘place/region’ sense (a hidden realm/pleroma) rather than moralizing language.
> *¶5 #6 [moderate]:* The hidden land is the containing plane where the fourteenfold order is fixed; ‘order’ is demanded by the discourse of aeons/praises/prayers as structuring principles.
> *¶5 #7 [moderate]:* After ‘They bore up under the enemy …’ the next clause contrasts the enemy’s assault with the incomparability of the Light-order; ‘nothing equals them’ fits the rhetoric and cosmological hierarchy.
> *¶5 #8 [moderate]:* Refers back to the ‘hidden plenitude’ as an interior fullness of the unrevealed land/pleroma.
> *¶5 #9 [moderate]:* Prepares for the naming of the mediating figure (Ambassador) as the mode of manifestation of the hidden plenitude.
> *¶5 #10 [moderate]:* Balances the previous clause: the plenitude is ‘revealed/made manifest’ in the Ambassador, preserving correspondential inside→outside flow (hidden fullness → manifested envoy).
> *¶5 #14 [moderate]:* The close stresses cosmic cohesion by will/counsel until the end; ‘counsel’ corresponds to governing truths (spiritual deliberations) that hold ultimates together.

**Assessment:** Most lacunae are formulaic seams in a fourteenfold cosmological catalogue (elements/sons/call-hearing; sons of the Living Spirit; fourteen praises/prayers). The largest uncertainties are in ¶5 where the damaged text likely contained a tighter chain linking the unrevealed land, its established order, the enemy’s pressure, and the manifestation of the hidden plenitude through the Ambassador; restorations there are correspondentially constrained but not verbally certain.

---

## Chapter 11: [Concerning the Interpretation of] all [the] Fathers of [Light], who are distinguished from one another.

**¶2** The beginning of each blessing and every prayer is the Mother of [L]ife, the first Holy Spirit.

**¶3** Also, [the] beginning [of] every good [co]unsel is the T[h]rd Am[assador]or, the king of the glorious realm that lies in this [wo]rld, [the image] of the King of the lights.

> *¶3 #4 [moderate]:* Coptic orthography here most naturally yields “Third Ambassador” without forcing a Greek/Christianizing spelling; keep the substrate’s fixed cosmological title.
> *¶3 #5 [strong]:* Restores “Ambassador” as the hypostatic mediator/manifestation of the Light-King’s counsel within the mixed world (a correspondential ‘sending forth’).
> *¶3 #7 [moderate]:* The Third Ambassador is regularly the “image/form” set in the world as the visible presence of the King of Lights; correspondentially, counsel (inner) begins from its outmost appearing (image) that governs order in the mixed realm.

**¶4** The beginning of [every glory and] each [hono]ur (?) is the Beloved of the Lights, who is honour[ed.

> *¶4 #1 [moderate]:* The series is formulaic (“beginning of X and each Y”); here the pair that fits honouring is “glory … honour,” i.e., splendour and its acknowledgment in ultimates.

**¶5** The beginning of a]ll [all the trappers] and hunters is the First [Man.

> *¶5 #1 [moderate]:* Retain the hunting/trapping image: in the oldest register these are not moral ‘sinners’ but powers/arts that seize and draw out; the First Man is the first ‘hunter’ by going out to meet Darkness and taking its assault into ordered combat.

**¶6** ⟨p.44⟩
... of] all fighters is [the] Living [Spirit], the Father of Life, who has distribu[ted about] his five sons from place to place.

**¶7** The beginning of all the archit[e]cts and builders is the Great Builder, who is glorious.

**¶8** Also, the first of all the porters of the gr[e]a[t]ness is the Pillar of Glory ... [of the greatness]

> *¶8 #3 [moderate]:* Balances the line: the Pillar of Glory is the primordial “porter/bearer” precisely because it bears up and conveys the Greatness between regions; the phrase echoes the title just given and keeps the impersonal cosmological register.

**¶9** The beginning of [all] the deliverers [is Jesus the Splendour]; or the one who delivers, as he frees whoever belongs [to him] from his words.

> *¶9 #2 [strong]:* Accept as a stable technical name in the cosmological list: the Deliverer-function is the light-redeeming agency that loosens bonds in the mixture (a correspondential ‘leading out’).
> *¶9 #3 [moderate]:* The deliverer “frees whoever belongs to him” (i.e., what is of his own nature/portion) from the binding force of hostile words/commands; this keeps the substrate’s impersonal notion of belonging by nature rather than faith-language.

**¶10** The beginning of every wisdom of truth is the Virgin of Light, [.

**¶11** The beginning of] all [the great]est honoured ones [of the aeons] is the Light Mind; who is the awakene[r of the] who sleep, the gatherer in of the ones who are sc[att]ered.

> *¶11 #2 [moderate]:* The “greatest honoured ones” are most coherently the highest orders/regions (aeons) rather than moral exemplars; Light Mind is their principle because mind/truth orders and awakens all degrees.
> *¶11 #3 [strong]:* Grammatically required for “awakener of the ones who sleep”; keeps the fixed paired functions (awakener/gatherer).

**Assessment:** Most gaps are short and formulaic and can be restored with high confidence. The main uncertainties are the larger semantic lacunae in ¶3, ¶4, ¶8, ¶9, and ¶11; these were filled by maintaining the correspondential list-structure (each cosmic office as ‘beginning’ of a natural function) and by selecting titles/phrases that keep the impersonal cosmological register of the oldest substrate.

---

## Chapter 12: Concerning the Interpretation of the Five Words that are proclaimed in the Universe

**¶2** [They] call it by this name is the Father, the l[ife] this unnameable, whom no one [knows] his name.

> *¶2 #1 [strong]:* The syntax requires a plural subject for “call it by this name”; the impersonal cosmological register typically uses “they” for the tradition’s speakers/knowers.
> *¶2 #3 [strong]:* The clause “whom no one ___ his name” is a fixed apophatic formula in this substrate: the First Principle is unnameable and unknowable; “knows” fits both grammar and correspondential sense (name = quality/essence).

**¶3** ⟨p.45⟩
The second [word is the Third]d Ambassador [the tree] of life, whom they also call the father of [life, who is after] [in the order of] the first Father.

> *¶3 #1 [strong]:* This is a standard triadic listing: after the Father (First) comes the Second term, here identified as the “Third Ambassador” in Manichaean/Coptic transmission. Restoring the phrase yields a coherent heading: “The second word is the Third Ambassador …”.
> *¶3 #2 [moderate]:* “Third Ambassador … of life” in this tradition generates/establishes the living structure (axis/Tree of Life) by which powers are ordered. “Father of the tree of life” is the most constrained completion before the following ellipsis.
> *¶3 #3 [tentative]:* The next phrase must link this hypostasis back to the First Father by order (discrete degrees): the Third Ambassador proceeds “after” the First Father while still being ‘of life’. This restores the missing relational clause without importing later devotional language.
> *¶3 #4 [tentative]:* To avoid a personal/filial preaching tone (“son of”), the oldest substrate typically expresses procession as rank/order. “in the order of the first Father” preserves the correspondential structure: second term derived according to the First’s degree.

**Assessment:** ¶2 is tightly constrained by the apophatic formula about the unnameable Father and restores securely. ¶3 preserves a recognizable triadic cosmological enumeration (Father → Third Ambassador as second term), but the internal appositions (“father of …”) are only moderately constrained; restorations there are guided by standard correspondential functions (life as ordered structure/axis) and by keeping the impersonal substrate register.

---

## Chapter 13: Concerning the Five Saviours, the Resurrectors of Those Who Are Dead, Together with the Five Resurrections

**¶2** ... i]t therein [wherein they have been laid low].

> *¶2 #1 [tentative]:* ¶2 is concluding a statement about lives/powers being in something and needing a rising; the substrate idiom is spatial and impersonal (“laid low” in mixture/bondage), not moralizing. This sets up the list of “resurrectors” as successive modes/agents of re-ordering.

**¶3** The second resurrector is [the Living Spirit].

> *¶3 #1 [moderate]:* In the cosmological sequence the “second” after the primal initiator is the Living Spirit, whose function is to raise out what has fallen by constructing ordered supports (world-structure) and beginning separations. Fits the register: a named hypostasis, not a clerical title.

**¶4** And the third resurrector is the Father, the First [established one], because by his word shall all lives [be] gather in and go to him, and they receive [their raiment of Light] and rest.

> *¶4 #1 [strong]:* The Father is repeatedly defined in this layer as the “First established one” (the fixed, self-subsistent root of order). The phrase is resumed in ¶6, so restoring it here tightens the parallelism.
> *¶4 #2 [moderate]:* By his word all lives “shall be gathered” (performative word/order). A simple verbal support is required before “gather in and go to him.”
> *¶4 #3 [moderate]:* What is received at the Father in this substrate is not ‘forgiveness’ but reinvestiture: the lives regain their proper Light-form/garment and thus “rest” in their own degree.

**¶5** The fourth resurrector is the Light Mind, because [he awakens the remembrance within them and turns their thought upward]. ⟨p.46⟩

> *¶5 #1 [moderate]:* Light Mind’s resurrecting work is interior: reorientation of mind/gnosis, the stirring of remembrance that loosens the binding forms. This keeps the impersonal correspondential logic (mind → awakening/turning) without later devotional coloring.

**¶6** The fifth resurrector to the good [lives] is the Father, the First established one [by his firmness]. [He gathers] all the powers, and he stands firm [in the boundary of Light]. He shall unveil the [seal] over them [and they know their own], and he draws them to him [from the midst of mixture], his will [works within them], and he [makes them stand] them, and he stands them up [in their own place] there, and they remain until [the great separation is completed].

> *¶6 #1 [strong]:* The phrase “to the good …” expects the class being raised; throughout this unit it is “lives” (living powers) rather than ‘souls’ in a later ecclesial sense.
> *¶6 #2 [moderate]:* Reasserts the Father’s defining property in this layer: stability/establishment as the ground for re-gathering.
> *¶6 #3 [strong]:* The next clause is “all the powers,” so the governing verb must be a συλλογίζεσθαι-type action: gathering/collecting into order.
> *¶6 #4 [tentative]:* The Father ‘stands firm’ not in a place of mixture but at the fixed limit/term of the Light-realm; “boundary” is the correspondential term for the outermost of a kingdom where separation is effected.
> *¶6 #5 [moderate]:* What is ‘unveiled’ over powers in this cosmology is the seal/sign of their own region (mark of belonging and protection), not a sermon or decree. The seal is a boundary-action in correspondential terms.
> *¶6 #6 [tentative]:* Unveiling the seal has the effect of recognition—each power perceives its proper form and degree, enabling ascent/collection.
> *¶6 #7 [moderate]:* The drawing is explicitly extraction: the powers are pulled out of the mixed region (Darkness/compound) into their proper side.
> *¶6 #8 [moderate]:* “his will …” needs a verb of operation/impressing: the Father’s will acts as formative force, not as command; this is substrate-cosmological causality.
> *¶6 #9 [strong]:* Parallels “resurrector”: to resurrect is literally to ‘stand up’ what was fallen; the text then continues “and he stands them up … there,” reinforcing the same root action.
> *¶6 #10 [moderate]:* The ‘there’ is not a generic heaven but each power’s proper station (correspondential: place = state/degree).
> *¶6 #11 [moderate]:* The terminal condition for remaining is not personal judgment but the completion of the cosmic separation/ordering—classic Iranian-Manichaean substrate telos.

**Assessment:** Most gaps can be restored by the internal correspondential sequence: successive resurrectors (hypostases) act by (1) structural extraction/separation and (2) interior awakening/recognition, culminating in reintegration at the Father as the First Established. The tightest constraints were the recurring epithet “First established one” and the required verbs around “all the powers.” Several place/term phrases (boundary, mixture, separation) remain slightly underdetermined in exact wording, but the substrate register and cosmological logic constrain the sense strongly.

---

## Chapter 14: The Interpretation [of] the S[i]lence, the Fast, [the Peace], the Day, [and] the Rest; [what] they are.

**¶2** [Concerning] the gatherer in [the ship of living waters] then he shall cau[se] the body [and purify it from] all the lusts of the world [and from] of the body in righteousness [so that the mixture of the body] is the Pill[a]r of Gl[or]y [the one who bears up the lights] because [it gathers them] ⟨p.47⟩ [from the world-mixture] up to the [limit of the height]

> *¶2 #1 [moderate]:* Opening formula introducing a cosmological function; impersonal expository register fits the substrate better than a personal/hagiographic lead-in.
> *¶2 #2 [moderate]:* The gatherer is consistently situated in/with the vessel of the Living Waters in this teaching-complex; this also anticipates ¶3’s explicit mention of the ship.
> *¶2 #4 [moderate]:* The gatherer’s action is to draw up and cleanse: separating mixed substance from the bodily/erotic influxes (opposite-sense ‘lusts’ as adhesive forces of mixture).
> *¶2 #5 [moderate]:* Balances the two sources of bondage just named: bodily impulses and worldly lusts, i.e., internal and external excitations that thicken embodiment.
> *¶2 #6 [moderate]:* Makes explicit the correspondential target: the ‘body’ as the composite plane where mixture occurs; the gatherer acts upon that composite to render it fit for ascent.
> *¶2 #9 [moderate]:* Defines the Pillar’s function in the oldest register: not ‘glory’ as praise but as the luminous supporting axis that carries the separated lights upward through discrete degrees.
> *¶2 #10 [moderate]:* Keeps the mechanical/cosmological tone: the Pillar is an instrument of collection and ascent, not a devotional object.
> *¶2 #11 [moderate]:* Correspondentially required as the source-region from which ‘lights’ are extracted: the mixed cosmos (world) where lusts and body bind.
> *¶2 #12 [tentative]:* Completes the ascent trajectory: the Pillar draws up ‘to the limit’ (boundary) of the upper region, matching Iranian/Manichaean boundary-cosmology without naming later ecclesial terms.

**¶3** The one they call [the Helmsman] it is the [Fi]rst [M]an who dwel[ls i]n the ship of living waters [and directs the] li]ght [of l]ight] he is c[alled the Mind of L]ight] that they have [received from the height] glorious [powers]

> *¶3 #1 [tentative]:* The ‘one they call … it is the First Man’ points to a functional title: the First Man as the steering/intellectual directive within the vessel of Living Waters (guiding the ordered circulation of light).
> *¶3 #5 [tentative]:* Keeps the nautical/cosmological correspondence: the First Man ‘dwells’ in the ship and therefore governs its course—an ordering function rather than a salvific sermon.
> *¶3 #6 [strong]:* Restores the expected object after the vessel context: the ship’s content/cargo is ‘light’; the broken trace ‘li]ght’ strongly constrains this.
> *¶3 #7 [tentative]:* Old substrate favors functional-intellectual naming: First Man as the luminous Mind/intellect operative within the waters; the broken ‘he is c[alled ...]’ suggests a title phrase with ‘Light’.
> *¶3 #8 [tentative]:* Provides the upstream origin of what ‘they have … glorious …’: light/mind is not self-generated in the mixed world but received from the upper boundary/treasury.
> *¶3 #9 [moderate]:* Most economical completion for ‘that they have … glorious …’: the ‘powers’ (luminaries/forces) belonging to the First Man’s array; avoids later institutional terms.

**¶4** [Again,] the enlighten[er who they cal]l it 'day' [and they] uni]verse as they shall [for it enlightens the] in the great aeon [whole] are spread in the universe, it is [be established] established in the [and its measures] and the so[u]ls [are lifted up in their courses]

> *¶4 #1 [moderate]:* Marks a new explanatory unit without introducing dialogue or authority; fits the stripped core style.
> *¶4 #3 [moderate]:* Links the act of naming (‘call it day’) to the teachers/knowers as a collective, consistent with impersonal exposition.
> *¶4 #4 [moderate]:* Supplies the causal ground for the name ‘day’: day corresponds to illumination (truth made manifest) flowing into the lower expanse.
> *¶4 #5 [strong]:* Completes ‘uni]verse’: ‘whole universe’ is the expected collocation and supports the cosmic scope of the enlightener’s operation.
> *¶4 #6 [tentative]:* Balances the later clause ‘it is … established’: describes the fixing of ordered structures in the great aeon after illumination.
> *¶4 #7 [tentative]:* Correspondentially: illumination yields order, number, and measure; ‘spread in the universe’ naturally leads to ‘measures’/‘orders’ being established.
> *¶4 #9 [tentative]:* Completes ‘the so[u]ls …’ with the cosmological motion-theme: souls/lights ascend by ordered ‘courses’ once the structures/measures are established; avoids later penitential language.

**Assessment:** Only short fragments are preserved, so most restorations beyond single-letter completions are constrained mainly by the correspondential structure: (1) a ‘gatherer’ who purifies and separates from mixture, (2) the Pillar of Glory as the axial instrument bearing lights upward to a boundary, (3) the First Man as the directing intellect within the ship of Living Waters, and (4) an ‘enlightener’ whose illumination constitutes ‘day’ and establishes cosmic measures and ordered courses. Exact lexical choices remain tentative where the Coptic traces are absent.

---

## Chapter 15: [Concerning the ... ...] Five [Parts ...] Worlds of [...]

**¶2** ⟨p.48⟩ [When the earth of darkness rose up] it moved it and [when it perceived the Light] they that are in the [regions] of the earth of darkness [this is the] first part.

> *¶2 #1 [moderate]:* The sequence describes the initial stirring of the dark land before the later ‘gushing’ of elements in ¶3. The oldest substrate typically begins with the uprising/stirring of the Dark Earth as a mass.
> *¶2 #2 [moderate]:* ‘It moved it’ points to an external stimulus; in the cosmological logic Darkness is set in motion upon sensing/encountering the opposed region.
> *¶2 #3 [moderate]:* Those ‘in the … of the earth of darkness’ are its internal districts/worlds; ‘regions’ matches the impersonal cosmographic register.
> *¶2 #4 [strong]:* The clause ends ‘first part.’ This needs a brief demonstrative/labeling link: ‘this is the first part.’

**¶3** Yet, after a long time, as there was not [any outlet], it gushed and streamed up [from its depth] the five elements of death that gushed [from it] which are smoke, fire, wind, water and darkness [from it] the second time [and then] they were sculpted [into forms] those in which death was established [into the worlds].

> *¶3 #1 [moderate]:* The gushing occurs because the substance had no vent/exit; this matches the pressure-to-efflux correspondential logic used to introduce the five elements of Death.
> *¶3 #2 [moderate]:* The elements ‘gushed and streamed up’ from below; ‘depth’ is the standard cosmological source-location for the Dark Earth’s efflux.
> *¶3 #3 [strong]:* Refers back to the Dark Earth as source of the five death-elements.
> *¶3 #4 [strong]:* Second reference to the same source; the text doubles the causal statement in a formulaic way.
> *¶3 #5 [moderate]:* Bridges to the ‘second time’ phase; simple temporal connective suits the schematic enumerations.
> *¶3 #6 [moderate]:* ‘They were sculpted … those in which death was established’ requires the notion of formation/figuration from elemental efflux into structured vessels.
> *¶3 #7 [moderate]:* Death becomes ‘established’ in the constructed domains; ‘worlds’ anticipates ¶4–¶5’s ‘five worlds’.

**¶4** The fourth part is the evil fruits [that ripened] in the five trees that are in the five worlds of the land of darkness.

> *¶4 #1 [moderate]:* ‘Evil fruits’ in the five trees implies a maturation/producing action; ‘ripened’ keeps the botanical correspondence without moralizing.

**¶5** The fifth time it spread itself out to its creatures that exist in the five worlds; the ones that were formed and generated from the five fruits of death. They are the five worlds of flesh, the five creations of darkness in which death has been established. It spread itself out to ten parts, five male parts and five female [parts].

> *¶5 #1 [strong]:* The sentence already says ‘ten parts, five male parts and five female …’; the missing word is the repeated noun completing the parallelism.

**¶6** Now, like the land of darkness, in that its spirit and its [breath] they nourished the five elements [that are in it]; this is also [how] the ten parts that are in male and female, the fire and lust of the flesh [nourish] them, as they yoke them with one another [are kindled in] they were established against the First Man. This is [the mystery] of the darkness, it was condemned for ever.

> *¶6 #1 [moderate]:* Darkness’ ‘spirit and its breath’ is a standard pair: internal animating principle (spirit) and its proceeding activity (breath), which ‘nourished’ the elements.
> *¶6 #2 [moderate]:* Completes ‘they nourished the five elements …’; the nourishment applies to the elements resident within the dark realm.
> *¶6 #4 [moderate]:* Parallelism: as spirit/breath ‘nourished’ the five elements, so the ten parts (male/female) ‘nourish’ fire and lust in the flesh.
> *¶6 #5 [moderate]:* Fire and lust are not merely present but ignited/activated ‘in’ the flesh by the male/female parts yoked together.
> *¶6 #6 [moderate]:* ‘This is … of the darkness’ requires an abstract heading-word; ‘mystery’ matches the technical, non-devotional usage continued in ¶7 (‘Alongside the mystery…’).

**¶7** Alongside the mystery of these twelve parts that came about in the darkness [they too were established] against the First Man. The twelve [parts are] the twelve spirits of error that came about from the twelve signs of the zodiac [and were established against the First Man].

> *¶7 #1 [moderate]:* Maintains the repeated formula ‘were established against the First Man’ by stating the emergence/setting of these twelve parts in the same hostile orientation.
> *¶7 #2 [strong]:* Needed to make a complete identification: ‘The twelve parts are the twelve spirits of error…’
> *¶7 #3 [moderate]:* Restores the closing hostile function, matching the refrain in ¶6–¶7 and keeping the impersonal cosmological cadence.

**Assessment:** Most gaps are recoverable because the chapter is a schematic enumeration (first part/second time/fourth part/fifth time) with repeated formulae (‘five worlds’, ‘death was established’, ‘against the First Man’). Exact particles are occasionally uncertain, but the correspondential structure constrains the main missing nouns and verbs (outlet/depth, formation into worlds, nourishment/kindling, and the identification of the twelve parts with zodiacal spirits of error).

---

## Chapter 16: [Concerning the Five] Greatnesses who [went forth] against the Darkness.

**¶1** “[When the assault came upon] the darkness, it was defeated by five greatnesses.

> *¶1 #1 [moderate]:* Opening is the standard cosmogonic hinge: the onset of the assault of Darkness and its defeat by the five Greatnesses; impersonal, structural phrasing suits the oldest layer.

**¶2** The first greatness is the Father of Greatness who exists in calm and hiddenness; as he is established in his light-earth, in his own essence.

**¶3** Four greatnesses were manifested and came forth of him. They were appointed to ten parts, and established in total twelve.

**¶4** The second greatness poured out and was manifested. It was revealed from the first greatness. It is the Mother of Life, the Great Spirit, together with the First Man and his five sons; these are the second greatness.

**¶5** ⟨p.50⟩
The third greatness is the Beloved of the Lights, the Great Builder, the Living Spirit, and the five sons of the Living Spirit. These are the third greatness.

**¶6** The fourth greatness is the Third Ambassador, and the Pillar of Glory, and all the light powers who were revealed from him. These are the fourth greatness.

**¶7** The fifth greatness who came from the Father is Jesus the Splendour, and all his powers whom he summoned, his emanations who were [revealed from him and established] They poured out and were manifested from him.

> *¶7 #1 [moderate]:* The fifth greatness is Jesus the Splendour with his powers/emanations; they must be described as ‘revealed/established’ from him before ‘poured out and were manifested’.

**¶8** This is, so that they were spread out and [It is in this way] fives: these five [sons] of the First Man [who are spread out to five places, supporting] the entire building; another five are the five sons of the Living Spirit who are spread out to five places, supporting [the entire building]

> *¶8 #1 [moderate]:* Bridges from list of five Greatnesses into their spatial ‘spreading out’ as fives in the cosmos.
> *¶8 #3 [moderate]:* These are the five sons/armours of the First Man as cosmic supports; parallels the next clause about the five sons of the Living Spirit spread to five places.
> *¶8 #4 [strong]:* The correspondential role is architectural: the fives function as supports of the constructed cosmos.

**¶9** [Thus they are established] total in twelve parts that [In this way they are] another two added to them who are the summons and the obedience. They are the father and the mother whom Jesus the Splendour by his advent has appointed as successors to his place [are established] the great earth and the [to rule over] in them.

> *¶9 #1 [moderate]:* Summative transition into the arithmetic (ten parts + two = twelve).
> *¶9 #2 [moderate]:* Introduces the count: ten parts, then two added.
> *¶9 #3 [moderate]:* Needed to complete ‘twelve parts that …’ with a verb of fixing/ordering (old-layer cosmic ordering).
> *¶9 #4 [moderate]:* The summons and obedience are appointed as successors to Jesus’ place to ‘rule/stand over’ the great earth and the elements in them.

**¶10** [The advent is like this:] of the First Man who came [as a noble man] belonging to [from his father’s house] existing in a kingdom [who is living honourably] esteem before they who belong to his household [and has] his enemies [great] and suffering by their wickedness; and they take from him [come upon him] and a richness; and they imprison [him in] captivity and take from him their companies (?). Yet, he [his weapons] by his humility; and he takes heart and restrains his enemy [endures] in silence.

> *¶10 #1 [moderate]:* Sets up the parable-like comparison that follows; keeps non-hagiographic, impersonal register.
> *¶10 #2 [moderate]:* The simile: First Man compared to a noble son/man.
> *¶10 #3 [moderate]:* Matches “they who belong to his household” later; establishes the ‘household/estate’ correspondence.
> *¶10 #4 [tentative]:* Gives the ethical-state baseline before enemies attack; but stays structural (honour/esteem).
> *¶10 #5 [moderate]:* Connects to “esteem before they who belong to his household”.
> *¶10 #6 [strong]:* Modifies ‘esteem’; typical Coptic narrative filler but fits sense.
> *¶10 #7 [moderate]:* Enemies ‘come upon’/assail him; corresponds to the assault of rulers upon First Man.
> *¶10 #9 [moderate]:* In the myth the First Man is stripped of his armours/sons; in the simile, enemies take his equipment/arms.
> *¶10 #10 [moderate]:* He ‘endures by his humility’ then restrains the enemy; endurance is the needed hinge.

**¶11** Just as with this man, this noble son whose enemies surround him, this also is how the advent of the First Man happened, [when he came among the] rulers, the enemies of the light.

> *¶11 #1 [moderate]:* Connects simile to the historical-cosmic event: First Man’s descent among rulers.

**¶12** In contrast, the advent of the Living Spirit and the commandment that he received, whereby he came upon all the rulers, it differs from that of the First Man. His advent [is not like] the advent of the First Man.

> *¶12 #1 [strong]:* Sets explicit contrast: Living Spirit’s advent differs from First Man’s.

**¶13** ⟨p.51⟩
The advent of the Living Spirit is like this, when he was sent from the greatness that he might bring up the First Man. He resembles a judge the king would send to overturn the violence to that man, the noble son against whom his enemies sinned. And he, this judge, [seizes them] hard and cruel [and is] and he gives the means [to them] by his righteousness and his strength; and he also judges all who are condemned according to their rebellious heart, and repays each according to his deserts. They who are worthy of blows he strikes with hard leather whips. Again, these who are worthy of cutting off their limbs, or indeed their [for deliverance] and he repays them accordingly. [tongues] and he binds and fetters them, and hangs by [Again, those whom it is fitting] and thus he does to them with a righteous judgement. And he kills them to flay their skin [their necks] so that they will be killed by a death [from them] until they die. Again, [they for] whom it is fitting [it is fitting] to banish from their dwellings he may remove according to a righteous judgement; just as they had faulted and sinned against the noble son.

> *¶13 #1 [moderate]:* The judge’s punitive action begins: he ‘seizes’ the violent ones.
> *¶13 #2 [strong]:* Link between clauses: ‘hard and cruel’.
> *¶13 #3 [strong]:* Completes ‘hard and cruel to them’.
> *¶13 #4 [moderate]:* Judge ‘gives the means’—i.e., the remedy/relief—to the oppressed noble son, by righteousness/strength.
> *¶13 #5 [tentative]:* List of punishments: limbs, tongues, etc.; ‘tongues’ fits juridical imagery (false speech) and common punitive catalogues.
> *¶13 #6 [moderate]:* Introduces next punitive class; matches the repeated ‘Again’ structure.
> *¶13 #7 [strong]:* ‘hang by …’ naturally takes ‘the neck’ as instrument of hanging.
> *¶13 #8 [strong]:* ‘flay their skin from them’ is the needed completion.
> *¶13 #10 [strong]:* Completes ‘Again, [they for] whom it is fitting …’

**¶14** Also, the associates in the mystery and the assistants of this judge, these who belong to him in great strength; in an instant and haste they shall do the will of their master.

**¶15** Now, just as this genuine judge, who was sent from the king to overturn the violence to this man, the noble son: this is also the case with the Living Spirit. He also was sent from the King of the lights to overturn the vengeance and violence to the First Man, against whom the King of the realms of Darkness had sinned. He faulted and sinned against him, and also his rebellious powers. Therefore, when (the Living Spirit) came, he bore up the First Man.

**¶16** Also, all the rulers, the powers of sin who had faulted and sinned against the sons of the First Man, he has judged them according to a righteous judgement. He has bound them in heaven and earth. He put each one to the place fitting for him; he weighed each of them according to his cruelty and oppression. While some of them he enclosed in the prison, others he hung head down. Some [among them] are crucified, others are sat down all the time. Some among them are fixed beneath their associates, restricted by a strong chain; others he made the holders of authority over their companions beneath, so that they may do their pleasure amongst these below them.

> *¶16 #1 [strong]:* Provides the subject for the next passive punishments: ‘Some among them are crucified…’.

**¶17** ⟨p.52⟩
Happen you know this [this,] that when the Father of Life established the judgements [in the beginning,] He was revealed to all the powers of darkness. He judged them as a true giver of judgement [and in truth] This is the first judgement that the Living Spirit gave the rulers [in respect of the] First Man. Indeed, he gave victory to the First Man corresponding to his victory; but he condemned the King of the realms of Darkness corresponding to that by which he had erred.

> *¶17 #1 [tentative]:* Begins an appeal to shared knowledge: ‘Happen you know this …’. Minimal restoration to make syntax work.
> *¶17 #2 [moderate]:* Judgements ‘established’ at the first ordering; fits cosmogonic sequencing.
> *¶17 #3 [moderate]:* Completes ‘a true giver of judgement …’; stresses correspondence/measure, not moral exhortation.

**¶18** [And] this chain, of this sort, became [a fetter upon] the earth till the end time when the universe will be dissolved; and it perishes in the great fire and is separated. While the light goes to its country, the darkness remains in the chain and fetter for ever.

> *¶18 #1 [strong]:* Simple connective into eschatological conclusion about the chain/fetter.
> *¶18 #2 [moderate]:* ‘this chain … became a fetter upon the earth’ gives the cosmological function of the bondage until the end.

**¶19** The advent also of the Third Ambassador, when he came to inspect they who are imprisoned in the zone: he is like a great king who comes and inspects craftsmen [and masons] and [their works] and [their tools]

> *¶19 #1 [moderate]:* Completes craftsmen triad consistent with ‘architects and artisans’.
> *¶19 #2 [strong]:* King inspects craftsmen ‘and their works’.
> *¶19 #3 [tentative]:* Often paired with inspection of works; plausible completion of the triad.

**¶20** Also, they who are good, of the craftsmen and architects and artisans whom he has there in each place: he shall see that they have worked well, and have fallen to his works in their fashion. He shall rejoice with great joy [in his heart] He shall thank the artisans who have constructed his works [and completed] his orders.

> *¶20 #1 [moderate]:* King rejoices ‘in his heart’ at good workmanship; idiomatic completion.

**¶21** Now, just as this great king who comes and inspects his works and fortifications, so also is the advent of the Third Ambassador. For, when he too came, he saw [his works] established and prepared and [in a] series of the rulers prepared and established there, above and below, outside and within, in the great pattern that his brothers had made. They had come before him to the contest, they who had fixed and created the universe before he came. He saw the gods and the angels established and controlling the fortifications [complete] guarding the entire ruling-power [and measured] which his first powers had done. They are the First Man and the Living Spirit and the Great Spirit, the Mother of Life.

> *¶21 #2 [moderate]:* Leads into ‘series of the rulers’.
> *¶21 #3 [moderate]:* Describes the prepared/established series as complete/ordered.
> *¶21 #4 [moderate]:* Cosmological ‘pattern’ language prefers measurement/ordering; fits ‘above and below, outside and within’.

**¶22** Therefore, when he saw them, that they had created everything with great wisdom, [and arranged] as they looked he plundered (?) [the bonds] in his advent, by which he came, he was unveiled to them. He says to the Living Spirit: “The judgement that you have [given is] good, what you uttered [and the sentence] you gave to them correspondingly; so that they were moved and raised up to that of which they were not worthy”.

> *¶22 #1 [moderate]:* He praises their wise ordering: ‘created everything … and arranged …’.
> *¶22 #2 [tentative]:* ‘as they looked he plundered (?) …’ likely refers to taking/loosening bonds; ‘bonds’ fits the inspection/judgement theme.
> *¶22 #3 [moderate]:* Introduces quoted commendation: ‘The judgement that you have given is good’.
> *¶22 #4 [moderate]:* Parallelism: ‘what you uttered’ = sentence/verdict; keeps juridical register.

**¶23** ⟨p.53⟩
The advent also of Jesus the Splendour, the time when he came to all [the powers] being just like a man if he is sent to pull out a [thorn] burn a difficult fig-garden with fire. Therefore, again, when he has first cut the bad trees with his axe and has pulled out [the roots] and their body with his fire, so that they should not return to sprout from this time, nor bear fruit bad to eat [hereafter]

> *¶23 #1 [moderate]:* Jesus’ advent comes ‘to all the powers’ (of mixture/zone) to cleanse.
> *¶23 #2 [tentative]:* Simile of pulling out a thorn/weed before burning; fits gardening imagery.
> *¶23 #3 [strong]:* ‘pulled out …’ naturally takes ‘the roots’.
> *¶23 #4 [moderate]:* Completes ‘nor bear fruit … to eat hereafter/from now on’.

**¶24** Afterwards, he also planted good plants: the tree of life that will make good fruit.

**¶25** Again, this is like this simile of the advent of Jesus the Splendour. It happened like this; because he too set out [into the mixture,] his power and his wisdom, in his [gentle]-ness [gentle] a king and a saviour [like] in his glorious wisdom he destroyed them. He bound and crushed them like the fire that shall burn and destroy trees. This is also how he performed his will in the zone, among the many powers, until he reached the form of the flesh of Adam and Eve, the first humans. [He perfected] his will in Eve, he [unveiled himself to her] He also gave hope to Adam, and the good news [that she might know] glorious [to him] He went up to the heights [to his country] of light.

> *¶25 #1 [moderate]:* Jesus ‘set out’ into the mixed region/zone to perform separation; ‘mixture’ is oldest-layer cosmological term.
> *¶25 #2 [tentative]:* Balances with ‘power and wisdom’: his manner/quality as he descends; corresponds to ‘humility’ motif without pastoral tone.
> *¶25 #3 [tentative]:* Fills ‘in his [...]-ness’; doubled adjective is possible but uncertain; intended sense: mildness/forbearance in operation.
> *¶25 #4 [moderate]:* Leads into ‘a king and a saviour’ as a likeness/simile of his function.
> *¶25 #6 [moderate]:* In the Adam/Eve episode, Jesus Splendour reveals/awakens Eve; ‘unveiling’ matches earlier idiom.
> *¶25 #7 [moderate]:* Eve’s awakening is epistemic (gnosis/recognition) in this layer’s register.
> *¶25 #8 [strong]:* Completes ‘good news … to him’ (Adam).
> *¶25 #9 [moderate]:* He ascends to the heights ‘to his country of light’.

**¶26** ⟨p.54⟩
Then the summons and the obedience, the great counsel that came to the elements, which are set in conjunction: it mixed with them, it was established in silence. It bears up [the light-soul] until the end time when it can arise and stand firm in the great fire. It will gather to it its own soul, and sculpt it in the Last Statue. You will also find it sweeps out and casts from it the pollution that is foreign to it. However, the life and the light that are in all things it gathers in to it, and builds upon its body.

> *¶26 #1 [moderate]:* What is borne up in the elements is the light-soul (living light) until it can rise in the great fire and be gathered into the Last Statue.

**¶27** The time when this Last Statue will be perfect in all its limbs, then it can become free and ascend from that great struggle through the Living Spirit its father, the one who comes and brings a [each] limb. He brings it up from within this gathering (?), the melting down and destruction of all things.

> *¶27 #1 [strong]:* He brings a ‘… limb’—i.e., each limb/member of the Statue; ‘each’ best completes the distributive sense.

**¶28** Now, this counsel, that summons and obedience, resembles thus cooled butter. If a person will take and set it in hot milk, it melts down and soaks in with this hot milk. It melts in another time in silence and quiet to the midst [in silence,] yet also, of the power and the taste of this milk [of it;] is drawn to (the butter), and (the milk) sets and becomes cold [also] in [and the strength] and the [whey] this milk it shall clarify and discard. However, the oil and the grease and the ‘beauty’ of the milk it shall gather in to it.

> *¶28 #1 [strong]:* Matches ‘melts in another time in silence and quiet’.
> *¶28 #2 [moderate]:* Completes ‘to the midst … of it’.
> *¶28 #3 [strong]:* Connective in the butter/milk analogy.
> *¶28 #4 [moderate]:* Pairs with ‘power and the taste’.
> *¶28 #5 [moderate]:* What is clarified and discarded from milk is ‘whey’/watery part; corresponds to foreign pollution removed from the counsel.

**¶29** ⟨p.55⟩
Again, this too is what the advent of that thought is like, the counsel of life, which is the summons and obedience that came to the elements. This is the one that at the end can arise and stand firm, and will gather in to it all the lives, and the light that has remained behind in everything. It can build them upon its body; it will gather them in to it [and will establish them] in its image for ever and ever [and it will endure] from this time.

> *¶29 #1 [moderate]:* Summarizes the gathering and building: it gathers and establishes them upon its body.
> *¶29 #2 [moderate]:* Closes with permanence ‘for ever and ever … from this time’; endurance suits the Last Statue’s fixed state.

**Assessment:** Most gaps could be restored at the level of required cosmological/juridical functions (emanation, spreading-out, judging/binding, inspection, purgation, final gathering). Exact wording is often only moderately constrained because the surviving Coptic is paraphrastic and several lacunae sit in simile-expansions (¶10, ¶13, ¶19, ¶28) where multiple idiomatic completions are possible. Core doctrinal terms (‘mixture’, ‘zone’, ‘summons and obedience’, ‘Last Statue’, judgement/binding) provide the tightest constraints; descriptive fillers (adjectives, inspection details, punishment catalogues) remain tentative in places.

---

## Chapter 17: The Chapter of the Three Seasons

**¶1** From the time when the light came forth and was crucified in the darkness, to the season when the Statue will ascend and the entire light, which was conjoined with the darkness, will be purified and cleansed. From that time on, from that time to the season of the end totals three seasons.

**¶2** Three seasons: from the time when the First Man went down to the abyss, and waged the war and the struggle against the King of the realms of Darkness and his other powers. He was victorious, while his sons remain behind with them in the worlds [of the five abysses] in the [abyss] until he shall catch all of them [afterwards] he bound them all up, so that not one of them escaped his hand. He won a great victory [over the King of Darkness] The First Man spent human lifetimes and generations in the land of darkness, until he prevailed [at last] ⟨p.56⟩ he fulfilled the will of the greatness that was commanded of him.

> *¶2 #1 [moderate]:* After the victory the text locates the sons remaining behind “in the worlds … in the …”: in the oldest Iranian-Manichaean substrate, the Darkness is structured as “worlds/realms” articulated as the five dark abysses. This is the standard correspondential geography (depths = degrees of obscuration).
> *¶2 #2 [moderate]:* Parallel to “went down to the abyss” and to later “swept the waste to the abyss”; the sons are said to remain in the abyssal regions of Darkness.
> *¶2 #3 [tentative]:* The clause “until he shall catch all of them … he bound them all up” needs a temporal hinge moving from their remaining-behind to the subsequent binding/capturing.
> *¶2 #4 [moderate]:* The immediate referent of ‘great victory’ in this season is the initial combat with the King and powers of Darkness; the substrate register states victory in structural terms, not moral exhortation.
> *¶2 #5 [tentative]:* Transition: “until he prevailed … he fulfilled the will …” requires a completion marker; the sense is endurance across ‘lifetimes and generations’ culminating in fulfillment.

**¶3** Then they sent the Living Spirit, the Father of Life. He brought the First Man up from the struggle, he brought him up to the land of light. This is the first period, from the descent of the First Man to his ascent.

**¶4** The second season is from the time when the First Man ascended. The Living Spirit came, and the Mother of Life. They set in order the things of the universe, they set up [the firmaments] the Father of Life and the Mother of the living set in order [all] the universe, up to the moment when they go out [to their places] it was reckoned to the second season.

> *¶4 #1 [moderate]:* “They set in order the things of the universe, they set up …” in the second season is the construction of the ordered heavens/structures (firmaments) as intermediates between Light and the mixed world.
> *¶4 #2 [tentative]:* Balanced repetition: “set in order … set in order … the universe” invites a totalizing object (“all the universe / all its orders”).
> *¶4 #3 [tentative]:* “up to the moment when they go out … it was reckoned” describes completion of arranging and withdrawing to their proper stations/regions; ‘places’ fits the impersonal cosmological register.

**¶5** [Afterwards they set in order] the universe [was purified] the Third Ambassador spread [his nets] that is in the [region of the] living; he [shone;] he separated beauty from all the powers and scooped it up to the heights, he swept the waste to the abyss, and he [made the world to stand] Now, from the moment when the Ambassador revealed his image he purified the light. Sin spurted up towards him. He hid his image. Sin came [up] from the darkness. It sculpted the trees and the abortions fell down. Afterwards they sculpted Adam and Eve in the flesh. Until the moment when Jesus was sent and he [out] as he performed there his will. He gave the hope to Adam, and ascended to the heights.

> *¶5 #1 [tentative]:* ¶5 continues the sequence of the second season into the third agent’s operation; an adverbial handoff (“afterwards”) plus an ordering-verb matches the chapter’s structuring by phases.
> *¶5 #2 [tentative]:* The line “the universe …” naturally takes the passive result of prior ordering: the cosmos becomes ‘purified/clarified’ as mixture is sorted. This anticipates the Ambassador’s later purification work.
> *¶5 #3 [moderate]:* “The Third Ambassador spread … that is in the … living” corresponds to the Ambassador’s function of eliciting/separating Light by a ‘net’/‘snare’ display (a technical image for extraction).
> *¶5 #4 [tentative]:* The phrase “that is in the … living” needs a locative/qualifying genitive; ‘region’ keeps the substrate cosmographic tone (zones/regions) rather than devotional phrasing.
> *¶5 #5 [tentative]:* After “he … he separated beauty” a verb of manifestation fits: the Ambassador’s appearance causes the beautiful/light to be drawn out. A simple ‘shone’ is correspondentially apt (light revealing light).
> *¶5 #6 [moderate]:* After sweeping waste to the abyss, the remaining operation is stabilization of the mixed construction: the world is ‘set/caused to stand’ as an ordered container for further separation.
> *¶5 #7 [strong]:* “Sin came … from the darkness” expects “up/out” movement (eruption from lower degree to confront the revealed image).
> *¶5 #8 [strong]:* With ‘from the darkness’ the natural complement is emergence ‘out’ (opposite of descent).

**¶6** Now, from the time when the Ambassador displayed his image until the season when the Statue will ascend at the end, shall be reckoned to the third season.

**¶8** [The first season] lasts long in the time that the First Man spent in the world of darkness. It is a very great count of time, more than the (other) two seasons!

> *¶8 #1 [strong]:* ¶8 compares durations and explicitly refers to “the time that the First Man spent in the world of darkness”; the subject that “lasts long” is therefore the first season/period.

**¶10** [He replies to them]: If you should say that [the first period] it is small, if you should [compare it] to him [another] and you consult [again] you will tell him: Twenty times! It is greater again in this other one that you will consult: Thirty times! Less also is this other one [than the intermediate season] people will receive the {} of the trees [for in that time] the years and the generations [fruit] earth to the heights. This is [...

> *¶10 #2 [tentative]:* The argument is a comparative calculation: ‘if you should say that … it is small’—the natural item under debate is the duration of the first period (First Man’s sojourn).
> *¶10 #3 [tentative]:* He instructs them in proportional comparison: “if you should … to him … and you consult … you will tell him: Twenty times!”; ‘compare it’ fits the mathematical/ratiological tone.
> *¶10 #4 [tentative]:* “and you consult … you will tell him” requires an object: consult ‘another’ (reckoning/measure) for a second ratio.
> *¶10 #5 [strong]:* “It is greater again in this other one” is the needed connective/adverb to mark a second step in the proportional series.
> *¶10 #6 [moderate]:* The sentence “Less also is this other one …” contrasts the third measure against the middle; the chapter repeatedly names the ‘intermediate’ (second) season as shorter.
> *¶10 #7 [tentative]:* The following clause moves to what happens during the long span: reception of the fruit/seed from trees, years, generations, and ascent of portions of earth/light; a temporal frame ‘for in that time’ fits.
> *¶10 #8 [moderate]:* “people will receive the {} of the trees” in this mythic cycle is the taking of fruit/seed producing bodies and generations (a natural correspondence of propagation in the mixed world).

**¶11** This is the third period, from the time when the Ambassador revealed his image until the Statue will ascend from the lump. It again, that time, is greater than the intermediate; but it does not come equal to the time of the First Man. For this intermediate season, in which the Father of Life and the Mother of Life constructed the universe with its orders, is shorter than that of the First Man.

**¶12** Great is the period of the First Man! It lasts longer than the season in which the Ambassador displayed his image, until the dissolution of the world and the Statue ascends. From the time too when the Ambassador displayed his image, until the dissolution of the universe [it is greater than the intermediate season] when the Father of Life and the Mother of Life shaped the universe.

> *¶12 #1 [moderate]:* ¶12 resumes the triadic comparison: first > third; third > second. The lacuna stands where the comparative to the second season is expected before recalling “when the Father of Life and the Mother of Life shaped the universe.”

**¶13** These are the [reckoned] three periods of time that occur. ⟨p.58⟩

> *¶13 #1 [moderate]:* Closing summary formula: “These are the … three periods of time that occur” regularly uses ‘reckoned/counted’ in this chapter’s register of measurement.

**Assessment:** Most gaps can be constrained at the level of cosmological function (three seasons; ordering of the cosmos; Ambassador’s extraction; eruption of Sin). Exact Coptic phrasing is less recoverable in ¶10 and parts of ¶5–¶4 where the underlying text likely had technical terms; restorations there are correspondentially consistent but remain tentative.

---

## Chapter 18: [Concerning the Five] War[s that the] Sons of [Li]ght waged with the Sons] of Darkness.

**¶2** The first war is that of the First living Man, which he waged against the King of the realms of Darkness, and all the rulers that had come forth from the five worlds of [Darkness]. He hunted them with his net, which is the living soul. He [cast them] in the snare and [bound them] the face of the [enemy] his virgin, the living fire [and in] they did not find how to escape his net [so that] he caught them like fish [also] the five counsels of the five elements [and he seized]. He cut and dug out the root of the five trees, [and he cut out the] root of the evil tree, like an axe.

> *¶2 #1 [strong]:* Five worlds are the five dark elements (smoke, fire, wind, water, darkness) under the King of Darkness; the substrate regularly calls them “worlds of Darkness.”
> *¶2 #2 [moderate]:* After “net (living soul)” the needed action is the throwing/setting of the net in order to seize rulers.
> *¶2 #3 [moderate]:* Parallelism requires two capture-verbs: cast/set the snare, then bind/fetter in it.
> *¶2 #4 [moderate]:* “the face of the …” in this war-context is the adversarial manifestation of Darkness confronted by the Living Man.
> *¶2 #5 [tentative]:* The phrase is enumerative: “his virgin, the living fire, and in … they did not find…” linking her action/presence to the trapping.
> *¶2 #6 [moderate]:* Purpose clause: living fire blocks escape from the net.
> *¶2 #7 [moderate]:* Reinforces repeated net-imagery: not only trapped, but “also” caught like fish.
> *¶2 #8 [moderate]:* Transitions from fish-simile to taking “the five counsels…” (inner deliberations of the five elements).
> *¶2 #9 [moderate]:* Matches the next sentence’s cutting/digging: extraction of roots corresponds to removing the inmost will of the five dark natures.

**¶3** The second war is that of the Living Spirit [which he waged against the rulers of the Darkness]. He has constructed the things and the worlds of light; he spread out the rulers and chained them in the middle [of the firmament; he arranged] the stars in [their courses; and he set] the earths [and he set] them in the three vessels. For the rulers rebelled and were set against the Living Spirit. They wished to [and he divided] since they knew and realised that he might bind and fetter them with a strong chain. Yet he, the Living Spirit, (appeared to them) with many aspects, with numerous [escape], with many hands and great cruelties. He was revealed to them, he imprisoned them with much skill. Not one of them was able to save (himself), and he {...} [powers]. Now, since he [could not find a way] he trapped them [had bound them] ⟨p.59⟩ [in their places and in their measures].

> *¶3 #1 [moderate]:* Second war is the binding/ordering of the already-captured rulers; the opponent is the same dark archontic host.
> *¶3 #2 [tentative]:* After “middle …” the substrate sequence is: suspend rulers in the mid-region/firmament and then arrange luminaries as measures.
> *¶3 #3 [tentative]:* Stars require “courses” (measures/time). Then the narrative returns to “he set … the earths …”.
> *¶3 #4 [moderate]:* Verb required before “the earths … them in the three vessels.”
> *¶3 #5 [tentative]:* Cosmic ordering: division into three vessels (often sky/air/earth or sun/moon/stars as vessels) presupposes an act of dividing/assigning.
> *¶3 #6 [strong]:* Rulers wish to “escape” before binding; fits immediate rationale “since they knew… he might bind…”
> *¶3 #7 [moderate]:* “many aspects… numerous …” in this layer is “powers/forces” (forms of operation), not devotional titles.
> *¶3 #8 [moderate]:* Idiomatic closure: none could save themselves; they “could not find a way” out of his skillful imprisonment.
> *¶3 #9 [moderate]:* Causal transition: “Now, since he had bound them…” leading to further trapping/setting in places.
> *¶3 #10 [tentative]:* Correspondentially, binding fixes each power into a determinate function/measure within the ordered cosmos.

**¶4** The third war is that of the Third Ambassador who has displayed his image [in the height] what is below [so that] scooped [and he] all light and the property [that was mixed in the bodies] the ones that are consumed [of the rulers] the entire ruling-power [by them] pride of the light [and he stripped] extending it [that it had taken]. A person comes, who is powerful [back to its treasuries]; he takes his riches [against the strong man], silver [from him], his pride and wantonness.

> *¶4 #1 [moderate]:* Third Ambassador displays image “in the height” so that what is below responds; classic lure/manifestation motif.
> *¶4 #2 [moderate]:* Purpose: image is shown so that the lower mixture yields up light.
> *¶4 #3 [strong]:* Links to the next verb “scooped … all light”.
> *¶4 #4 [moderate]:* What is “below” is the mixture; “scooped out all light” presumes light is mixed/hidden in bodies/forms.
> *¶4 #5 [moderate]:* “property … the ones that are consumed” points to the rulers consuming light; thus “property of the rulers” (their plunder).
> *¶4 #6 [strong]:* Clarifies “the ones that are consumed by them” (the rulers) as consumers of light.
> *¶4 #7 [moderate]:* He removes “the entire ruling-power” of its stolen light; stripping is the correspondential act of taking away borrowed splendor.
> *¶4 #8 [moderate]:* Explains “pride of the light” as usurped adornment taken from Light.
> *¶4 #9 [moderate]:* Light is gathered and returned upward into storehouses/treasuries (vessels of Light).
> *¶4 #10 [moderate]:* The parable-like line in this substrate is functional: a powerful one comes “against the strong man” to plunder his goods.
> *¶4 #11 [strong]:* Completes “he takes his riches from him…”.

**¶5** This is what the ruling-power is like [in its treasuries], all riches [of its robberies] as they travel [in the worlds] light [from the mixture]. He cleansed and purified it.

> *¶5 #1 [moderate]:* Ruling-power compared to a hoarder/robber; “treasuries” keeps the cosmological register of stored light/wealth.
> *¶5 #2 [moderate]:* “all riches …” are ill-gotten: light taken by robbery from the mixture.
> *¶5 #3 [tentative]:* “as they travel … light …” implies movement through the cosmos/worlds during purification/separation.
> *¶5 #4 [strong]:* Purification is separation of light from the mixed substance; matches “He cleansed and purified it.”

**¶6** The fourth war is this that Jesus the glorious one waged against all the rebels who had rebelled above and below. When the light was purified from them by the image of the Third Ambassador, their chains loosened [and they fell again into]. The glorious one in the fourth war; he came and brought [the fetters and he quelled] a rebellion of the entire ruling-power. He girded himself [to them] all of it another time. He constructed the whole structure and he made [to cease] a great contest; he humiliated therein. [with the armour of light] he fulfilled the will of the greatness, [he gave the] hope to Adam [and he instructed him to look] to his light ship.

> *¶6 #1 [tentative]:* When chains loosen, the rebels relapse into agitation; requires a fall/renewed motion leading to renewed binding.
> *¶6 #2 [tentative]:* Jesus’ war re-imposes fetters and suppresses rebellion; aligns with “brought … a rebellion … girded himself … all of it another time.”
> *¶6 #3 [moderate]:* “he came and brought …” requires indirect object: brought (judgment/word) to them, producing/ending rebellion.
> *¶6 #4 [moderate]:* “brought … a rebellion” in this substrate sense is “brought it to cease/end,” not incited it.
> *¶6 #5 [moderate]:* Girding corresponds to taking on protecting/truth-form; oldest layer prefers impersonal “armour of light” over later devotional phrasing.
> *¶6 #6 [strong]:* Kept as given; fits the function: imparting hope/knowledge to Adam (first human mind) toward ascent.
> *¶6 #7 [tentative]:* Hope to Adam is operational: instruction/turning of the inner sight toward the “light ship” (vehicle of ascent).

**¶7** The fifth war is this that the counsel of life [waged against the last mixture] which is the summons and the obedience [of the great call] great [power] and it placed [a seal] upon the [living] and the [dead] ⟨p.60⟩ [and it separated the root] of death, which [had mingled] with it. And it was victorious over [it] for ever, and it gave the kingdom [of light for ever].

> *¶7 #1 [tentative]:* Fifth war (counsel of life = summons/obedience) addresses the final phase: separating the remaining mixture in the world.
> *¶7 #2 [moderate]:* “summons and the obedience” are the paired operations of the Call; “great call” suits oldest register.
> *¶7 #3 [moderate]:* “great … and it placed …” requires a noun like “power/force” describing the counsel’s efficacy.
> *¶7 #4 [moderate]:* The counsel “placed a seal” corresponds to fixing boundaries so death cannot mingle again.
> *¶7 #5 [tentative]:* “upon the … and the …” likely “living and the dead”; first term restored as “living.”
> *¶7 #6 [tentative]:* Completes the pair living/dead as discrete states separated by the seal.
> *¶7 #7 [tentative]:* At this stage, the work is separation at the root (inmost) of death from life; fits “of death, which … with it.”
> *¶7 #8 [moderate]:* Death “had mingled with it” (with life/light) describes the mixture-condition being undone.
> *¶7 #9 [moderate]:* Victorious over “it” = the last rebellion/death-power just mentioned.
> *¶7 #10 [moderate]:* Kingdom given is the stable dominion of Light (order restored permanently).

**Assessment:** Most gaps here are not constrained by surviving letter-traces, so restorations rely on the internal sequence of the five ‘wars’ and standard correspondential operations in the oldest cosmological layer: (1) capture by net/soul, (2) binding and cosmic arrangement into measures, (3) lure/manifestation to extract light from mixture, (4) re-fettering and structural completion with imparted hope/knowledge to the first human, (5) final sealing/separation of life from death. Several short connective fills are tentative because multiple Coptic-compatible formulae could fit without the manuscript’s exact wording.

## Chapter 19: Concerning the Five Releases: what they are

**¶2** [The first loo]sening and saving is that of [the] First Man, since when the First M[a]n was freed fro[m the prison of] the rulers by the Living Spirit, who had come [for the salvation] of the First Man, much light and [much] power was saved from the entire ruling-power. They ascended with the First Man to the aeons [of] greatn[ess]. This [is the first] release.

> *¶2 #5 [moderate]:* The Living Spirit ‘comes’ in response to the first crisis: he descends/comes for the saving (λύτρωσις) of First Man. Substrate register prefers impersonal function (“for the salvation”) over later hagiographic motives.

**¶3** The second saving is that of the Liv[ing] Spirit, since whe[n] the Father of Life and [the] Mother of [Li]fe constructed [structure of the universe] and [w]orlds [five]rlds, the earths and their [rulers; im]measurable light was saved f[r]om them [through the First] Man, who w[a]s devoured [and bound in the mixture] lig[ht ...

> *¶3 #5 [moderate]:* After Father of Life + Mother of Life act, what is ‘constructed’ is the ordered cosmological frame (firmaments/worlds/earths). “structure of the universe” matches the correspondential step: forming receptacles/containments to extract and govern the mingled light.
> *¶3 #7 [moderate]:* The stock cosmogonic list is ‘five firmaments and worlds’ (or ‘five worlds’), i.e., a fivefold ordering of the mixed region. The fragment ‘... wo]rlds’ constrains a numeral/adjective before ‘worlds’.
> *¶3 #10 [moderate]:* Light is ‘saved from them’ by mediation ‘through the First Man’ (the primal mixture-source). This keeps the logic: what was devoured from First Man becomes the recoverable light that the later constructions extract.
> *¶3 #12 [moderate]:* After ‘was devoured’ the substrate typically specifies the state: the light is bound/fastened in the mixture and in the bodies of the rulers. This prepares the next section’s ‘wars’ and later ‘loosenings’.

**¶4** ⟨p.61⟩
... ... th]ree wars [and he set in motion] to the time when he [from these] above [ascended] them upon the [the whole] universe.

> *¶4 #1 [tentative]:* The broken sentence culminates in a temporal sweep (‘three wars … to the time when…’). The missing start likely describes initiating/ordering the conflict-process leading to enthronement above the universe.
> *¶4 #2 [tentative]:* The phrase ‘three wars …’ normally is ‘three wars with these (powers/rulers)’. ‘from these’ fits the following ‘above … them’.
> *¶4 #3 [moderate]:* The clause ‘to the time when he … above … them’ requires a verb of ascent/exaltation.
> *¶4 #4 [moderate]:* Before ‘universe’ a totalizing adjective is expected (‘whole universe’).

**¶5** [The third red]emption is that of the Thi[rd Ambassador, since when] displayed [his image in the forms] he [war upon the r]uling-power; he [loosened the bonds of] from them [much light] So this is [how] the [light] his image that [was gathered] in the Th[ir]d Ambassador.

> *¶5 #2 [moderate]:* This unit parallels ¶2–3: “X saving … since when …”. So the lacuna should supply the connective and temporal clause rather than devotional framing.
> *¶5 #3 [tentative]:* Third Ambassador saves by epiphany of the Image in the shaped forms (sun/moon/zodiac; or ‘male and female forms’) to provoke separation. ‘in the forms’ matches the mechanism: showing the archetypal Form within forms to draw out light.
> *¶5 #4 [moderate]:* The narrative usually: he displayed his image, he made war upon the ruling-power, he extracted/loosened light. So supply ‘war upon the’.
> *¶5 #5 [tentative]:* The Third Ambassador’s action is loosening/unbinding what held the light in the rulers (bondage imagery consistent with ‘release’ list).
> *¶5 #6 [moderate]:* Matches the repeated measure-language (‘much light’, ‘without measure’).
> *¶5 #8 [moderate]:* “the … his image that … in the Third Ambassador” expects the object ‘light’ as what responds/flows by means of the Image.
> *¶5 #9 [tentative]:* The image-display causes the light to be drawn together/collected out of the rulers. ‘gathered’ fits the causal ‘So this is how…’.

**¶6** [Th]e fourth s[av]ing is that of Jesus the Splendour, since whe[n he was] re[ve]aled in the zone he displayed [his im]age in front of the firmaments and purified [the light] that is above. He established the first righteous o[one in the place] . He took the likeness [of the Just Judge] he made himself like the angels in [of splendour] un[til] he travelled and descended to the form of flesh. He set in order [t]he earths and all the fastenings. He also loosen[ed the bonds of] light without measure in the entire structure. He gave [the] summons and the obedience to the elements, he formed [J]esus the Youth. He as[ce]n[d]ed and rested himself in the light [land].

> *¶6 #7 [tentative]:* After purification above, the next act is installing a first ‘righteous one’/judge/measure in the region as regulator. ‘in the place’ keeps the impersonal cosmological function (a station installed).
> *¶6 #8 [tentative]:* In the substrate this figure is a functional office: just measuring/judging to separate and order. Fits after ‘established the first righteous one’.
> *¶6 #9 [tentative]:* ‘He took the likeness …’ likely specifies the luminous mode before angelic likeness and descent to flesh; ‘of splendour’ matches Jesus Splendour’s own register.
> *¶6 #12 [moderate]:* “He also loosen[ed …] light without measure” requires ‘loosened the bonds of’ (or ‘opened the treasuries of’) to yield the light.

**¶7** The fifth release and saving is that of the great counsel, w[hich is the s]umm[ons an]d the obedience, since it also [calls back] the soul from the [land] of greatness.

> *¶7 #3 [moderate]:* Great Counsel/Summons-and-Obedience functions as the attractive call that draws the soul/light back upward (inside→outside: internal consent responding to the call).

**¶8** ⟨p.62⟩
[Again] the Living Spirit [and] as he gave [first] the release [through]
The first [power, since when] ascended and he [was established] as he [was giving] wisdom [in order with]
The fourth [the third] its wisdom [power]
And also, the fifth [power: it revealed] into it, and it sets it in order [all] its light image in this Last Statue.

> *¶8 #1 [tentative]:* This section recapitulates the five in a schematic list; a discourse-marker like ‘Again’/‘Thus’ is expected at the head of the page break.
> *¶8 #2 [tentative]:* Likely a conjunction linking to prior sentence; minimal restoration.
> *¶8 #3 [moderate]:* The list immediately follows: ‘The first … The fourth … And also, the fifth …’. So restore the ordinal heading.
> *¶8 #4 [tentative]:* “as he gave … the release …” expects an instrument/agency marker (‘through’ the Living Spirit).
> *¶8 #5 [moderate]:* Parallels the earlier pattern: “The first … power, since when he ascended…”.
> *¶8 #6 [tentative]:* After ‘he ascended’ one expects ‘he was established’/‘he rested’ in his station.
> *¶8 #7 [tentative]:* Leads into “as he … wisdom …” (distribution/communication of wisdom).
> *¶8 #8 [tentative]:* Wisdom is set in order/arranged in the cosmos; phrase links wisdom to ordering.
> *¶8 #9 [moderate]:* We have ‘The first … The fourth … And also, the fifth …’; missing entries are ‘the second’ and ‘the third’. Given space before ‘The fourth’, restore ‘the third’ heading line.
> *¶8 #10 [moderate]:* Each entry names a ‘power’.
> *¶8 #11 [tentative]:* “The fourth … its wisdom …” needs a verb of manifestation/teaching matching ‘revealed’.
> *¶8 #12 [tentative]:* “it sets it in order … its light image” calls for totality: ‘all its light image’.

**¶9** These five grea[t] powe[rs ...] were revealed. They came to a profit [in the worlds] they were established upon [the foundations] First Man [and] when he came to/ward [he was healed] from the struggle. He set in order and constructed [and rested] universe [the entire] the First M[an ...] his advent.

> *¶9 #3 [tentative]:* They are ‘revealed’ and ‘came to a profit’ in the ordered ‘worlds’ they establish/occupy; fits the ensuing ‘were established upon …’.
> *¶9 #4 [moderate]:* “established upon …” expects ‘foundations’/‘bases’ (cosmic supports).
> *¶9 #5 [tentative]:* Small connective; unrestorable beyond conjunction but fits syntax.
> *¶9 #6 [tentative]:* “First Man … when he came toward … from the struggle” commonly culminates in restoration/healing after the rescue.
> *¶9 #7 [tentative]:* Pairs with being healed/freed; also matches later ‘ascended and rested’.
> *¶9 #8 [moderate]:* “constructed … universe” commonly ‘the entire universe’.

**¶10** In [t]his way [in the order] they st[an]d with [one another]

> *¶10 #2 [tentative]:* “In this way … they stand with …” expects a schematic conclusion: they stand ‘in the order’/‘in their ranks’ with one another.
> *¶10 #4 [moderate]:* Completes “they stand with …” naturally and in impersonal register.

**¶11** ⟨p.63⟩
... because of the light [th]at had rushed from [the powers of] the Amba[s]sa[d]or.

> *¶11 #2 [tentative]:* “because of the light that had rushed from … the Ambassador” requires a source-domain: the light rushes from ‘the powers of the Ambassador’/his manifestation.

**Assessment:** Many bracketed items are trivial letter-restorations and can be filled with strong confidence. Where the manuscript has full gaps (‘...’), only the correspondential structure of the fivefold ‘savings’ constrains the reconstruction: repeated causal formula (‘since when…’), recurring operations (constructing the cosmic frame; displaying the Image; loosening bonds; purification above; summons/obedience drawing upward). Those restorations are mostly moderate/tentative because multiple synonymous verbs and stock epithets could fit without the underlying Coptic line.
