## Chapter 20: The Chapter of the Name of the Fathers

**¶3** Now, the Father, who is the first established thing, shall be called ‘the Father of Greatness’. His greatness is the great earth where he lives, he being established in it [as in a foundation; and his greatness is this:] the essence of the light that swathes all the rich gods and the angels and the dwellings [and the Light-Mind] is set over it.

> *¶3 #1 [moderate]:* The clause needs to (a) complete the ‘established in it’ formula and (b) pivot to define ‘greatness’ as the Father’s own domain/ground (earth). The oldest substrate is structural and impersonal: ‘established’ implies a fixed base/foundation rather than a narrative action.
> *¶3 #2 [tentative]:* The sentence lists what is ‘set over’ the Father’s ‘great earth’: in the substrate this is the ordering/noetic principle of Light that invests and governs the divine ranks (‘gods’, ‘angels’, ‘dwellings’). ‘Light-Mind’ fits the correspondential function: the essence of Light as intelligence/wisdom that encompasses and orders the rich ones and their habitations, without importing later ecclesial framing.

**¶4** Furthermore, they shall call the Third Ambassador ‘father’. His greatness is the light ship of living fire wherein he lives, he being established in it.

**¶5** Once again, Jesus the Splendour shall himself also be called ‘father’. His greatness is the ship of living waters where he lives, he being established in it.

**Assessment:** Only two lacunae, both in ¶3. The immediate syntactic frame (‘established in it …’ / ‘… is set over it’) constrains the needed grammatical function, but the precise lexemes are not forced by surviving wording. Restorations therefore aim to preserve the substrate’s impersonal cosmological register and the correspondential roles: (1) establishment/foundation and definitional pivot; (2) a noetic ‘essence of Light’ set over the divine orders.

---

## Chapter 21: [C]oncerning the Father of Gr[eat]ness: [ho]w he is established and determined.

**¶2** Very great is [the land of] light in the presence of they who will hear and know it, due to the first Father of Greatness. How he ex[ists and is] established in his five light limbs, which are [the great store]houses that have neither limit nor measure to the[them, to every] mind, thought, insight, counsel and [consideration, and] his twelve light limbs that are his twelve wisdoms.

> *¶2 #1 [strong]:* Fixed phrase in this cosmological register: the immeasurable χώρα/τόπος of Light, not a moral ‘light’ but the spatial/ontic realm of the Father’s extension.
> *¶2 #3 [moderate]:* Correspondentially the five ‘limbs’ are also five treasuries/storehouses (repositories of the divine qualities) without limit or measure.
> *¶2 #4 [tentative]:* The clause contrasts limitless measures with the finite knower: ‘to them … to every mind’.
> *¶2 #5 [moderate]:* Completes the pentad of inner faculties in a pre-exhortative, structural way: mind–thought–insight–counsel–consideration.

**¶3** Now, there are five great light lim[bs] in each one [there are also] five grea[t] springs of praise gush for[th from them, and] five great [voices of] lig[ht in them]

> *¶3 #2 [moderate]:* Balancing statement: five limbs in each one → five corresponding springs of praise.
> *¶3 #4 [tentative]:* Praise is treated as an efflux; the springs ‘gush forth’ and the syntax needs a continuation into the next member.
> *¶3 #5 [moderate]:* In this substrate, praise proceeds as sound/utterance from living sources; prepares for ¶6 ‘living voice’.
> *¶3 #6 [tentative]:* Correspondential closure: the five great [voices of] light ‘in them’ (the limbs/aeons).

**¶4** The first is his light that enlig[htens all] it gushed forth from him and pour[ed into] a[l]] aeons.

> *¶4 #1 [moderate]:* First limb-function: illuminating (making things visible/intelligible) across the whole pleroma of aeons.
> *¶4 #2 [moderate]:* Light as an emanative outpouring ‘into all aeons’.

**¶5** The second is the perfume of the [Father] and pervades all the aeons of greatness.

> *¶5 #1 [moderate]:* Second limb is a pervasive ‘perfume’ proceeding from the Father—an ontic fragrance filling the aeons (not devotional).

**¶6** The third is his living voice of [truth and] all his aeons [and fills]

> *¶6 #1 [moderate]:* Living voice characteristically bears/utters ‘truth’ in this register (wisdom-truth correspondence).
> *¶6 #2 [moderate]:* Voice as efflux that ‘fills’/saturates the aeons, parallel to ‘pervades’ in ¶5.

**¶7** The fourth is his [gentle]-ness [soft] gushes forth from him [out]ut [upon all the aeons]

> *¶7 #1 [tentative]:* Fourth limb as a quality of the Father’s proceeding presence; ‘gentleness’ fits the pattern of abstract -ness attributes.
> *¶7 #2 [tentative]:* Keeps the impersonal cosmological tone: a mildness/softness that ‘gushes forth’.
> *¶7 #3 [strong]:* Fixed with surrounding ‘... o]ut’.
> *¶7 #4 [moderate]:* Standard expansion: what proceeds from the Father extends upon/through the whole realm.

**¶8** ⟨p.65⟩ The fif[th] is his great glorio[us splendour] that [the image, which]hich [nifested]nifested all the tim[e], whi[ues to stand in]ues [within] all of them. It was sculpted in them [and enlightens] sealed in the [them, and] sealed [also] as they send [within their boundaries] in [one to another] they too receive nourishment [measure] immediately [by his] living voi[ce of truth] joy [and are filled with] set up anew. It shall gush forth from it [great] they shall [and their order is] and become rich in the [when] too is beautiful [praise] portray[al forms] adornment [which] and they live [in every likeness] for ev[r]r.

> *¶8 #2 [moderate]:* Fifth limb: glory/splendour as the form-manifesting radiance that can be ‘sculpted’ and ‘sealed’ in the aeons.
> *¶8 #3 [moderate]:* The relative clause ‘which has been manifested…’ expects a referent like ‘image/form’ rather than a personal title; matches the sculpted/sealed vocabulary.
> *¶8 #6 [tentative]:* Glory as a stable, standing form within the aeons; ‘continues’ → ‘continues to stand’.
> *¶8 #7 [moderate]:* Spatial containment consistent with ‘sculpted in them’.
> *¶8 #8 [moderate]:* Returns to the function of the manifested splendour: it acts on ‘all of them’.
> *¶8 #9 [strong]:* Links ‘sealed in them’ to the next clause.
> *¶8 #10 [moderate]:* Parallelism: sculpted in them … sealed in them … sealed also …
> *¶8 #11 [tentative]:* Correspondentially ‘seal’ marks determination/limit that preserves each aeon’s proper form without measure-confusion.
> *¶8 #12 [tentative]:* Explains the transmission: as they send/receive between aeons in ordered communion.
> *¶8 #13 [tentative]:* What is ‘sent’ in this context is a measured portion/dispensation of glory/nourishment.
> *¶8 #15 [moderate]:* Restores ‘living voice of truth’ consistently with ¶6 and makes the nourishment immediate through uttered order.
> *¶8 #16 [tentative]:* Links nourishment to the affective/intellectual state that follows (‘joy … set up anew’).
> *¶8 #17 [moderate]:* Modifies ‘joy’ as a state of the aeons under renewed ordering.
> *¶8 #18 [tentative]:* ‘set up anew’ corresponds to re-establishment of cosmic order/form in the aeons.
> *¶8 #19 [moderate]:* Introduces the conditional/temporal sequence for the next efflux (‘It shall gush forth…’).
> *¶8 #20 [moderate]:* What gushes forth from splendour/image is praise (echoing ¶3 springs of praise).
> *¶8 #21 [tentative]:* The aeons become rich in ‘all forms’—the correspondential result of sculpted/sealed glory (form as ultimate of internal qualities).
> *¶8 #22 [moderate]:* Relative link to the following aesthetic predicates (beautiful/portrayal/adornment).
> *¶8 #23 [tentative]:* Matches ‘portrayal’ language: the manifested image appears in ordered likenesses across the aeons.
> *¶8 #24 [strong]:* Completes ‘for ev[e]r’.

**Assessment:** Most gaps can be constrained by the internal correspondential structure: five limbs → five effluxes (light/perfume/voice/quality/splendour), and the fifth limb’s ‘image’ logic requires sculpting/sealing, ordered transmission, nourishment, renewed order, and praise. Several mid-length restorations remain tentative where only general substrate-parallelism (emanation, sealing as determination, praise as efflux) constrains the wording but not the exact Coptic phrasing.

---

## Chapter 22: [On] the Land of Light

**¶1** ⟨p.65⟩
[This is the boundary / the firmament of] the Land of Light.

> *¶1 #1 [moderate]:* Opening formula fits an impersonal cosmological exposition: the text is locating a limit or surrounding that defines the Land of Light in ultimates (a boundary/firmament), rather than a narrative action.

**¶2** [There is] in the great land of greatness [th]s I will reveal to you [its measure and its form] above.

> *¶2 #1 [moderate]:* Standard existential incipit for a descriptive catalogue of a region’s properties.
> *¶2 #3 [tentative]:* Correspondentially, “great land of greatness” is defined by qualitative determinations (measure/form) that can be ‘revealed’ rather than praised; keeps impersonal, pre-institutional register.

**¶3** [its]ace is [a likeness / a pattern of] living [and living / from]om [the great Light / the Father] upon him.

> *¶3 #2 [tentative]:* The passage is moving into image-language: face as the formed appearance of an inner life; “likeness/pattern” matches correspondential doctrine (inner causes appear as forms).
> *¶3 #3 [moderate]:* Keeps the syntactic hinge “living … from …”; “and living” coheres with the repeated ‘living’ epithets in this chapter (living air, living ambrosia).
> *¶3 #4 [moderate]:* In the oldest substrate the source is the impersonal Great Light/Father of Greatness; this constrains what ‘upon him’ can mean (influx rests upon the manifested face).

**¶4** [There is] light and [power and / understanding] face [turned] out; he has [a]ace of the [Greatness] in the [aeons] now fi[vessels] of prais[e and hymn / glorification] in the [aeons] living [am]bros[i]a [and] five [powers] compound [together] they become an image [and] the li[v]ing air [that is] also living.

> *¶4 #1 [moderate]:* Resumes descriptive mode.
> *¶4 #2 [tentative]:* A discrete-degree pairing: will/power with intellectual understanding; keeps pre-Christian cosmological virtues rather than ecclesial terms.
> *¶4 #3 [moderate]:* Gives a coherent clause: “face … turned out”.
> *¶4 #5 [moderate]:* “face of the Greatness” is a common substrate title: the manifested appearance of the realm/source.
> *¶4 #6 [moderate]:* The location is ‘in the aeons’; matches ¶8 where aeons dwell in living air.
> *¶4 #7 [tentative]:* “now five vessels of praise” suits the subsequent ‘five … compound … image’: five receptacles/forms composing a single image.
> *¶4 #8 [tentative]:* Restores a two-term doxological binomial in impersonal register (qualities of utterance as forms), without introducing later liturgical specifics.
> *¶4 #9 [moderate]:* Keeps the setting consistent: praise occurs ‘in the aeons’.
> *¶4 #12 [moderate]:* Connects living ambrosia to the next enumerand (“five …”).
> *¶4 #13 [moderate]:* “five powers” is a standard way to name a quintad of living constituents that become one image.
> *¶4 #14 [strong]:* Supports “compound … they become an image”.
> *¶4 #15 [strong]:* Links to the following clause about living air.
> *¶4 #17 [tentative]:* Gives the explanatory apposition: “the living air … also living, that is …”.

**¶5** [As] those five shall [be freed] from all the contests of the greatness [and are established in rest,] and a beautiful [garment] to the limbs of their body [h]hich exists [there in] the land of light; their [true]]ife.

> *¶5 #2 [moderate]:* Correspondentially, the five (powers/vessels) are released from ‘contests’ (mixtures/struggles) back into ordered form.
> *¶5 #3 [tentative]:* Opposes “contests” with “rest/establishment,” a common cosmological polarity.
> *¶5 #4 [moderate]:* A beautiful ‘garment’ corresponds to an invested form around inner life; fits “to the limbs of their body”.
> *¶5 #6 [strong]:* Makes the clause read: “which exists there in the land of light”.
> *¶5 #7 [moderate]:* “their true life” contrasts with contested/mixed states; keeps impersonal ontology.

**¶6** Just as all the gods and the rich ones and the [angels who]ho were summoned came from the Father, so also all [the mountains]tains [and rivers,] springs and trees [and all fruits] gush forth [from the same root.] great is a [likeness] every thing that you [see / perceive] neither water nor [fire]

> *¶6 #3 [moderate]:* Natural correspondential series: mountains–springs–trees; rivers fit between mountains and springs as outflow forms.
> *¶6 #4 [moderate]:* Trees correspond to perceptions/knowledges; ‘fruits’ are effects/uses proceeding from them.
> *¶6 #5 [tentative]:* “Gush forth” needs a single origin analogous to all beings coming from the Father.
> *¶6 #6 [moderate]:* “great is a likeness” introduces correspondential statement: natural things are likenesses of higher realities.
> *¶6 #7 [moderate]:* Two verbs matching outward sight and inward perception, fitting correspondential teaching method.
> *¶6 #8 [moderate]:* Completes the elemental contrast: neither water nor fire (i.e., not material elements, but living realities).

**¶7** These ar[elements] which [are images / correspondences]

> *¶7 #1 [moderate]:* Refers back to water/fire; sets up explanation that these are not the physical elements but their correspondential forms.
> *¶7 #2 [moderate]:* Explicitly states the correspondential principle of the catalogue in ¶6.

**¶8** The [n]-th [is the] great light, the living air [and the firmament / the boundary] in which the aeons of greatness dwell, being established therein. [of the / great ang]el[s] that [stand] and the [powers / orders] the gi[fts] of the great Father [and the / treasures] in the [aeons.]

> *¶8 #1 [minimal]:* Completes “The n-th”: ordinal marker is too damaged; minimal restoration.
> *¶8 #2 [strong]:* Supplies syntax for the descriptive sentence about the great light/living air.
> *¶8 #3 [tentative]:* Matches ¶1’s boundary language; living air functions as an enclosing medium where aeons dwell.
> *¶8 #4 [moderate]:* Restores “great angels” as a class established in that realm.
> *¶8 #6 [moderate]:* “angels that stand” is a stable cosmological posture (fixed order) rather than narrative motion.
> *¶8 #7 [tentative]:* Pairs with angels: orders/powers as structural ranks in the Land of Light.
> *¶8 #9 [tentative]:* “gifts of the great Father and the treasures” fits the distributive imagery of a realm stocked with living goods.
> *¶8 #10 [moderate]:* Closes by locating gifts within the aeons/realm already mentioned.

**¶9** Blessed is whoever will k[know the / correspondences]

> *¶9 #1 [tentative]:* The beatitude is reinterpreted in oldest-layer register as knowing the science of correspondences (not faith/obedience).

**Assessment:** Much of the chapter is a descriptive cosmological catalogue: boundaries of the Land of Light, quintads that compound into a single living image, and the correspondential relation between higher orders (Father/aeons/angels/powers) and lower forms (mountains/springs/trees). Because the surviving text is extremely fragmentary, many restorations are constrained more by the internal correspondential logic (ordered emanation, quintad-as-image, boundary/firmament, natural forms as likenesses) than by tight philological anchors; confidence is therefore mostly moderate/tentative, with strong confidence only where the lacuna is a single letter or a necessary grammatical join.

## Chapter 23: [ ... ] which [ ... ]

**¶2** What the essence of [the two natures]—no person could recount or unveil [or say how it] exists [from ever and to ever]—all [powers] and [aeons] unveil [their origin] [in the] eternity [of] great [Father] again and the [aeons] tend [to] the beginning of eternity and the end [and no one can] set that one down.

> *¶2 #1 [moderate]:* Opening is defining the inmost being of the primal duality (Light and Darkness) prior to mixture; oldest substrate speaks of 'two natures' rather than later personal titles.
> *¶2 #3 [moderate]:* Standard eternity formula in this stratum: not duration but unoriginated continuance.
> *¶2 #4 [moderate]:* The discourse immediately turns to what can and cannot 'recount' or 'unveil'; that work is ascribed to powers/aeons, not human persons.
> *¶2 #5 [moderate]:* Pairs naturally with 'powers' in this cosmological register; both can 'unveil' the structure of eternity.
> *¶2 #6 [moderate]:* What is unveiled is not moral teaching but origin/constitution (correspondential 'how it exists').
> *¶2 #7 [tentative]:* Connects to the following term 'eternity' as a locative/ontic frame ('in the eternity').
> *¶2 #8 [tentative]:* Yields the phrase 'eternity of the great …'
> *¶2 #9 [moderate]:* Even in the oldest layer, 'Father' can function as name for the highest Light principle as source (not Christian paternity).
> *¶2 #10 [moderate]:* Balances 'great Father' with his 'aeons' as the ordered extensions of Light.
> *¶2 #11 [tentative]:* Introduces the clause about tending toward beginning/end as conceptual limits.
> *¶2 #12 [moderate]:* Closes the sentence: the beginning/end of eternity cannot be 'set down' (defined) by any recounting.

**¶3** Now, my [child] the land of death [and the abyss] nature of the essence [and the whole] which exists [ver]ver and ever [from ever] in [set] with one another.

> *¶3 #1 [moderate]:* Vocative address; the core layer often uses pedagogical 'child' without hagiographic frame.
> *¶3 #2 [moderate]:* Pairs with 'land of death' as Darkness' domain: land + abyss (depth).
> *¶3 #3 [tentative]:* Prepares 'nature of the essence' as totality, not a part.
> *¶3 #5 [moderate]:* Completes the eternity formula: 'which exists from ever for ever and ever'.
> *¶3 #6 [tentative]:* With 'in … with one another' the sense is 'set/placed' (ordered) in relation; not moral union.

**¶4** As for the eternity of [the two regions] which exists from ever, no person is able to [understand] how they exist. For no other power [is manifes]ted to them, in that it might speak or recount about their origin, how [xist]xist. However, from the time when it stirred itself and rose up against the light [in order that] it might come and rule over the land of the living.

> *¶4 #1 [moderate]:* Maintains impersonal cosmography: two regions/realms existing from ever; avoids later community terms.
> *¶4 #5 [moderate]:* Introduces purpose clause: Darkness rises so that it may come to rule the Living land.

**¶5** ⟨p.70⟩
[The Fi]rst Man came forth against it and prevented it, [he kept] it apart from the house of the living. He prevented it. From that time, when the light was mixed with [the] darkness, and life [th]th death, there was a measure of approval and an ability for [the] first-born [and the a]postle[s] to teach about it. They speak and unveil how the reproach and the fight did hap[pen], [how] the light [was] entwined with the darkness, which they [caught] life in death.

> *¶5 #11 [moderate]:* They 'unveil' how Light was entwined with Darkness—i.e., caught/held within it—so that the next clause 'life in death' reads as entrapment.

**¶6** [And] stirred itself in its essence, its th[ought and counsel] in its own land. It [was gathered in] the entire land of darkness in its [forms] of light in the abyss. For ⟨p.68⟩ [a spark] essence in [i]t [it was moved] because a [likeness] they reckoned [a great treasure] on its side [and desired] this first nature [to devour] it, because its body is made strong [and harder] than the Adamant [stone] every [kind] as it exists [se]se.

> *¶6 #1 [tentative]:* Begins new exposition of Darkness' motion after stirring; simple connective fits the narrative sequence.
> *¶6 #2 [moderate]:* Darkness' 'thought' in its own land is its planning/contrivance; the pair 'thought and counsel' is idiomatic in this register.
> *¶6 #3 [tentative]:* Darkness consolidates itself to mobilize its realm for ascent.
> *¶6 #4 [moderate]:* Its land exists in 'forms' (ugly shapes) corresponding to interior states; prepares later description of monstrous creatures.
> *¶6 #5 [moderate]:* Refers to Light perceived in the abyss as a 'spark' or glimmer, provoking the assault.
> *¶6 #7 [moderate]:* Explains reaction to the spark/light in the abyss: Darkness is stirred/moved.
> *¶6 #8 [moderate]:* They 'reckoned' it according to a likeness (correspondential misreading): Darkness interprets Light by its own form.
> *¶6 #9 [moderate]:* Darkness supposes the light is a treasure on its side; common Manichaean-cosmology motif in impersonal form.
> *¶6 #10 [moderate]:* Desire follows reckoning: it wants to seize what it imagines.
> *¶6 #11 [moderate]:* Oldest layer frames Darkness' relation as consumption/appropriation, not moral temptation.
> *¶6 #12 [strong]:* Comparative leading to Adamant: 'strong and harder than the Adamant'.
> *¶6 #13 [moderate]:* Completes 'Adamant stone' as the hard ultimate used for correspondence of fixed falsity/obduracy.
> *¶6 #14 [tentative]:* Generalizing statement: 'every kind as it exists in the universe'.

**¶7** [Its root] of death [is called] envy, which [rules] the land of [darkness and its consi]deration in [all] wickedness [and cruelty] for as it [stirs] the wounds [up] against this land [and makes war]

> *¶7 #1 [moderate]:* Introduces the interior source of the realm: the root of death is envy/jealous craving.
> *¶7 #2 [moderate]:* Gives definitional tone: root of death 'is called' envy.
> *¶7 #3 [moderate]:* Envy functions as ruling impulse in the dark land.
> *¶7 #4 [moderate]:* Restores phrase to 'darkness and its consideration'—the dark mode of thought/intent.
> *¶7 #5 [strong]:* Envy fills the land with 'all wickedness'.
> *¶7 #6 [moderate]:* Standard pairing with wickedness in this mythic-psychic cosmology; matches later line in ¶15.
> *¶7 #7 [moderate]:* Envy 'stirs' and thus produces assaults; matches earlier 'stirred itself'.
> *¶7 #8 [strong]:* Completes 'stirs up'.
> *¶7 #9 [moderate]:* Gives the outward effect of envy in correspondences: war/assault against the living land.

**¶8** Then he will run [away]rom the land [of] darkness. It drew [back] his members [for its depth] is deep [and it hid itself] in them.

> *¶8 #1 [moderate]:* Phrase 'run away from the land' fits pursuit/flight motif after aggression begins.
> *¶8 #2 [strong]:* Yields 'from the land of darkness'.
> *¶8 #3 [moderate]:* It 'drew back his members'—recoiling/withdrawing from danger.
> *¶8 #4 [moderate]:* Explains the pull: darkness is deep; depth corresponds to obscurity and entanglement.
> *¶8 #5 [moderate]:* Concludes: darkness hides itself in the members (mixture/implantation).

**¶9** [It gathered] every [out into its worlds.] It spread itself out. It generated itself [of the five dark elements]; which [are] smoke, fire, wind, water and darkness. [And from them] sprouted trees. It came up [it also] it formed its own self in the [and from them] it generated its own self with its birth [and] numerous [tions]tions, not resembling each other; [in] the storehouses of its abyss, many [dark] creatures [were born] destroyers and dragons and evil beasts. It was established and [and] and grew strong [it stood fast] in the ugly forms of those in the darkness [in its powers] dark, and by the [they were] impetuosity, by their wickedness and hatred they slaughter [their] each other as they war with one another.

> *¶9 #1 [moderate]:* Continues the self-expansion of Darkness: gathering then spreading then generating.
> *¶9 #2 [moderate]:* Gives destination of spreading: its 'worlds' (sub-regions) within the abyss.
> *¶9 #5 [moderate]:* From the elements arise growths and bodies; prepares 'sprouted trees'.
> *¶9 #6 [moderate]:* Leads into a second action: it sprouted trees; it also came up/formed itself.
> *¶9 #7 [moderate]:* Connects 'came up' with its formation from elemental bases.
> *¶9 #8 [moderate]:* Smooths syntax: 'with its birth in numerous emanations'.
> *¶9 #12 [moderate]:* Creatures in the storehouses of the abyss 'were born'—generation language rather than creation.
> *¶9 #13 [strong]:* Links list: destroyers and dragons and evil beasts.
> *¶9 #14 [moderate]:* Cosmic stabilization: established and stood fast, then grew strong.
> *¶9 #15 [moderate]:* Its strengthening is in its powers/forces; fits later 'world and its powers'.
> *¶9 #16 [moderate]:* Subject shifts to inhabitants: 'those in the darkness … they slaughter'.
> *¶9 #17 [moderate]:* By the 'impetuosity' and wickedness/hatred, 'they slaughter their' own—reflexive violence.

**¶10** Then the counsel of their ruler humbled its world and its powers. It sensed the essence of the [light of the land of the living] It conspired to wage a war [and to contend] with the exalted kingdom and the [great earth]

> *¶10 #1 [moderate]:* The ruler senses the essence/scent of the living Light, provoking conspiracy to invade.
> *¶10 #2 [moderate]:* War is framed as contention/strife against the exalted kingdom.
> *¶10 #3 [moderate]:* Matches later 'that light earth' (¶15): the luminous earth/region opposed to dark abyss.

**¶11** However, he, the Father of Great[ness, who knows all things] it also knew [the coming] war, to disturb the [land of] light [and to trouble] in the wisdom of his [power]-ness [great] he transformed himself against it [and sent a power] against it.

> *¶11 #1 [moderate]:* Restores epithet in oldest register: Father of Greatness as omniscient principle, not narrative persona.
> *¶11 #2 [moderate]:* He knew 'the coming war' as imminent motion from Darkness.
> *¶11 #4 [moderate]:* Parallel to 'disturb': trouble the light by invasion/mixing.
> *¶11 #5 [tentative]:* In wisdom of his power-ness: might/strength as attribute.
> *¶11 #6 [moderate]:* Forms 'great-ness' (megalosyne): typical Coptic rendering for a superlative attribute.
> *¶11 #7 [moderate]:* Oldest layer: response is an emanated power/aeon dispatched against Darkness; avoids later ecclesial language.

**¶12** Indeed, at that time [a power went forth] go against [it] God [the Living]

> *¶12 #1 [moderate]:* Continuation of ¶11: at that time an emanated power proceeds to meet the assault.
> *¶12 #2 [moderate]:* Refers back to the dark assault/war as object of going against.
> *¶12 #3 [moderate]:* Restores 'Living God' in cosmological sense (principle of Life), not confessional Christian phrase.

**¶13** [Then]om its [garments] poured it out [and] in the elements: in the smoke, the fire, the wind, the water and the darkness. It came up from the [great] depth, it being set in the trees [and stood] against it.

> *¶13 #1 [moderate]:* Narrative connective from sending/going forth to resulting mixture in elements.
> *¶13 #2 [moderate]:* What is poured out is the Light-armor of the First Man, later called 'five garments'.
> *¶13 #3 [strong]:* Joins verbs: poured it out and set it in elements.
> *¶13 #5 [moderate]:* Trees '… and stood against it'—the set light in trees stands as resistance/containment.

**¶14** Also, at the time when it generated itself [from the trees] it formed the fruits, it still being set in them. They summoned the five garments, the sons of the Fir[st] Man.

> *¶14 #1 [moderate]:* At generation in the trees it forms fruits; consistent with Light bound in vegetal forms.

**¶15** And at the time when it generated its own self [f]rom the fruits through its offspring, they fell upon the earth and [took] strength from the elements and were nourished by them. [Then envy arose,] wickedness, together with cruelty [it being] in the ruler, the leader of all the demons. He hum[bled] his worlds and his powers; he gathered them all into him. He made himself ready for the battle; he set himself to the war with that light earth, and the great King who dwells in it.

> *¶15 #2 [strong]:* They fell on earth and 'took strength from the elements'.
> *¶15 #3 [moderate]:* Reintroduces the interior motive (envy) as the driver behind the ruler's consolidation for battle.
> *¶15 #4 [moderate]:* Links clause: wickedness with cruelty, it being in the ruler.

**¶16** At that very moment the First Man approached his garments, the gods [ho shine]ine forth. He spoke with them, that he might entrust to them a [charge] he [reve]aled to them and taught them about everything that [was hidden in] them. He assumed them. He arranged them [was to come upon] He draped them over one another upon his limbs [and armed himself] danger, it [And when the] the heights of the earth [came down upon] with all its powers [from the beg]inning, it being immeasurable [to the end]

> *¶16 #2 [moderate]:* He entrusts to them a charge/commission—functional instruction in oldest register.
> *¶16 #4 [moderate]:* He taught them about what was hidden in them (their interior powers correspond to their forms).
> *¶16 #5 [moderate]:* Completes: everything that was to come upon them—foreknowledge of combat/mixing.
> *¶16 #6 [moderate]:* At this point he 'assumes' and 'arranges' the garments; 'armed himself' matches draping over limbs.
> *¶16 #7 [moderate]:* Introduces the opponent's approach and the onset of danger.
> *¶16 #8 [moderate]:* The danger/powers of Darkness 'came down upon the heights of the earth' (upper boundary of mixture).
> *¶16 #10 [moderate]:* Formula for total extent: with all its powers from the beginning to the end, immeasurable.

**Assessment:** Much of the chapter is recoverable at the level of cosmological function (dual natures, stirring of Darkness, perception of Light as treasure, generation of five elements, vegetal binding, consolidation for war, arming of First Man). Exact micro-phrasing is often not tightly constrained because many brackets likely represent short Coptic connectors/epithets; where the narrative logic demanded specific mythic motifs (spark/treasure, five garments, envy as root, light-earth vs abyss), restorations are moderately secure. Several small particles remain tentative because multiple Coptic equivalents could fit.

## Chapter 24: [... / ...]

**¶1** [There is no measure nor rule to the time that the Father spent in his Great Spirit; but] a great ju[dge] now also a ti[me] that they sculpt[ed] he was revealed in the Father, the first established thing.

> *¶1 #1 [moderate]:* Opening formula elsewhere in this chapter contrasts measurable grains with immeasurable duration in the Father. The substrate register prefers impersonal cosmological statement (no dialogue frame).
> *¶1 #2 [strong]:* Completes “judge”.
> *¶1 #3 [strong]:* Completes “time”.
> *¶1 #4 [strong]:* Completes “sculpted”.

**¶2** W[he]n [the Father called forth his Great Spirit;] the two summonses that he called [and he brought forth] the Mother of Life [and he established] a long duration in the first Father [in his Great Spirit]

> *¶2 #2 [moderate]:* The sequence in ¶14 presupposes Father → Great Spirit → First Man, etc. “called forth” matches emanation logic of oldest layer more than institutional “summons”.
> *¶2 #3 [moderate]:* Pairs with “called” and leads into listing emanations.
> *¶2 #4 [moderate]:* Establishment in inner storehouses is a recurring motif (¶5, ¶7, ¶16).
> *¶2 #5 [moderate]:* The Great Spirit is repeatedly the medium/realm in which sculpting and establishing occur (¶9–11, ¶14–16).

**¶3** For someone, if he might set a measure and rule [in the whole universe] the earth {particles} and the {pebbles} of the mountains and [of the rivers] of the sea and the body of the firmaments. Should they be poured [out from one vessel into another,] the body of the four mountains and the three vessels that surround the universe; if they shall all be poured out [then they would still not exhaust] these fine {granules}.

> *¶3 #1 [moderate]:* Balances the ensuing inventory (earth, mountains, sea, firmaments) and sets up ¶4 “entire universe”.
> *¶3 #2 [tentative]:* Standard cosmological list: earth/mountains/sea/rivers/firmaments; “rivers” fits the missing natural domain between mountains and sea.
> *¶3 #3 [tentative]:* The imagery is of measuring/pouring granular matter into “vessels” as a correspondential measure.
> *¶3 #4 [moderate]:* Required for the rhetorical comparison: even if poured out, they would not finish counting the “fine granules”.

**¶4** Happen you know that the grains of the earth {particles} are measurable, [and those of] the [entire] universe. It is possible to count the {particles} of the earth each year [and count] the sand grains of the entire universe; but the duration of the time that the Great Spirit spent in the Father, [the] first established thing, one will not be able to make a count of it!

> *¶4 #1 [strong]:* Accepts apparatus: balances measurable earth and measurable universe.
> *¶4 #3 [moderate]:* Parallelism: “possible to count… each year … and count the sand grains…”.

**¶5** He first sculpted her like this. He established her in his inner storehouses in quiet and silence. When [they had] need of her she was called and came forth of the Father [of Greatness]. She looked at all her aeons of light!

**¶6** Once again, from the time when she was called to [to come forth and] go to [the place where she was sent;] she gave [power] upon his [bod]y [and clothed him with] all the aeons [the five aeons;]; she also [she gathered] all of them. This [is also the case] with the First Man.

> *¶6 #1 [moderate]:* Maintains the repeated pattern: called → came forth → went to appointed region.
> *¶6 #2 [moderate]:* Oldest layer tends to impersonal “place/region” rather than mission language.
> *¶6 #3 [moderate]:* She “gave power upon his body” fits Mother-of-Life empowering First Man (investiture/anointing).
> *¶6 #5 [moderate]:* Correspondential investiture: aeons/gods as “garment/armour” of light.
> *¶6 #6 [tentative]:* The Mother’s endowment culminates in the fivefold powers that later are “called” as five shining gods (¶8, ¶12).
> *¶6 #7 [tentative]:* Balances “she also … all of them”: gathering/ordering the aeons into him.

**¶7** This is how the Man spent a long time being anointed in the Mother, set fast in her inner storehouses [in quiet and silence]; the extent of time that I have recounted to you—from when the First Man went down to the contest, till the time when the [Statue co]mes i[n his coming forth;] this time [is without measure,] he appeared [in the Father.]

> *¶7 #1 [strong]:* Directly echoes ¶5 and matches “inner storehouses” motif.
> *¶7 #2 [moderate]:* Accepts the editor’s tentative segmentation: anticipates “Last Statue” at ¶8, ¶28–29.
> *¶7 #3 [tentative]:* Restores sense: measure of time from descent to contest until the Statue comes in its coming-forth (i.e., the final formation).
> *¶7 #4 [moderate]:* Reinforces the chapter’s theme: durations are immeasurable compared to grains.
> *¶7 #5 [moderate]:* Ends the clause by returning to the Father as the locus of immeasurable duration.

**¶8** Again, from when he was called and came forth, till the time when he [himse]lf called the five shining [gods] who are his sons; they themselves also spent a long time existing in their father! It is the time that occurred from the coming down of the First Man till the going up of the Last Statue. The five gods spent this much duration being anointed in the First Man. Yet, [at the] time when they were awaited, they were called and came forth.

**¶9** Listen also to this, how I would teach you it. At the time when the Mother of Life was called from the Father of Greatness, when she expelled herself and came down by her own wish, from the heights of the Father to the earth, [she would spend thousands of] years and many ten thousands until she reached the earth. [Fo]r there is no measure nor rule to the heights of the Father. Rather, [just as] he had called her in his Great Spirit, so he set her upon [the] land of greatness through one of these three that are counted to the twelve hidden ones within the light veil, the ones [esta]blished after the [great region] region of the south.

> *¶9 #1 [strong]:* Accepts apparatus; matches hyperbolic duration motif.
> *¶9 #6 [tentative]:* The “region of the south” is a fixed quarter; “great region” matches cosmographic terminology without later ecclesial overlay.

**¶10** [The] matter of the Mother [L]ife is like this. When [the Father called her he established her in the place he pleased,] to establish her there as the instruction of the people that [the hearers] many [aeons of light,]; and she examined everywhere that [as if it were] a blink of an eye, or like a [swift] bird [that flies.]

> *¶10 #3 [strong]:* Accepts apparatus; suits the teaching-point: instantaneous establishment despite “thousands of years” in appearance.
> *¶10 #4 [minimal]:* Substrate likely had “people”; Coptic “instruction of the people” survives. “hearers” is later community language, so I avoid it here; but the sentence wants a category of recipients. Best oldest-layer equivalent is simply “those below”.
> *¶10 #5 [tentative]:* She “examined everywhere” across aeons; fits cosmological survey.
> *¶10 #6 [strong]:* Sets up simile: blink of eye / flight of bird.
> *¶10 #7 [moderate]:* Quality required for the bird simile.
> *¶10 #8 [moderate]:* Completes the simile structure.

**¶11** This also is lik[e ... the] Mother of Life in [measure] whenever [nor rule] cast her out [she was] upon her to the place [and he] him. He perfected her in his Great Spirit and set her in [the place he pl]eased, according to [his will].

> *¶11 #1 [minimal]:* Too little context to restore exact phrase; leave ellipsis as given style.
> *¶11 #2 [tentative]:* Parallels ¶10’s point: apparent travel vs instant establishment; “no measure” motif.
> *¶11 #3 [strong]:* Fixed pair throughout chapter (¶4, ¶9, ¶16).
> *¶11 #4 [moderate]:* Needed for syntax: “whenever … she was cast out…”.
> *¶11 #5 [moderate]:* Returns agency to Father perfecting/setting her.

**¶12** [Th]is [also is how] they called the [five g]ods from the [Fi]rst Man. They were set before him, as he wished that [their likeness]-ness [shining] down, that they would rule in them [came] one another. He ordered and distributed them [down into the borders,] the son. He stripped himself of them [and would not strike] and he [to each their portion;] he girt and bound himself to [from the son] in a [as garments] of silence upon the border of the earth [a bond]

> *¶12 #5 [tentative]:* The five are set “before him” as forms/powers to be assumed; “likeness” is common for manifested forms in emanational schemes.
> *¶12 #6 [strong]:* Matches “five shining gods” (¶8).
> *¶12 #7 [moderate]:* Needed for verbal flow: he wished that shining-ness came down.
> *¶12 #8 [moderate]:* The five rule within the boundary-region between Light and Darkness; “borders” is consistent with ¶13.
> *¶12 #9 [tentative]:* “rule in them … one another” implies ordered non-conflict among the five powers.
> *¶12 #10 [strong]:* Matches “ordered and distributed them”.
> *¶12 #11 [tentative]:* The phrase “the son” likely refers to First Man as son of Mother of Life; distribution “to the son” fits investiture.
> *¶12 #12 [moderate]:* Manichaean/older Iranian motif: the powers as armour/garments of light.
> *¶12 #13 [tentative]:* “girt and bound himself to … in a … of silence” calls for “bond/cord”.

**¶13** Again, he too, the First Man, spent a long period and [time] until he gave power to his sons; until he assu[m]ed them. After he came to wear them he stood fast in the heights between the borders. Again, he spent a duration and time like this, corresponding to such amount and measure as the raindrops and dew and falls of mist that come down in the universe. This is how he spent this duration also, standing between the borders, until the hour when he cast himself down to the contest!

**¶14** Whenever the Father of Greatn[e]ss called the Great Spirit and the First Man and the gods of the glorious one, who are provided to enable them to reveal him; at that very time [he] sculpted the Great Builder in the [land of] light, [and] the Living Spirit with the mighty and active gods who came [from] him. Also, at that time, he sculpted the [Amb]assador, with Jesus the Splendour and the Virgin of Light and the Pillar of Glory, and the gods who came from them. [All these the Father of Greatness sculpted at] a single time. They came forth of one another, one after one; the time when [their single moment was one, both the going-forth] and the work.

> *¶14 #8 [tentative]:* Closes: “They came forth…; the time when … and the work.” Oldest layer stresses simultaneity-in-origin though sequential in procession.

**¶15** [It is also the same] from the time when the darkness raised itself up to [with the time] essence of the light [that occurred] these three emanations [from the beginning,] from the will of the Father against the enemy [the boundary] darkness [and the mixture] according to the will [that came to be] the universe [was prepared] separate out [and the separation] and he rejoices [of the light] in his kingdom; until [the time when the face] of the Father will be unveiled over [all] the gods of glory, and all the rich ones of [the riches] the em[emanations] who came forth against the enemy.

> *¶15 #1 [moderate]:* Introduces the next measured interval: from uprising of Darkness to final unveiling.
> *¶15 #2 [strong]:* Needed to connect to “from the time when… until…”.
> *¶15 #3 [strong]:* Standard connective phrasing.
> *¶15 #4 [moderate]:* Cosmogonic horizon: first assault of Darkness.
> *¶15 #5 [moderate]:* The uprising is “to the boundary” of Light; border theme in ¶12–13.
> *¶15 #6 [moderate]:* Core substrate logic: conflict leads to mixture; later separation is salvation-physics.
> *¶15 #7 [strong]:* Syntax support.
> *¶15 #8 [moderate]:* The three emanations are prepared “from the will of the Father against the enemy”.
> *¶15 #9 [moderate]:* Pairs with mixture; matches “separate out … and he rejoices”.
> *¶15 #10 [strong]:* What is separated out is Light from Darkness/Matter.
> *¶15 #13 [moderate]:* “the rich ones of …” wants a noun: riches/treasures of light.
> *¶15 #14 [strong]:* Completes “em[anations]”.

**¶16** [From] that time when he sculpted these three [emanations] till the time when the Father would unveil his face, [and they] go in to his hidden storehouses, and he calls them [again and again,] establishes each one of them to his place. There is no measure nor rule to this period until that time.

> *¶16 #2 [strong]:* Back-refers to “these three emanations” in ¶15.
> *¶16 #4 [tentative]:* He calls/establishes each to its place in ordered return; iterative phrasing fits impersonal cosmological procedure.

**¶20** ... he gives [the Instructor] about this Matter, this thought of death [instruction] which I have told you; for [this] is the way that [of destruction,] came up to its storehouses. It has [Matter] in its five elements.

> *¶20 #1 [minimal]:* Likely later layer; oldest substrate would say “the teaching” not a person. But the Coptic sentence needs a subject “he gives … about this Matter”. Best substrate-compatible is “the instruction gives”.
> *¶20 #2 [minimal]:* Replaces personal “he gives” logic with impersonal teaching register; but kept minimal due to uncertainty.
> *¶20 #4 [tentative]:* “thought of death” aligns with dissolution/destruction motif of Matter.
> *¶20 #5 [moderate]:* Must be the entity returning to its storehouses; fits the last clause “in its five elements.”

**¶21** Again, it also [measures] the period and the time that it spent, until it revealed itself to its elements, before it came to the trees. It spent a great period and a long time established [concerns] before it came up to the trees. It was [in the elements,] in the trees. It spent a long time and duration [in them] existing in them, before it sculpted the fru[its formation] and sculpted the fruits.

> *¶21 #1 [tentative]:* “it also … the period and the time” wants a verb of measuring/considering.
> *¶21 #2 [tentative]:* Connects to “the period and the time that it spent”.
> *¶21 #3 [strong]:* Sequence: elements → trees → fruits; thus “revealed itself to its elements”.
> *¶21 #4 [strong]:* Resumes “its elements”.
> *¶21 #5 [tentative]:* Completes “fru[its ...]”: the process is sculpting/formation within fruits.

**¶22** Again, it spent a long [time] in the fruits. Not little is the period and the time that it spent existing in the fruits, before it sculpted [their shapes,] after a long time [afterwards] the d[emon]s of its five worlds in various forms [can not (?) ] be measured [in number,] in p[erfec]tion of the fruits in the demons and fiends. They fell upon their earth and [of nourishment] were nourished. Not little is the period that they [spent in the wo]rlds, until they were nourished amongst [the mixture,] to make [war] that land which shines, and the great [King] who dwells there.

> *¶22 #2 [tentative]:* Before demons are sculpted “in various forms”; “shapes” parallels.
> *¶22 #3 [moderate]:* Temporal hinge: long time in fruits before sculpting demons.
> *¶22 #6 [moderate]:* What cannot be measured is number/extent of forms.
> *¶22 #8 [tentative]:* Links demons feeding on fruits and being nourished.
> *¶22 #10 [tentative]:* They were nourished “amongst …” i.e., mixture of elements.
> *¶22 #11 [moderate]:* They make “war” against the shining land/King; sets up ¶23 war/Perfect Man.

**¶23** [You] know that [also know] that in [it happened] at the time when he [the enemy stirred up] its essence. It assumed [and seized] Matter [a portion of] to the war [the Living Soul] the Perfect Man. [From the time] when the war occurred [when this seizure occurred,]

> *¶23 #2 [strong]:* Standard didactic connective.
> *¶23 #3 [moderate]:* Introduces the narrated event of Matter’s arousal.
> *¶23 #4 [tentative]:* Matches ¶15 “darkness raised itself up”; now localized to Matter’s essence.
> *¶23 #5 [tentative]:* Matter assumes/captures light in war.
> *¶23 #6 [moderate]:* Consistent with light-particles captured and later separated.
> *¶23 #7 [tentative]:* Oldest layer often speaks of “soul/light” captured; “Living Soul” is plausible substrate term behind later personifications.
> *¶23 #9 [moderate]:* Sets temporal anchor for the fivefold schema in ¶24–28.

**¶24** [The first is the assault;] he came to be in some [bondage,] when they ensnared a [portion of light.]

> *¶24 #1 [tentative]:* Fits immediately: “he came to be in some … when they ensnared…” i.e., first phase is attack and capture.
> *¶24 #2 [moderate]:* They “ensnared” a portion; bondage is the state.
> *¶24 #3 [moderate]:* Required object of ensnaring consistent with particle imagery.

**¶25** [The second is the building;] that he built [and the fashioning of the spheres,] the universe was established. The zone [from above] down until the time when it will be dissolved.

> *¶25 #1 [strong]:* Matches: “that he built … the universe was established.”
> *¶25 #2 [tentative]:* “zone … down” implies ordered cosmic zones/spheres established for the mixture.
> *¶25 #3 [moderate]:* Completes “The zone [...] down until…”.

**¶26** The third is the [fourth thing?] the things and the universe will [fixing] from the time when they were established there, till the time when their end will be complete and their existence ceases [endure]

> *¶26 #1 [minimal]:* This paragraph’s ordinal is ‘third’; lacuna likely the label: “The third is the …”.
> *¶26 #2 [tentative]:* “the … the things and the universe will …” suggests fixing/ordering that persists until end.
> *¶26 #3 [moderate]:* Required verb: will endure from establishment till end.

**¶27** [The fourth is the dissolution when they will dissolve the universe] there, and all things are destroyed. Obliteration reaches them in that great fire, [which will] burn them for fourteen hundred and sixty-eight years.

**¶28** [The fift]h is the way that the Last Statue will be sculpted [from] the remnant of all things. The light separates out to its place, and goes up and rules in its kingdom. However, [the da]rkness [is gathered together,] and they take it in to the tomb [of darkness,] and they are bound with it in an eternal chain.

> *¶28 #4 [moderate]:* Opposes “light separates out”; darkness is consolidated for confinement.
> *¶28 #5 [moderate]:* Completes “tomb …” as “tomb of darkness” (the final prison).

**¶29** Now, from [the tim]e when this first war occurred, till the time of this chain [until the end of the universe,] these five things [the light is in separation.] light.

> *¶29 #2 [moderate]:* The chain persists to cosmic end; matches five-things schema ending in final binding.
> *¶29 #3 [moderate]:* Summative thesis: from first war to chain is the whole period of separation of light from mixture.

**Assessment:** Many brackets are only letter-completions (strong). Where whole-phrase gaps occur (esp. ¶10–12, ¶15, ¶20–26), the correspondential constraints let us restore the structural sense (immeasurable duration; call/emanate/establish; investiture of five powers; mixture vs separation; fivefold historical phases). Exact Coptic lexical choices in several places remain uncertain (notably ¶10 #4, ¶11 #1, ¶20 #1–2, ¶26 #1), so those restorations are tentative/minimal.

---

## Chapter 25: Concerning the Advent of Five Fathers from the Five Limbs of the Father

**¶2** The [Ambassador]sador [came] from his mind; the Beloved of the Lights [from his thought]; the Mother of Life from his insight; [Jesus] the Beloved from his counsel; the Virgin of Lig[ht f]rom his consideration.

> *¶2 #1 [strong]:* The sequence lists the five emanated hypostases (the “five fathers” in ¶3). In the oldest cosmological register this first is the emissary/manifestation proceeding from Mind; Coptic/Greek usage regularly renders it as “Ambassador.”
> *¶2 #2 [strong]:* Fixed by the parallelism of the five clauses: each hypostasis “came” (proceeded) from a distinct interior faculty of the Father.
> *¶2 #3 [strong]:* Correspondential series of interior principles: mind → thought → insight → counsel → consideration. Each is a distinct discrete degree/source for a distinct advent; “thought” is required here to preserve the fivefold mapping.
> *¶2 #4 [moderate]:* In the Kephalaia this name often overlays an older function-title (the revealer/beloved proceeding from counsel). Here the structure demands the fourth of the five advents; the transmitted core has retained the label “Jesus the Beloved,” which fits the fivefold scheme without introducing later exhortation.
> *¶2 #5 [strong]:* Mid-word restoration to complete “Lig[ht f]rom” → “Light from,” matching the repeated “from his …” pattern and keeping the procession formula intact.

**¶3** So in this way were summone[d these five] fathers. These five advents came from the five limbs [of the Father]. One came from another and was manifested in its season.

> *¶3 #1 [strong]:* Completes “summone[d these five] fathers,” the required transition naming the group as five and preparing the identification of their advents.
> *¶3 #2 [strong]:* These five advents correspond to the “five limbs” as discrete powers/regions belonging to the Father of Greatness; “of the Father” is demanded by the emanation logic and the possessive mapping in ¶2.

**Assessment:** All lacunae are tightly constrained by the fivefold correspondential scheme (five interior faculties → five advents → five limbs). Only ¶2 #4 retains an inherited Manichaean/Christianized label (“Jesus”); functionally it remains the fourth emanation from Counsel, so the restoration is still secure at the structural level.

---

## Chapter 26: Concerning the First Man and the Ambassador and the Ship; what they resemble.

**¶1** The First [Man] is like a great distinguished man whose treasure has been taken away from him. He came to pursue his treasure. This is how the [First] Man too came after his sons from the heights, so that he might redeem them and retrieve his treasure, which had been scattered amongst his enemies.

**¶2** Once again, [he is like a man] whose two sons were taken away from him by [robbers. He] came out toward them to redeem them. Again, this is how the First Man [too, and he] shines in the heights because of his two sons, who were separated from him. They are his living soul that exist in two powers, which makes four.

> *¶2 #2 [moderate]:* The comparison requires an impersonal hostile agency that seizes the ‘sons’ as treasure; ‘robbers’ matches the didactic simile register (non-moral, functional: seizure/abduction). The following ‘He came out…’ must resume the subject of the simile.
> *¶2 #3 [tentative]:* The clause must restate the doctrinal counterpart to the simile: the First Man comes forth toward the separated powers to redeem/recollect them. A minimal connector (‘too, and he’) preserves the substrate’s impersonal explanatory cadence and links to ‘shines in the heights because of his two sons’.

**¶3** Once again, the ship [of the day] is like a great spear [for its appointed conflict. The Great Spirit too, which is in it, is like a wise crafts]man who might beautify and set aright [all] the armour for war.

> *¶3 #2 [moderate]:* The ‘ship of the day’ (solar vessel) in the oldest cosmology is an ordered instrument for the daily combat/transit that carries and gathers Light. The lacuna must supply a purpose-clause that leads naturally into the artisan simile (‘craftsman… armour for war’).

**¶4** Once again, the Living Spirit is like a combatant who might go towards a king's son who has been taken prisoner of war, and he redeems him from the hands of his enemies.

**¶5** [The Amba]ssador too is like a great man [who has come forth] because of the treasure [which is scattered] hand of his prisoners [from the] these light powers [and he gathers up the] sons and their soul, so that they would [be purified, and the soul might] be brought forth in the land wherein it was imprisoned.

> *¶5 #2 [tentative]:* The Ambassador’s function in the substrate is emissive: a sending-out toward the mixed region to reclaim Light. ‘come forth’ keeps the cosmogonic, non-institutional register while preparing ‘because of the treasure…’.
> *¶5 #3 [moderate]:* Echoes ¶1’s ‘treasure… scattered amongst his enemies’; the Ambassador’s mission is defined by the dispersed Light/treasure in the hostile realm.
> *¶5 #4 [strong]:* The phrase must read ‘…from the hand of his prisoners…’; only a short preposition is needed to restore the idiom and maintain the parallel to ¶4 (‘from the hands of his enemies’).
> *¶5 #5 [moderate]:* The continuation requires an operative verb describing the Ambassador’s work upon the ‘light powers’ prior to ‘sons and their soul’. ‘gathers up’ matches the correspondential action of recollection/reassembly of scattered Light.
> *¶5 #6 [tentative]:* The end-goal stated is extraction ‘in the land wherein it was imprisoned’; the missing clause should express the necessary intermediate state-change of the mixed soul/light (purification/separation) before it can be brought forth. This keeps the oldest substrate’s technical process-language rather than moral exhortation.

**Assessment:** Most gaps are short connectors or purpose-clauses constrained by tight parallelism across the similes (treasure/sons seized → coming forth → redemption/recollection). Two larger restorations (¶3 #2; ¶5 #6) are moderately constrained by the cosmological function (solar vessel as instrument of daily conflict/gathering; ambassadorial action as purification/separation leading to extraction), but exact wording remains tentative due to lack of surviving lexical anchors.

---

## Chapter 27: Concerning the Five Forms that exist in the Rulers of Darkness.

**¶1** Consider the ruler, the leader of all the powers of Darkness. Now, five forms exist in his body, corresponding to (the archetype) of the five creations that exist in the five worlds of Darkness.

**¶2** Indeed, his head is lion-faced, which came about from the world of fire. His wings and his shoulders are eagle-faced, after the likeness of the children of the wind. His hands and his feet are daemons, after the likeness of the children of the world of smoke. His belly is dragon-faced, after the likeness of the world of Darkness. His tail is the form of the fish, which belongs to the world of the children of water. These five shapes are in him, and they came from the five creations of the five worlds of Darkness.

**¶3** ⟨p.78⟩
Should he wish, he shall walk on his two feet [like the children of the] world of smoke. Also, when he wants, he shall [walk upon] four [(feet)] on his hands and feet [and he shall run] like the children of fire. Should he wish, he shall rise up on his wings, like the children of the wind. Also, should he wish, he shall plunge down to the waters like the children of water. Again, should he wish, he shall crawl on his belly like the children of Darkness. These five shapes are in him.

> *¶3 #1 [moderate]:* The sentence contrasts his bipedal gait with the smoke-world’s proper form; the next clause explicitly mentions the world of smoke, so the comparison phrase must precede it.
> *¶3 #2 [moderate]:* He alternates forms at will: walk (biped), then another locomotion on four (quadruped) corresponding to fire’s swift predation. “walk upon four” matches the following “four … on his hands and feet”.
> *¶3 #3 [moderate]:* The syntactic frame requires the number to govern a body-part term before “on his hands and feet.” The natural completion is “four (feet)”.
> *¶3 #4 [moderate]:* Fire-children correspond to vehement, rapid motion; after “on his hands and feet” a verb of swift movement is demanded before “like the children of fire.”

**¶4** Also, there are three other (qualities) in him.

**¶5** The first: [When he wishes, he shall gather] his powers [and disperse]

> *¶5 #1 [moderate]:* The “first” quality must be an active sovereign function over subordinate powers, parallel to the later qualities (conceal/reveal; invulnerability). “Gather” fits the correspondential idea of concentration/command.
> *¶5 #2 [moderate]:* Paired opposites are typical in this substrate (bind/loose; gather/disperse). It also prepares the second quality (hiding/showing) by the same inside→outside control logic.

**¶6** The second: Shall a [man conceal by] his magic arts. When he wishes, he shall make an invocation over himself, and hide from his powers. When he wants, he shall show himself to them and strike a death-blow by his magic arts. Any word he might utter at that moment, he shall [work sorcery] by it.

> *¶6 #1 [tentative]:* The clause needs an infinitive construction: “Shall a … his magic arts.” In this register the point is concealment and sudden manifestation through art/word.
> *¶6 #2 [moderate]:* Adjusted from editor’s “wreak magic” to a more substrate-technical phrasing: effective speech produces operative change (word-as-force), not moral ‘wreaking’.

**¶7** The third: His body is strong, a[nd]] [so that the] nails and claws of his powers are not able to penetrate it. No iron nor copper body will equal him [in strength]; they will not be able to destroy him, because he was formed and fashioned from the hard-hearted counsel of Matter, the mother of demons and fiends.

> *¶7 #2 [moderate]:* Connects the strength statement to the proof: nails/claws cannot penetrate. A causal linker is required here.
> *¶7 #3 [moderate]:* The comparison “No iron nor copper body will equal him …; they will not be able to destroy him” demands a measure of resistance/strength rather than a moral predicate.

**¶8** Again, there are three others in him.

**¶9** When he wants, his fire shall {blaze), and his entire body be made like the [likeness] fire.

> *¶9 #1 [moderate]:* The idiom “made like the likeness of fire” is a Coptic calque for complete assimilation of quality (here: fiery state) rather than mere appearance.

**¶10** Also, when he wishes, he shall send out frost, and his entire body freezes like a [likeness] snow.

> *¶10 #1 [strong]:* Parallelism with ¶9 requires the same term: assimilation to the snow/frost state.

**¶11** The third: Whenever his powers are stood before him, he shall look at them and understand; for [whatever ex]ists in their heart he shall understand, what is in their heart and their face so long as they are stood before him. When they retreat before him and distance themselves from him, he shall not understand what is in their heart. There is no life in him whatsoever, but his 'life' is the bile of anger that is apparent on his face and in his fear. Shall a [also foresee] destruction that is establ[lished be]fore him.

> *¶11 #1 [strong]:* The bracket is mid-word and already supplies the necessary letters; no restoration beyond reproducing them is warranted.
> *¶11 #2 [tentative]:* The line concludes a triad of faculties: fiery alteration, icy alteration, and mind-reading while present. The final sentence needs a concluding capability oriented to inevitable outcome: he ‘foresees’ the destruction set before him (i.e., the determined terminus).
> *¶11 #3 [strong]:* Mid-word continuation; the letters supplied are the correct completion of “established be-”.

**Assessment:** Most gaps are short connective or parallel-structure restorations constrained by the five-form locomotion sequence and the repeated triadic pattern. The main uncertainties remain ¶5–6 (the exact verbs naming the first quality and the phrasing around concealment by arts) and ¶11 #2 (the concluding faculty), where multiple substrate-consistent options exist.

---

## Chapter 28: [Concerning the T]welve Judges [of] the Father

**¶2** Twelve judges exist; the great mighty givers of judgement! They were sent and came from the exalted greatness to this place. They were assigned to different works according to the command that the Judge, who belongs to the greatness, entrusted to all these judges. Now he, the great Judge, he who transcends all the judges, who exists and is established in the aeons of greatness; he is the Father of Greatness.

**¶3** The first judge is the First Man, he who humiliated the ruler of Darkness in the beginning. He judged him according to a judgement of righteousness; for he had exalted himself against a kingdom that was not his own.

**¶4** The second judge is the Living Spirit, he who came and bore up the Man. He too made a judgement on all the powers of darkness; which had wounded the garments of the First Man.

**¶5** The third judge is the Mother of Life, the Great Spirit, she who beautified and set up the heavenly part of the universe, according to her pleasure; which [is beneath] her. She judged the rulers above; she fettered and set them firmly in the place that is fitting for them.

> *¶5 #1 [moderate]:* The Mother of Life is the ordering/architectonic will that fashions the upper structure; the clause needs a relational verb tying the cosmos she establishes to her (the source/measure). “beneath her” fits the correspondential vertical order: the heavenly part is arranged under her dominion/pleasure, without importing later devotional phrasing.

**¶6** The fourth judge is the Great Builder, he who built the new aeon of joy. He judged, according to a judgement of righteousness, the storehouses of the enemy; that death may not well up from this time on. He has constructed a prison for the enemy. Also, upon the crown of the building he has constructed a throne for the First Man and all the fathers of light; they who engaged the struggle with the evil one and were victorious over him.

**¶7** ⟨p.80⟩
The fifth judge is the great King of Honour, he who dwells and is established in the seventh firmament. He is the judge of all the firmaments who gives a true judgement, according to a judgement of righteousness, upon all the powers and all the kingdoms of the firmaments.

**¶8** The sixth judge is [the Th]ird Ambassador, he who came and displayed his image. He purified the light [that was mingled in the mixture] which his own [image drew to itself] also of the enemy, he has [bound and set in order]

> *¶8 #2 [moderate]:* The Third Ambassador’s function in the substrate is to manifest the Image that draws out/purifies the Light bound in the cosmic mixture. The phrase must specify what Light is being purified: Light mixed into the enemy’s formation (mixture), not moral ‘sin’.
> *¶8 #3 [moderate]:* The syntax requires a relative clause: “which his own … also of the enemy”. In this cosmogonic register, the Ambassador’s Image attracts both the proper Light and the Light held in the enemy’s domains; “drew to itself” expresses the correspondential action of likeness upon like.
> *¶8 #4 [moderate]:* After purification/attraction, the remaining action is judicial/ordering: he constrains what belongs to the enemy and fixes the separation. “bound and set in order” matches the judges’ recurring acts (fettering, fixing, separating) in this chapter.

**¶9** The seventh judge is ... ... the worlds [as he gives the victory to] they who are steadfast; but he condemns the ones who are convicted ... ... he shall become for them the steps of the way (?) [of ascent]; but the convicted he sweeps and casts out to the depths.

> *¶9 #2 [tentative]:* The fragment “he shall become for them the steps of the way (?) […]” calls for a directional qualifier. For the steadfast, the judge becomes the ordered degrees/steps of their passage upward; “of ascent” fits the correspondential ‘steps’ motif (graded elevations), and keeps the impersonal cosmological register.

**¶10** The eighth judge is Jesus the Splendour, the father of all the apostles; because after the Ambassador had displayed his image and the light was taken [up] a mass of rulers rose up from above and below. Then they sent Jesus the Splendour. He came ... ... he descended and made a judgement in the firmaments. He separated the steadfast from the convicted.

> *¶10 #1 [strong]:* The light is ‘taken up’ after the Ambassador’s manifestation—standard vertical motion in the cosmological narrative (extraction/elevation of Light). This triggers the hostile reaction of rulers ‘from above and below’.

**¶11** The ninth judge is the Virgin of Light, [she who took the heart] of the powers by her image, gathering her own ones into her. She makes a judgement on the ruler of the moist, and the ruler of the dry.

**¶12** The tenth judge is the Judge who dwells in the atmosphere, judging all mankind. He makes a separation between they who are good and they who are evil, setting apart the righteous from the sinners.

**¶13** ⟨p.81⟩
The eleventh judge is the Light Mind, he who shall come and appear in the world. And he chooses the holy church, and he unveils [the mysteries of the righteous judgement] and separates light from darkness, and sets the truth apart from lawlessness.

> *¶13 #1 [moderate]:* Light Mind’s coming is revelatory and discriminative: unveiling what is hidden so separation can be made. The object unveiled should be structural/gnostic (mysteries, measures, judgements), not ecclesial exhortation; “mysteries of the righteous judgement” matches the chapter’s judicial theme and the function of Mind as discriminating truth.

**¶14** The twelfth judge is the great counsel, which is the summons and the obedience, which is appointed in the elements. It is the one that makes a separation between the good and the evil ones. At the end also it can gather itself together and sculpt its own self in the Last Statue. And it separates light from darkness.

**¶15** These are the twelve great and mighty judges who were sent. They came from the great Judge, he who transcends all the judges. He is the Father that is hidden and immeasurable, the one who exists; who is established in the aeons of greatness upon the great throne of honour, which is the King of Honour [who is established] in his kingdom.

> *¶15 #1 [moderate]:* The closing apposition identifies the “great throne of honour” as “the King of Honour … in his kingdom.” The gap should supply a linking predicate consistent with earlier formulae (“dwells and is established”). “who is established” maintains the repetitive substrate cadence and avoids later devotional expansion.

**Assessment:** Most gaps are constrained by the chapter’s fixed list-structure and repeated judicial actions (separating, fettering, fixing, elevating). The most uncertain point is ¶9’s brief damaged description of the seventh judge; only the directional logic (“steps” implying graded ascent vs. casting down) tightly constrains it, not the exact wording.

---

## Chapter 29: Concerning the Eighteen Great Thrones of all the Fathers

**¶1** Eighteen [great thrones for all the fathers] exist and are appointed, which are established in eighteen places! While there are nine among them [appointed in] the outer aeons, they exist [i]n all the worlds of the damage.

**¶2** The first throne is the throne of the Father, the God of [trut]h, the King of the aeons of greatness, the one who exists; who is established [in the aeon of the heights] he alone, in his own essen[c]e, [being without beginning and without end] apart. This is the first throne [in the solitude of his light] the one that surpasses all the thrones.

> *¶2 #2 [moderate]:* The Father’s throne in the oldest substrate is fixed ‘above/within the height’—the unmingled region prior to any ordering of the worlds. A locative phrase is demanded after ‘who is established … he alone’ to mark transcendence and separation from the mixed cosmos.
> *¶2 #4 [moderate]:* The line stresses the Father’s self-existence and aseity: ‘he alone, in his own essence’ calls for a standard substrate pair ‘without beginning/without end’ (unconditioned, not temporally generated).
> *¶2 #5 [tentative]:* Completes the correspondential triad: (1) height, (2) self-essence, (3) separation/solitude. ‘Apart’ already follows, so the missing phrase should intensify the unmingled state as ‘solitude of light’ rather than devotional language.

**¶3** ⟨p.82⟩ The second throne is that of the [gl]orious Mother of Life. She is the origin of all the emanations that have come to this world.

**¶4** The third throne is constructed for the father, the First Man. He is the one who humiliated the entire first enmity, which existed in the worlds of darkness.

**¶5** The fourth throne is that of the Beloved of the Lights, the great honoured Beloved of the aeons of greatness.

**¶6** The fifth throne is that of the glorious Great Builder; the great architect who built the new aeon for a newness, for [a dwelling-place of rest] for [the] fathers of light; also for a place of binding and a prison for the enemy and his powers.

> *¶6 #1 [moderate]:* After ‘for a newness, for …’ the substrate expects the positive end of the New Aeon: a stable resting-dwelling for the Lights, contrasted with its negative end (prison/binding for the Enemy) stated immediately after.

**¶7** [The sixth thro]n[e] is that of the Living Spirit, the [glorious] mighty one, he who bore up the First Man out from the [land of darkness]; who also set in order and constructed the world [s] both for a cleansing of the light, [and] for a binding [of the] powers of the enmity.

> *¶7 #5 [strong]:* Only the initial letter is required by the apparatus; the sense is ‘constructed the world(s)’.

**¶8** The seventh th[rone is] that of the Third Ambassador, the king of the zone, also the master of all advices.

**¶9** The eighth throne is that of Jesus the Splendour, who [is] the releaser and redeemer [of] all souls.

**¶10** The ninth throne is the throne of the great M[ind, he to whom] all the churches shall gather; and all the [life] that will be purified from the world returns to him.

**¶11** These are the nine thrones of ho[no]u[r] appointed in the outer aeo[ns], <which are established> for the fathers of the glorious realm; [in the kingdom of the King of] Honour, for ever and ever, amen.

**¶12** In contrast, these are the other nine thrones that are appointed in this world of the zone.

**¶13** Three thrones in the ship of [the d]ay: one is that of the Ambassador, the second i[s that of] the Great Spirit, the third is that of the Living Spirit.

**¶14** ⟨p.83⟩ Once again, there are another three in the ship that [belongs to the night]: the first is the throne of Jesus the Splendour, [the second is that of] the First Man, the third is tha[t of the Virgin of] Light. These six thrones are established in the two ships.

**¶15** Also, the seventh throne is established in the seventh firmament. The great King of Honour sits upon it.

**¶16** The eighth throne is established in the atmosphere. The Judge of truth sits upon it, he who judges all mankind. Three paths shall be distinguished before him: one to death, one to life, on[e] to the mingling.

**¶18** Th[ese] are the [nin]e great [th]ro[nes of honour] that are established in this world of [the zone] of the glorious realm, the one that the [righteous ones] honour them [with pure mind] and the [powers of the Light].

> *¶18 #4 [moderate]:* The clause is summarizing the nine ‘zone’ thrones; the natural completion after ‘nine great thro-’ is ‘thrones of honour’, matching the earlier idiom (outer aeons: thrones of honour).
> *¶18 #5 [moderate]:* ‘this world of …’ in this section consistently names ‘the zone’ as the mixed cosmos beneath the outer aeons; the summary must restate that location.
> *¶18 #6 [tentative]:* The closing line contrasts who honours these thrones in the mixed world: not a churchly register, but the substrate category of those aligned with Light—‘righteous ones’ (those ordered to truth) fits the impersonal cosmological voice.
> *¶18 #7 [tentative]:* Honouring in the substrate is correspondential: an act of interior alignment (mind/truth) rather than cult. ‘Pure mind’ matches the role of the Great Mind in ¶10 and keeps the register non-devotional.
> *¶18 #8 [tentative]:* The final member should close the cadence: honouring is performed by the Light’s agencies within the zone. ‘powers of the Light’ balances ‘enemy and his powers’ earlier and avoids later institutional terms.

**Assessment:** Most lacunae are minor letter/word restorations. The major gaps are ¶2 (locating and characterizing the Father’s throne) and ¶18 (the concluding summary). Those were restored by aligning with the substrate’s standard cosmological oppositions: Height/solitude/unmingled Light versus the zone/mingling/enemy powers, and by keeping the voice impersonal and structural rather than ecclesial or devotional.

---

## Chapter 30: Concerning the Three Garments

**¶1** Happen you know this, my beloved ones, that when the Living Spirit came he brought the three garments; that of wind, that of fire, and that of water. He assumed them from [the Mother of Life] he has [poured his powers] in them in those three garments [and] he established [them] for all the things of [this universe]e, both above and below. He arranged them in the [place where] he was displayed. Afterwards [he completed] his works [afterwards he gathered] the [which]e stripped them off before [the righteous]ghteous ones.

> *¶1 #1 [moderate]:* In the oldest cosmogonic sequence, the Living Spirit acts from/with the Mother of Life; the three elemental garments are assumed from the living treasury she provides, not from a later ecclesial source. This also fits the technical use of “garments” as elemental investitures.
> *¶1 #2 [moderate]:* The point is not moral action but infusion: he “pours”/instills powers into the garments so they become operative as cosmic media (wind, fire, water).
> *¶1 #3 [tentative]:* Connects the clause “in those three garments … he established them” with minimal, substrate-style linkage.
> *¶1 #6 [tentative]:* The garments are set as a structural disposition of the cosmos; “place where” leads naturally into “he was displayed” (i.e., manifested/appeared in ordered space).
> *¶1 #7 [tentative]:* After manifestation comes completion/finishing of works in the established media; this is the standard correspondential progression: investiture → ordering → completion.
> *¶1 #8 [tentative]:* Leads into the stripping-off: once the works are done, the powers invested in the garments are recollected/withdrawn.
> *¶1 #9 [minimal]:* The clause needs a relative: “the … which he stripped them off…”. The surviving “h” at the end suggests the editor saw a word ending in -h; in Coptic a relative/connector is plausible here in the transmission, but the sense required is simply a relative link.
> *¶1 #10 [strong]:* The preserved start “the ri-” constrains strongly. In this substrate register, “the righteous ones” are the purified/ordered recipients witnessing the withdrawal of the invested media.

**¶2** ⟨p.84⟩
Now, when he was about to loose [them, and he] divested himself of them from their (clasp) and he [dwelled]n them, and thus these three g[ments]nts are arranged [in order] from below the edges of the zone as they clothe i[it all]l [the region] up [up to the middle] above [up to the summit] up [the one who is above] above them strips them of the zone from that place at its topmost point; and it is placed below them and ra[raised up]p to the heights above, from which it had been sent o[o]t at their beginning.

> *¶2 #1 [moderate]:* The syntax requires: “loose … them, and he divested himself…”. The bracket already includes “n them,”; restoring “and he” yields coherent action-sequence.
> *¶2 #2 [tentative]:* He is said to be “in them” (the garments) as an operative presence; “dwelled in them” preserves the impersonal cosmological sense of indwelling force.
> *¶2 #4 [tentative]:* A technical phrase for their fixed disposition/arrangement as cosmic strata around the zone.
> *¶2 #6 [tentative]:* The garments “clothe” the zone and its surrounding region; a spatial term is demanded by the subsequent “from below… up… above…”.
> *¶2 #7 [tentative]:* The passage describes graded vertical extension (below edges → upward tiers). A median station is typical in these stratified descriptions.
> *¶2 #8 [tentative]:* Balances the prior step and prepares for the stripping “at its topmost point.”
> *¶2 #9 [tentative]:* Subject needed for “above them strips them of the zone”: i.e., the transcendent agent/force above the garments performs the stripping at the summit.
> *¶2 #11 [strong]:* Completes “o[u]t”.

**Assessment:** Most gaps can be restored only to the level of required cosmological function (investiture → ordering → completion → withdrawal), because the surviving wording is thin. The strongest constraints are the fixed phrases ‘this universe’, ‘garments’, ‘the righteous’, and the vertical/zone mechanics in ¶2; specific named sources for the garments and the exact verbs (‘poured/placed/dwelt’) remain moderately to tentatively constrained.

---

## Chapter 31: Concerning the Summons, in which Limb of the Soul it descended to the First Man.

**¶2** Indeed, the First Man dispersed himself and he [extended each] limb of the living soul as I have written for you [as a] of the living soul; he has [the portions] he did not know [the way back] but the blessed, glorious Man came [in] secret; in his image; in his shape; in his love; in his holy virgin, she who is the virgin of light, the soul of the father.

> *¶2 #1 [moderate]:* The sequence requires an action of dispersion into members: the First Man spreads himself into the powers/limbs of Soul.
> *¶2 #2 [tentative]:* Introduces the specification of ‘each limb’ as a portion/measure of Living Soul, matching the following clause ‘of the living soul’.
> *¶2 #3 [tentative]:* Balances the syntax: ‘…as I have written for you … of the living soul’; the oldest layer speaks in impersonal ‘portions/measures’ rather than moral terms.
