> *¶2 #4 [moderate]:* He ‘did not know …’ must point to the lost orientation/return-route of the dispersed members; a standard correspondential move (loss of way = loss of inner order).

**¶3** After the fashion of this fleshly body: as the root of all the limbs hang upon the head, so that should one of the person's limbs be cut off, while the head exists he has hope for [the other limbs again] but if his head should be cut off, the entire body will die and he is lost.

> *¶3 #1 [moderate]:* Head-body correspondence: while the head remains, restoration of severed members is still possible; ‘again’ anticipates re-gathering in ¶7–8.

**¶4** This is also the case for the Firs[t] Ma[n]. He is the head, while his sons attach to it the limbs [of] his soul. Or, conversely, like the living air, on which all flesh entirely lives as it breathes and [has its life therein] therein. His head is placed on the body of the Pillar of Glory in the heights of the universe.

> *¶4 #4 [moderate]:* Breath/air correspondence: all flesh lives by breathing and ‘has its life therein’ (in the living air).

**¶5** [This i]s also the case [for] the father, the blessed Man; as he is like the head that is upon the body and the air that upon the Pillar is set i[in the height] being made strong by his light virgin, [she] who is his soul that he clothes [as his garment of light]

> *¶5 #3 [moderate]:* Requires a locative complement to ‘set’: air upon the Pillar is set ‘in the height(s)’, matching ¶4 ‘in the heights of the universe’.
> *¶5 #5 [moderate]:* Virgin/Soul as the luminous vesture strengthening and clothing the Father-Man; ‘garment’ is a stable correspondential term for the outer form of an inner soul.

**¶6** [He sent before him] t[h]ose [powers]: the mind, thought, insight, counsel and c[on]sideration that he produced and sent forth from hi[m to] do his will. He sprang and travelled behind them. [For the sake] of his living soul, which is entwined among the rebels; as they are like the limbs of its body, and [of this] universe.

> *¶6 #1 [moderate]:* Introduces the list of five mental powers as emissaries; oldest layer is structural (‘sent before’) not exhortative.
> *¶6 #3 [strong]:* The following items (mind, thought, etc.) are a pentad of powers/limbs proceeding from him.
> *¶6 #6 [tentative]:* Bridges: he follows them ‘for the sake of’ recovering the living soul entangled among rebels.
> *¶6 #7 [tentative]:* Completes the comparison: the rebels hold the soul-members as limbs of a body, and these limbs are ‘of this universe’ (i.e., spread through the cosmic frame).

**¶7** And when they were sent, at [that ti]m[e] he was found with the Virgin of [Lig]ht and [he] stood up, asking and entreating for a p[o]wer. [And the Father of Greatness] gave him peace and a ki[ss of light] he [gave] him goo[d] tidings [to the First] Man. The Man him[s]elf gave [powers to draw back] his limbs and gathered his soul i[in its order] he built it in its place like this tower [and established it]

> *¶7 #6 [moderate]:* The responder who grants power/peace is the transcendent source; using the substrate title rather than later ecclesial phrasing.
> *¶7 #7 [moderate]:* Kiss = conjunction/communication; ‘kiss of light’ keeps the register cosmological and matches the surrounding Light/Virgin imagery.
> *¶7 #10 [moderate]:* ‘good tidings … Man’ needs a qualifier identifying which Man: the First Man as head of the dispersed soul.
> *¶7 #12 [moderate]:* He ‘gave …’ must be the operative forces for recollection/recollection of members; correspondentially, powers draw dispersed effects back to their principle.
> *¶7 #13 [moderate]:* Gathering the soul ‘in …’ most naturally ‘in its order’ (restoration of right arrangement), matching correspondential inside→outside re-ordering.
> *¶7 #14 [moderate]:* After gathering, he ‘built it in its place’; ‘established it’ fits the tower/Pillar imagery of cosmic construction.

**¶8** [He] shaped it and beautified it skillfully [then] for[m in] the voices that he sent [so that] he might sink in and quench [the living] so[u]l that was crushed by the enemy. They were gathered in. They came, set firm once more, in the image of their father.

> *¶8 #1 [strong]:* Subject needed for the crafting verbs ‘shaped… beautified…’.
> *¶8 #2 [moderate]:* Temporal connector from ¶7 construction to ¶8 shaping/beautifying.
> *¶8 #3 [tentative]:* Completes ‘for[m ...]’: ‘form in’ introduces the medium/likeness in which the voices operate.
> *¶8 #4 [strong]:* Introduces purpose clause for sending voices.
> *¶8 #5 [strong]:* Precedes ‘soul that was crushed’: standard phrase ‘living soul’ in this chapter; keeps the technical term consistent.

**Assessment:** Most restorations are constrained by the chapter’s governing correspondence: head→members (principle→derivations), air/breath→universal life, and re-gathering→re-ordering into the cosmic Pillar/tower. Exact micro-phrasing in ¶2 and parts of ¶6–8 remains only moderately constrained because the Coptic surface has been stripped of framing and we lack parallel lines; those fills keep to the impersonal Persian-cosmological register and preserve the internal logic (dispersion, loss of way, emissary powers, conjunction/‘kiss of light’, and reconstitution of the living soul).

---

## Chapter 32: Concerning the Seven Works of the Living Spirit

**¶1** The Living Spirit has done seven [wo]rks with his strength.

**¶2** [The fi]rst: He brought the First Man up from the contest, the way a pearl is [brought] up from the sea.

**¶3** [The second: He spread about the one who had rebelled, he crucified h]im in every body.

> *¶3 #1 [strong]:* The sequence enumerates the seven cosmological works; here the ‘rebel’ is the powers of Darkness bound out into material extension. The verb-pair ‘spread about / crucify’ is a standard correspondential description of fixation through extension into bodies; only the initial of ‘him’ is missing.

**¶4** The third: He [tr]ampled, piled up, and pierced the essences of death.

**¶5** [The f]ourth: He constructed the ships of light.

**¶6** The fi[f]th: He evoked his five sons, and distributed them about. They [took] possession of the zone, they bore up under all the universe's weight. Also, he evoked three living words. He set [them] upon the th[re]e vessels, another one upon the giant. [Again, he evoked the] summons, that it might be mixed with the five shining men.

> *¶6 #5 [strong]:* Keeps the impersonal, technical listing of operations: after the five sons and the three living words, a further call (the summons) is introduced as another functional emission to be mixed with the five luminous bearers.

**¶7** ⟨p.86⟩ The sixth: When he had constructed the zone he aro[se] and s[e]nt forth a multitude of powers and many [angel]s; so that they would bound the circuit of the z[o]ne on every si[de], un[t]il he finished [co]nstructing the works. And at the end [of] all the [wor]ks he to[ok s]ome of them into his storehouses, and he appointed others at the watch-posts.

**¶8** The [sev]enth: A[t the] time when the Ambass[ador dis]pl[aye]d his glorious imag[e], the L[iving] Spirit [established] many [go]ds and angels. They [stood in their lots and their stations] Th[ey to]ok hold of it, that the entire building [might be held fast and remain firm] not [should not mingle / should not war] one another against [the] light, which he had [set as a boundary and measure]

> *¶8 #9 [moderate]:* After ‘established many gods and angels’ the correspondential next step is assignment of fixed functions/places (lots, stations) in the ordered building, matching ¶7’s ‘watch-posts’ and storehouses.
> *¶8 #11 [moderate]:* The clause ‘They took hold of it, that the entire building …’ demands a purpose: stabilization/fastening of the cosmic construction (zone + vessels), i.e., fixing the boundary so mixture does not overflow.
> *¶8 #12 [tentative]:* Oppositional pairing expected: (1) mixture/confusion, (2) mutual assault. The following phrase ‘one another against the light’ calls for a negative preventing hostile outbreak; ‘mingle’ also fits the substrate concern with controlled mixture.
> *¶8 #14 [moderate]:* Completes the sense: the Light is what provides limit/measure; the powers are set so they do not turn upon one another against the Light which functions as boundary/order in ultimates.

**¶9** Also another gr[ea]t and glorious work he will enact at the e[nd] is [the] Last [Statue], which he will bring up to the aeons of [light]; and he [gathers the scattered light-substance] in, and [he ta]kes hold, and he smites [the remnants of darkness], and [he] builds, and he [sets it in order], and he [purges t]hem.

> *¶9 #6 [moderate]:* The ‘Last Statue’ is the final configurated receptacle that collects what has been separated/purified; correspondentially it is a terminal form that can receive and retain the liberated Light.
> *¶9 #8 [moderate]:* In the end-operation sequence (‘takes hold … smites … builds …’) the object opposed to the gathered light is the remaining Dark mass; it is seized and struck down/contained so the Light can be elevated.
> *¶9 #10 [moderate]:* Follows the chain of technical verbs: enter → seize → smite → build → arrange. ‘Set in order’ matches the substrate register (cosmic ordering rather than moral exhortation).
> *¶9 #11 [tentative]:* The last verb should name the final separating action applied to what remains (‘them’ = the mixed residues): purging/refining is the correspondential closure of the process begun with bounding and bearing the universe’s weight.

**Assessment:** Most lacunae are trivial letter/word restorations fixed by the Coptic surface. The substantive gaps occur in ¶8–¶9; these can be partially constrained by the internal correspondential sequence (establishment → stationing → holding fast/bounding; end-time: gathering light → seizing/smiting residue → ordering/purging), but exact wording remains uncertain without additional manuscript traces.

---

## Chapter 33: Concerning the Five Things that he constructed with the hard Bodies of the Rulers

**¶1** The Living Spirit constructed five great things from veins and skull[s in the] hard [bodies].

> *¶1 #2 [strong]:* Five cosmic constructs are fashioned from the hard remains (veins/skulls) of the defeated Powers; the substrate idiom regularly calls these their ‘bodies’ (material ultimates), not moralized ‘corpses’ or devotional phrasing.

**¶2** The first is the disc that is placed upon [the sh]oulder of the Porter.

**¶3** The se[con]d are the arches of [the] pillar that is before him.

**¶4** [The th]ird is the [mou]ld of the fo[r]m for the gehennas.

**¶5** The fourth are the [seven] pillars that he has erected in the great sea, in [the] seven parts of the universe.

> *¶5 #1 [strong]:* The series is architectonic: disc (support), arches (ordering), mould (form for imprisoning spaces), then seven pillars set into the great sea across the seven cosmic regions. The parenthetical uncertainty is scribal; correspondentially the sevenfold division is required here.

**¶6** The fifth are the four walls that surround the vessels.

**Assessment:** All lacunae are either straightforward mid-word restorations fixed by surrounding letters or are tightly constrained by the cosmological sevenfold structure (¶5 #1). No place required speculative rephrasing beyond removing the editorial question mark on ‘seven’.

---

## Chapter 34: Concerning the Ten Things that the Ambassador began by his Advent

**¶2** ⟨p.87⟩
The first: The Great Builder came forth to build the new aeon.

**¶3** The second: The ships moved through the heights in the heavens.

**¶4** The third: The wheels begin to send the life to earth and the upper worlds.

**¶5** The fourth: [The pure air was separated from the dark air, and each was set in its own region.]

> *¶5 #1 [moderate]:* The sequence lists ten discrete cosmological operations. After Builder/ships/wheels (construction, conveyance, distribution of life), the next demanded act is a separation at the atmospheric/median level (air as the intermediate that carries influx). This matches the standard Iranian-Manichaean cosmogonic logic: a sifting of the mixed substance into purer and darker strata before the subsequent opening of the ship-doors to discharge/receive the remaining refuse (¶6–7).

**¶6** The fifth is the doors of the ships, that they were opened up [ ...

**¶7** The sixth is the doors too [ ... ... that they were opened to receive the waste.

**¶8** The seventh is [the Sin], which he had separated from the rulers by cause of the Third Ambassador; it fell upon the earth.

> *¶8 #1 [strong]:* Fits the oldest-layer usage: Sin here is not moral exhortation but the concretized residue/condensate separated from the rulers through the Third Ambassador’s action, then precipitating to earth.

**¶9** The eighth: Night and day were separated [from one another, and each received its measure]

> *¶9 #1 [moderate]:* After the fall of the Sin upon earth (a precipitation of gross darkness), the next ordered operation is temporal differentiation: the separation of alternating states (night/day). The added clause ‘each received its measure’ supplies the correspondential necessity of fixed alternation/limits (measure, boundary) as part of cosmic ordering, without importing later devotional language.

**¶10** The ninth: The abortions [fell] to the ground.

**¶11** The tenth: Transmigrations came about in the zone.

**Assessment:** Two fills (¶8, ¶10) are secure because the immediate syntax and technical cosmological usage constrain them. ¶5 and ¶9 require reconstruction at the level of cosmological logic rather than surviving wording; the proposed restorations follow the ordered sequence of (1) construction, (2) conveyance, (3) distribution, (4) separation/stratification, (5–6) opening of channels for discharge/reception, (7) precipitation of residue, (8) temporal ordering, (9) descent of malformed products, (10) cyclic transmigration in the zone. Exact original phrasing for ¶5 and ¶9 remains only moderately constrained.

---

## Chapter 35: Concerning the Four Works of the Ambassador

**¶2** The first is his image that he displayed in a glorious image.

**¶3** The second is all the doors of the firmaments that were opened at that time. They open [before him] and shut behind him.

> *¶3 #1 [strong]:* The doors of the firmaments open in the advance of the descending/ascending Power and close after it, marking ordered passage through discrete heavens. The pair “open … and shut behind him” constrains the missing adverbial complement to the symmetric “before him.”

**¶4** The third is the light and the releasing that was purified by his image. That light is more than the light that he shall [see]

> *¶4 #1 [moderate]:* The clause compares two lights: the purified/released light exceeds “the light that he shall …”. In the oldest substrate this is a contrast between essential light and the derivative light apprehended in manifestation; “see” fits the correspondential idea of lower, perceived light versus higher, intrinsic light.

**¶5** The fourth: He has [made apparent], by the perfection of people, his light ship. He has [raised up in] the living soul that shone forth in perfection and fullness over the worlds of enmity.

> *¶5 #1 [strong]:* Accepted as fitting the substrate register: the light-ship is not ‘built’ but ‘manifested’/made visible through the perfection (completion) of the human vessels.
> *¶5 #2 [tentative]:* The sentence requires a verb of elevation/setting-forth: he has (i.e., caused to be borne) the living soul, which then “shone forth … over the worlds of enmity.” “Raised up in” keeps the impersonal cosmological mechanics: the soul is elevated into the light-vessel, then its radiance extends over the hostile worlds.

**Assessment:** Most gaps are tightly constrained by the parallelism of cosmological ‘doors’ (open before / shut behind) and by standard substrate mechanics (purified light contrasted with perceived light; light-ship as manifestation). The final lacuna allows several near-synonyms (set/placed/raised/brought) for the causation of the living soul’s elevation; the chosen fill matches the required ascent-and-radiance sequence but remains tentative.

---

## Chapter 36: Concerning the Wheel that exists in front of the King of Honour

**¶1** ⟨p.88⟩
[Agai]n the [teaching] speaks about the wheel that exists in front [of the King of Honour who dwells in] the seventh firmament [in the height], the root of the rulers above and below in a great and cruel fetter; as the entire will of the great King of Honour is placed in [it]. Also, their entire enmity, which is in the firmaments, is [gathered] and ensnared in it.

> *¶1 #2 [moderate]:* The oldest-layer register avoids personal citation; the clause needs an impersonal subject: “the teaching speaks about the wheel…”.
> *¶1 #3 [strong]:* Fits the cosmological locating of the Wheel before the enthroned King in the 7th firmament; no better constraint from context.
> *¶1 #4 [moderate]:* The phrase after “seventh firmament” requires a locative completion in the same cosmological style; “in the height” maintains impersonal substrate diction (upper region) rather than later devotional expansions.
> *¶1 #6 [moderate]:* Parallelism: enmity in the firmaments is ‘gathered and ensnared’ in the Wheel, matching the fetter/containment logic and the later ‘root… ensnared and fixed’.

**¶2** It exists in a blaze of splendour [with] ornamentation and great beauty. Now, there are twelve seals in it. The seal between i[s] every firmament and the rulers who are there [of the rulers] are in it; for should one of them wish to escape, he will be bound [in it]. If he should wish to cause a trick in his bond [it will be] apparent and known in [that] wheel [immediately]. The spheres and stars and [the] leader of all the powers exist there, and can be known by it, in front of the King of Honour.

> *¶2 #3 [moderate]:* The seal functions as a boundary-lock between each firmament and its archontic occupants; “rulers” is demanded by the repeated concern with their attempted escape.
> *¶2 #4 [moderate]:* The sentence requires the location of binding: the would-be escapee is bound “in it” (i.e., in the Wheel/fetter).
> *¶2 #5 [strong]:* The apodosis needs a simple disclosure verb: any trick ‘will be apparent and known’.
> *¶2 #7 [moderate]:* Completes the disclosure mechanism: what is attempted becomes known ‘immediately’ in the Wheel, consistent with the mirror/manifestation theme in ¶6.

**¶3** The twelf[t]h seal that is in it is its five shining men that are conjoined and enmeshed through the entire zone; because every place where they are cramped, and every place where they have space, is displayed by that wheel. For the root of all the firmaments above and below, and that of their rulers, is ensnared and fixed in it.

**¶4** The observation [of the wheel] is set right at all times opposite the great King o[f Honour so that] so that fear, wh[ich is placed there,] will assume all the powers of mixture [and seize] their root affixed there, so that they cannot make an esc[a]pe from the fetter of their bond.

> *¶4 #1 [moderate]:* The clause needs the object of ‘observation’: the ‘observation of the wheel’ (i.e., its constant display/disclosure) is set opposite the King, matching the mirror logic and the later ‘recognised and revealed by that wheel’.
> *¶4 #2 [moderate]:* The syntax demands continuation into purpose: it is set opposite the King “so that fear…”; keeps to structural causality rather than exhortation.
> *¶4 #3 [moderate]:* Fear is defined as something stationed/opposed before the King; “placed there” coheres with the repeated ‘placed in it / set opposite’.
> *¶4 #4 [moderate]:* The clause needs a verb for what fear does to the powers of mixture: it ‘assumes’/takes hold; “and seize” tightens the fetter imagery (capture).

**¶5** That wheel rese[m]bles, in this respect, a chain in front of the chief guard, for when [a fugitive is seen] in th[ose who are bound, he is recognised] in the [ cha]in.

> *¶5 #2 [moderate]:* Analogy requires a concrete instance: like a chain before a chief guard—when someone tries to flee, he is detected ‘in those (links)’.
> *¶5 #3 [tentative]:* Carries the chain-analogy forward: among ‘those who are bound’ the escapee is identified; keeps impersonal, administrative-cosmological tone.
> *¶5 #4 [moderate]:* Mid-word fragment indicates ‘chain’; the surrounding text already resumes with ‘in the … in the …in’. Best restoration is to complete the word-form already implied.

**¶6** This is also the case for all the rulers who are in the firmaments, and every power of the zone: If they should wish to escape, they shall be recognised and revealed by that wheel. [As] the wheel is like a great mirror, for [the di]scrimination of all things [also] in it.

> *¶6 #3 [moderate]:* Completes: “the discrimination of all things … is in it” → “is also in it,” reinforcing the Wheel as universal differentiator/manifestation (mirror).

**Assessment:** Most gaps can be constrained by the chapter’s correspondential mechanics: the Wheel functions as fetter + mirror (binding and disclosure), so restorations cluster around ‘gathering/ensnaring’, ‘recognition/revelation’, and constant ‘placement/opposition’ before the King in the seventh firmament. A few places (¶4–¶5) remain only moderately/ tentatively recoverable because the surviving syntax is thin, but the underlying logic (fear/guard-chain analogy) keeps the restorations within the oldest cosmological register.

---

## Chapter 37: Concerning the Three Zones.

**¶2** When he built and constructed the worlds, he suspended the zones on their zones, and within another zone also. The first zone is the five powers of darkness, males and females.

**¶3** This is why he hung one zone on a zone! He hung the five powers of Darkness on the five shining men, the sons of the Man; because all the enmity is entwined and bound in them.

**¶4** Also, within the zone are the five sons of the [Living] Spirit; because the entire edifice is suspended from them, it being [also as was]ard (?) him too, the oth[others were hung] after them.

> *¶4 #2 [moderate]:* The clause needs an impersonal connective: the whole edifice is “suspended from them,” and this is “as was heard/said,” introducing a transmitted cosmological datum, not exhortation. The preserved tail “he]ard (?)” fits “was heard.”
> *¶4 #3 [moderate]:* After mentioning “the five sons …” the text adds further items “after them” in the same suspension-series. The surviving “oth[ers” and the verb implied by ¶2–¶3 (“hung/suspended”) constrain the restoration to additional beings/zones being hung subsequently.

**¶5** And this is the one who came to [behold, to]rmaments above [the place] which is in one zone, is the great [King of Honour] who e[xists and is set] within the [treasury and boundary] of the universe.

> *¶5 #1 [tentative]:* The syntax “came to … the firmaments above …” calls for a simple deictic/transition plus preposition, not a salvation-homily. “behold, to” keeps the impersonal instructional tone and prepares the cosmographic ascent to the firmaments.
> *¶5 #2 [tentative]:* After “the firmaments above … which is in one zone,” the missing noun should name a cosmographic location contained within a zone. “place” matches the substrate register (topos) without importing later institutional terms.
> *¶5 #4 [tentative]:* The broken “e[xists … ] within the …” requires a second predicate describing stable emplacement within a cosmic structure. “exists and is set” preserves the impersonal cosmological diction and parallels the teaching’s concern with suspension/placement.
> *¶5 #5 [tentative]:* The final phrase must be a containing structure “of the universe” in which the great King is located. In the oldest cosmography this role is a limiting/containing region (boundary) that also holds (treasury). This keeps correspondential function: a terminal, containing degree for ordering the whole.

**Assessment:** Most gaps can be constrained by the chapter’s governing correspondence: zones suspended upon zones, with specific powers/sons as structural supports. Exact lexical choices for ¶5 remain uncertain because the preserved Coptic cues are minimal, but the restorations keep the impersonal cosmographic register and the suspension/placement logic.

---

## Chapter 38: Concerning the Light Mind and the Apostles and the Saints

**¶1** You have told us that the Light Mi[nd] is this one who shall come and assume the saints. [You sai]d it to us like this: He too is one among the gods [and] many gods are with him. You have also told us: [W]hen he enters withi[n the body of] the flesh and binds the ol[d ma]n with his five counsels, he [set]s his five [couns]els upon him in the five limbs [of his] body.

> *¶1 #3 [moderate]:* Syntax requires a connective: “one among the gods … many gods are with him.” The oldest-layer register prefers simple coordination rather than explanatory gloss.

**¶2** [So], no[w], where is he? In that the old man is chained in the body! For I see how rebellions arise there despite his bondage, from time to time.

**¶3** Also, secondly, I a[s]k you: If then he is a great God, unchanging and im/measurable, how could he come and appear in the smallness of the bo[dy]?

**¶4** The third thing I want you to recount to [me concerning this: how] a holy one is the Mind, and he is pure; [how then does he endure] the defilement of this body?

> *¶4 #1 [moderate]:* The question-series asks for a causal account (pure Mind vs defiled body). A minimal connective and interrogative restoration fits the impersonal explanatory tone of the substrate.
> *¶4 #2 [moderate]:* The missing clause must frame incompatibility of purity with bodily defilement, not moral exhortation. “Endure” keeps it cosmological/structural.

**¶5** Fourthly: If truly the Light Mind exists in [the s]aints, why is his likeness not displayed to us, the way that he is?

**¶6** The fifth thing I want, for you to tell me and explain about your apostola[te]. Look, it is not clear to me! For they oppress and persecute you in the world.

**¶9** All that you have unveiled you have [spoken] in our presence. Yet, I wanted to kno[w that I might see the] L[igh]t Mind, the way that he is.

> *¶9 #1 [moderate]:* “unveiled … spoken in our presence” is the most economical substrate-like wording; avoids later homiletic expansions.
> *¶9 #2 [moderate]:* The speaker wants direct cognition/vision of the Light Mind; “see” matches later ‘give vision to they who see’.

**¶10** If I shall repeat [again] to you about these things that you are so set upon, and they become true f[o]r you after you are so set upon them, will you understand what you [seek]? On your account I will give vision to they who see! I will make the living fountain overflow for the thirsty, that they may drink and liv[e].

> *¶10 #1 [moderate]:* Fits the rhetorical pattern of reiterated instruction and the conditional: “If I shall repeat again to you…”.
> *¶10 #3 [moderate]:* “will you understand what you seek?” matches the epistemic theme (vision/understanding) and avoids devotional diction.

**¶11** All the error, when the en[e]my of the lights constructed it, he constructed after the likeness of a man. The head of the universe is the beginning of the garments. [H]is neck is the nape of the garm[ents]. His stomach is the five unfolded ones (?), which a[re the folds] of the garm[e]nts. His ribs are all the firmament[s]. His navel is the sphere of the stars and the signs of the zodiac. And, also, [the par]ts [that] come from his navel to his hip are [the bands] that come from the sphere t[o the corne]rs of the four worlds. His loins are the th[ree eart]hs that are below [that are set] upon the head of the Porter. His [His thighs are the zone] from the [stretch from the knee] to the earth upon which the Porter [is stood] firm. His shins and his feet a[re the mountains] and the entire zone that belongs [to the abyss below]

> *¶11 #4 [moderate]:* The sentence defines stomach as “five unfolded ones” and then must identify them as parts/folds of the ‘garments’ (cosmic layers). “folds” preserves the garment-correspondence.
> *¶11 #9 [moderate]:* From navel to hip corresponds to extending structures from the central sphere to the four corners; “bands” fits ‘garment’ anatomy and cosmic fastenings.
> *¶11 #12 [moderate]:* Loins = lower tripartite earth-region ‘below’, supported ‘upon the head of the Porter’; “set” keeps the architectural register.
> *¶11 #13 [moderate]:* After loins and before shins/feet, thighs naturally map to the zone/region immediately above the lowest strata.
> *¶11 #14 [tentative]:* Required to continue the anatomical mapping down the body before “to the earth upon which the Porter is stood firm.”
> *¶11 #16 [moderate]:* Shins/feet typically correspond to the solid supports of the world; “mountains” fits cosmological correspondences and the ‘foundation’ theme.
> *¶11 #17 [moderate]:* Completes the descent: feet + lowest zone belong to what is beneath/underlies. Fits later ‘abysses below’.

**¶12** ⟨p.91⟩
His heart is mankind. His liver is the four-footed animals. His lung is the race of birds that [fly in] the air. His spleen is the race of fish that swim in the waters. His kidneys are the world of reptiles that creep [up]on the earth. His outer skin is the wall that [... which] surrounds the piercing and the great fire. His [encircles them] the vessels of the great fire. His [inner skin is] of darkness. His gall is the [... His] great intestine is the breadth of the great [bowels are the storehouses] of the worlds. His veins [of the smoke] all [the spri]ngs and wells. His e[eyes (?) are the sun and the moon] His feet are his [heels]

> *¶12 #4 [moderate]:* Outer skin as a ‘wall’ must be a surrounding enclosure around the great fire and its piercing. “encircles” is structurally demanded.
> *¶12 #5 [moderate]:* Pairs naturally with “outer skin”; the next clause refers to vessels of the great fire, suited to an ‘inner’ layer.
> *¶12 #7 [tentative]:* Digestive organs correspond to receptacles/containers; ‘storehouses’ suits ‘vessels’ and the great fire’s containment.
> *¶12 #8 [tentative]:* Opposed emanation of the great fire is smoke; complements ‘fire’ without moralizing.
> *¶12 #10 [moderate]:* Eyes correspond to luminaries that give sight; in this mythic anatomy, sun/moon are the natural correspondences. Fits the uncertainty marker.
> *¶12 #11 [tentative]:* After eyes, the text returns to feet; “His feet are his …” expects a sub-part. Heels fits anatomy and cosmic ‘ends’.

**¶13** This is how each of the worlds h[ave been ha]rmonised (?). Five gods are fastened in him. [And] they are his soul and his life [These five] man is a sinner [while the] the Living Spirit and the Ambassador [and also] the five sleepless guardians.

> *¶13 #2 [moderate]:* Transitions from cosmography to indwelling powers; minimal connective.
> *¶13 #3 [moderate]:* Prepares “they are his soul and his life” referring to the five gods fastened in the cosmic man.
> *¶13 #4 [moderate]:* Sets contrast: cosmic man as structure vs ordinary man as sinner.
> *¶13 #5 [moderate]:* Introduces a further list: Living Spirit, Ambassador, five sleepless guardians.

**¶14** He has [appointed first] the Keeper of Splendour, the mind that is [set above] in the wo[r]ld of the mind that is above. He has [the whole array] who]le [all] of the powers of hea[ven that] are in [the camp] of the great King of Honour, [who is the thought that exists i]n the seventh firmament. He has humbled [And] also of the Adamant of Lig[h]t, who is the insight [insight] from {} he has given [the first mixture] because of lust [He has] also appointed the King of Glory, who is the coun[sel and] patience over the three images; that of wind, that of fire, and that of water. He has handed over to him the [set as] do evilness. [power to chastise] the Porter, who is the consideration [those who] the wi]sdom in the [He appointed also] which is below. He has made him [made firm] the foundation [below the three earths]

> *¶14 #1 [moderate]:* The paragraph enumerates installations of cosmic governors; “appointed first” matches sequential commissioning.

> *¶14 #19 [moderate]:* Porter as ‘consideration/wisdom’ stabilizes the lower foundation; “made firm” matches foundation language.

Furthermore: The Ambassador refined from them five intellectuals of life. Also, the summons and the obedience were situated there. Now, they made six sons of the Living Spirit, together with the six sons of the First Man!

**¶17** Furthermore: Jesus the Youth was set there; who is the image of the living word, of the utterance and the obedience.

**¶18** He has made strong these camps, those above and those below [by their boundaries], (so that) each one of them will be secure in the circuit of his watch-district, so that neither uprising nor treachery are made in his watch-district.

> *¶18 #1 [moderate]:* The strengthening of camps aims at security in each watch-district’s circuit; “boundaries” fits correspondential containment.

So, also, is the watch-district of the Mind, which is the body of the flesh. Sin raises [up], from time to time, its agitations in the body.

> *¶43 #2 [moderate]:* After foolish words, a parallel object is needed; “thoughts of error” fits mind-clouding theme.

> *¶51 #6 [moderate]:* Disturbance arises “in the … of the Light Mind”; consistent technical term is watch-district.

> *¶52 #2 [moderate]:* Watch-keepers are ‘set fast’ yet invisible; “fastened” echoes earlier fastening vocabulary.

Again, this too is like the Mind, himse[lf] a great one, and exalted; but he is bent over and [has] become small, to this little worthless [bo]dy.

> *¶53 #5 [moderate]:* Explicit parallel to Light Mind “bent over… become small”.

**¶54** [Even so] the gods in the outer [z]one: they are transcendent and purified, they are set [f]a[st in] the mingling of the totality, (but) are not defiled. [This too is like the] Light [Mind]. While he is placed in hi[s camp,]

> *¶54 #7 [moderate]:* “placed in his …” must complete to “his camp” per repeated motif: each power stays fast at its camp.

**¶55** ⟨p.100⟩
Again, behold the mightiness and the activity of the Light Mind: how vast he is over all the watch-districts of the body! He stays fast at his camp. He shuts off all deliberations of the body from the beguilements of Sin. He limits them and distributes them out. He sets them down at his pleasure.

**¶57** I have taught you] the deeds of the [Light] Mind! Whoever has an open and percept[ive] eye, [he can] appear to him. Whoever has not that eye, he can not appear to him.

**Assessment:** Most small/mid-word lacunae are mechanically restorable (strong). Larger gaps (esp. ¶11–14 cosmographic anatomy and the watch-district upheavals) can be reconstructed only to a moderate/ tentative level because the Coptic layer compresses technical lists; restorations were constrained by the chapter’s governing correspondence (cosmic Man ↔ cosmos; five members/virtues ↔ five bound powers; watch-districts ↔ bodily organs) and by consistent internal vocabulary (camp, watch-district, set fast, bind/loosen, foundation/fastenings). A few fills remain tentative where multiple parallel technical terms are possible (e.g., specific lower-body mappings; Adamant’s firmament placement; some organ-to-cosmos identifications).

---

## Chapter 39: Concerning the Three Days and the Two Deaths

**¶4** The first day is our father, the Man. From the time when the First Man went down to the abyss of darkness and drowned in it; and the Living Spirit was sent, who went and bore him up. After the Man had ascended, the Father of Life and the Great Spirit set things in order, and the worlds above and below.

**¶5** Furthermore, the glorious Ambassador came and dwelt in the ship of the day. He displayed his glorious image, and he purified the light out of all things. He walked in the heights of the heavens. Till the time when the world will be dissolved, all things destroyed, the great fire released, and the Last Statue ascends. These three deeds were begun on this first day, when they sent the First Man with his five sons.

**¶6** ⟨p.103⟩ In contrast, [the first death] is the darkness that mixed with the five sons of the First Man.

> *¶6 #1 [strong]:* Contrasting the three great days (works of liberation) with “deaths”: the first ‘death’ here is the primordial mixing of Darkness with the five sons—i.e., the initial death-state of Light by mixture, not moral death.

**¶7** The second day is the time when the fathers of light, who were victorious in the struggle, will sit on their thrones in the new aeon and dwell on the new earth. And they reign in the new aeon till the Father unveils his image above them.

**¶8** The {length of the sojourn} in the new aeon equals the measure of the first day spent in affliction. They will reign like this and rejoice in the new aeon.

**¶9** The great third day [is the time] when the Father will unveil over them his image ... ... and he raises them up [and he shows them the glory of his greatness] and he receives them into his hidden treasury ... and he gives [from their aeons into his light] his soul. He can pour out upon them ... his ambrosia and his sweet perfume, which takes away all affliction that they have seen with their eyes. [to them from] fullness and a joy eternal! When his grace would sate them, he gives to them his [and] and they are made anew in them.

> *¶9 #1 [strong]:* Fits the triadic schema: first/second/third ‘day’ each defined as a ‘time when…’.
> *¶9 #2 [moderate]:* After ‘unveils over them his image’, the next action in the oldest register is manifestation of Glory/Greatness to the enthroned victors; avoids later devotional phrasing.
> *¶9 #3 [moderate]:* The line ‘and he raises them up … and he receives them into his hidden treasury’ demands a motion upward/out of their allotted aeons into the Father’s own Light (treasury = inmost preservation).
> *¶9 #4 [strong]:* Syntactic necessity: ‘and he gives [to them from] his soul’ matches the subsequent ‘pour out upon them … his ambrosia’. It is distribution from the Father’s own substance (Life).
> *¶9 #5 [moderate]:* The clause ‘He can pour out upon them ... his ambrosia’ needs a simple connective (‘and’) in this register; the object list then follows (‘ambrosia and sweet perfume’).
> *¶9 #6 [moderate]:* Typical exclamatory particle before ‘fullness and a joy eternal!’; preserves the impersonal doxological style without inserting later liturgical expansions.

**¶10** Also, he can summon them once more in peace and silence, and perfect their stature in same measure as his first aeons. And he sets each power in its aeon, in its dwelling-place. They will dwell in the new aeon in their aeons!

**¶11** Now, when they dwell in their aeons, they will become rich beyond measure, for ever, beyond time. From then on they will not count that season amongst them; nor the number of the days, nor the hours, generation after generation, for ever and ever. The Father will not be hidden from them, from this time on. Rather, he has accomplished (his) unveiling to them [in the great third day] he being revealed to them for ever.

> *¶11 #1 [moderate]:* The sentence contrasts former hiddenness with a completed unveiling; anchoring it ‘in the great third day’ ties ¶11 back to ¶9’s third-day unveiling as the decisive moment.

**¶12** These are the three great days, [which the Father of Greatness has given number to in the reckoning].

> *¶12 #1 [strong]:* Accepted as fitting the substrate: cosmological ‘reckoning/measure’ language (ordering of times by number) is characteristic of impersonal correspondential instruction.

**¶13** In contrast, the two deaths that I have proclaimed to you are these.

**¶14** ⟨p.104⟩ The first death is from the time when the light fell to the darkness, and was mixed in with the rulers of darkness; until the time when the light will become pure, and be separated from the darkness in that great fire. The remainder left behind there can build and add to that Last Statue.

**¶15** Now, the second death is the death in which the souls of sinful men shall die; when [they will] be stripped of the shining light that illuminates the world. And also they are separated from the living air, from which they receive [living] breath; and they are deprived of this living soul, which [was set] in the world; and they are separated and swept away from this Last Statue [to the outer darkness] and bound on [their bonds] the female will become [a fire] their torture and their affliction; for their deeds [will lead them into] those three places;

> *¶15 #3 [moderate]:* The ‘living soul’ here is the world-soul/breath-principle established in the cosmos; ‘was set in the world’ matches the impersonal cosmogonic diction and the preceding ‘from which they receive living breath’.
> *¶15 #4 [moderate]:* After loss of light/air/soul, they are ‘separated and swept away’ from the Last Statue toward the opposite region; ‘outer darkness’ is the correspondential opposite of the Statue’s gathered Light (kept minimal, not evangelical).
> *¶15 #5 [moderate]:* ‘bound on …’ requires the instrument/state of binding; ‘their bonds’ preserves the mythic-judicial register without importing later penal language.
> *¶15 #6 [tentative]:* Parallel to the first death’s purifying ‘great fire’ but in opposite sense: for the sinners the female/demonic principle becomes ‘a fire’ of torment; keeps to elemental correspondences (fire as consuming passion in the opposite sense).
> *¶15 #7 [moderate]:* Bridges to ‘those three places’: their deeds ‘will lead them into those three places’ (a triadic distribution of punitive regions). This is the minimal verb required by the syntax and cosmological causality.

**¶16** These are the two deaths. The first death is temporal; [but] the second death is eternal. It is the sec[ond deat]h!

**Assessment:** Most gaps are syntactically and structurally constrained by the three-day/ two-death schema. The largest uncertainties are in ¶15 where the punitive geography (‘those three places’) and the role of ‘the female’ are only partially preserved; restorations there are correspondentially consistent (movement away from gathered Light; binding; fire in opposite sense) but remain tentative without parallel witnesses.

---

## Chapter 40: Concerning the Three Things that were established by the Light

**¶2** Three things would be fashioned by the Light through necessity. For there can be no other way nor other result, to allow them to stay without coming about. For the darkness, the enemy, must be bound by them; he who stood up against the Light from the beginning. Due to this, these things have no (other) result, to allow them to stay without coming about.

**¶3** Now, the first thing is the new luminous earth, which was set and built on top of this earth [and they placed the fetter beneath] it, namely that alien body [which they] built upon it.

> *¶3 #1 [moderate]:* The sentence explains the new luminous earth as a superposed structure; in the substrate logic it is the upper ‘earth’ laid over the lower as a binding-layer. A clause about placing/setting a fetter or foundation beneath fits the necessity-theme (binding the Enemy) and prepares for the next apposition (“namely that alien body … built upon it”).
> *¶3 #2 [strong]:* After “that alien body” the verb “built upon it” requires a subject; the simplest old-layer connective is a relative clause resuming the agents (“they”) who fashion the binding structures.

**¶4** The second thing is this: They will [bring together the dark fire into it] and they [will shut it in within] the new earth, the coffin [and the prison] ⟨p.105⟩ of the dark fire, the companion of the enemy.

> *¶4 #1 [moderate]:* Second necessity: the ‘coffin’ is defined as containment of the dark fire. The substrate sequence is: gather/collect → shut in → bind. “Bring together … into it” supplies the mechanical, impersonal operation appropriate to the cosmological register.
> *¶4 #2 [moderate]:* Pairs with the prior gathering: once collected, it is enclosed. “Shut in” matches the coffin/prison imagery without moralizing.
> *¶4 #3 [moderate]:* Apposition: “the new earth, the coffin … of the dark fire” naturally expands with a second title. In this teaching-layer the coffin is simultaneously the prison prepared for the hostile power and its fire.

**¶5** The third thing is the souls of all the sinners who have been condemned by their deeds. They were [set to lamentation, and there is] over them woe; they have been condemned according to the wicked deeds they had committed, and due to this they will be crucified on the enemy, he who had raised himself up against the greatness.

> *¶5 #1 [moderate]:* The line needs a predicate leading into “woe”; the oldest-layer idiom is an assignment to a state (lamentation) rather than exhortation. “Set to lamentation” frames “woe” as their fixed condition under judgment by deed.

**¶6** They will seal up this final lump when all the likenesses and images of every shape will be nailed in it. Also, those will be bound by this last fetter for eternity, and be placed as a footstool and a mat and a rag for this coffin [of the new earth] which is the prison of the enemy. It will be placed, being a boundary-marker and a great void between the two, between the darkness and the enemy, so that they cannot reach one another, nor can they hear their voices; rather they will become sterile as they are separated from each other for ever and ever.

> *¶6 #1 [strong]:* The ‘rag/mat/footstool’ are said to be for ‘this coffin … which is the prison of the enemy’; that coffin was identified in ¶4 with the new earth. Restoring “of the new earth” tightens the correspondence: the sinners’ forms become the lowest serviceable layer of the enclosing earth/coffin.

**Assessment:** Most gaps can be constrained by the correspondential mechanics of the passage: three ‘necessities’ that function as binding/containment structures separating Darkness from the Enemy. Exact verbs in ¶4–¶5 remain somewhat fluid (gather/enclose/appoint-to-lament), but the required operations (collect → shut in → imprison; condemn → crucify upon the Enemy; make a lowest layer serving the coffin/new earth) are tight.

---

## Chapter 41: Concerning the Three Blows that befell the Enemy because of the Light.

**¶1** The Darkness itself, the Enemy, has received three bad blows with three wars; and worse dangers from the Light in those three wars.

**¶2** The first: (The enemy) was brought out to the middle and separated from his dark earth, whence he had departed. He was vanquished in that first war, caught and [bound] by the living soul.

> *¶2 #1 [strong]:* Fits the sequence of the first war: seizure → binding by the Living Soul; standard cosmological constraint (immobilization of the adversary’s motions after separation).

**¶3** The second blow is the time when he will be dissolved and melted, and he perishes in this great fire. He is destroyed and overthrown, from the images that are all by which he exists. And he will be gathered in [to the] fetter corresponding to his archetype, and be made like he was from the first beginning.

> *¶3 #1 [strong]:* The Enemy is “gathered into the fetter” as into a receptacle matching his own archetype (his primordial form/state). The pre-institutional register prefers this spatial/containment phrasing.

**¶4** The third blow that will befall the Enemy is the 'rolling back' [at the] end when everything will be separated, and male will be divided [from] female. Now, the male will be bound in the lump, but the female [will be thrown] in to the tomb. For it will be divided [and be shut up, divided] stone in their midst, for generation upon generation, for ever and ever.

> *¶4 #1 [strong]:* The “rolling back” is explicitly an eschatological reversal occurring “at the end,” when the mixed is undone and separated into its own kinds.
> *¶4 #2 [strong]:* Sexual division is expressed as a separation of contraries in ultimates; the wording is tightly constrained by the antithetic pair male/female.
> *¶4 #3 [moderate]:* Oppositional fate: the male fixed/bound in a lump (mass, coagulum); the female cast down into the tomb (receptacle of generation). The verb of forcible casting matches the punitive correspondential sense.
> *¶4 #4 [tentative]:* The clause requires (1) an act of closure/confinement, then (2) the means: “by a great stone in their midst,” yielding perpetual separation ‘generation upon generation’. This keeps the impersonal cosmological register (no judicial speech), and supplies the missing verbal hinge between “it will be divided” and “stone.”

**¶5** ⟨p.106⟩ This is the way that the binding of the enemy will come about, in a fetter that is burdensome and strong, one from which there is never escape; because [they have achieved] his binding and bound him for ever. And they have achieved his being set apart, and he has been set apart for ever.

> *¶5 #1 [strong]:* Refers back to the agents of Light accomplishing the final confinement: the binding is an effected, irreversible state; the repetition (“achieved… achieved”) is characteristic of this teaching style.

**Assessment:** Most gaps are formulaic and tightly constrained by the three-war scheme (capture/binding; melting/reconstitution into the archetypal fetter; final separation of contraries). Only ¶4 #4 required a substantive reconstruction to bridge the syntax; the sense is clear (perpetual confinement by a separating stone), but exact wording remains tentative.

---

## Chapter 42: Concerning the Three Vessels

**¶2** Happen you know, my beloved ones, that when these vessels were discharged, they were discharged in [great] slaughter, but those [are in] these vessels from them.

> *¶2 #1 [moderate]:* Discharge of the vessels is described as a violent evacuation from the rulers; the substrate idiom contrasts the later beneficent ordering with an initial expulsion “in great slaughter,” i.e., a convulsive purgation in the body of the Archons.
> *¶2 #2 [moderate]:* Sense requires a relative clause: ‘those (vessels) are in these rulers … the Living Spirit discharged these vessels from them.’

**¶3** ⟨p.107⟩
They are like the form of a man who has fallen into a purulent sickness. Other wounds and diseases are in his body, and even bile and other poisons are in his inner limbs. He achieves good health by a wise doctor, who gives him one remedy to drink that causes his purulent sickness to break out above, and gives him another medicine to drink that causes his sickness to discharge below, and he gives him yet another medicine to drink that cures his wounds for him [and he heals him] calmly and silently.

> *¶3 #1 [moderate]:* Completes the triad of remedies: one brings eruption above, one discharges below, and a final medicine ‘heals’ the wounds, done ‘calmly and silently’ (i.e., without tearing).

**¶4** Through the skill of a wise doctor, with his effective medicines to consume that take away all sicknesses in this manner and dissolve all magic, the doctors shall [also, when] they work cures through the aroma of the medicine, other wounds [they smell out] what is hidden, they at last tear them out from [the places of] manifested above and below [and] the aroma of edible medicines that were placed upon them.

> *¶4 #1 [tentative]:* Transition to general medical method: ‘the doctors shall also, when they work cures…’
> *¶4 #2 [moderate]:* Correspondentially the ‘aroma’ is perception/discernment; doctors ‘smell out’ hidden wounds.
> *¶4 #3 [tentative]:* Doctors tear out hidden harm ‘from the places’ where it is lodged in the body; prepares for “manifested above and below.”
> *¶4 #4 [moderate]:* The phrase ‘manifested above and below and through the aroma…’ needs a simple linker; ‘and through’ fits the Coptic connective style.

**¶5** This is also what these vessels of water, of darkness and of fire are like, which are hidden in the body of all the rulers above; while the Living Spirit is like the wise doctor. These three healing drugs are the three garments that the Living Spirit assumes upon his body: of wind, of water, and of fire. Through them he has set in order the things below. For by those three garments he has been displayed to the rulers who are above, with hard-heartedness, with shaking and fear, with derangement; from them he discharged the three vessels, those of water, of darkness and of fire.

**¶6** The case of these vessels is just like a child who is in his mother's womb and is swathed by seven garments. The first is the marrow, the second is the bones, the third is the tendons, the fourth is the flesh, the fifth the veins, the sixth the blood, the seventh the skin of the body that covers him. The other garment also [is the womb itself; it swathes him] this child. The marrow and the bones, the sinews and flesh, the veins and blood and skin, together with the garment that she wears and the linen that swathes her, total nine. Together with the nine of the child, these and the others, they shall complete and make eighteen garments. Now, you shall find the soul of this child is entwined within all these.

> *¶6 #1 [moderate]:* The logic requires an added ‘garment’ beyond the seven embryonic coverings: the uterus/womb as an encompassing envelope. Then the mother is analogized with her own garments (clothing + linen) to make nine; nine of mother + nine of child = eighteen.

**¶7** ⟨p.108⟩
And look see: When the smell of the action of the demon, which brings about the stench of birth, will reveal the child; immediately [the womb] shall [it opens] all [these things] and it emerges from a crack that has become visible, while no tear nor wound occurs in the body of the child. In the same way as this child opens up the womb of his mother, so is it also with these vessels of water, of darkness and of fire. The actions that open up his mother to the child, and she shakes him out and reveals him; these are the three living garments that the Living Spirit assumed on his body.

> *¶7 #1 [moderate]:* Agent that ‘reveals’/opens so the child emerges is the womb becoming manifest/open.
> *¶7 #2 [moderate]:* Matches the comparison in the next sentence: ‘In the same way as this child opens up the womb…’ So here: ‘immediately it opens…’

**¶8** For by the aspect of the garment of water [he discharged the water] from the rulers; he moved [their moisture;] they are the poison and death with which their veins and entrails are filled.

> *¶8 #1 [moderate]:* Garment of water corresponds to washing/evacuation of wet impurity from the rulers; thus it ‘discharged the water’ (the vessel of water) from them.
> *¶8 #2 [tentative]:* What remains described as ‘poison and death… in veins and entrails’ is the rulers’ internal wetness turned corrupt; ‘moisture’ is the natural correspondence of such interior fluids.

**¶9** By the aspect of the garment of wind, in which he was revealed [to the rulers;] he has swept away and erased the entire destructive shadow and dirt, discharged it down to the earth. Afterwards he swept it out and confined it in the vessel of darkness that surrounds the universe.

> *¶9 #1 [strong]:* Needs an object of manifestation: ‘in which he was revealed to the rulers…’ parallels ¶5 and ¶8.

**¶10** Also, by the garment of living fire, through its splendour and vigour, he discharged the fire of the darkness from all the rulers and threw it down upon this earth. He swept it another time from the earth and confined it in the vessel that surrounds all the worlds, and they call it the wall of great fire.

**¶11** Now, those three garments, through which he established these three things, he did not peel them off all at once; but he stripped them off one by one at the appropriate time [until he had swept them] down to the earth [and confined them]

> *¶11 #1 [tentative]:* The stripping of garments is staged ‘at the appropriate time’ according to the completion of each sweeping/discharge.
> *¶11 #2 [tentative]:* Completes the sequence: sweep down → sweep again → confine in the surrounding vessel/wall.

**¶12** In this way the universe of the [twelve great deeds] his vessels [and the management of] these vessels like [a healing]

> *¶12 #1 [strong]:* The next paragraph explicitly says the management of the vessels brought ‘twelve great deeds’; ¶12 introduces that framework.
> *¶12 #2 [moderate]:* Bridges ‘universe’ and ‘his vessels’ to the later phrasing in ¶13 (‘use of the matter, and the management of these vessels’).
> *¶12 #3 [tentative]:* The whole section is medical correspondence: vessels as disease-material; the cosmos benefits by ‘healing’ operations.

**¶13** The use of the matter, and the management of these vessels that were swept out and discharged from above to earth, have brought an advantage and benefit of twelve great deeds. Afterwards, they were swept from the earth and pushed outside to the edges of the worlds and to the {extremity}.

**¶14** The first deed is this: So that evil-doing does not increase in the universe, neither in the creations nor in the trees [because]tself up in three things.

> *¶14 #1 [strong]:* Clause needs causal link: ‘…in the trees, because it has set itself up in three things.’

**¶15** The [first is lust]

> *¶15 #1 [moderate]:* The triad of evils developed in ¶16-19: lust (from water), forgetfulness (from darkness), burning/anger (from fire). So deed 1’s ‘three things’ are these.

**¶16** The second is error [in the rulers.] Indeed, about this I have spoken to you as I [have taught you.] For, if it were so, if [the vessel of water] had not been swept down from above, but rather the waters existing up till now in the rulers above [and from] torrents that would flow [from it] and the droplets that would [drip] down, so lust would have sprouted [also]n the trees and in the fruits as well as all flesh [from top] down [to]oday [for you] you could not find a single person [pure] in the [whole creation;] a saint does [as] is master of [himself.]

> *¶16 #1 [tentative]:* Concludes ‘The second is error …’ by locating the hypothetical: if water had remained ‘in the rulers’.
> *¶16 #2 [tentative]:* Self-reference is kept impersonal/technical: ‘about this I have spoken… as I have taught you.’
> *¶16 #4 [tentative]:* Needs two sources for waters/torrents: ‘…in the rulers above and from the torrents that would flow…’
> *¶16 #5 [moderate]:* Refers back to the retained vessel/waters above: torrents ‘would flow from it’.
> *¶16 #6 [strong]:* Parallelism: torrents flow; droplets drip down.
> *¶16 #7 [strong]:* ‘…in the trees and in the fruits as well as all flesh also…’
> *¶16 #8 [tentative]:* Completes ‘…all flesh … down…’: descent imagery of seepage from above into generation.
> *¶16 #9 [strong]:* Gives ‘today’ a complement: ‘down to today’.
> *¶16 #10 [tentative]:* Rhetorical address: ‘today for you could not find…’
> *¶16 #11 [moderate]:* Contrast demanded: because lust would pervade all, you could not find a single ‘pure’ person.
> *¶16 #12 [tentative]:* Lust would infect trees, fruits, flesh—thus ‘in the whole creation’.
> *¶16 #13 [tentative]:* Introduces comparison: ‘…a saint does as…’
> *¶16 #14 [moderate]:* Concludes: saint ‘is master of himself’ (i.e., restrains lust). Substrate register prefers mastery/state over moral exhortation.

**¶17** Not only this, but if the vessel of darkness had not been swept out and thrown down to the earth, nor swept from the earth and cast outside the region of the worlds into this pit; again the drops of darkness, which will drip down from it, and the torrents that will continue to flow down from above to the earth and assume the trees and all flesh, would have multiplied, and the forgetfulness that is in a thousand people today you would have found in just one person. There is not one among mankind [who knows] him [by] the thought and the knowledge of the [Light.]

> *¶17 #1 [tentative]:* Sentence requires a qualifier for ‘not one among mankind’: not one ‘who knows…’
> *¶17 #2 [tentative]:* ‘…him by the thought and the knowledge…’ linkage.
> *¶17 #3 [moderate]:* Forgetfulness is loss of remembrance of the Light; thus ‘the thought and the knowledge of the Light’.

**¶18** Furthermore, if the fire were in the rulers of the worlds of the heavens until today, and had not been swept out and cast down to the earth, and swept from the earth and shut up inside the vessel of fire that surrounds the worlds; [the sparks of] this fire [would drip down] above through the torrent [and the torrent of fire] below;

> *¶18 #1 [tentative]:* Fire retained above would send down ‘sparks’/emanations through torrents—standard natural correspondence of fire’s dissemination.
> *¶18 #2 [moderate]:* Completes parallel to water/darkness: droplets/torrents of fire.
> *¶18 #3 [tentative]:* Restores the balanced pair: ‘above through the torrent … below’ = from above by a torrent and below by a torrent of fire/heat.

**¶19** ⟨p.110⟩
and the dark fire would have multiplied in the tree and in fruit and in all flesh. The power of the fire that is now in a thousand people, you would have found it occurring in a single person today.

**¶20** Behold, I have taught you about this single evil that was purified by three archetypes [of water, of darkness, and of fire]

> *¶20 #1 [moderate]:* The ‘single evil’ (the mixed corruption in the rulers) is purified by three archetypal operations/garments corresponding to the three vessels just treated.

**¶21** The second deed: Al[l] the heavens [were purified] of the sediment and the pollution [that was in them,] so that [no darkness] surpasses in them [any more.]

> *¶21 #2 [moderate]:* Second deed concerns the heavens being cleansed; ‘were purified … of the sediment and the pollution’ fits.
> *¶21 #3 [tentative]:* Specifies location of sediment/pollution: in the heavens.
> *¶21 #4 [tentative]:* Purpose clause: cleansing so that ‘no darkness surpasses in them’ (i.e., does not prevail/multiply there).
> *¶21 #5 [moderate]:* Standard closure of the purification purpose.

**¶22** [The third]: The heavens and the firmaments [were freed from]urden [and were] relieved from the weight-carriers [below,] and it ceased being heavy. This rich one [was set in its place] above.

> *¶22 #2 [moderate]:* Heavens/firmaments relieved of burden; thus ‘were freed from (the) burden’.
> *¶22 #3 [tentative]:* Connects to ‘relieved from the weight-carriers’.
> *¶22 #4 [tentative]:* The heaviness ceases; the ‘rich one’ (the luminous element/treasure) is set ‘below/above’—here likely ‘below’ as the realm that was weighted; but next says ‘above’. So this slot supplies the contrasting location: weight was from below.
> *¶22 #5 [moderate]:* ‘This rich one … above’ requires verb: ‘was set in its place above.’

**¶23** The fourth: That they might purify and cleanse their dwelling places [and their firmaments]

> *¶23 #1 [tentative]:* Parallel pair: ‘their dwelling places and their firmaments’ purified.

**¶24** The fifth: Because the greatness and the richness that dwells in these ships, as it [was gathered,] you might find them living in these ships, purified and holy.

> *¶24 #1 [tentative]:* Greatness/richness dwelling in the ships ‘as it was gathered’ (concentrated) there after sweeping.

**¶25** The sixth: That you might find the mixed, incomplete, and unconfined temples above at rest and purified; since rest will reach them and pass through them and purify them.

**¶26** The seventh: [By the] nature alone of the vessels, which will advance and extend to their places and sites [pure] in [the boundaries.]

> *¶26 #1 [tentative]:* Introduces seventh deed explanation.
> *¶26 #2 [moderate]:* ‘nature alone of the vessels’ = their ‘pure’ nature (separated from mixture) advancing to their sites.
> *¶26 #3 [tentative]:* Their ‘places and sites’ are at the edges; thus ‘in the boundaries’.

**¶27** The eighth: That great enclosures and walls would be made, and confine everything.

**¶29** The tenth deed: That they would become releasers and purifiers of everything at the end of the universe like the fire [that] burns [everything.]

> *¶29 #1 [strong]:* Simile setup: ‘…at the end of the universe like the fire that burns…’
> *¶29 #2 [moderate]:* Fire correspondence: final purgation consumes ‘everything’ impure; fits ‘burns …’.

**¶30** The eleventh deed: [The twelfth deed:] will make a crossing [for the Light] and the entire ruling-power [shall pass.]

> *¶30 #1 [tentative]:* Keeps enumeration coherent: ninth is absent in excerpt; tenth and eleventh follow; this line introduces eleventh in text, so lacuna likely supplies its header.
> *¶30 #2 [tentative]:* ‘will make a crossing’ is a passage for Light/elements to the outside; hence ‘for the Light’.
> *¶30 #3 [tentative]:* Completes: ‘…and the entire ruling-power … shall pass…’

**¶31** ⟨p.111⟩
to the outer land, for they were not envious of this [crossing] a man who is richer than them; because the aeons of light are outside [them,] the place [of rest,] of peace, the place of honour and grace, the place where the joy of the householders who dwell in it shall never pass away nor recede.

> *¶31 #1 [moderate]:* Refers back to the made ‘crossing’ to the outer land.
> *¶31 #2 [moderate]:* ‘outside them’ = outside the worlds/structures being discussed.
> *¶31 #3 [moderate]:* List of attributes: place of peace, honour, grace—often preceded by ‘rest’.

**¶32** The twelfth: That you might find all the deeds of loss hidden from the aeons of light, because of the wall of fire that surrounds the entire building; and these unclean things are displayed (only) in its [outer side.]

> *¶32 #1 [moderate]:* Wall of fire hides deeds of loss from the aeons of light; the unclean things are displayed only on its ‘outer side’ (toward the pit/outside).

**¶33** There was a need for these twelve great deeds because the vessels that were discharged and swept out from the firmaments to the earth, and were also swept from the earth to the {extr[emity}] the three words of life watch over them that [that are]e set over them in great power [at the last time.] that they might watch over them at the last time; because of this, they are firmly set thus in these fetters, in the way that I have told you.

> *¶33 #1 [strong]:* The bracket contains a broken word ‘extr[emity)’; restore to match the normalized ‘extremity’ used earlier in the same chapter.
> *¶33 #2 [strong]:* Syntax: ‘the three words of life watch over them that are set over them…’
> *¶33 #3 [moderate]:* Closes the function of the watchers/words of life in substrate register: guarding until the final time.

**Assessment:** Many lacunae here are not letter-constrained and the excerpt likely omits connective tissue (especially in the list of twelve deeds), so several restorations remain tentative. Where the correspondential structure is tight (three vessels/three garments; lust–forgetfulness–fire-power; womb/garments arithmetic; wall/fire as outer display), the fills are more constrained and confident.

## Chapter 43: Concerning the Vessels.

**¶2** As for what you have asked me about the vessels, you must understand this, that when the Father of Life was about to discharge these three vessels he poured the three upon this earth, one by one at its (appointed) hour and season. He discharged them and cast them down to the earth.

**¶3** Then he bailed and scooped them from this earth in these three garments, they of wind and of water and of fire, which swathed his body. He poured and made them flow into the three pits [... w]hich he had [es]tablished during the construc[tio]n of the worlds [and when he had poured them in,] they [it happened that] something remained behind on the face of the earth from these three vessels, so that you can find their mark and type displayed in the universe.

> *¶3 #1 [moderate]:* The phrase must name the three receptacles into which the elements are poured; the following clause “which he had established…” requires a plural antecedent. Likely “pits … which,” with the remaining visible letter w beginning “which.”
> *¶3 #4 [tentative]:* After the construction of the worlds, the narrative needs a temporal/causal hinge leading to the residue (“something remained behind…”). A neutral procedural clause fits the oldest impersonal register.
> *¶3 #5 [moderate]:* Introduces the consequential remainder on the earth’s surface; keeps the correspondential logic: containment is never total in ultimates, hence a ‘remainder/mark’ persists.

**¶4** The element that is left in water is the pungency and the salinity of this salty inner sea that surrounds the worlds.

**¶5** And the remnant that is left from the darkness is this black mountain that exists in the surroundings of the sunrise. For when the sun would shine over the world no sunlight is visible there, because the darkness that gushes from it manifests above it.

**¶6** And the remnant that remains behind from the fire, upon the earth, are all the mountains and islands from where fumes of fire gush up and are visible. For these things are the remnant of the great vessel, the vestige that was conjoined with this fire that comes up from the earth; together with the mountains and hills [and all the barren plains,] places where no fruit sprouts on the trees [and likewise] greater than that place from the limb and [there is no heat ... there is no moisture] the trees shall not bear fruit in that place.

> *¶6 #1 [moderate]:* The context lists volcanic mountains/islands and then shifts to sterile regions (“places where no fruit sprouts”). A third natural correspondent of fire’s residue is arid/burnt ground.
> *¶6 #2 [tentative]:* Bridges from the volcanic ‘fumes of fire’ to the next sign (sterility).
> *¶6 #3 [tentative]:* The lacuna sits between ‘limb’ (i.e., extremity/border) and ‘trees shall not bear fruit’; the correspondential cause of barrenness is lack of the two generatives in nature—heat and moisture—here withdrawn by the dominance of the fiery remnant.

**¶8** Happen you know this! When the Father of Life discharged these vessels from above upon the earth, he poured them out for this reason: so that also the water and the darkness and the fire that exist in these three earths would be drawn and ascend towards they that had come down from above, and the earths would be purified and cleansed. Due to this he poured these vessels down to the earth, of water and of darkness and of fire, so that also the water and darkness and fire that exist in the earths would be drawn up towards they that had descended from above.

**¶9** Now, first of all, he discharged the water, the waste, the poison of the rulers who are above. He cleansed the firmaments of the [water of the rulers;] he poured down the dark waters. [Just as the] waters fell from heaven to earth, so also the waters below in the earths were drawn and came up from below towards the waters that had come from above to the earths. The waters [were] purified of the dark poison that exists in them. Then he swept them outside and made them a great vessel and a wall of sea that surrounds the universe, and on which the ships sail.

> *¶9 #1 [moderate]:* The sentence demands what was removed from the firmaments: the upper ‘waste/poison’ is explicitly water. The genitive ‘of the rulers’ matches the preceding ‘poison of the rulers who are above.’

**¶10** After the water, he discharged the darkness. He purified the firmaments and dried from them the darkness with which they are weighed down. He discharged it down to the earth. Just as he had purified the firmaments, so also he purified the earths. The darkness that is in the earths was drawn up towards the darkness that had been discharged from above. He swept them both out and shut them in that [great vessel] of darkness [as a wall ... opposite] the water.

> *¶10 #1 [moderate]:* Darkness is ‘swept out’ and then ‘shut…in that ___ of darkness’; the same cosmological pattern as the water-vessel in ¶9 implies a containing vessel for darkness before the boundary with water.
> *¶10 #2 [tentative]:* Requires a boundary relation between the imprisoned darkness and the sea: darkness is enclosed in its vessel ‘as a wall’ set over/against the water, preserving discrete regions (sea-wall vs. darkness-wall) in the cosmos’ circumference.

**¶11** And he discharged the fire from heaven to earth, [and] that time again the fire that was shut [in] the earth below was drawn and came up towards its kind. Fire was added to fire. Afterwards, he swept it out and cast it into the outer underworld, which [he had constructed for] them outside the universe.

> *¶11 #1 [strong]:* The clause needs a simple conjunction: “he discharged the fire … and at that time again…”

**¶12** In the same way that he purified the firmaments above as a place for the light-givers to journey [and made them a road] so also he purified the earths below and swept from them the fire and the darkness and the water [and established their courses.] He has purified them for the ascent of these three powers, they of the wind and the water and the fire, which ascend before the Porter and traverse the earths.

> *¶12 #1 [moderate]:* The firmaments are purified ‘as a place for the light-givers to journey’; the missing parallel is the prepared ‘road/path’ (ordered traverse) in the cleansed region—an impersonal functional term suited to the substrate.
> *¶12 #2 [moderate]:* Completes the procedural sequence: sweeping out the three impurities results in fixed orderly ascent/paths for the three powers before the Porter; “courses” matches the cosmological-register of regulated movement.

**Assessment:** Most gaps can be constrained by the text’s repeated threefold procedure (discharge → draw up like-to-like → sweep out → contain as vessel/wall → leave a natural ‘mark’). Exact phrasing in ¶6 and the boundary description in ¶10 remains tentative because the surviving context gives function more clearly than specific wording.
