## Chapter 44: Concerning the Sea Giant

**¶2** After the Living Spirit constructed things and the worlds, he came up and set himself upon this earth. He gave a sign to the King of Glory, who turned the three wheels and caused the wheels that are found [in the heights] up above all the mountains; he spread forth the wind to them, he breathed on them, he stirred them up, he washed the earth with the waters. And the waste and the accretion of the three vessels, they of water and of Darkness and of fire, which had been discharged upon the earth, he gathered them and deposited them in the sea that is within the walls and the vessels; because of this sea waters are salty. For they have received salt and bitterness from the washing out and cleansing of the three vessels.

> *¶2 #1 [moderate]:* The three wheels are cosmic mechanisms turned by the King of Glory; the clause needs a locative for where the wheels are ‘found’ before being stirred by wind/water. ‘In the heights’ keeps the oldest cosmological register and matches the subsequent ‘up above all the mountains.’

**¶3** When the Father of Life came, rising in his ascent to the heights, he discharged the wheel of the stars; he washed it out and loosened all the sediment that was in it. He cast it down to this great inner sea like [a stone] of the mountain. And that waste, the sediment of the washing out of the wheel, he cast it down and mixed it in with the sea waters. Its own thought collected it together; and the thought gathered the waste together in this way. It made it fast and fixed it [there as a foundation]

> *¶3 #1 [moderate]:* The wheel’s sediment is cast into the inner sea ‘like’ something from the mountain; in correspondential physics, what is washed out of the star-wheel is a heavy, earthy precipitate. ‘Stone of the mountain’ (i.e., a rock falling) fits the weight/precipitate logic better than a liquid or living comparison.
> *¶3 #2 [moderate]:* The thought collects, binds, and fixes the sediment: the function is stabilization—making the precipitate a fixed base within the inner sea, preparing the later ‘constructed/moulded’ giant as residue-solidified. ‘As a foundation’ fits the mechanical-cosmic tone (not moral).

**¶4** The sea giant had stamped upon him the seal of the seconds and the hours, the seal of the days and months and years, the impression of the stars and the signs of the zodiac [in them all.] He came into existence from them and was discharged from their power and cast into the sea. The images and the seals and aspects and doctrines and counsels of [the wheel of the sphere] were sealed upon the body of that giant, because he is the residue of them all. Consequently, each star that will shine, and each sign of the zodiac that will turn: of one he shall be its inducement, and of another its confirmation.

> *¶4 #1 [strong]:* After listing seals of time (seconds→years) and of heaven (stars/zodiac), the sentence needs a closure indicating the totality of these impressions. This also prepares ‘He came into existence from them…’
> *¶4 #2 [strong]:* The immediately prior context is the star-wheel and zodiacal turning; the ‘images…doctrines and counsels’ belong to that same astral mechanism. Restores the substrate’s impersonal astral-cosmology rather than any later communal/ethical source.

**¶5** He was discharged into the sea like this, the way a cloud might discharge rainwater into the midst of the sea. They compelled him totally through the action of the power and the helmsmanship of the angels. He was swept down to the mill, the place where he was constructed in the setting up of the universe; and the sea also conceived him [and bore him] for its part [and fashioned him] it being his chief sculptor and chief craftsman. It sculpted him in a likeness, he being changed more than the other residue of the sea bodies.

> *¶5 #1 [strong]:* ‘The sea also conceived him …’ expects the paired act: conception followed by bearing/bringing forth, before the clause about the sea’s role as sculptor/craftsman.
> *¶5 #2 [strong]:* The phrase ‘for its part … it being his chief sculptor’ requires a verb of active formation assigned to the sea. ‘Fashioned’ keeps the craft register that immediately follows (‘sculptor…craftsman…sculpted’).

**¶6** This matter is comparable with a craftsman who might take the stamped impressions of many coins and cast them on the fire, and mould them [to a single] image. These thousands of impressions and thousands of shapes [are perfected] and make a single body [from them all.]

> *¶6 #1 [strong]:* Keeps the existing editor’s fill; it matches the correspondential point: many stamped forms are reduced into one ultimate body.
> *¶6 #2 [strong]:* Keeps the existing editor’s fill; it fits the process of many impressions becoming complete in one formed body.
> *¶6 #3 [moderate]:* The analogy concludes that the single body is constituted out of the many prior impressions; this bridges cleanly to ¶7 where the giant is ‘from many doctrines and counsels.’

**¶7** This is also the case with the sea giant, who too was moulded and sculpted by the power of the lust inside him from many doctrines and counsels that belong to the residue of the wheel of the sphere.

**¶8** The archetype of the giant was constructed for three things.

**¶9** The first: That the sphere would be relieved and purified of the burden present in it. In the same way that the ten firmaments were purified and relieved of the burden of the three vessels; so was it also done to the sphere, that it too might be relieved and purified of the burden and the waste inside it.

**¶10** The second: That he might add a {fetter} and a prison to the wheel and the signs of the zodiac that are bound to it, so that when they wish to go up above, you can find the sea giant dragging them back as by an iron {fetter}, and they are unable to go up; and when the powers of the wheel would take a decision on a guileful thing, and it enters their mind to come down to the earth, they are not able to descend because of this stake that is nailed to the wheel upon the {} of the firmaments. This is one. However, this other one, because of its sleepless watchers that are set over it, it is turned all the time.

**¶11** The third thing: That the wind and the water and the fire that gush from the circuit of the sea, and the rivers that flow upon the face of the earth discharging into the sea, and the rainwaters that come to ground, and the waste and rebirth that come down to it; that sea will be a hostel and gathering place for all of these.

**¶12** As for all the power and the life that comes down to the sea, and in the breath that the sea giant will breathe and draw it into him that he might disturb the sea [so that a] fullness and a [a deficiency] becomes between his movement [and its ebb and flow] purify; but the sea [is cleansed by his stirring.]

> *¶12 #1 [moderate]:* The syntax needs an infinitival-purpose/causal connector after ‘disturb the sea’ leading into ‘a fullness and a … becomes’. ‘So that a…’ supplies the expected consequence construction; the surviving final ‘a’ is preserved as the article.
> *¶12 #2 [moderate]:* Paired opposites are demanded: the giant’s inhaling/disturbing produces alternating states in the sea—fullness vs lack. This matches the tidal/pulsation mechanics implied by ‘movement’.
> *¶12 #3 [tentative]:* ‘becomes between his movement … purify’ needs the oscillatory medium linking his motion to the sea’s cleansing action. ‘Ebb and flow’ gives the natural correspondence of alternating influx/efflux that stirs and purifies sediments.
> *¶12 #4 [tentative]:* The final contrast ‘but the sea …’ expects the conclusion: although he draws power/life and disturbs, the net effect on the sea is purification/cleansing (as earlier: washing/loosening sediment).

**Assessment:** Most gaps are structurally constrained by the chapter’s correspondential mechanics: astral sediment is cast down, fixed as a base, then re-formed as the sea giant bearing the seals of time and zodiac. The hardest restorations are in ¶12, where the preserved text is too fragmentary to recover exact wording; the proposed fills keep the oscillation/purification logic (influx/efflux → fullness/deficiency → cleansing) consistent with the oldest cosmological substrate.

---

## Chapter 45: Concerning the Vessels

**¶1** Understand also this other (matter): Before these vessels were discharged and swept down from heaven, first three ditches were fashioned and constructed for them, in which they would be bound and fettered.

**¶2** Before the sea giant was discharged from the sphere, the Father of Life constructed a place for [him in]n this great sea that is within the walls, whereon the ships sail, and where the fullness shall [be bound therein]

> *¶2 #2 [moderate]:* The sequence in ¶1–4 is a list of pre-constructed containment-places (ditches, sea-place, plain, gehennas). The sea-giant likewise receives a prepared binding-place; the idiom expects a clause of confinement rather than a soteriological ‘fulfillment’.

**¶3** Also, before the [earth-giant] was displayed to the worlds [and before] the earth [and it]ell to the sea and its thought shaped it, the Father of Life constructed a place for it in [the deep places] below the four mountains, above the mountains of the heavens above [the heavens] that [and it becomes] a great plain it digs in Matter in the midst of the worlds. When the Adamant of Light was sent to it, he hastened after it to that place, smote it and threw it between mountains in the place made ready for it [and its bond] is set fast upon it till the end of the universe.

> *¶3 #1 [moderate]:* The passage parallels ¶2’s ‘sea giant’: a second discharged power is the earth-bound counterpart whose fall prompts preparation of a place ‘below the four mountains’.
> *¶3 #2 [tentative]:* The sentence is stacking temporal clauses (‘Also, before…’) with a second ‘before’ introducing the next act in the chain of correspondential preparation.
> *¶3 #3 [moderate]:* The syntax requires a subject pronoun resuming the fallen entity: ‘the earth … fell to the sea and its thought shaped it’.
> *¶3 #4 [moderate]:* A prepared ‘place for it’ is specified as beneath cosmic supports (‘below the four mountains’). ‘Deep places’ preserves the impersonal cosmographic register of the substrate (a mapped region, not a named hell).
> *¶3 #5 [tentative]:* The line contrasts levels: below the four mountains / above the mountains of the heavens / above … . The missing term is the upper region being referenced.
> *¶3 #6 [tentative]:* The clause ‘that … a great plain it digs in Matter’ needs a verbal hinge expressing resultant formation: the prepared region turns into a plain carved in Matter.
> *¶3 #7 [moderate]:* After being smitten and thrown between mountains, something ‘is set fast upon it till the end’. In the substrate’s mechanics this is the restraining bond/fastening, not a moral sentence.

**¶4** Before the waste and dirt of the darkness were swept out from creation, gehennas were constructed in which the waste might be covered till the dissolution of the universe.

**¶5** Before the rebels rose up, they whose corps rebelled in the ruling-power above and below, they [were cast] outside, and also the tyrants in whose heart it lay [to tyrannise]rannise amongst the murk and the clouds and winds and spirits and storms; for them were constructed in the great outer sea seven hostels, places whence they are thrown into the depths and the darkness [until the end]

> *¶5 #1 [moderate]:* Parallel to later ‘they are thrown into the depths’: rebels and tyrants are first ‘cast outside’ (excluded from ordered regions) before being assigned hostels in the outer sea.
> *¶5 #3 [moderate]:* The whole chapter repeats the refrain ‘till the dissolution/end of the universe’. The hostels function as holding-stations ‘until the end’.

**¶6** Also, before the abortions [fell down] to the earth, there was set apart [a place of gathering]: they are gathered in and dwell therein.

> *¶6 #1 [moderate]:* The abortions, like other swept-down residues, ‘fell down to the earth’; the gap likely contained the descent-verb plus particle.
> *¶6 #2 [moderate]:* Follows immediately: ‘they are gathered in and dwell therein’. So the prepared region is defined by its function (collection/containment) in correspondential, impersonal terms.

**¶7** Again, before the watchers rebelled and came down from heaven, a prison was fashioned and constructed for them in the depths of the earth, below the mountains.

**¶8** Before the children of the giants were born, they who had [no] knowledge of righteousness in them nor divinity, thirty-six cities were assigned and constructed for them wherein the children of [the giants would] live; they who would come to beget from each other, they who shall spend ten hundred years alive.

**Assessment:** Most gaps can be constrained by the chapter’s repeated cosmographic pattern: before each class of discharge/rebellion/birth, a corresponding containment-region is prepared (inner sea-place, deep plain between mountains, gehennas, outer-sea hostels, gathering-place, prison, cities). Exact phrasing is firm where the surrounding syntax demands a specific verb or refrain (‘to tyrannise’, ‘until the end’), but several restorations remain tentative where the Coptic underlying formula could vary without changing the correspondential function (e.g., the precise hinge-verb around the ‘great plain’).

---

## Chapter 46: Concerning the Ambassador.

**¶2** Know that before night and day were differentiated in the universe, the Third [Am]bassador was summoned. And afterwards, he was sent to this void and outer waste that he might purify the light, and separate night and day from each other.

**¶3** ⟨p.117⟩ Before the light was released and freed and ascended through this zone, the Father of Life made beautiful for it the ships of light. He constructed them so that it might be purified in them, and be sculpted and fashioned in good order.

**¶4** [Bef]ore Sin had multiplied and had made a kingdom in the flesh, before the acquitted and the condemn[ed] were set apart from each other, the Judge [w]as sent forth and revealed. His throne was created and placed [i]n the living atmosphere, so that he would be a just Judge, so that there the righteous and the sinners would be set apart and tested and separated from each other.

**¶6** Again, [befo]re mankind had been engendered, some were named ['rich' and] others 'poor' on earth [and their lots were laid] upon them. ⟨p.118⟩ Before they die, they who will die are marked out by the stars and the signs of the zodiac in the sphere. They are appointed for them; in them are their births. And their root is bound up with their zodiacal signs; and they are compelled by them and brought to an equal judgement in accordance with their deeds and their sins.

> *¶6 #3 [moderate]:* The passage is an a priori ordering sequence: before generation, social distinctions (‘rich/poor’) and destinies are already apportioned. In the oldest correspondential register, this is not moral exhortation but the assignment of “lots” (measures/portions) that later appear in time as conditions “upon them.” This also prepares the immediately following astral determinatives (stars/zodiac) that ‘appoint’ births and bind the root to the signs.

**¶7** Again, before the universe is dissolved, [and the] entire earth, the destroyers were appointed and instructed for its dissolution.

**¶8** And before the ne[w] aeon was [built and] made be[auti]ful and marked out, [the] Great Builder [w]as summoned, the first architect and the angels who are with him, who would build and make beautiful the new aeon.

**Assessment:** Most lacunae are trivial letter-restorations fixed by the surrounding words. The only substantial gap (¶6 #3) can be recovered in sense (pre-temporal apportioning of conditions/lots before embodiment), but the exact wording remains only moderately constrained.

---

## Chapter 47: Concerning the Four great Things

**¶1** The wheel of the stars, which is displayed to you, is a great mighty thing! Also, it is one among the four great things; since the whole ruling-power is assigned to four parts.

**¶2** The first part is the powers who occur in the ten firmaments that exist above the wheel, and the ruling-power that is bound in them, as it is imprisoned within them in the heavens that are above.

**¶3** The second part is the eight earths that are below; the four that are composite, and the four places of darkness. The second part extends from this earth, upon which mankind walks, towards the underside.

**¶4** The third part is the walls and the vessels that surround the worlds. They are the four mountains and the three vessels; with what is in them, and the ruling-power that is trapped within them, and the powers who are masters over them.

**¶5** The fourth part is the sphere of the stars that is appointed with great diligence, as it is ordered in a [so]any [measures]; that of the Father of Life, [and that of the Mother of the Living] the living.

> *¶5 #1 [moderate]:* The phrase needs an adverb qualifying the ordering of the sphere: “ordered in a so many …”. This fits the idiom for a counted arrangement (i.e., by number/measure) in the cosmological register.
> *¶5 #2 [moderate]:* Completes the thought begun by “ordered in a so many …”: the stellar sphere is arranged by fixed measures/portions (correspondentially: determinations of influx and rule). This also anticipates the later emphasis on apportioning/weighting powers (¶6).
> *¶5 #3 [strong]:* Retain the dyad as a structural pairing of Life-principles appointing/ordering the sphere (not a devotional invocation). Capitalization of Living matches the cosmological agent-register.

**¶6** ⟨p.119⟩ Its [ordination] very [firm] with diligence, because of the great and cruel powers, and the titanic figures, [who are] in it; they being suspended from it. So, all the first-born and the leaders of all the rulers, they who were existing in the three parts, these were garnered. They were collected together, seized, bound and assigned on this wheel. Each was apportioned by these three parts. They were distributed, entwined, and weighted against their companions of the same size.

> *¶6 #1 [moderate]:* The sentence “Its ___ very ___ with diligence” calls for a noun meaning ordering/constitution. “Ordination” matches the impersonal cosmological tone and continues ¶5’s ‘appointed/ordered’ theme.
> *¶6 #2 [moderate]:* Yields a coherent clause: “Its ordination [is] very firm with diligence…” i.e., tightly set/fastened because of the violent powers attached to it. Correspondentially: fixed bounds are required where enormous contrary forces are constrained.

**¶7** Still, for its part, the wheel is equal to them in its strength; the powers of the enmity who are bound on it. Indeed, they were suspended on the circling wheel because of their cruelty; so that, due to the wind and the motion in which they go round, they comprehend not the place wherein they are set. For if they had been bound in the heavens, the heavens would not have had the strength to support them. Again, if they had been assigned on the earths, on the fixtures that are below, they also would not have had the strength to support them. Rather, they were suspended from the wheel of the circling stars; so that the power and the light that should come up from below can be received, and given to the heavenly ones above them. Conversely, whatever will fall from above, whether cold or hot, the wheel of the sphere will also take it and cast it out upon the entire earth, upon all the wood.

**¶8** Behold, the ordinance of the wheel is in this form. For the powers who are entwined on the wheel are like this: a king mastering his kingdom by this mighty legionary force of all the leaders of the kingdom; and with all the armour of the soldiery that is collected together for him; and also with the mass of the property of the kingdom; and the beautiful lustful aspect of the women who are collected together for him; together with [his] property [of gold and silver] and those cattle [also] that have been distributed about among his retinue. Yet, if you ask about that king, he is matched with all his retinue. His retinue, for its part, is matched against his entire kingdom. Judgement shall come forth from him, vindication and condemnation, to his entire kingdom; by his wisdom, skill and strength.

> *¶8 #1 [strong]:* The list of royal comparanda moves from troops/armour/property/women to additional categories; possessive “his” is required before “property” in the analogy.
> *¶8 #2 [moderate]:* Restores a standard ‘treasure/property’ collocation in the substrate register: the condensed wealth of a kingdom (metals) corresponds to concentrated natural power/resources gathered to the rulers on the wheel.
> *¶8 #3 [moderate]:* Balances the catalogue: “and those cattle also that have been distributed…”—a final item of distributed resources matching the preceding “distributed about among his retinue.”

**¶9** Now, just as for this king, who is matched against the measure of his entire kingdom by his wisdom, skill and strength; so also is it for the powers who are bound on the sphere as compared to the ruling-power, and the powers who exist in the entire zone. Like the king towards his retinue; so too is it that this totality is gathered to the sphere, to seven leaders, to twelve signs of the zodiac. As all the cities are gathered to the king, again they gather to the leaders of the universe. All that is done in the universe above and below: the battles and the disorders and the captivity and the hunger and the lust and the property; so shall they increase and diminish through these leaders. They are the motivaters of the whole created order; the totality being gathered in to them the way that I have told you. I have opened your eyes!

**Assessment:** Most gaps are recoverable by the chapter’s internal correspondential logic: (1) repeated stress on ordering/appointment by measure (¶5–6) and (2) the tight royal-analogy catalogue (¶8). Exact lexical choices remain somewhat uncertain in ¶5–6, but the required semantic slots are strongly constrained (number/measure; firm ordering; titanic powers suspended).

---

## Chapter 48: Concerning the Conduits.

**¶1** There are three conduits that exist through the entire zone, from the abyss to its heights.

**¶2** The first conduit is the root of all the powers above, they that exist in all the heavens. They are cast off and fettered on the earths below; because the earth below is the ‘stripping off’, the garment and the body of the powers that are above the heavens.

**¶3** For when the Father of Life constructed the heavenly worlds, he divested them of their bodies, [he] threw them to the earths below.

**¶4** So, the upper worlds are of the soul and the spirit; but the worlds below are of the body and the carcass [of the body and the carcass] ⟨p.121⟩ Now as for this, he [bound and gathered] the powers of heaven; he sealed it on their bodies and their carcasses that are on the earths, so that when life comes up from the fullness of these earths, it can be entirely drawn up to the heights in the roots of what is heavenly, that are fastened in their bodies. And also, all life can be purified in that place.

> *¶4 #1 [moderate]:* Balances the discrete degrees just stated: above = soul/spirit; below = body/carcass. The conduit teaching requires naming the lower degree explicitly before describing sealing on bodies.
> *¶4 #2 [tentative]:* The next clause is ‘he … the powers of heaven; he sealed it…’. The verb must govern the fastening of roots/conduits and the ordering of the powers into that system; ‘bound and gathered’ preserves impersonal cosmological register.

**¶5** However, the waste that will be swept from the purified one who ascends to the heights will be let fall to the ground through the conduits of these conduits, and will be poured down [to the earths below] and thrown to the underside.

> *¶5 #1 [moderate]:* The waste (dross) must descend through the same channels in the opposite direction (opposite sense), completing the circulation described in ¶4–6.

**¶6** This is the first conduit, which comes from the powers of the heavens to their bodies and their carcasses that are upon the earth; also, from their bodies and [their] carcasses that are on the earths to the powers of the heavens.

**¶7** The second conduit is the one that comes from the temples and dwellings and cities that exist in the heavens down to the ground, to the five shapes of tree that grow upon the earth. The life comes up from the trees to the temples and cities. Life too, and the residue of the heavenly ones, comes down to the trees in a conduit.

**¶8** And the third conduit is all the powers and householders who dwell in all the firmaments, whose roots come down from them to the five worlds of flesh that creep upon the earth, being fixed to one another. Now, the power and the life that shall be gathered in from the worlds of flesh are dispersed among them in various shapes. The powers above shall draw them to the upper side through conduits.

**¶9** [The waste]te too, and the lust and the evil-doing and the anger that will be greater in the powers of [heaven]n, shall be poured to the ground through their various conduits. They shall be discharged upon mankind and the other remaining animals. When what is heavenly will wash the waste and the stench and the poison down on the creations of the flesh below, in their turn the creations shall be greater in lust and anger and evil-doing against each other through the action of their fathers who are on high.

> *¶9 #1 [strong]:* Context requires ‘waste’ (dross) continuing from ¶5; ‘The was…’ is a damaged beginning of ‘waste’.
> *¶9 #2 [strong]:* Completes ‘powers of heaven’.

**¶10** Still, look at the wheel of the stars! It has no [root in this earth], but its roots are bound in the totality. The wheel of stars, then, receives life through the power and the life that ascend from the earths through conduits and the firmaments above. It (also) takes life from those conduits that receive up to the firmaments and the heavenly worlds above it, it takes life out from that place.

**¶11** Look, in that the wheel has no root in this earth, from where did the stars and signs of the zodiac find this authority? They became masters over these five fleshes and five trees. Even though you have told us that the wheel has no root there! For you said to us that every thing, if it can be reduced from the earth, the heavenly powers above draw it through a conduit. Also, we see that every thing that a person will attain, whether it be wealth that comes into his possession or poverty that will accrue to him, or his sickness and health; he attains it through the signs of the zodiac and the star under which he shall be born.

**¶12** Nevertheless, what I proclaimed to you, namely that the wheel has no root therein, I have recounted to you properly. It is set aright in its position. For when the Living Spirit, the Father of Life, constructed the heavens and the firmaments above, he bound their roots into the earths below; in their carcasses that he had stripped from them. Yet, when he constructed the wheel, [he] did not bind its root into the earths, but he bound the root of the wheel by the sea giant.

**¶13** And when [the] Ambassador came, he displayed his image and purified and brought the light out from all [the] heavens. Matter, however, which is present in all the rulers, spurted out against the image of the Ambassador. It went up in order to reach it, but reached it not. It dropped back and fell to the [under]side. Now, when it had fallen from above, it separated out into three parts.

**¶14** The first part fell to the wheel; another part fell to the earth; an[other part fell to the sea]a. ⟨p.123⟩ [By means] of the part that fell to the sphere, [the root]oot was bound in the trees and flesh. Therefore, because of this, they received authority and became masters in the five worlds of the flesh and the five worlds of the tree.

> *¶14 #1 [strong]:* Threefold separation must match wheel/earth/sea already listed; completes the triad.
> *¶14 #3 [strong]:* ‘[the r]oot’ must be ‘the root’ (not ‘ruler’ etc.), because the whole section explains how authority arises from roots being bound.

**¶15** Through the nature that came down [upon]n the wheel the root was bound in the trees. Also, by means of the abortions that came to the ground, another root was bound in the fleshes.

**¶16** Therefore, because of this, the stars and signs of the zodiac received authority over the trees and the fleshes: through the root of Sin that fell down; and [the root]ot of the abortions that were aborted there, they fell to the ground.

**¶17** However, the[se] other conduits that [the] Living Spirit has bound in the e[a]rths, they that the soul of the elements comes up through to the heavens; those (conduits) are not bound to the wheel. Rather, they are plundered from them by the powers on the wheel, that hang from it.

**¶18** Just as you have instructed us and put together this lesson on [the three roots] you have told us about the prototype of the conduits. The first is the one that comes from the firmaments to the earths, and the one that comes from the wheel to the sea giant. That is only one. So, the other root is the one that comes [fr]om [the temples] to the trees. The third is bound by means of the abortions in the f[leshes]; also in the fleshes the fathers of the abortions who are in the heavens [and the rulers] are bound and twined, and the [y] that [draw from them their share of life]

> *¶18 #1 [moderate]:* The speaker sums the ‘prototype of the conduits’ and immediately enumerates three: (1) firmaments↔earths plus wheel↔sea-giant as one system, (2) temples→trees, (3) abortions→flesh. Thus the missing object of the lesson is the triadic root-pattern.
> *¶18 #5 [tentative]:* The line speaks of ‘fathers of the abortions who are in the heavens … are bound and twined’—these are the heavenly begetters/archontic powers; adding ‘and the rulers’ keeps to the cosmological (non-parenetic) register and explains who is bound/twined with the flesh-lines.
> *¶18 #7 [tentative]:* The clause after ‘and the y that …’ must describe reciprocal influx: the heavenly ‘fathers’ act through the bound roots and take what rises. This matches ¶17 (wheel-powers plunder conduits) and ¶23 (they draw life from one another).

**¶20** You have inquired about a [great root] and you have asked about this lesson in superiority, great [and about the earths] the earth from which much life ascends [through which] the Father of Life has drawn them. ⟨p.124⟩ He has fastened them from the powers [and from which] he has bound them in the earths. (These conduits) are the roots of the ent[ire] ruling-power that the life of the living soul is drawn up through, it coming up from the whole earth, from all the mountains, fields and islands.

> *¶20 #1 [moderate]:* The question ‘about a …’ and ‘lesson in superiority’ introduces the hierarchy of roots; ¶20 then describes ‘these conduits … roots of the entire ruling-power’. So the missing term is the primary ‘great root’.
> *¶20 #2 [tentative]:* Fits the following phrase ‘… great … the earth from which much life ascends …’; the inquiry is about the superior root-and-earth arrangement, not moral instruction.
> *¶20 #3 [tentative]:* Needed to link ‘great … the earth from which much life ascends … the Father of Life has drawn them’: the conduit/root is that ‘through which’ ascent occurs.
> *¶20 #4 [tentative]:* Completes the double motion: ascent from earth; drawing/fastening by the Father of Life ‘from which’ (source-basis) he drew/bound the conduits.

**¶21** After that first great root is the root of the trees, which is bound to the temples and the buildings above. For there also is much great life coming up from the tree and the entire Cross, being drawn up from them to the upper side like a great host.

**¶22** What, however, is lesser [than mankind] of their conduit [in the measure] he takes up [is that which] the roots [only from] the earths, they [that are in] the whole earth in all the worlds. Again, these of the trees are present in the whole earth, whether small or great; but the root of mankind [are spread through] is not present in the whole earth, other than only this world of the south. Therefore, even now these fleshes do not make [their root] in the entire world of the south [increase] there is a small part of them in [because] of the upper side of the world of the south in [the regions] in number than the [more]

> *¶22 #1 [tentative]:* Section contrasts the ‘great root’ (earth), then trees, then what is ‘lesser’—mankind/flesh-roots are explicitly said to be not present everywhere. Thus the comparative is ‘lesser than mankind’ (i.e., mankind is the lesser class vs. tree/earth).
> *¶22 #2 [tentative]:* The phrase ‘What, however, is lesser … of their conduit …’ requires a measure/degree term, consistent with discrete degrees and hierarchical roots.
> *¶22 #3 [tentative]:* Provides grammatical bridge: ‘What… is lesser … is that which … he takes up…’.
> *¶22 #4 [tentative]:* ‘he takes up … the roots … the earths’ implies restricted sourcing; mankind’s root is later said to be only in the south-world.
> *¶22 #5 [tentative]:* Links ‘the roots … the earths’ to their location: ‘roots that are in the earths’.
> *¶22 #6 [moderate]:* Matches the next clause ‘the whole earth in all the worlds’—the earth-roots are distributed universally.
> *¶22 #7 [strong]:* ‘but the root of mankind … is not present in the whole earth’ requires explicit subject ‘their root’ (mankind’s root).
> *¶22 #8 [moderate]:* ‘these fleshes do not make … in the entire world of the south’ calls for a verb of multiplication/spread; ‘increase’ fits the next ‘there is a small part…’.
> *¶22 #9 [tentative]:* Explains why they do not increase: because only a small portion is located in a limited region.
> *¶22 #10 [tentative]:* Needed for ‘in … of the upper side of the world of the south’; ‘regions’ suits cosmographic register.
> *¶22 #11 [minimal]:* Comparative ‘… in number than the …’ requires ‘more/less’; with ‘small part’ it implies ‘more … than the …’ is damaged, but ‘more’ is the necessary comparative marker.

**¶23** I have instructed you about the roots [and the conduits that bind] them, [I] have taught you that they are bound by their [roots in the earths] with one another. They stir one another, they draw life from one another through the action of the powers above, which stirs the nature that dwells in them all.

> *¶23 #1 [tentative]:* He is summarizing the teaching: roots + conduits are the binding means; prepares for ‘bound by their roots … with one another’.
> *¶23 #3 [moderate]:* The line ‘bound by their roots … with one another’ needs the locus: roots are in/into the earths (as throughout ¶2–6, ¶20–22).

**¶24** Now, when the Matter that is in them will be crushed and despoiled, then that Matter too shall [crush] its members and overturn them and [scatter] each other [tear apart] stir [one another] ⟨p.125⟩

> *¶24 #1 [tentative]:* Opposite influx: when Matter is crushed/despoiled it acts back on its vessels; the verb must match violent deformation that follows (‘overturn’).
> *¶24 #2 [tentative]:* Matter, once released from order, disperses and dismembers its forms—fits ‘its members’.
> *¶24 #3 [tentative]:* Pairs with ‘overturn them’ and ‘each other’; describes mutual destruction among bound forms when dross dominates.
> *¶24 #4 [moderate]:* Completes the reciprocal agitation: ‘… stir …’ naturally resolves as ‘stir one another’.

**Assessment:** Most minor bracketed restorations (articles, partial words, ‘root’) are tight and certain. The larger gaps (¶4, ¶18, ¶20, ¶22, ¶24) can be constrained by the correspondential triad of conduits/roots and the bidirectional ascent/descent of life vs. waste; however, exact phrasing is not recoverable in several places, especially ¶22 and ¶24, where only the argumentative direction and required technical terms (‘root’, ‘earths’, ‘increase/spread’, reciprocal stirring) are secure.

---

## Chapter 49: Concerning the Wheel and the Conduits

**¶2** The reason that the conduits shall not be cut, for they are not cut, is because they are spiritual. Thus the conduits are like the waters through which ships sail. You shall find the prow that is in position in the front part of the ship divides the waters, casting them back and forth. So, when it parts the waters and that ship opens up the water with its cleaving prow, immediately and without delay the waters mingle with each other again behind the ship; and the path of the ship shall not be discernible in the midst of the waters.

**¶3** Just as when a bird flies up in the air, for its path is not visible in the air, and in the way of mankind, in the sun within the conduits. They also shall not be tangled up in them, and neither shall you find their path through the air known.

**¶4** This is also the case with the wheel. It turns through the conduits and the air. The wheel shall not cut the conduits, nor are the conduits themselves entangled in the wheel.

---

## Chapter 50: Concerning these Names: God, Rich One, and Angel; who they are.

**¶3** There are these three archetypes [in the land of Light, and there is no measure to apply to] them!

> *¶3 #1 [strong]:* The paragraph asserts the unmeasurability of the three archetypes. In ¶6 the same formula recurs explicitly (“There is no measure to apply to them!”), so the missing clause in ¶3 should match that fixed cosmological refrain in the oldest, impersonal register.

**¶4** So, the ones who are called 'god', are the gods whom the Father has summoned from himself. He has established them after the likeness of his greatness.

**¶5** Conversely, these who are named 'rich [ones]', are the evocations of the first rich gods of the Father; because, when the Father had summoned (the first), they themselves summoned evocations. They called them 'rich'.

> *¶5 #1 [strong]:* The triad is being defined by names/titles: gods → rich ones → angels (¶4–6). The contrast formula requires the substantive “rich ones” (a class), not an abstract “rich”. This also matches the later resumptive “They too, the rich ones…” (¶6) and keeps the correspondential hierarchy coherent (evocation of evocation).

**¶6** They too, the rich ones, have summoned their evocations. They call them 'angel'. These are the three archetypes who occur in the land of light. There is no measure to apply to them!

**¶7** Once again, listen! Other persons who are named 'god' are the emanations who have come from the Father; the evocations of the Father whom he summoned forth. They came out to the contest and humiliated the enmity.

**¶8** Conversely, the 'rich ones' are the evocations of these first living words. They too, the rich ones, have come and performed and fulfilled the pleasure of the greatness, in the worlds that are above and below.

**¶10** Again, they are called 'god', all the gods who belong to the household of the Great Spirit.

**¶11** Conversely, the ones who are called 'angel', are all the angels who belong to the household of the Beloved of the Lights.

**¶12** The ones who are called 'rich', are all the rich ones who belong to the household of the glorious Ambassador.

**Assessment:** Both lacunae are tightly constrained by internal parallelism. ¶3 repeats the fixed refrain about the immeasurable archetypes; ¶5 demands the plural class-title “rich ones” to preserve the three-tier emanation/evocation structure.

---

## Chapter 51: Concerning the First Man.

**¶2** When the [First] Man came forth against the ene[my] ⟨p.127⟩ garment [of light] the darkness with the garment of [of] living fire. With that garment he withered the tumescence (?) of the enemy; he brought down i[t]s foolery and its vanity.

> *¶2 #2 [strong]:* ene[my ...] requires the completion “enemy”; the substrate idiom regularly sets “the enemy” = Darkness as a collective agent.
> *¶2 #3 [moderate]:* The clause contrasts two vestures: (a) the First Man’s own vesture when he “came forth,” and (b) the answering vesture “of living fire” with which he clothed Darkness. In the oldest layer the First Man advances clothed in light before adding/issuing the specific elemental garments. Thus the missing garment is the general “garment of light.”

**¶3** Now, this one alone was sufficient for them. [Y]et, si[n]ce the living fire was weakened, because of this he added [to it another four]r garments: that of the wind, that of the water, that of the light, and that of the a[i]r. And this, (why) has he given the four? He gave them s[o] th[a]t they would be helpe[r]s to t[h]at god, wh[ich i]s the living fire; so that [the darkness would come upon it] not with [in] the living f[ire], and penetrate it; and [the poison]ison and the [enmi]ty cut it off.

> *¶3 #3 [strong]:* Context demands the standard tetrad added as helpers (wind, water, light, air).
> *¶3 #10 [moderate]:* The sentence sets the function of the four garments: to be auxiliaries so that Darkness does *not* enter/abide within the living fire and pierce it. A phrase like “the darkness would come upon it” matches the inside→outside correspondential logic (assault/pressure preceding penetration).

**¶4** While the fire received a bl[ow and] a wound in its body; yet still, [it struck back and consumed] all the powers of da[rk]ness because of it [and it bound them] It [was by] necessity [a] brothers, to cause them to receive a blow and a woun[d to]o. The power of the en/emy mixed in with them. Rath[er, ex]cept [the air]. The darkness did not mix in with it, nor was the burning of the enemy able to penet/rate it.

> *¶4 #2 [moderate]:* After “the fire received a blow and a wound,” the counter-action is required: despite being wounded, Fire still acts upon Darkness. In the substrate register Fire’s opposite-sense operation is to burn/consume the dark powers.
> *¶4 #4 [moderate]:* Following “all the powers of darkness because of it,” the next move in the teaching is restraint: the wounded elements still serve to bind/hold Darkness in mixture. This anticipates the later statement that other elements must also take wounds through mixture.
> *¶4 #5 [tentative]:* Links the preceding efficacy of Fire to the ensuing ‘necessity’ clause: the necessity arises *by* this (i.e., by Fire’s engagement/wounding).
> *¶4 #6 [minimal]:* Minimal connective needed before “necessity”; exact Coptic particle uncertain.

**¶5** Never-theless, even the one that remained behind without the wound, which is the air, it stripped off garment and power. It gave it to its [fo]ur brothers in its place; so that by that [ga]rment of the living air they would become strong, and [they would have strength and] profit, and endure, namely its other four brothers. And it [be]came for them [a remainder of living breath] life.

> *¶5 #3 [moderate]:* After “become strong,” the parallel triad continues: strength → profit → endurance. The gap supplies the bridging phrase that coordinates the list in an impersonal, technical register.
> *¶5 #5 [tentative]:* The air “stripped off garment and power” and gave it to the four; correspondentially this leaves Air as the un-wounded container/medium, retaining a residuum as ‘breath’ (life-bearing atmosphere) rather than a fifth material garment. Hence it “became for them … life” by remaining as living breath sustaining the four in mixture.

**Assessment:** Most lacunae are trivial letter-completions and are secure. The major restorations are ¶2 #3, ¶3 #10, ¶4 #2–6, and ¶5 #3 & #5. These were constrained by the inner logic of the elemental-garment tetrad (helpers preventing penetration), the wound/mixture sequence (Fire wounded yet still consuming/binding Darkness), and the special status of Air as the un-mixed medium that yields its garment/power to strengthen the other four while remaining as life-bearing breath. Exact connective particles in ¶4 remain least constrained.

---

## Chapter 52: Concerning the [ ... ] of the Light

**¶2** [Consider] how far [is the light]ht that came forth from [the First Source] in that place in a [single will] alone [they were established] ⟨p.128⟩ the greatness of the light with one another for ever, and nor did one of them transgress the command and order that his brother had entrusted to him forever.

> *¶2 #1 [moderate]:* Opening imperative fits the impersonal, instructional register: introducing a measurement/comparison of degrees (“how far...”).
> *¶2 #2 [strong]:* Restores the clause as a comparison of extent/degree of Light; the preserved tail “the lig]ht” constrains this closely.
> *¶2 #3 [moderate]:* Oldest-layer correspondential idiom prefers an impersonal origin (“Source/Root”) over later personalizing titles. Fits “came forth from ...”.
> *¶2 #4 [moderate]:* In the Light-realm, unity is by one will/order; prepares the next sentence about non-transgression of command and entrusted order.
> *¶2 #5 [tentative]:* Provides the needed verbal support: in that place/in a single will alone/they were established... then the statement of perpetual concord follows.

**¶3** However, the darkness, the enemy, was doomed and overcome; because of the arising of disharmony it was divided within itself. Even the kingdom was divided within itself; [and] ev[en] the [powers, who had] left their abyss behind and come up fo[llow-ing] it, were doomed because of the disunity that was present in all of them. The darkness was destroyed, and its other powers were humiliated, because the p[ower] of that first light functions in the same way as the kingdom of this world.

**¶4** As long as its [associates in the mystery]ystery are in agreement with it, th[at] kingdom [stands firm] and they marvel at it and it receives glory, the one that [is in concord with it] and it surpasses and is even more exalted than this other kingdom, which is about [to burst out] against [it].

> *¶4 #3 [moderate]:* Opposition is being drawn: a kingdom persists as long as concord remains. “Stands firm” is the natural effect of interior agreement (correspondence: unity of will → stability of form).
> *¶4 #4 [tentative]:* The clause “the one that [...] and it surpasses” needs a referent for the one receiving glory: the higher kingdom is glorified because it is in concord (unlike Darkness). Keeps the substrate’s structural causality.

**¶5** Still, the kingdom, [the one that] its associates in the mystery [are in agreement] upon, is compelled to condemn that (other) kingdom; and the[y] are humiliated and come under the hand of this kingdom that is set up against it.

**Assessment:** Most lacunae are trivial letter/word restorations fixed by the surviving Coptic/English scaffolding. The only freer reconstructions are in ¶2 and ¶4, where the correspondential logic demands (a) Light as unified will/order with non-transgression, and (b) kingdom-stability as the outward form of inward concord; exact wording remains somewhat open there.

---

## Chapter 53: Concerning the First Man.

**¶1** At the time when the First Man was sent to the war in the first garment of [living fire], which he gave to the first nature that exists [in the abyss of Darkness] in that single garment. [By means of it] he hunted Matter, the thought of death, [Sin] and all its other powers.

> *¶1 #2 [moderate]:* The “first nature” here is the primal Dark Nature/Matter; the garment of Living Fire is given over into the abyss as the medium in which the hunt/snaring occurs. The sentence needs the locative that ties the single garment to the Dark deep.
> *¶1 #3 [moderate]:* Requires an instrumental transition: the one garment is the sole means by which the First Man can seize/hunt the Dark powers in their own domain.
> *¶1 #4 [moderate]:* Standard oldest-layer triad here: Matter = the thought of death; alongside it stands Sin as a ruling dark power (cosmic, not moralizing). Fits “... and all its other powers.”

**¶2** ⟨p.130⟩ When the First Man came up from the abyss, [and had stripped off the garment] the Living Spirit brought the rulers whom he had hunted [in the bonds of his victory] element, that [in each] of [the land of] darkness, that he might construct from them this universe and fashion everything appropriate for the greatness. From that time on, the place of the storehouses of the elements [Darkness] he destroyed and laid waste the [were emptied] all that were in it.

> *¶2 #1 [tentative]:* The ascent from the abyss implies separation/stripping of the mixed garment from the First Man before the Living Spirit’s subsequent use of the hunted rulers to build the cosmos.
> *¶2 #2 [moderate]:* The Living Spirit “brought the rulers” as captives; the correspondential image is binding after the hunt/snare motif continued from ¶1/¶3.
> *¶2 #3 [tentative]:* The phrase “... element, that ... of the land of darkness” calls for distribution: rulers apportioned by element/kind for cosmic construction.
> *¶2 #5 [strong]:* Completes the standard technical collocation “land of Darkness,” the primordial region supplying the captured powers.
> *¶2 #6 [moderate]:* The storehouses of the elements are left as residues after the rulers are removed for construction; “emptied” matches the marsh simile in ¶3 (only waters/reeds left).
> *¶2 #7 [tentative]:* What is “destroyed and laid waste” is not the removed rulers but the remaining structure/bases of the dark storehouses; “foundations” fits the cosmological, non-devotional register.

**¶3** Just like a marsh, of which the fish and birds are snared in a net, and only the waters are left behind and the reeds and the plants that grow in it, this is also the case with the storehouses of death. The [First] Man snared their counsels and [ind]in that were [in them], and the storehouses were left behind in their [ing]ng place.

> *¶3 #2 [strong]:* Restores “kind” (their kind), i.e., their species/orders—consistent with storehouses holding grouped powers.
> *¶3 #4 [strong]:* Restores “dwelling.”

**¶4** So then, the case of the land of darkness is like this, as is that of the storehouses that were in it.

**¶5** Just as the body of man has many limbs in it, [the land of darkness] has [storehouses from] its beginning [and members, and it has] all [the limbs] that hang from the great head of the [body]. A single root is the root of them all, because they all hang from the great head that is on top of the body. Should a limb be cut from the tip of [the]se small [l]eads, the great head that is on top of them [l]l stays in its place the way that it is [and yet] you find this person alive. Al[l] these sm[sma]eads are firmly set in it [and do not slip away]

> *¶5 #3 [tentative]:* Parallelism demanded: as the human body has many limbs, so the land of Darkness has storehouses and members “from its beginning.” This keeps to structural exposition (not Mani’s institutional diction).
> *¶5 #7 [strong]:* Restores “little” (these small heads).
> *¶5 #8 [strong]:* Restores “all.”
> *¶5 #9 [moderate]:* Needed concessive: even if a limb is cut from the small heads, the great head remains, and the person is still found alive.
> *¶5 #11 [strong]:* Restores “small heads.”
> *¶5 #12 [tentative]:* Explains the image: the small heads remain fixed/fastened in the great head/body; prepares for ¶6’s cutting and the resulting “corpses.”

**¶6** Wh[en] they will cut from them [the limbs] all of them, these little [heads] shall [fall down upon] the corpses, because they might see [their root] be firmly set [the root] in the body, which they joined wi[th it ...]

> *¶6 #2 [moderate]:* What is cut off corresponds to limbs/members; the “little heads” are the terminal governing points of those limbs in the body-image.
> *¶6 #4 [tentative]:* Needed verbal link to “the corpses”: the severed little heads (with their limbs) fall onto bodies as dead members.
> *¶6 #5 [tentative]:* They “might see” the root/attachment by which the heads were set in the body; reinforces the single-root doctrine in ¶5.
> *¶6 #6 [tentative]:* Completes: “be firmly set … in the body” by “the root.” Keeps the correspondential anatomy coherent.

**¶7** In exactly this way, it is also the case that the storehouses of darkness are the same, the things from which the [darkness gushed] forth.

**Assessment:** Most lacunae are short and mechanically restorable (mid-word completions, fixed collocations like “land of Darkness”). The harder gaps are the narrative/argument connectors in ¶1–2 and the anatomical-correspondential exposition in ¶5–6; these were restored by maintaining the consistent imagery (hunt/snare → binding/captivity; storehouses as bodily limbs hanging from a single great head/root) and by avoiding later Manichaean devotional or institutional phrasing. Several longer fills remain tentative because multiple Coptic originals could yield similar English without the manuscript letter-traces.

---

## Chapter 54: [Concerning] the Quality of the Garments

**¶1** At the time when the F[ir]st [Man, he arose in his living power] he gave the leadership to his five [sons, and he appointed over them] Living Spirit and the Mother of Life [— these two great Powers] as the entire ruling-power is entwined and crucified [in the mixture of the limbs] they were poured out and mixed with each other, the light with the darkness and the darkness with the light.

> *¶1 #2 [moderate]:* Opening cosmogonic move: the First Man rises/stands forth and then delegates. Oldest-layer register is impersonal: a state-change, not exhortation.
> *¶1 #3 [moderate]:* The syntax demands delegation: leadership given to five sons, then a further appointment/ordering involving Living Spirit and Mother of Life as ruling powers.
> *¶1 #4 [moderate]:* The next clause explains that “the entire ruling-power is entwined and crucified”; this points to the paired powers acting as the binding/ruling principle in the mixture.
> *¶1 #5 [moderate]:* “Entwined and crucified” correspondentially marks extension/expansion into ultimates (limbs/members) and fixation there; this is the standard description of Light bound in the mixed creation.

**¶2** At that time the Living Spirit called one of his sons, one rich with power. He gave to him the leadership of the garments. Now, he himself [took from him the measures of the mixture] of [from the darkness that had been taken captive] he hung them and set them up [from the Father of Life] of the Father of Life [by his command] he set up all things [in the height] he opened [in the wisdom] ten firmaments [as boundaries and measures] the wheel of the stars [the gates] them and the [and the luminaries].

> *¶2 #1 [tentative]:* The Living Spirit’s act here is technical: measuring/ordering the mingled substances before fashioning the ‘garments’ (cosmic structures).
> *¶2 #2 [tentative]:* Garments/firmaments are constructed from the subdued dark matter; this keeps the oldest-layer cosmotechnical tone.
> *¶2 #3 [moderate]:* The repeated later clause “of the Father of Life [...] he set up all things” implies an earlier mention grounding the work in the Father-of-Life’s wisdom/power.
> *¶2 #4 [tentative]:* Delegation logic: the son receives leadership; the hanging/setting-up is performed under higher command/order.
> *¶2 #5 [tentative]:* Garments are ‘hung’ and ‘set up’ above/between regions; “in the height” matches the cosmological architecture register.
> *¶2 #6 [moderate]:* The phrase “of the Father of Life” naturally takes “wisdom/understanding” as genitive content in this layer (technical craftsmanship of the cosmos).
> *¶2 #7 [tentative]:* “He set up all things” typically means establishing limits, measures, and stations—correspondentially, the ordering of degrees and spaces.
> *¶2 #8 [moderate]:* After “he opened [...] ten firmaments” the next expected object is openings/gates/doors through the firmaments for ascents/descents of powers and lights.
> *¶2 #9 [moderate]:* Sequence: ten firmaments → wheel of the stars → luminaries/planets; these are the visible regulators of times and separations.

**¶3** ⟨p.132⟩ The Living Spirit is like this: Just as [a wise craftsman] and an architect who might build a dwelling for the king, and divide it up into separate houses and [separate chambers] everything that is required for beauty [for its adornment] and he cleans them. And also the stones [he sets them in order] them for beauty and {loveliness}, and he arranges them and makes them so that should the king come and inhabit that palace, he might glorify him.

> *¶3 #1 [moderate]:* The simile needs a general artisan figure before “and an architect”; oldest-layer likes paired craft-images for ordering/formation.
> *¶3 #2 [moderate]:* Architect divides a royal dwelling into discrete rooms; ‘chambers’ keeps the palace-cosmos correspondence tighter than a vaguer ‘things’.
> *¶3 #3 [moderate]:* “everything that is required for beauty [...] and he cleans them” points to adornment/finishing work of ordered structures.
> *¶3 #4 [moderate]:* After furnishing/adornment and cleansing, the craftsman’s act is arranging into harmony so the king may inhabit; corresponds to cosmological ordering.

**¶4** Furthermore, the Father of Life resembles a wise and skilled carpenter [who takes a piece of wood] before it was worked [before it was planed] before it was established and made beautiful and [before it was fitted together] carpenter [the carpenter’s art] works in it, but [it is not yet present] in him in his [but it is within] in his understanding, and he brings the saw and the adze, and cuts it into separate pieces [thought] it and he pulls the [and he splits] it with the {plane} and he smooths it. He shall make many [shavings from] wooden implements, arranging them shape by shape; he cuts and produces wooden implements from it, wooden implements of different kinds, (such as) dishes, bowls. This wood, that [off of] since previously it had not been worked before it was sealed by [ll]ll and wisdom of this dextrous carpenter. He cut it up into different sorts of implements useful to his master.

> *¶4 #1 [strong]:* The carpenter simile requires the material (wood) as given before ‘before it was worked’.
> *¶4 #2 [moderate]:* Fits the tool-sequence later (plane, smoothing) and the progression from raw to finished.
> *¶4 #3 [tentative]:* “established and made beautiful” implies joining/setting; fitting precedes establishment.
> *¶4 #4 [moderate]:* The contrast is between raw wood and what is brought forth by art/skill; keeps the impersonal technical register.
> *¶4 #5 [moderate]:* Opposition: the finished form is not ‘in’ the wood externally yet; it exists in the artisan’s mind/understanding.
> *¶4 #6 [moderate]:* Sets up “in him in his [...] in his understanding”: the form pre-exists inwardly in the craftsman (correspondence: archetype in wisdom).
> *¶4 #7 [strong]:* Triad: within him in his thought and in his understanding; standard pairing for interior design/plan.
> *¶4 #8 [tentative]:* After “cuts it into separate pieces [...] it” the missing verb is a second action like splitting/cleaving, matching carpentry process.
> *¶4 #9 [strong]:* “he pulls the [...] it with the plane” naturally takes “shavings” as what is pulled off by planing.
> *¶4 #10 [strong]:* Connects “pulls the shavings from it”; grammatical connector in the description.

**¶5** So is it also with the Father of Life. In his wisdom he has [formed the universe] in limbs [the great body] palace [as a dwelling] in it or in a [vessel] useful [for the service] the ships of light [and the stations] all the other works [in the firmaments] in the [prepared] so that when the great king comes, he who is the Third Ambassador, who [is to come forth in the last separation] he might dwell in this universe [as in a palace]

> *¶5 #1 [moderate]:* Transition from carpenter-work to cosmic work: Father of Life ‘forms’/‘fashions’ the cosmos as an ordered dwelling.
> *¶5 #2 [tentative]:* “in limbs [...] palace” suggests the cosmos as a body with members; ‘great body’ matches correspondential body-palace analogy.
> *¶5 #3 [moderate]:* Explicitly carries the palace analogy: the cosmos is a dwelling prepared for the ‘great king’ (Third Ambassador).
> *¶5 #4 [moderate]:* “in it or in a [...] useful” points to smaller ‘implements’: vessel fits both carpentry list and cosmological ‘ships of light’.
> *¶5 #5 [moderate]:* “useful [...] the ships of light” calls for functional/service language: these structures serve the work of separation/ascent.
> *¶5 #6 [tentative]:* Alongside ‘ships of light’ and ‘other works’, ‘stations’ (places/standings of powers) is expected cosmological architecture vocabulary.
> *¶5 #7 [moderate]:* Locates the works: ships and stations operate through/within firmaments; matches earlier ‘ten firmaments’.
> *¶5 #8 [strong]:* Purpose clause: prepared so that when the great king comes he may dwell; ‘prepared’ keeps the palace-building correspondence.
> *¶5 #9 [tentative]:* The Third Ambassador’s appearing is tied to the great separation/judgment of mixture; oldest-layer phrasing avoids Christian coloring.
> *¶5 #10 [strong]:* Completes the palace simile explicitly: universe as palace/dwelling for the royal presence.

**¶6** ⟨p.133⟩ ... live. The light, however, that is in it [the one who separates is the great Mind that is called the Just Judge] wise, and he takes it up to the land; but the waste and the darkness will be gathered and thrown down to the abyss.

> *¶6 #1 [tentative]:* Line describes discriminating light from waste/darkness and consigning the latter to abyss; oldest-layer needs an impersonal separating faculty (Mind/Judge), not pastoral salvation language.

**Assessment:** Most gaps can be constrained by the internal craft/palace correspondence (ordering, measuring, hanging firmaments, gates, luminaries) and by the standard cosmogonic sequence (mixture → construction of firmaments/garments → preparation of a cosmic dwelling → final separation). Exact wording remains tentative where the Coptic Vorlage could have used several near-synonyms (measure/boundary/station; thought/plan/understanding), but the demanded functions are stable and guide the restorations tightly.

## Chapter 55: Concerning the Fashioning of Adam

**¶1** There are some amo]ng us telling us that we [are not aware] that the (Ambassador) consents about [that]t f[figure] of flesh; he who shall be fashi[oned and shall appear] at another time. And next, he says: [The Ambassador]dor displayed his image in the un[universe]. The rulers and the powers of the universe saw h[his image], and they formed their shapes after his likeness, who are Adam and Eve. Behold, you see that god con[sen]ts to its happening; because of this, he has displayed his image to the rulers; and they constructed [their own image from it] If he was not consenting to humankind, he would not have uncovered his image, nor they fashioned mankind after his likeness!

> *¶1 #1 [moderate]:* The opening contrasts hearsay claims with the speaker’s correction in ¶2–7: the claimants ‘say we know’ but the substrate voice refutes. The idiom ‘are not aware’ fits Coptic turns and avoids devotional register.
> *¶1 #4 [moderate]:* Keeps impersonal cosmological sense: a ‘figure of flesh’ is a formed appearance that manifests ‘at another time’ (i.e., later in the mixture).
> *¶1 #9 [moderate]:* The logic: rulers saw the image and ‘formed their shapes after his likeness’ → they ‘constructed’ a derived image (Adam/Eve). The fill preserves the correspondential point: imitation from a seen pattern, not divine consent.

**¶2** Regarding the Ambassador, the occasion when he came displaying his image, he [d]id not come so th[a]t he might reveal his image to the ru[l]ers in the universe. Rather, he came and displayed to the universe be[cause of h]is soul and his son, who is [the Living Soul] every [bond], that he might enable him to live [from death] and make for him a release; and to free (the soul) from every bond and all the fetters, in which it is entangled and bound.

> *¶2 #5 [moderate]:* In the oldest cosmogonic logic the Ambassador’s manifestation is for the sake of the Soul (the bound light) and ‘his son’ crucified in the totality (explicit in ¶7). ‘Living Soul’ keeps the Iranian-cosmological register.
> *¶2 #6 [strong]:* Pairs with ‘every bond and all the fetters’.
> *¶2 #7 [moderate]:* Release/freeing language in this layer commonly opposes ‘Life’ vs ‘Death’ (cf. ¶14 ‘root of death’).

**¶3** Still, you, look and see! The rulers and the authorities, in that they had no form in [the likeness of] him, they lusted for his image. They reflected upon themselves, that [they] had no [one] like him in their creation. They sealed hi[s im]a[g]e in their heart, within their soul. Afterward[s, they] built it according to his likeness; they formed Adam and [Ev]e. They sealed the image of the exalted one in their [soul]. So, they [co]pied with an imitation; but they have not [copied it]n truth! Rather, just as [a painted image] it is made as these, for [i]t came [from the hand of an artisan] but it is not l[ik]e him in truth. [In the same way] is this figure of ma[mankind. They copied]ed it against the image of the exalted one [in semblance only, not in its own truth]

> *¶3 #1 [moderate]:* They ‘had no form in … him’ → i.e., no form corresponding to his likeness; this sets up lust for the image.
> *¶3 #11 [moderate]:* The contrast required is ‘imitation’ vs ‘truth’: like a painted/inscribed likeness that resembles but lacks the inward reality (correspondentially: outward form without inner degree).
> *¶3 #13 [moderate]:* Fits the analogy: produced ‘from’ a maker, yet not the living original; keeps impersonal craft-language typical of the substrate.
> *¶3 #15 [moderate]:* Avoids homiletic ‘again’; gives the correspondential transition.
> *¶3 #17 [moderate]:* Restates the governing thesis of the chapter: the rulers reproduce a seen stamp outwardly; the inward reality of the exalted image is absent.

**¶4** Similarly the [t]ree, for while it is like the [image, yet] not [copi]ed in truth.

> *¶4 #2 [moderate]:* Tree as a further correspondence: it may look like the archetype but lacks the true inward pattern.

**¶5** This matt[er is comparable to] this: As a great, fre[e] woman [dwells] within her [palace, being] rich in wealth [and many] po[ss]essions; she is also beautiful in her beauty, and is virtuous in her modesty. She walks in a free and reputable position. The fame of her beauty has spread in every c[i]ty. She sur[surpasses all women] Behold h[her] chamber [and behold,] one who is mad, because [of] the nobles and the great ones. Al[son], they lust for her beautiful face. They did not [to look upon] her, because she is hidden in her palace [openly] within in a [and she does not] meet that woman [so much as] his so[n]; or rather the head [of, and]hat garment. This free woman shall [at once] leave the cha[mber] behind her and come to the street [and uncover] her head [from her head,] and she reveals her [face and her beauty openly] because of her beloved broth[er]. This wom[an], on whom no man ever looked, nor did they ever see her fa[ce], as she neither desires nor rejoices [in the outer world] leave her chamber behind her, and come in the midst of mankind [so that the many might see] and everyone view her. The hones[t] men and the nobles, even the servants too, and the [crowds] look at her.

> *¶5 #3 [moderate]:* Sets the scene: she is ‘within’ her house/palace.
> *¶5 #4 [moderate]:* Balances the description of wealth/possessions and hiddenness.
> *¶5 #5 [moderate]:* Pairs naturally with ‘rich in wealth … possessions’.
> *¶5 #8 [moderate]:* Keeps impersonal descriptive tone; motivates universal fame and lust.
> *¶5 #10 [moderate]:* Bridges to the chamber/hiddenness clause.
> *¶5 #13 [moderate]:* They lust for her face but cannot ‘look upon’/see her because hidden.
> *¶5 #14 [moderate]:* Contrasts with hiddenness in palace.
> *¶5 #15 [moderate]:* Prepares for ‘meet that woman…’/lack of access.
> *¶5 #16 [moderate]:* Intensifier for inaccessibility.
> *¶5 #18 [moderate]:* Links ‘his son; or rather the head … that garment’: the beloved relation is the reason for appearing.
> *¶5 #19 [moderate]:* Temporal pivot: she leaves chamber and goes out.
> *¶5 #21 [moderate]:* She unveils head/face—parallel to the Ambassador ‘displaying image’.
> *¶5 #22 [moderate]:* Completes the veil/clothing imagery.
> *¶5 #23 [moderate]:* The point is manifestation/visibility to all powers; ‘openly’ strengthens the correspondence.
> *¶5 #27 [moderate]:* Matches ¶6: not consenting to the outer world except for constraint.
> *¶5 #28 [moderate]:* Corresponds to powers/rulers seeing the displayed image.
> *¶5 #30 [moderate]:* Completes the social range: nobles, servants, and the crowds.

**¶6** ⟨p.135⟩ In accord with this simile: Again, [this] too [is] how [it is] the Ambassador becomes [in the same manner] as this free woman [not] according to wantonness, in pride, nor [consented]nsented to the outer world, she [for constraint], she revealed her face and her [beau]ty [to all who were outside;] but only due to the constraint of grief [about] her [bel]ov[oved one].

> *¶6 #3 [strong]:* ‘Again, this too is how…’ needs a copula to introduce the application of the simile.
> *¶6 #4 [moderate]:* Keeps the impersonal didactic linkage.
> *¶6 #6 [strong]:* Matches the key thesis: manifestation ≠ consent to the rulers’ work.
> *¶6 #7 [moderate]:* Pairs with ‘only due to the constraint of grief’.
> *¶6 #9 [moderate]:* Clarifies the ‘outer world’ audience of the display.

**¶7** Now, this too is the way of the Third Ambassador, when he came and display[ed his] image with a show towards all the powers. [He did] not [co]me so that all the rulers and the author[it]ies would sculpt a form [after h]is likeness. R[ather], he came because of his son being crucified in the totality; [fo]r he would release him, free him, and rescue him from oppres-sion. Yet, when the rulers looked o[n] him, [th]ey lusted for his image. They fashioned after his [like]ness Adam and Eve, without the consent of the gre[at]ness.

**¶9** [Understand]nd also this other (matter) that I will teach you. For, [a]s with Adam and Eve, whom the rulers fashioned and [se]aled after the image of the exalted one, so too this [i]s how a manifold divinity and many powers and angels [received] the stamp of that image, which was displayed to them. For, in the majesty of the l[ig]ht and (the image's) love, they glowed with their desire and they were undone. Th[ey] were eager for their lust; like a m[man who looks] to the majesty of a lusted after face, and [his heart burns] and he is eager for the {} of the desire.

> *¶9 #5 [strong]:* Completes ‘angels … received the stamp’.
> *¶9 #9 [moderate]:* The parallel requires an internal ignition corresponding to lust/desire; ‘heart burns’ matches the glow/undoing of powers.

**¶10** ⟨p.136⟩ This is [als]o the case with those powers, when they looked on that majestic and beautiful [image]e, they () [fashioned] a first image. They received the stamp of th[that image], which was displayed to them. They copied [out, which]ich th[they copied]pied out in a likeness, it being made [in visible form] the ones that they had put forth, which had received a [stamp of this image]age; but inward even to the rocks [there are places] which occur from place to place. The {} of that image, which was displayed in the universe [stamp] they received a stamp, it being revealed there, and an impress.

> *¶10 #3 [moderate]:* They ‘( ) … a first image’ → they fashioned/made an initial copy.
> *¶10 #5 [moderate]:* Links ‘They copied … which they copied out in a likeness’.
> *¶10 #7 [moderate]:* Keeps the inside/outside contrast: outward visibility vs inward stone-like hardness below.
> *¶10 #9 [moderate]:* Prepares for ‘which occur from place to place’.
> *¶10 #10 [strong]:* ‘The {} of that image…’ repeats the technical term: stamp/impress.

**¶11** Now, this new birth that they begot in a new [manner] in the world, is Adam and Eve. [There is not a] form in their world since the beginning like it; because that [im]age [occ]urs not in the worlds b[el]ow.

> *¶11 #1 [moderate]:* ‘in a new … in the world’ → ‘manner’/‘mode’ of birth.

**¶12** Not all the figures that have been fashioned were fashioned by birth from displayed intercourse. Rather, there are other figures fashioned [without intercourse]

> *¶12 #1 [moderate]:* Balances the negation: not all forms are by ‘displayed intercourse’; other forms are fashioned otherwise (as in ¶13–15).

**¶13** One of them is the sea [giant]ant, the 'sweeping' from the sphere, which the [Father] of Life discharged into the sea. Its own fire and thought fashioned it [in] a figure.

**¶14** The second figure is the one [that] fell to the earth. It made the sea its womb. Its own l[us]t became its sculptor. It alone fashioned it to a nature, which is the root of death. Yet, when it came up from the sea, it [rose up] that it might destroy the works [of the Father] of Life. They immediately sent against it the Adamant of Light, the great 'instruction of vigour'. He humbled it in the districts of the north. He has trampled underfoot and set h[him]self upon it, until the end of the world.

> *¶14 #3 [moderate]:* The figure comes up from the sea after being formed there.
> *¶14 #4 [moderate]:* Matches ‘destroy the works … of Life’—the works belong to the Father/Life side; keeps older cosmological naming.

**¶15** ⟨p.137⟩ [The] third figure is the nature that fell on wh[at] is dr[y. It] fashioned the tree and set itself there. [and from it came] new figure that was fashioned [and it]isplays.

> *¶15 #4 [moderate]:* After tree-formation, a further ‘new figure’ emerges/displays—continuation of the generative sequence.
> *¶15 #5 [moderate]:* Connects ‘that was fashioned … displays’.

**¶16** So, in accord with the essence they [resemble]ness; but yet th[ey] did not come with the King of Dark[ness to the world]rld. Rather, their fashioning [came] about [from the mixture, for] they are composite and have been displayed in the world.

> *¶16 #1 [moderate]:* ‘in accord with the essence they resemble the darkness’—i.e., their forms correspond to dark substance though produced by subsequent fashioning.
> *¶16 #5 [moderate]:* Explains why they did not ‘come with the King of Darkness’ yet appear: they arise from later mixture/composition and thus are ‘composite and displayed’.

**Assessment:** Most gaps are short or mid-word and restore cleanly. The larger restorations (¶1 #1, ¶3 #11/#13/#17, ¶5 narrative gaps, ¶10 #7/#9, ¶14 #3–4, ¶16 #1/#5) are constrained by the chapter’s core correspondential argument: the ‘displayed image’ is a necessary manifestation for release, not consent; the rulers’ products are outward stamps without inward truth. Exact phrasing remains somewhat tentative where long narrative portions are missing, but the restored sense stays within the oldest cosmological register and avoids later devotional/institutional language.

## Chapter 56: Concerning Saklas and his Powers

**¶2** The sin that spurted out from the rulers, which is Matter, shot up [tow]ards the image of the Ambassador. It was cut off from [tha]t place and came down to the earth, for they did not accept [it wi]thin the firmaments. When it came do[wn to] the earth, it formed the tree. It was established within the wood and formed the fruits. And when [the] abortions fell [to] the ground, to the earth, at the [second] time [it rose again into the fruits / and clothed itself in them]

> *¶2 #8 [moderate]:* The substrate logic is a two-stage descent/ascent: Matter (Sin) first falls to earth and forms the tree (vegetative ultimate), then at the ‘second time’ it re-enters via the fruits (seeded ultimates) to approach the rulers again. This matches ¶4’s recap: ‘afterwards it came up in the fruits’.

**¶3** ⟨p.138⟩
[When it came up in the] fruits, it assumed the[m] and spoke in the ruler, their leader. He says to his companions: Come! Give me your light, and I will construct for you an image aft[er] the likeness of the exalted one. What he said, they did: they gave it to him, and he constructed [an image of flesh after their likeness].

> *¶3 #1 [moderate]:* The immediate antecedent is the abortions/seeds that become fruits; the narrative requires the explicit ‘coming up in the fruits’ transition that ¶4 later summarizes.
> *¶3 #4 [tentative]:* The ruler proposes making an ‘image after the likeness of the exalted one’; within the oldest layer this becomes the mixed ‘flesh’ image (a vessel) constructed from stolen light—preparing for Adam/Eve formation in ¶4-5.

**¶4** The sin that s[purted out from the rulers / and descended to the earth] which is the Matter that saw the im[age of the Ambassa]dor and that formed the tre[e and was established i]n [i]t; afterwards it came up in the [fruits and assumed them]. It went in to the rulers [and they took it through the fruits; then] they formed Adam and Eve after the [likeness of the] exalt[ed one]. Through the energy of the sin that had s[een the image of the] Ambassador, it went into the (rulers) [through the fruits. It] assumed them and they [t]oo sculp[ted in them; and they] begat each of them according to his destiny [from one another in their begetting]; one he resembles, one he does not resemble.

> *¶4 #1 [moderate]:* This clause recapitulates ¶2-3: Sin/Matter issues from the rulers, is cut off, falls to earth, then returns upward through fruits. The recap needs both origin (from rulers) and descent (to earth).
> *¶4 #5 [moderate]:* Required by the causal chain: Matter rises in fruits, ‘assumes’ (clothes itself with) them, then speaks in the ruler (¶3) and later ‘assumed them’ again in ¶4.
> *¶4 #6 [moderate]:* The gap sits between ‘It went in to the rulers’ and ‘they formed Adam and Eve’. The rulers must ‘take’/‘receive’ the sin-energy mediated by fruits as the immediate efficient cause of forming the image.
> *¶4 #12 [moderate]:* Keeps the parallelism: sin ‘assumed’ the fruits, then ‘they too sculpted’—i.e., the rulers, now animated by that same energy, sculpt bodily forms and propagate them.
> *¶4 #13 [tentative]:* The immediate sequel is: ‘they begat each of them according to his destiny ...; one he resembles, one he does not resemble.’ The missing phrase must introduce variance in propagation/likeness within bodily generation.

**¶5** Behold, I have taught you this lesson, how the rulers shaped Adam and Eve by the action of the sin that went into the [m] through the fruits, and he was ordained after the image of the exalted one.

**¶6** The moulder placed in the form of Adam and Eve limbs, outside and within, for perception and activity. He was apportioned house by house. For everything that his perceptions and elements will receive externally there are internal storehouses and repositories and cavities; and what is received in to them is stored in them. Whenever they will be questioned about what is deposited in their internal storehouses, they bring out what they have received within and give it to the questioner who requested it of them.

**¶7** In this way his faculty [is set in five divisions:] outer [limbs] to look at [the forms and] every type with [its storehouses]

> *¶7 #1 [moderate]:* The chapter turns from formation of the body to its correspondential ‘houses’ per sense. The transition ‘In this way his faculty…’ naturally introduces the fivefold arrangement (sight/hearing/smell/taste/touch) leading into ¶8-12.
