> *¶7 #3 [moderate]:* Eyes are first treated; they ‘look at’ forms/images. The next paragraph elaborates images seen as good/evil, loveable/detestable, lustful etc.
> *¶7 #4 [moderate]:* Fits the established technical vocabulary: each faculty has ‘houses and cavities and repositories and stores’.

**¶8** ⟨p.139⟩
Also [the fa]culty of the eyes [has] houses and cavities and repositories and stores within, so that every image it might see, whether good or evil, whether loveable or detestable or lustful, it can receive into its storehouses and repositories. Also, when the faculty of the eyes is pleased to send out the image that it saw and took in, it can go in to its storehouses [at the] time and think and seek [for the likeness that it seeks] and it brings it out [and] gives it to the questioner who requested it and the one who wan[t]ed it—whether it be something from lu[st, or a]n image of love [or else / something] hateful. And thus shall that faculty (of the eyes) produce and do what it does in each category.

> *¶8 #4 [moderate]:* After ‘think and seek ...’ the object is the stored image/likeness; the faculty searches its repositories for the particular image requested.
> *¶8 #7 [strong]:* Completes ‘lust’ → ‘lust, or an image…’.
> *¶8 #8 [moderate]:* The triad requires a third class balancing love and lust with hate; the surrounding text already supplies ‘something hateful’ afterward. This restores the connective (‘or else’) plus the lead-in (‘something’).

**¶9** The faculty of the ears has its own storehouses also. Every sound it might receive, whether good or evil, shall be taken in and placed in its houses and inner repositories, and it is guarded in its [inner repositories] for a thousand days. After a thousand days, if someone comes and asks that faculty about the sound that it heard [at] this time and took into its storehouses, immediately it [sh]all g[o] into its repositories and seek and review and search after this word, and send it out from where it was first put, the place in which it was kept.

> *¶9 #1 [moderate]:* The ears’ deposit is ‘guarded in its ... for a thousand days’; ‘inner repositories’ is the stable term used throughout for the guarded storing-place.

**¶10** [In] like order, the faculty of scent [s]hall function just as that of the eyes and that of the auditory organs. Every [o]dour it shall smell it shall take in [to it] and [d]eposit in its inner storehouses. Every time it will be [a]sked by a questioner, it shall go in [to its repositories] and [to its inner] storehouse and remember [that odour] only these things.

> *¶10 #7 [moderate]:* Matches the repeated procedural sequence: asked → go into repositories/storehouses → seek/review → bring out.
> *¶10 #8 [moderate]:* Prepares for ‘storehouse’ immediately following in the line (‘... storehouse’).
> *¶10 #9 [moderate]:* The faculty ‘remember[s] ... only these things’; the remembered object is the specific odour previously deposited.

**¶11** ⟨p.140⟩
However, even the mouth and the tongue within it, and the taste organ, have a faculty dwelling in them. Again, that faculty too, of taste, has thus cavities and repositories set apart for it. It too receives these tastes and gathers them in. A[nd] at any moment when someone will ask of a taste, if [it is stored within it,] it shall send it out and remember tha[t] taste. It shall snare and give even the mar[k of] th[a]t taste; give its memory to the qu[est]ioner who asks for it.

> *¶11 #2 [moderate]:* The condition clause needs the depositional premise: if the taste was received and stored, then it can be sent out/remembered on request.

**¶12** Again, the fac[ul]ty of touch by the hands is also s[o]: When it might touch, touch shall [receive i]ts memory. And it [takes] it in to its inner repository [until] som[eo]ne will ask this faculty for the memory. [Immediately], it shall go in again and bring out the memory of this touch that it made, and give it to whoever asks for it.

**¶13** And the faculty of the heart that rules over them all is much the most like this. Every thing that these five faculties will receive and put in store for the faculty of the heart it shall receive and guard. Any time that they will ask for their deposit it shall send out and give every thing that they gave to it.

**¶14** For the counsel of the heart is like the king. All the ad[v]isers of his city and the leaders of his a[r]my are placed with him. Any time if his genera[ls] will entreat him for whatever they wish, he shall give them the ad[v]ice and opinion by which they move.

**¶15** Furthermore, it is like a cellarman: [Eve]ry thing that will be entrusted to it by them, and given to it, it shall receive and put in separate vessels and places. Any time [when they ask / it opens / and gives]

> *¶15 #2 [moderate]:* The cellarman simile must mirror the ‘asked → open → bring out → give’ mechanism stated repeatedly for each faculty; the three-part lacuna fits that fixed procedural cadence.

**¶16** ⟨p.141⟩
... in the place that it put it.

**¶17** This also is the way of the faculty of the body. The vision that the eyes will see and give to it, and it receives it in, it shall bring out again from the storehouses wherein it had placed it; and give it to whoever asks for it.

**¶18** Again, [the] sound that the ears will hear and give to it, and it receives it from them. At any time when someone will ask it for that sound, it shall bring out and give the sound of the ears to whoever has asked it for that sound. It shall bring out and give the sound of the ears to whoever has asked.

**¶19** Again, [the] faculty of smell in this same pattern; and that of taste is of the same [ty]pe. The touch by the han[ds; and if it is asked] they shall bring it out in to [its storehouses / its repositories] And whenever they will as[k it] for it, it shall bring it out and gi[ve it].

> *¶19 #3 [moderate]:* The phrase must complete ‘touch by the hands …’ and lead into ‘they shall bring it out…’. The sense is: touch stores ‘its memory’ and, upon request, produces it.
> *¶19 #4 [moderate]:* Two coordinated storage terms recur everywhere (storehouses + repositories). The plural ‘they’ here refers to the faculties in general following this same pattern.

**¶20** Indeed, watchmen are a[t] these doors guarding them, and bolts are fastened on the doors at the hands of the guards that guard them!

**¶21** Just like watchmen guarding a camp with towers and watch-posts. As the holding rooms come out from there and the bolts are upon the gates, the guards control the bolts of the gates while they sit at the entrance [of] the watchposts. So, someone who belongs to their city as a fellow [cit]izen of them, belonging to their country, for him they shall open [the ga]te and receive him in by their will. However, the foreigner who comes they shall prevent, and leave him standing at the gate. They shall not yield to let him enter. Again, another one whom they will receive in, with great trouble before [they] take him in, you shall find many holding rooms [between gate and gate] that [and towers] there are a mass [of guards / of watchmen] them. The number of d[oor] bolts [also is great] are [in their places] Whenever someone will wish [to enter, one of the guards takes] the bolts at his hand, opens the gate and brings out whoever is hidden within.

> *¶21 #5 [tentative]:* The image intensifies: difficult entry leads through multiple holding rooms and successive gates—matching the later ‘mass of guards’ and many bolts.
> *¶21 #6 [tentative]:* Completes the fortified-camp correspondences (watchposts, towers, holding rooms).
> *¶21 #7 [tentative]:* Balances ‘many holding rooms ... that ... there are a mass ... them’: i.e., rooms corresponding to a mass of guards/watchmen stationed throughout.
> *¶21 #9 [tentative]:* After ‘The number of door bolts ... are ...’ a quantitative predicate is required; ‘great’ matches the piling-up effect.
> *¶21 #10 [tentative]:* Bolts ‘are ...’: they are fixed ‘in their places’ under the guards’ control.
> *¶21 #11 [moderate]:* Leads directly into ‘the bolts at his hand, opens the gate and brings out…’. The actor must be ‘one of the guards’ manipulating bolts.

**¶22** This body too is like the mighty camp. And the gates of the camp with their guards are like the orifices and organs of the body. Now, the orifices of the body are of sight, hearing and smell; and they that send out words. There are many sentinels and a mass of guards placed over the limbs of the body, guarding their orifices. And the faculty in it is the directive of the body, the queen of the entire camp; so that whenever it wishes it can open, or when it wishes it can shut.

**¶23** However, if and wh[en] the Light Mind comes, it shall tu[rn i]n to the orifices of the body. The rulers, who [a]re the door gua[rds], shall h[in]der and restrain it from en[ter]ing. They shall bind the circu[it] of the body so that the foreign[er] can not enter it.

**¶24** Nevertheless, the Light Mind by its wisdom and awe and diligence shall hum[ble] the guards who are set at the body's orifices. When it finishes humbling them it shall take fr[o]m them bolts to all the orifices of the body. Now, the orifices that had been opened before to the parades of lust, to receive in the sights of pleasure, to transfer the [wor]ds of the practice of li[f]e's concerns, to receive in the t[astes] of the different foods and unclean meals together with the sin therein; whoever stops receiving th[is] in, his heart and mind shall follow after.

**¶25** So, now, bec[ause] the b[olt]s to the body of the righteous person are in the hands of the Light M[in]d within, he is open [to receive] in all that is pleasing to God. He is op[en to take] in by his eyes the visions of lov[e and mercy] and righteousness. Indeed, due to this, the si[ghts and] the [ad]ornme[nt of the gar]ments of kings, which are woven with gold; and the garments of silken wom[en], which are woven with gold and pearls, made beautiful for the shape of lust; first they were lovable before the eyes of the chosen person. Yet, now, by the power of the Light Mind that has suffused him and lived in the body he has humbled lust, the goddess of the body. The parades of lust have become loathsome in the presence of the righteous person. How-ever, the aspects of divinity and righteousness and humility and scorned clothes are pleasing to him.

> *¶25 #6 [moderate]:* The ‘visions of love ... and righteousness’ calls for a paired virtue in the same register; ‘mercy’ (compassion/charity) matches the later ‘knowledge of charity’ in ¶26 and keeps the impersonal ethical-cosmological tone.

**¶26** Again, [these] ears were first opening to empty sounds and to the melodies of lust, to the secrecies of [wic]kedness. All these were pleasing at first, at the time when he he[a]rd them. Yet, now, by the power of the Light Mind, the sounds of lust and the words of magic and evil mysteries have become loathsome in his presence; as he is not pleased to hear them from this time on. Rather, he likes all the time to listen to the sounds of the lessons of righteousness, the words of the psalms and of the prayers, the praise of the hymns and the lessons of truth, and the knowledge of charity.

**¶27** [S]imply, all orifices in similar fashion were opening at one time to wickedness, but now have opened to the good. Sin was ruling in the body this time, doing anything it liked, having none in command.

**¶28** Yet, [n]ow, authority has been taken from the Sin in the org[ans] of the body. The Living Spirit has become lord over all its limbs. It has bound them with a chain of peace, and sealed them with the seal of truth. It has opened the orifices of the body to the good, and because of this the good enters them through the ears and the eyes. It dwells in the heart. The H[o]ly Spirit reigns. It does all that it pleases. It annuls [the] will of [S]in, the begin-ning and the end. [In contrast], the will of the [Holy Spirit] comes about, and the purpose of the Light Mind.

> *¶28 #7 [moderate]:* Corrects the scribal/editorial misspelling ‘Holly’; the oldest-layer register treats this as the pure pneumatic principle opposing Sin’s will.

**¶29** With wisdom and skill make yourselves secure at the circuit of the body's orifices, so that the sin that dwells in the body has no power over you. Nor does it take from you your light, nor scatter it from place to place, nor (drive) it from site to site in various forms.

**Assessment:** Most lacunae are trivial letter/word repairs with high certainty. The larger gaps occur in ¶2, ¶3, ¶4, ¶7, ¶8, ¶15, and ¶21; these were restored by following the chapter’s fixed correspondential mechanism (deposit in inner storehouses → request → seek/review → bring out) and the cosmogonic sequence (Matter/Sin descends → forms tree → rises via fruits → animates rulers → image-formation). Restorations in the fortified-camp simile (¶21) remain the least constrained and are therefore tentative.

---

## Chapter 57: Concerning the Generation of Adam.

**¶2** Now, no[te] that Adam is mighty in his stature, and great is his size. Even his years are many! He spent many years living in the world. And he is not the only one we have found to be mighty; but all the others who were begotten in his generation, many are their years too. Still, these people who are born today, their stature has been diminished in power; even their years are lessened. Also, why is the birth today, of [the]y who are born, altered compared with that of these first [who travelled from place to place] place to place? See, even the stars and signs of the zodiac continue in their positions. Why now have the age and the years of these last ones diminished, and he has also become smaller, compared to these ancient ones who belong to these first generations?

> *¶2 #3 [moderate]:* The question contrasts present diminished births with the first generations, then immediately notes the fixed courses of stars/signs. The oldest-layer logic requires a clause describing the first humans as moving under the same heavens (i.e., through lands/regions) so the objection stands: the cosmic order appears unchanged, yet lifespans/stature changed. Restores a syntactically needed subject (“who”) and a neutral, impersonal cosmological-historical descriptor rather than moralizing language.

**¶3** They increased, have turned, and diminished; because there are five types of authorities and leaders appointed in the sphere of zodiacal signs, and the heavens above it. They have names they are ca[lled] by: the first name is the year; the sec[on]d is the month; the third is the day; the fou[rth] is the hour; the fifth is the moment. Now, these five positions, and these five houses, exist in the sphere and the heavens. These places have five powers, and they are made masters over them. The master of the year exists; the master of the month exists; the master of the day exists; the master of the hour exists; the master of the moment exists. In turn one is above his companion; and in turn the one above is made the master over the one below him.

**¶4** So, mankind is begotten, and [the animals]mals. They are born in these powers. And, thus, these powers have received authority since the beginning of creation till the end of the world. Now, in the beginning of the generations the powers who were masters over the years reigned. Therefore, because of this, the offspring born in their generations and their lifetime: their years were found to be greater. They attain a long life span in the generation of Adam, and [that]t of Sethel his son, and that of the ones who came after him.

> *¶4 #1 [strong]:* Required by parallelism: ‘mankind … and the animals’ as the two classes generated under the temporal powers.

**¶5** After they ceased to reign, namely the powers who are masters over the years; the powers who are masters over the months themselves reigned. Like the months, for they are less than the years; again this [is] also the case with the offspring w[who were] born in them. Their life span is less [than the life span of the ones born] in the generation of the powers who master the years!

**¶6** When the authority of the powers who master the months was completed; the powers of the days received authority after them. Therefore, like the days, for they are less than the months, this also is the case with the offspring who were born in them. Their life span is less than the life span of the ones born in the powers who are masters over the months. Again, even so the offspring who were born in the hours, and of the moments. Their {conduit} is less than that of the months and the days.

**¶9** Now, can you recount to me this other (matter): By what sort of birth was the entire offspring of mankind made from one man and one woman, Adam and Eve?

**¶10** For the manner of these first generations is that they are richer in their light and purer in stature. Even their years are greater. Thus, again, even their offspring is much more than the offspring of this tim[e]. They were spending a longer time [forming] in [the womb]omb of their mother. Afterwards, they [bore at once] a number in a single womb. There are times five were brought forth from a single womb, and times six were brought forth from [a] single womb! There are some lesser than this; again, some greater than this. Indeed, because of this the offspring of Eve multiplied, and of her children. The earth was filled by reason of them alone; because the offspring from the generation of the people of old, who they bore into the world, differ greatly from those of today. Their conception and their birth is not like latter births, brought forth nowadays from the woman's uterus.

> *¶10 #2 [moderate]:* The sentence needs a durative gestational verb before ‘in the womb’. In the oldest substrate register, the point is physiological/temporal (longer gestation under purer/stronger first conditions), not ethical; ‘forming’ fits the impersonal correspondential causality.
> *¶10 #4 [moderate]:* Required by the following: ‘Afterwards, they … a number in a single womb’ then examples of five/six. The substrate point is multiplicity-per-gestation as an effect of stronger first constitution; ‘bore at once’ supplies the needed action without later pastoral coloring.

**Assessment:** Most lacunae are trivial letter/word completions constrained by immediate syntax. Two larger gaps (¶2 #3; ¶10 #2–#4) are recoverable at the level of function: they must support the cosmological argument that fixed heavenly courses remain while temporal ‘masters’ shift, producing diminished stature/lifespan and reduced fecundity. Exact wording is less tightly constrained, but the restored phrases keep the impersonal, structural register of the oldest substrate.

---

## Chapter 58: The Four Powers that grieve

**¶2** ⟨p.147⟩ The first who grieves is the first Mother of Life. She is sad because of her children who are set in affliction, for they were conjoined with the Darkness and the [poison]son. They have been bound with the entire ruling-power. For what was she grieving, [wa]tching over them taking these afflictions? Whenever she might [s]ee them and how suffering is brought upon them, she shall grieve and be unhappy on account of them.

> *¶2 #1 [strong]:* Fixed expression in the cosmological substrate: the Darkness mingles with the Light through its tainting ‘poison’; paired with ‘Darkness’ and followed by binding under the ruling-power.

**¶3** ⟨p.148⟩ [The s]econd one who is troubled of heart is the First Man, whose [great]ness and kingdom is established in the li[ght] ship [of the Living Spirit]. And he too is troubled of heart because of his five sons who are set in the midst of danger. For any time he may see that they are given trouble, always he might hear the sound of their weeping as they cry out because of the blows and wounds of the enemy who assaults them daily; and as he sees them like this constantly, he shall g[ri]eve and mourn on account of his [s]o[n]s.

> *¶3 #4 [moderate]:* In the oldest cosmogonic register, the First Man’s ‘greatness and kingdom’ are said to be set/established in the Light’s ordered vehicle or ‘ship’, governed by the saving Power that retrieves and orders—the Living Spirit—rather than later ecclesial or hagiographic framing. This also keeps the impersonal, structural tone: the Light-ship as the organized realm where his station is fixed.

**Assessment:** Most lacunae are trivial letter-restorations or fixed terms. The only substantive gap is the light-ship clause; it is constrained by the cosmological structure (First Man’s station in the ordered Light realm) but the exact epithet after ‘light ship’ remains less than certain.

---

## Chapter 59: The Chapter of the Elements that wept

**¶2** The garments, the sons of the First Man, wept bitterly three times. Then they were silent. There is a fourth time of weeping, for they will weep at the end. And then their crying can stop, and they will never again weep from this time on.

**¶3** Now, the first time of their weeping is when they had seen the darkness, the enemy being about to burst out against them. They understood everything that would happen to them. They were aware of the one that would rise and come upon them, but by necessity they hastened to him and fought with him. They mastered him, they detained him. They bound him with their body and their limbs, they overcame his death and his fire and his darkness.

**¶4** [The] second time that they wept is [when the]ir father, the First Man, [ascend]ed from the [abyss] of darkness and left them behind; that is the time when he stripped [them off] and left them below. Indeed, when th[ey] s[aw] how the First Man had risen out of the abyss and [l]eft them behind, and that they were abandoned in the enmit[y, th]ey wept and were sore of heart. Still, what could they do, wishing [to follow after him, they subjected] their heart to every affliction. They [remained b]ehind and grew [s]ick under the pressure of the weighty burden of all the works, until the end time.

> *¶4 #9 [strong]:* Restores the fixed surrounding word as “enmity, they…”, keeping the impersonal cosmological register (state of hostile mixture) rather than a moralizing gloss.
> *¶4 #10 [moderate]:* The syntax demands a concessive/helpless transition: they cannot prevent the Father’s ascent, yet they ‘subject’ (submit) their heart to affliction. Correspondentially: once the higher principle withdraws, the lower powers can only endure the imposed state of mixture and burden until the ordained end.

**¶5** [The] third time that they wept is when the Living Spirit stripped off the three garments of activity, placing them beneath all things. He gave them a sign to lift their heads and rise to the heights from that place; and to pass and journey through all the earths until they should reach the land of tranquilli[t]y and peace, the place of rest prepared for them. So, when they had seen him, that he had separated himself from them, while they themselves remained behind after him and had not ascended; at that moment when they stayed behind these three garments wept. After their crying, they turned their [hear]t to all the heights that were concealed from them, and were of one mind. For [they would] soar up and come to the heights; and pass over all things until the time that was ordained for them all [together] at the end of the worlds.

> *¶5 #5 [moderate]:* The clause “until the time that was ordained for them all ___ at the end of the worlds” calls for a collective completion: their ascent and traversing of ‘all things’ culminates as one integrated return. This fits the substrate’s impersonal, structural teleology (ordained time; collective reversion).

**¶6** [The f]ourth time when they will weep is when the [S]tatue will be taken up on the last day, and they will weep [for] the souls of the liars and blasphemers; for they may give [no help to them] because their limbs have been severed [from the bonds] of the darkness. And also [th]ose souls, when the Statue will go up and th]ey are left alo[n]e, they will weep in that they will rema[in] behind in affliction for ever. For they wi[ll be cut off] and separated from the Last Statue. An[d it is] a necessity to take these souls who are ready for loss as retribution for the deeds that they have done. They go i[n to] this [darkne]ss and are bound with the darkness; just as they desired it and loved it, and placed their treasure with it. At that very moment, when the Last Statue rises up, the[y wi]ll weep. And then they will scream out loud because they will be severed from the company of this great Statue. And they remain behind for ever. This great weeping [for the tearing-away] is terrible, it occurs in front of the souls and [all those] who are ready for loss in accordance with their deeds. And they [will] weep with that crying, they will never be silent; because they will not attain rest from this time on.

> *¶6 #4 [moderate]:* The garments’ lament is ‘for the souls’ at separation; correspondentially, once the great collectivity of Light is taken up, there is no remaining instrument of conjunction for the severed. Hence they can ‘give no help’.
> *¶6 #5 [moderate]:* The phrase “because their limbs have been severed ___ of the darkness” requires a noun for what one is severed from. ‘Bonds’ matches the mechanical/cosmological register (binding, fetters) used elsewhere in the chapter and avoids moralizing terms.
> *¶6 #14 [moderate]:* “This great weeping ___ is terrible” calls for a cause/appositive: the weeping is ‘for’ the act/state of separation itself. In correspondential terms, tearing-away from the collective form of Light is the felt horror.
> *¶6 #15 [moderate]:* Pairs naturally with “the souls and ___ who are ready for loss,” giving a totalizing scope without introducing later-community categories. It keeps the register generic: souls + all those destined by their own works.

**¶7** So, they too are the garments of these elements. This fourth time of weeping is heart-rending for them. It is much worse for them than these other three times of weeping before. This fourth time the weeping is not for themselves. Rather they weep and are heartsore for these soul[s] who will be cut off from peace, because rest will not come about for them, to allow them to rest from torment for ever.

**Assessment:** Most lacunae are trivial or mechanically constrained (articles, single letters, fixed phrases). Three substantial gaps required restoration by correspondential logic: (¶4 #10) a helpless-but-necessary response to the Father’s ascent; (¶6 #4–5) the impossibility of aid after severance and the ‘bonds’ of Darkness; (¶6 #14–15) the weeping defined by the act of separation and its scope. These are moderate-confidence because multiple Coptic-compatible phrasings could fit, but the chosen restorations best match the oldest substrate’s impersonal cosmological mechanics (ascent/withdrawal, binding, severance, ordained times) rather than later moral or ecclesial diction.

---

## Chapter 60: Concerning the Four Fathers; what they are like.

**¶1** Just as the corporeal body has limbs that are hidden, so it also has others that are visible. They can be seen upon it, they can be grasped and receive blows and wounds, whilst others can neither be grasped nor receive blows.

**¶2** So it is also with this entire greatness, as we resemble it. Some of it is hidden, whilst some of it is visible; some of it is far away from the blows and suffering, whilst some of it receives blows and wounds.

**¶3** Now, thus the directive of the body that lives within, hidden in the storehouses inside the body and unseen. So it is also how the Father, the God of truth, exists; as he too is hidden in his kingdom, not displayed before this outer desolation.

**¶4** Thus, too, the body has two eyes that are visible and seen, and are even the body's source of illumination; so there are also these two light ships that shine and are seen in the entire universe and the whole of creation. The light has never been hidden, just like the two eyes of the body.

**¶5** The living soul too is like this; it is established in apparent silence. In its apparent silence it is grasped and receives blows from these five fleshes, which destroy (the soul) and strike it. It can be likened to the mystery of the corporeal body, as it can be grasped and mastered, can receive blows and wounds.

**¶6** ⟨p.152⟩ Also, thus this tongue that speaks, proclaims, reprimands and reveals about everything happening in this body, inside and outside, also the considerations and bodily knowledges. The tongue, which is outside, makes it manifest. It also proclaims about the honour and shame of the body. And it speaks, unveiling the pain of the body that is sick from its sores.

**¶7** This is also what Jesus the Splendour is like, who shall be sent from the greatness. He manifests and reveals about everything, both the external and the internal, both what is above and what is below. Be it about the outer aeons that are hidden and not displayed, or about the ships of light that are seen: he is the one who unveils about them. He is also the one who reveals about their glory, and the greatness that dwells in them.

**¶8** And regarding this beating and wounding of the living soul, Jesus is the one who reveals it. He also preaches about (the soul) and its peace. He reveals about its cleansing and healing.

---

## Chapter 61: Concerning the Garment of the Waters: how great is its Measure.

**¶6** [At] the time when the First Man assumed the elements, and stood [fi]rm against the first enmity that had come about in the darkness, he defied these great and powerful rulers. They are immeasurable, greater than the extent or size of thousands and ten thousands of parasangs. At the start, when he spread the fight against them, he gave them fire and wind; and afterwards he gave the garment of the waters to the rulers of death. So they, the rulers, drank in and consumed those waters, [the way that I told] you [before, that they could not be filled]. Many [waters] poured forth from the garment of waters [and from them] the rivers flow. The mouth of the rulers [was opened, and] those [gullets of], the warriors of destruction [and they] laboured and brought them forth.

> *¶6 #3 [strong]:* Keeps the didactic, impersonal teacherly cadence of the substrate: reference back to an earlier correspondential explanation (how the rulers ingest an element and it becomes torment).
> *¶6 #4 [moderate]:* The logic requires an explicit point: the rulers consume the water yet remain insatiable; this sets up ¶7 (“as they shall not … to a single stomach, to their fill”).
> *¶6 #6 [moderate]:* Bridges from “Many waters poured forth” to the cosmological effect (“the rivers flow”): the rivers are the outflow/effect of those waters.
> *¶6 #7 [moderate]:* Needs a verb to connect “The mouth of the rulers … those …”: in the substrate, the ‘mouth’ is the receiving/ingesting organ; it ‘opens’ to draw in the element.
> *¶6 #8 [tentative]:* Correspondentially fits with mouth/stomach imagery and the theme of devouring rulers; also prepares for ¶7’s “descent to its stomach.”
> *¶6 #9 [moderate]:* Restores a minimal grammatical link: “the warriors of destruction … laboured and brought them forth” needs a subject-conjunction tying rulers/warriors to the action.

**¶7** There were seven (rivers), like the great river Tigris [that you] see, flowing in to the mouth of each one of them, as they shall not [be gathered] to a single stomach, to their fill; because in turn the descent to its stomach shall burn them with its fire and heat. Those rulers were like the [bellies] of [serpents]; they were not emptied; rain and [mist fed] their existent days.

> *¶7 #2 [moderate]:* Required by sense: the seven rivers flow into each mouth “as they shall not be gathered to a single stomach” (i.e., not consolidated; each ruler has its own intake).
> *¶7 #3 [tentative]:* Completes the simile: “like the bellies of …” matches the imagery of mouth/stomach, and describes capacious, never-emptied receptacles.
> *¶7 #4 [tentative]:* Old substrate regularly casts the devouring powers as serpent-forms; “bellies of serpents” coheres with insatiable ingestion and underworld physiology.
> *¶7 #5 [tentative]:* Parallel to “rain”: “rain and mist fed their existent days” (i.e., continual supply sustaining their state). Keeps natural correspondences (rain/mist) as influxes.

**¶9** [This] is how great a struggle, since it has [bound up] these creatures of destruction, the mighty giants. Behold, they are entwined, bound, and [shut up] in every place. They are hidden, not revealed before the eyes of every flesh that exists on the land of death [until the end]. These rulers [shall be] revealed. The entire universe of flesh will be destroyed and dissolved [in that dissolution]; all pass away [with its forms]. They will be destroyed with their angry voice, which is mightier than the thunder.

> *¶9 #1 [strong]:* The paragraph resumes by pointing to what has just been described (the waters/rivers and their torment), so a deictic “This is how great…” fits the didactic register.
> *¶9 #2 [moderate]:* Requires a verb of encompassing/holding: the struggle ‘has bound up’ or ‘has constrained’ the destructive creatures (continuing “entwined, bound…”).
> *¶9 #3 [moderate]:* Completes the triad “entwined, bound, and …”: the substrate idea is containment/immurement of the giants within the structure of the world.
> *¶9 #4 [moderate]:* The concealment is temporal: they are hidden from common flesh “until the end” (i.e., until the dissolution). Matches ensuing cosmic dissolution language.
> *¶9 #5 [strong]:* Supplies the copula/auxiliary needed: “These rulers shall be revealed.” Revelation here is cosmological disclosure at the final unbinding, not devotional.
> *¶9 #6 [moderate]:* The universe of flesh is destroyed/dissolved; “in that dissolution” anchors the clause in the same event and keeps the impersonal, process-focused tone.
> *¶9 #7 [tentative]:* Natural-effects language: “all pass away with its forms” (i.e., the formed appearances of fleshly cosmos). Fits the substrate’s emphasis on forms as ultimates.

**¶10** Now, as this small stream is reckoned [as nothing] before the vastness of these waters of which I have recounted to you. For only one river from thousands of rivers of water, from the secrets that are not revealed, is the river Tigris upon the earth.

> *¶10 #1 [strong]:* Sets the proportion: the visible stream is “as nothing” compared to the immeasurable hidden waters; matches the comparative rhetoric of ¶10.

**¶11** See [then look] and be amazed at the measure of the elements of the waters. Marvel at the stature of the Man who bore them. And understand how great is that god, active in power, the one whom these waters [are lesser] than the stature of the First Man who [assumed] this garment of such size; and how much was made.

> *¶11 #1 [moderate]:* Imperative in teaching mode: “See … and be amazed” commonly has a preceding “look/consider” to direct attention to correspondential measure.
> *¶11 #2 [moderate]:* Grammar and sense: “the one whom these waters are lesser than” (i.e., even these vast waters are less than that god’s power/measure). Keeps cosmological scaling.

**Assessment:** Most gaps are constrained by the internal physiological-cosmological sequence (mouth → descent → stomach → burning; outflow → rivers; concealment → final revelation/dissolution). Several places (¶6 #8; ¶7 #3–5; ¶9 #7) remain only moderately/tentatively recoverable because multiple synonymous images could fit (throats/gullets; bellies/jaws; rain/dew/mist; forms/figures), but the underlying correspondential logic—insatiable ingestion, punitive descent, and cosmic containment until the final dissolution—strongly determines the kind of wording required.

---

## Chapter 62: Concerning the Three Rocks

**¶2** Three great rocks, which exist, were appointed by the greatness. They were set in these outer worlds.

**¶3** So, the first rock is the [Pillar] of Glory, the Perfect Man, which was summoned [by the] glorious Ambassador. He set it in this zone; [and he] brought forth its base (?) from the earth to heaven. It bore up under the [en]tire uni[verse], and became first of all the weight-carriers in [it]s powers. In its firmness it has been set upright, and has made fast every [t]hing above and below.

> *¶3 #3 [moderate]:* The sequence is: summoned by the Ambassador → set in this zone → then an additional action introducing the ascent/extension “from the earth to heaven.” A simple connective preserves the impersonal, technical cadence of the substrate without adding later dialogic or devotional material.

**¶4** [The] second great and glorious rock is the new earth of light, which was set upon the earth of darkness. It has squashed the fastenings of the five storehouses of death. It has established firmness, with great firmness, over and beyond the earth of darkness. It has flattened, crushed and fixed the root of death.

**¶6** [These] are the three great rocks, they that have come forth in great confidence, in three great and honoured places. They were appointed and distributed in three great places of these [self]same worlds. They were made fast according to the will of the greatness.

**Assessment:** Most lacunae are trivial letter/word restorations tightly constrained by the surviving Coptic phrasing. Only ¶3 #3 required reconstruction beyond a fixed reading; the minimal connective “and he” best matches the substrate’s impersonal, structural exposition and the required action-sequence (summoned → set → extended/raised from earth to heaven).

---

## Chapter 63: Concerning Love

**¶2** ⟨p.156⟩Love is the Father of Greatness, who dwells in his glorious land. The entirety of the divine has been revealed [there]in! These two are a single living body, the Father and his love; because he has given himself for every thing, as he exists in his aeons. Indeed, due to this, they called the Father, the Lord of the totality, 'love'. For he gave the victory to his aeons and his limbs.

**¶4** Hate is the first death that welled up. It revealed the land of darkness. This one called 'hate', because it has ruined all its perdition's offspring; it sinned even against a power foreign to it, and ruined it. Due to this they shall call it 'hated'; for it has caused destruction, both wounding in flesh, and what is not its own.

**¶5** Today again it causes wounding and destruction in all flesh. It kills them all with diverse deaths; these that are displayed today in every thing, in every type. Even the Cross of Light, the living soul, likewise too it ruins and kills them; as it did at the start. Indeed, due to this have they called it 'hated', this one from the hated; the envy from the envy; the devil from the devil; the bad that came about from the bad.

**Assessment:** Only one lacuna, already tightly constrained by the preserved context (“revealed therein” referring to the Father’s glorious land). No alternative reading is demanded by the correspondential structure.

---

## Chapter 64: [Concerning Adam]

**¶2** Three great things that are distinct were revealed in Adam the first human. Indeed, due to this, he was found to be better and more outstanding than the conjoined powers that are in heaven and on earth.

**¶3** The first: The image of the exalted one was placed upon him. The creators and moulders of his body sealed him after the shining form of the image that had been displayed to them above. Indeed, due to this, the image of Adam was found to be surpassingly beautiful beyond any conjoined power above and below.

**¶4** The second thing: Adam was shaped in his structure through the light of the first-born of heaven and [earth; through] the five sons. Indeed, due to this, his structure was found to differ from the structure of even the other creations; [sin]ce the form of all creations is different. As for Adam, the formation of his soul fits over the correct distribution of the elements. Therefore, he has intelligence surpassing that of the other creations and beasts.

> *¶4 #1 [moderate]:* The construction requires a triad of agencies shaping Adam: the Light of the First-born of Heaven, (the First-born of) Earth, and the Five Sons. Restoring “earth; through” yields a smooth correspondential sequence: celestial light (heaven) → natural receptacle (earth) → differentiated powers (the five sons) as mediating forms.

**¶5** The third are the teachings and counsels and the seal of all the powers above and below. The creators, who set him in order, gathered them and sealed them in him. Adam and his consort Eve became a dwelling and home for the signs of the zodiac and the stars; and the months, the days and the years. For the seal of the entire universe is stamped upon Adam. Indeed, due to this, heaven and earth moved because of him; trouble and disorder arose on his account between they that are good and they that are evil. So, the good induce him to life because of their image and shape placed upon him; while the [wi]cked were drawing him to death, so that they would have power through him and they would conceive the kingdom, and through him humiliate the entire universe.

**¶6** Furthermore, understand that the rulers determined and formed Adam so that they would be masters through him and conceive the kingdom because of seven things.

**¶7** ⟨p.158⟩
The first: He became the enlightener in their creation; instead of the light of the image of the Ambassador who had displayed himself to them above. For they all desired the likeness of the image of the Ambassador. Their heart was drawn after it.

**¶8** The second: So that they would scoop up the light through him and restrain and obstruct it from its ascent to the heights.

**¶9** The third: So that because of the image of the Ambassador that is placed upon him the entirety of the divine that is above and below would be humiliated; on account of the likeness of the splendour, of the beauty of his superiority. For he is superior in his appearance to all creatures.

**¶10** The fourth: He is a seal-ring and a new impression, so that all births who will be begotten might be brought forth after his seal.

**¶11** The fifth: That might be displayed through him all the skil[ls] of the powers, they above and below, secret and manifest; and that they might determine through him the totality, and every thing be revealed in him.

**¶12** The sixth: That there would come to be a great protection for them through the image of the exalted one that was placed upon him; for as the heavenly would [fear] even rulers; and no evil thing would be done to them on account of Adam.

> *¶12 #1 [moderate]:* The point is protective power accruing to the rulers from the exalted image in Adam: heavenly beings would “fear” even rulers (i.e., be constrained by the awe/authority carried in the image), so hostile actions could not be executed against them on account of Adam. This matches the substrate’s impersonal mechanics of restraint by higher form, not moral exhortation.

**¶13** The seventh: That he might become chief and king and master over all creatures; and they might conceive the kingdom through him.

**Assessment:** Most gaps are trivial letter-restorations. The two substantive restorations are constrained by the cosmological sequence (Heaven/Earth/Five Sons) and by the logic of protection as constraint-by-awe: the exalted image in Adam induces fear/restraint even across ranks, preventing harm to the rulers ‘on account of Adam.’

---

## Chapter 65: Concerning the Sun.

**¶2** He says, it is the gate of life and the vessel of peace, to this great aeon of light [that is set above this world]. ⟨p.159⟩ However, since Satan knows that it is the gate of the souls' departure, he placed an exclusionary judgement in his law that no one worship it, saying: Whoever will worship it can die. He has otherwise called it: The light that will be nullified. He has hindered the souls from themselves turning their faces towards the light. He has caused them to deny the light of their being.

> *¶2 #1 [moderate]:* The correspondential sequence defines the sun as a liminal vessel: a ‘gate of life’ and ‘vessel of peace’ belonging to the great aeon of Light yet appearing to the world below. A short locative clause is required to mark its placement as an upper boundary between aeon and cosmos, preparing the later language of ‘departure of souls’ and Satan’s interdiction.

**¶3** Now, people are blind about everything! They have not understood the greatness of this great light-giver. They have denied the grace of this great light that shines upon them. They have not perceived its greatness and its divinity. Nor, also, have they understood the good things that it does for them, even today, as they are entrenched in their body; when it comes to this world and shines. Behold: How many are the charities that it does for mankind! Yet they, for their part, deny its graces, which they know not.

**¶4** Every day, would this great light-giver shine its light, it shall do seven good things for them. Shall they not see them?

**¶5** The first good thing that it does for them is its light, which it shines upon them. It opens the eyes of all mankind. They see underfoot as they walk by it. It takes away the blindness of the night, which made them blind with its darkness.

**¶6** The second: When (the sun) is come to the world, with its coming it shall bring calm and peace to the entire world. With its coming it shall also take away from them all the fear and trembling, which their heart is filled with the whole night; for it shall take away the darkness present by its light, and sweep it out. Also, this is how it extinguishes fear by its calm.

**¶7** The third is its vigil; because when (the sun) comes to the world all sleeping mankind shall arise and awake. And the watchmen too shall cease from watching. The guards who sit at the gates of camps, they break vigil at the watchposts, because of its light that has shone upon them in the world.

**¶8** ⟨p.160⟩ The fourth: It shall nourish, and give strength, taste and scent, to the trees and fruits and vegetables, and all the herbs, and the flowers and grasses, which are upon the entire earth.

**¶9** The fifth: When (the sun) shines in all the world, the evil reptile and the sharp-toothed beasts that are filled of wickedness, they shall flee to hiding in their dens.

**¶10** The sixth: When it shines its light, the wound of any person is eased from pain. Its light shall melt away the evil arts of every doer of wickedness.

**¶11** The seventh: (The sun) shall display and manifest to the world the sign of the splendour of the aeons of light, from which it has come out; it here in this world being their sign. It brings light to the whole of creation.

**¶12** Behold: How great is the foolishness that has befallen the sects of error! Not only that they have not perceived the mystery of the divinity of this great light-giver; but also, even all the benefits that it does for them. They forget them. They do not perceive them.

**¶13** Now, just as I have shown you the sun, which does seven good things in the world, hear also what I teach you about the night, for it has all wickednesses therein. Every occasion, would the night fall, it shall do seven evils in the world.

**¶14** The first wickedness that the night shall do in the world is the darkness, with which it shall fill the entire world; because when the sun sinks from the world and draws to it its rays, at that instant the shadow of the night shall spread out over the whole world. The eyes of the people fill with blackness. They neither see nor wonder with their eyes, because of the darkness of the night which shall fill the whole world.

**¶15** The second evil thing that the night shall do is its fear; because when its shadow looms forth, its fear and its trembling shall possess all of mankind. The entire world shall fill of the night.

**¶16** The third: When the night falls, all creatures take up revolt. They rebel in their heart, to do wickedness and perdition.

**¶17** The fourth: The heaviness of sleep shall lay upon them, and they fall asleep; they slumber and doze, and they are like these corpses in the night.

**¶18** The fifth is its ugliness; again, when the night would fall, the form of the people shall be hidden in the darkness and the ugliness of the night. The beauty and the form of men and women shall not be displayed before one another, because of its darkness and its ugliness, which it shall spread out over all their forms.

**¶19** The sixth occasion is when the night would fall on the world. At that instant evil people shall come out to do wickedness; the adulterers, and the robbers and poisoners. The evil beasts shall come out, and all the reptiles that are filled with wickedness, from their dens. And they stalk in the night. Also, the demons and fiends find power to do wickedness in the night. Again, the blows and buffets and wounds and discharges shall torment people in the night; because in it the whole of evil shall find strength.

**¶20** The seventh: The night shall display the sign of the darkness, its father. It has arisen out of its essence; because this night that came out of this first darkness has been displayed in the world. Note this night, the shadow of this first darkness, the one that is entwined and bound in all things above and below.

**¶21** Behold, I have taught you about these seven wickednesses of the black night; the ones that it shall do daily in the world, in the night. The sects have not seen this black night: what it is; nor from whence is its essence. That all these evils find strength in it!

**¶22** There are five archetypes occur in the sun, it making them [manifest] as it finds the world.

> *¶22 #1 [strong]:* The syntax requires a verbal complement: ‘There are five archetypes occur in the sun, it making them ___ as it finds the world.’ In the oldest cosmological register the sun does not ‘teach’ but ‘shows/displays/manifests’ forms; this matches the repeated ‘display/manifest’ used for both sun and night as signs of their essences.

**¶23** ⟨p.162⟩ The first is its light; for it illumines by its light the world, and all the creatures who exist in it.

**¶24** The second is its beauty; because when it shines, it shall flood beauty and loveliness upon all creatures and everything.

**¶25** The third is its peace; for, again, when the sun would shine on the world, all people shall receive affection through it, and give peace to one another.

**¶26** The fourth is the life of the living soul, which it shall release from all the bonds and fetters of heaven and earth.

**¶27** The fifth: It gives a strength to the elements; and also it gives a scent and a taste to the entire Cross of the Light.

**¶28** So, you yourselves see that its light illumines more than all lights that occur in the world. Also, its beauty is more beautiful than all the beauties of mankind. Again, its peace is more surpassing than all the powers and guards who exist in the world. Its releasing action, by which it releases the living soul, is a full day ahead of all releasing actions! The strength that it gives to its limbs is a great strength, being mightier than all strengths!

**¶29** Once again, there are another three archetypes made apparent by the sun, in respect of the mystery of the first greatness.

**¶30** The first is the filling up of the disk of its ship; because its ship has filled up every season, and shall not wane at all, the way that the ship of the moon wanes. This continuous filling, by which it is filled, displays the mystery of the Father, the great greatness; from whom all the powers and the gods have come forth. He shall never wane, nor shall lack ever exist in him!

**¶31** The second: (The sun's) light surpasses the light of all the stars, and all the lights that occur in the universe; corresponding to the mystery of the light of the Father; for his light surpasses and is greater than the light of his aeons.

**¶32** ⟨p.163⟩ The third: This sun is very high above all else. It surpasses the height of all the mountains and hills that exist upon the entire earth, corresponding to the mystery of the Father of Greatness; for he is high above all the household of his light-earth. Also, because the Father himself is high above all the heights and the mountains of his light-earth. He is 'filled up' in his image [all] the time and every hour from eternity to eternity!

**¶33** [Behold]ld the two mysteries I have taught you, O my beloved ones! The mystery of the night and that of the day, which daily come forth in the world. Now, the mystery of the light and the mystery of the darkness are displayed daily in the created order. The sects have not seen them in their error!

> *¶33 #1 [strong]:* Restores the vocative demonstrative opening that frames the teaching as a disclosure of correspondential ‘mysteries’ in day/night.

**¶34** The mystery of the first great light is the sun, which is this second light-giver, the one that comes daily to the world. It displays all the marks of its graces, displaying and signifying on its own behalf that it is good, out of the essence of the good. It came from the good Father and appeared in this world.

**¶35** In contrast, this night that is made of fear exists after the mystery of the darkness. Behold too the mystery of its essence! It displays it in the world in all these wickednesses. It displays this mystery daily in the world, but the sects know it not in their error. They do not distinguish the mystery of the light from that of the darkness.

**¶37** You are the children of the day and the children of the light. However, you <[who bear] the character of the darkness> are the children of the night and the children of the darkness.

> *¶37 #1 [moderate]:* The sentence contrasts two genera by correspondence: those who are ‘children of day/light’ versus those who ‘bear’ (i.e., carry/embody in life) the character of Darkness. A participial/relative clause is demanded before ‘the character of the darkness’ to mark reception and embodiment rather than moral accusation, consistent with the impersonal substrate.

**¶38** ⟨p.164⟩ Yet, blessed are you, my beloved ones! Blessed are your souls, for you have known the mystery of the day and that of the night. You have understood, that the day exists after the mystery of the light, but the night after the mystery of the darkness. They have not arisen from one another. Blessed is he who can know this mystery and distinguish these two essences, that of the light and that of the darkness, the ones that have not arisen from one another; so that he can inherit life eternal!

**Assessment:** Most gaps are tightly constrained by repeated formulae (‘display/manifest’, ‘behold’) and by the correspondential structure (sun/night as signs of their prior essences). ¶2 and ¶37 allow more than one equivalent phrasing, but the required function (upper limen of the light-aeon; embodiment of the darkness-character) narrows the restorations to a small semantic range.

---

## Chapter 66: Concerning the Ambassador.

**¶2** Two great mysteries are apparent in this shining sun, at its rising and setting. Now, at the moment when it rises and is visible in the universe, all creatures of flesh shall lift up their heads fr[om their sleep] towards the splendour of the sun, and open their doors. While at evening time, when the sun sinks from the universe, all people from every place and even all beasts shall go in to their dark hiding places. The population of the entire universe shuts their doors and dark night overcomes them. They go inside and stay within the dark night in their hiding places.

**¶3** Very great is this mystery revealed by the sun! For when the sun rises and [be]come[s] visible at the beginning of the day all people open their doors, and all of humanity and every creature emerges onto the ear[th] to destroy this Light Cross that is established i[n] silence.

**¶4** This pertains to the mystery of the First Man, because when he came out from the grea[t]ness he shone forth over all the children of darkness. At that mome[nt] all the children of darkness came forth from their storehouses, its powers and its armies, abandoning their caves and their black abyss. They destroyed the [outer] body of his five sons, who were swathed over hi[s b]o[dy, in t]hat first struggle. ⟨p.165⟩

> *¶4 #3 [moderate]:* Kept the transmitted restoration. In the substrate logic the assault of Darkness first falls upon the First Man’s manifested covering/form (the exterior body as presented in ultimates), i.e., the outer formation of the five sons ‘swathed’ over him for combat; this fits the correspondence of first contact occurring at the level of effects/ultimates.

**¶5** Again, when the sun sinks from the universe and sets, and all people go in to their hiding places and houses and conceal themselves; this also pertains to the mystery of the end, as it presages the consummation of the universe. For, when all the light will be purified and redeemed i[n] the universe at the last, the collector of all things, the Last Statue, will gather in and sculpt it/self. It is the last hour of the day, the time when the Last Statue will go up to the aeo[n of] light. The enemy too, death, will go in to bondage; to the prison of the souls of the de[ni]iers and blasphemers who loved the darkness. They will go in with it to bondage and the dark night overcomes th[em]; and thenceforth this name 'light' will not shine upon them.

**¶6** Behold the two mysteries made visible daily in the sun, which is this splendrous light-giver.

**Assessment:** All lacunae here are either trivial letter/word continuations or already tightly constrained by fixed Manichaean-cosmological phrasing preserved in Coptic translation. Only ¶4 #3 required a correspondential check; the received ‘outer’ fits the substrate’s inside→outside sequence (conflict first in ultimates), so no change was needed.

---

## Chapter 67: Concerning the Light-Giver.

---

## Chapter 68: Concerning Fire.

**¶1** This fire, which burns and is apparent today in the world, it does five works.

**¶2** The first work that it does is this: It incinerates and burns wood, and everything that shall be placed upon it.

**¶3** The second: It separates four aspects of the wood, which are these: fire and light, ash and smoke.

**¶4** The third: It gives a taste and an {aroma} to whatever will be cooked on it. It also gives heat and cheer to whoever will be close to it.

**¶5** The fourth work that [it] does: All craftsmen shall work the utensils that they will fabricate in it.

**¶6** The fifth: It shall display light, and the people see by [it].

**Assessment:** Both lacunae are trivial pronominal restorations tightly constrained by the parallel syntax: each refers back to “this fire” as the instrumental means (‘in it’, ‘by it’). No alternative reading fits the correspondential structure of fire as the mediating agent of transformation and illumination.

---

## Chapter 69: Concerning the Twelve Signs of the Zodiac and the Five Stars

**¶2** Whether these twelve signs of the zodiac that are fixed on the sphere, or these five stars that circle upon them; those and these are rulers, all of them, according to their essence. They are all enemies and rivals to each other ⟨one oppresses another, they threaten each other⟩. They plunder one another by the action of the great craftsman who has formed all the worlds. He has gathered [them] and bound them on the sphere above, which circles constantly. Now, while it circles and tumbles them, so they shall do everything that they desire, above and below, according to their pleasure. Nevertheless, a guardian is over them, a debt-collector above them; compelling, extorting, and taking what is theirs away from them!

**¶3** Now, this is how it can rightly be understood. They were [s]eized from the five worlds of Darkness, and bound on the sphere. Two zodiacal signs were taken per world. Gemini and Sagittarius belong to the world of smoke, which is the mind. In contrast, Aries and Leo belong to the world of fire. Taurus, Aquarius and Libra belong to the world of wind. Cancer and Virgo and [P]isces belong to the world of water. Capricorn and Scorpio belong to the world of Darkness. These are the twelve rulers of depravity, the ones that wicked[ness] shall not [escape] For they cause all the evil and [harm in the world]rld, whether in tree or in flesh.

> *¶3 #4 [moderate]:* The clause defines the zodiacal rulers as enduring sources of depravity: wickedness does not “escape” them; rather, it is held within their jurisdiction and flows out through them into effects. This keeps the impersonal, structural register (rule/jurisdiction), not a moral exhortation.
> *¶3 #5 [moderate]:* Parallelism demands a second comprehensive term after “all the evil”: i.e., evil and its effects/afflictions. “Harm in the world” matches the correspondential line immediately following (“whether in tree or in flesh”), i.e., effects in all natural forms.

**¶4** ⟨p.168⟩ And understand also about these five stars, the leaders: from where were they formed? The star of Zeus (Jupiter) was generated from the world of smoke, which is the mind. In contrast, Aphrodite (Venus) came about from the world of fire. And then Ares (Mars) belongs to the world of wind. Hermes (Mercury) belongs to the world of water. Cronos (Saturn) belongs to the world [of] Darkness.

**¶5** And also, the two ascendants are counted to fire and desire, the dry and the moist. They are the father and the mother of all these.

**¶6** And also, the sun and the moon, which are counted in with them as a rule ⟨[counte]d to their kind, but why did they count them in with them?⟩ so that they would reign over them. You will discover they subjugate them at every moment.

**¶7** These seven we have named, the five stars and the two ascendants: They are the doers of wickedness that do every wicked and bad act in every land, the whole of it, above and below, in every creature, in the dry and the moist, in tree and flesh.

**¶8** Once more I reveal to you about these signs of the zodiac: [They] are distributed, appointed on four sides, three per angle in these four places. And they are fixed to this revolving sphere. Aries, Leo and Sagittarius, they three belong to a single side. In contrast, Taurus, Capricorn and Virgo, these other three belong to another side. And then, Gemini, Libra and Aquarius belong to another side. Scorpio, Pisces and Cancer belong to another corner. Now, they are placed like this, appointed to these four parts, and distributed on the sphere.

**¶9** So, when the side of Aries, Leo and Sagittarius will be plundered by the guardian who is over it, who extorts from it and the leaders who move upon it; at that instant shall affliction strike all the four-footed creatures below. However, when [the side] of Taurus, Virgo and Capricorn will be plundered; [affliction] shall ⟨p.169⟩ at once befall the herbs, together with the vegetables and all the fruits of the trees. Yet again, when the side of Scorpio, Pisces and Cancer will be plundered; scarcity shall befall the waters upon the earth, and drought be from place to place. Conversely, should the side of Gemini, Libra and Aquarius be plundered; deformity and stuntedness shall befall the form of mankind from place to place.

**¶10** Behold now, I have explained to you about these twelve signs of the zodiac: They were fashioned from the five worlds of Darkness, and bound on the sphere.

**¶11** Also, I have taught you about the other five, the stars: They too came about from the five worlds of the land of Darkness.

**¶12** I have recounted to you also about the [t]wo ascendants: They are set to the mystery of fire and desire. They are the dry and the moist, the father and the mother.

**¶13** Again, I have revealed to you about the sun and the moon: They are strangers to them. Still, because of the constraint of the arrangement, in that the thing receives light, and as they belong and plunder them; because of this they were counted in with them in relation to the calculation of the number. For, the sun and the moon are from out of the greatness, not belonging to the stars and the signs of the zodiac.

**Assessment:** Most lacunae are trivial letter/word restorations fixed by grammar and the zodiacal list. The two substantial gaps in ¶3 are constrained by the cosmological logic: the rulers are jurisdictions binding wickedness, and their operation produces evil and its general effects throughout nature (tree/flesh). Exact phrasing remains somewhat elastic, but the restored sense is tight.

---

## Chapter 70: Concerning the Body: It was constructed after the Pattern of the Universe.

**¶2** This whole universe, above and below, reflects the pattern of the human body; as the formation of this body of flesh accords to the pattern of the universe.

**¶3** Its head is like the first-fruits of the five garments. And, from its neck down to the site of its heart, it resembles the pattern of the ten firmaments. And again, the heart accords to the wheel of the rotating sphere. And, from its heart down to its intestines (?), is like this atmosphere that extends from the sphere down to the earth. The [male] part of the body corresponds to this great earth. And also, from its intestines (?) down to its loins is like the three earths. And also, its shinbones to the pattern of the space in which the Porter stands. Its footsoles to the great earth upon which the Porter is stood; and the four fastenings that are under his feet. Its liver to the vessel of fire. Its flesh to the vessel of darkness. Its blood to the vessel of water.

> *¶3 #1 [strong]:* The body-to-cosmos mapping here requires the generative/active side corresponding to the productive earth below (the great earth as recipient/field). The transmitted editor fill fits the correspondential pairing (male = earth’s outward/productive plane in ultimates).

**¶4** Now, this is how the small body corresponds to the macro-cosmos in its firmaments, in its orderings, in its mountains, its walls and its vessels, as I have made clear to you!

**¶5** Once more, I reveal to you: There exist five great camps in this great outer zone, of which the five sons of the Living Spirit are masters.

**¶6** So, in the first watch-station above, of which the Keeper of Splendour is master, on the battlement of all the works, is found his authority over the three heavens. Then the ones below him [are appointed under the authority of] the great King of Honour.

> *¶6 #1 [moderate]:* The syntax demands a mediating clause: after the Keeper of Splendour’s authority over the three heavens, the next level ‘below him’ must be described as subordinated, before naming the great King of Honour. Old substrate prefers structural/administrative language (‘appointed under authority’) rather than narrative or devotional phrasing.

**¶7** The second watch, of which the great King of Honour is master, his own authority (extends) over the seven firmaments beneath him.

**¶8** The third watch, of which the Adamant [is master; his authority] reigns from the firmament down below to the [earth]. [And he] invigorates, according to his authority, the sphere and the worlds of the atmosphere; and the other four worlds that are appointed on this earth.

> *¶8 #1 [strong]:* Restores the parallel formula used in ¶7–10: ‘watch, of which X is master, his authority…’.
> *¶8 #3 [strong]:* Required to resume the subject (the Adamant) and maintain the cadence of successive governing actions (‘his authority… And he invigorates…’).

**¶9** ⟨p.171⟩
The fourth watch, over which the King of Glory has power, is the three wheels. And his authority lies over the three earths upon the head of the Porter.

**¶10** The fifth watch is the watch over which the Porter is authoritative. Again, he is master, according to his authority, over this great earth he stands upon, and the four fastenings under his feet.

**¶11** Affliction has come about in these five watches from time to time.

**¶12** So, in the watch of the Keeper of Splendour, sin desired to spurt up towards the image of the Ambassador. Yet, they barred it from that place, and it fell back down in shame.

**¶13** Again, an earthquake and treachery occurred in the watch of the great King of Honour, which the watchers who had arisen at the time and had [stood firm against it] they who were sent came down, until they were humbled.

> *¶13 #1 [tentative]:* The clause describes the watchers’ response to ‘earthquake and treachery’: correspondentially, a stabilizing resistance in the station of Honour. This also prepares the descent/humbling of those ‘who were sent’ (the corrective emissaries) by contrasting their action with the watchers’ stance.

**¶14** Also, in the watch of the Adamant, the abortions came to the earth and fashioned [the] figure of flesh.

**¶15** Again, in the watch of the King of Glory, a commotion happened in these three earths above the Porter. The pathway was obstructed for the ascent of these three wheels; that of wind, that of water, and that of fire.

**¶16** Also, in the watch of the Porter, the fastenings beneath were laid bare and strained their chains. A great earthquake befell that place.

**¶23** Also, the teaching of wisdom, which circulates in that body: to the likeness of the Virgin of Light, who goes up and comes down in this zone, above and below.

**¶24** Also, love and joy, faith and truth, in which the person should live: they are to the likeness of these two light ships. For, the living soul should go up in them and become free through them; and it ascends from the abysses below and arrives at the heights above.

**¶25** Once more, understand this other (truth): There exist four worlds in this body of the flesh, and there exist countless times seven rulers in the body of the four worlds!

**¶26** So, the first world is from its neck up; and also the seven rulers who are in this upper world are these: the two organs of sight, and the two of hearing, and the two of smell. The other one is the mouth, which is the organ of taste.

**¶27** The second world is from its neck down to its entire upper torso. And also, the seven rulers who are in this second world are: its two arms that accord to the pattern of its two ears, which occur in its first world above; and its two breasts that are to the pattern of its organs of smell; and the two eyes of its heart; and the gullet of the stomach between the two breasts, which in the chest is the pattern of the mouth.

**¶28** ⟨p.173⟩
Once more, there exist seven rulers in the third world of the body, which are these: the fat, the lung, the spleen, the liver, the gall, and its two kidneys.

**¶29** Also, the fourth world below. There are another seven there of this same kind: its two buttocks (?), and its two testicles, and its two loins; and the member out of which the seed comes, that begets all the forms.

**¶31** Aries and Taurus, Gemini, Cancer, Leo, Virgo, Libra, Scorpio, Sagittarius, Capricorn, Aquarius and Pisces; these are the twelve signs of the zodiac that occur in the worlds above, on the sphere. They are interlocked and bound on this turning wheel, the one in which they are [set in their places], fixed, and planted. And, regarding all these twelve signs of the zodiac, this is how they were appointed and made leaders [as rulers] on the sphere beneath the heaven that is the wheel of the stars. Now, they are appointed like this, they are thus, so that you can find the head of [as guardians] and you find the tail too [in the head of Aries and in the tail of Pisces]

> *¶31 #1 [moderate]:* The zodiacal signs are described as bound to the turning wheel; the natural correspondential action is fixing/placing them in their appointed stations on the sphere.
> *¶31 #2 [moderate]:* Immediately followed by ‘fixed, and planted’: the threefold fixation language commonly accompanies appointment to rule/order in the oldest cosmological register.
> *¶31 #3 [tentative]:* After ‘appointed and made leaders’, the substrate typically adds a functional title: guarding/keeping the ordered courses on the sphere (not moral exhortation).
> *¶31 #4 [moderate]:* The passage’s intent is to identify the sequence’s beginning and end (‘find the head… and the tail too…’). In zodiacal order, Aries is the head (first) and Pisces the tail (last).

**¶32** Again, they occur like this, these that we have recited, one after another in this body. They are counted by order and number from the head to the feet. Its head is Aries. Its neck and its shoulders are Taurus. Its two arms Gemini. Its upper torso is Cancer. Its stomach is Leo. Its belly is Virgo. The vertical spine and its intestines (?) are Libra. Its genitals Scorpio. Its loins are Sagittarius. Its knees are Capricorn. Its shinbones are Aquarius. The soles of its feet are Pisces.

**¶33** Behold, these are also distributed one after another. They exist in this body, as if turned to the side and bent to the pattern of the twelve signs of the zodiac. They too are thus, arranged one against another, head to tail, as they occur on the wheel [on which they go] up.

> *¶33 #1 [moderate]:* The sentence compares the body-bent arrangement with how the signs occur ‘on the wheel … up’; the missing words must describe their motion/course upon the wheel.

**¶34** Also, they are counted by order and number of the body and all its members. So, we have proclaimed that these are turned to the side, bent, and spread out; because from its head down to its hip shall count six to its left, and another six to its right.

**¶35** ⟨p.174⟩
The six that are down its right are these that I will recount: the right temple is Aries; its right shoulder is Taurus; its right arm is Gemini; its right rib-cage is Cancer; its stomach is Leo; the right side of its reproductive organs are Virgo.

**¶36** Conversely, the other six about which we have spoken, which are to its left, in contrast they come from its hip to its head: its left [hip] is Libra; its left rib-cage is Scorpio; its left breast and left kidney is Sagittarius; its [left] elbow is Capricorn; its left shoulder is Aquarius; its left temple is Pisces. This is how the creator of the body himself has appointed them. He has [set] them in order and arranged them, one against its companion, head to tail.

> *¶36 #1 [strong]:* The left-side count in ¶36 is said to run ‘from its hip to its head’; thus the first left member corresponding to Libra should be the left hip.

**¶38** Listen to this other lesson that I will proclaim to you. Understand that there exist many powers in this body. They are the house-dwellers who are made the leaders in it. There are eight hundred and forty times ten thousand rulers made chiefs in the human body! They are distributed and set firm, quartered according to house. The count and number of their houses is two hundred and ten times ten thousand.

**¶39** When all these rulers come creeping and moving within the body, they will meet one another; and they shall beset and destroy one another, and [grind and consume the limbs] in them there. When [until]] they shall erupt from the body of the person who [will] die; and make putrid boils and sores and burning wounds in the body. Either they might have made him sick within; or else they might come forth upon his outer side. Those wounds shall first be squeezed and discharge their liquid, and empty out their pus, until they are squeezed [again and again] and the suppuration forced out from the wound. Afterwards, their scars shall close over; and they are healed and obtain relief from the places that came out upon them.

> *¶39 #1 [tentative]:* After ‘beset and destroy one another’, the next effect in the body should be interior wasting before eruption outward; this matches the correspondential movement from internal conflict to external manifestation (sores/boils).
> *¶39 #2 [moderate]:* The bracket shows traces consistent with a Coptic particle like ‘until’; the sense requires a temporal threshold leading to eruption from the dying body.
> *¶39 #4 [tentative]:* After ‘until they are squeezed … and the suppuration forced out’, the text expects an iterative adverbial phrase describing repeated pressing/expressing until emptied.

**Assessment:** Most lacunae are formulaic and restore securely (titles, parallel governance clauses, body/zodiac correspondences). The harder points are the narrative mechanics in ¶13 and the pathological sequence in ¶39, where multiple readings could fit; restorations there follow the inside→outside correspondential logic (stabilization vs. treachery; internal conflict → external eruption → repeated discharge → healing) but remain tentative in exact wording.
