> *¶3 #3 [strong]:* The logic is: when separation comes, none can take the house’s valuables with him. A negation is required to block possession/appropriation.
> *¶3 #4 [moderate]:* Matches the earlier “house for rent” and keeps the correspondence: worldly supports are hired/temporary, not owned. “rental house” also prepares the borrowed-vessels image.

**¶8** When, however, they come forth from their body, they travel on their way and pass by in the place above, and go in to the life. They shall be purified in the heavens, and they are plucked just as a fruit that ripens is plucked from the tree. In just the same way, this alms-offering that passes over to the elect is given likeness in many forms. It is purified and goes in to the land of the living. Similarly, the souls of the catechumens who shall not enter (another) body resemble (the alms).

**¶13** Every person who has received the hope and the faith and has separated the light from the darkness, and has perceived the mysteries of the living soul, he has received the right hand of peace from the Light Mind

**¶20** As for the rest, he shall be questioned about a single part; and receive blows for (those sins) and retribution. Afterwards he is purified, whether indeed above or below. He shall be purified according to the worth of his deeds, and cleansed and washed and adorned. Afterwards, he is sculpted a light image; and [he glides]es up and reaches the land of rest, so that the place where his heart is, his treasure also will be there.

> *¶20 #1 [strong]:* Retain the editor’s restoration: the clause needs a smooth, non-effortful ascent after purification and formation of the “light image,” expressing ascent by affinity/weightlessness rather than struggle.

**¶24** the first light, the one that brings understanding through the Mind and [the] riches of its knowledge.

**Assessment:** Most gaps are tightly constrained by the ‘lodging/rent/borrowed vessels’ correspondence: worldly relations and goods are temporary instruments, returned to their owner. Exact phrasing of the travel-separation clause is less recoverable, but the required logical joints (knowledge of separation, negation of taking, identification of the house as rented) are clear.

---

## Chapter 92: The Apostle is asked: Why when you drew every thing in the Picture (- Book), did you not draw the Purification of the Catechumens who shall be cleansed by Transmigration?

---

## Chapter 93: A Catechumen asked the Apostle: When I would give an Offering to the Saints, shall I inflict a Wound on the Alms?

**¶4** For this matter is like to two people, if a personal enemy and an opponent of theirs will hit one first and make a sore and a wound on his body. Afterwards he smites him again and [injures him] and he hits over his blows and [heap blow upon blow] blows that have made sores on him and [the] ⟨p.237⟩ strikes him these two times [he] accuses his enemy who has struck him. And this judge questions him to his face about the [and] of the sores that he has left as marks on his body. And they [number] on him as if he had split his head. And he is led to judgement and sentence, and is punished before the judge of the world. He makes retribution for a sore in the sore place, and adds to him the wound that he had left on this person who had not yet sinned against him. He shall not omit the retaliation of the opponent, as he is judged [is] according to enmity.

> *¶4 #1 [moderate]:* The sequence is: strike → make sore/wound → strike again and do further harm. The next clause speaks of blows over blows; a verb of additional injury fits the forensic comparison.
> *¶4 #2 [moderate]:* Corresponds to the following phrase 'and he hits over his blows': the idiom is iterative violence (adding strokes), preparing for the judge inspecting accumulated marks.
> *¶4 #3 [moderate]:* Introduces the set of blows already mentioned: 'the blows that have made sores'.
> *¶4 #4 [moderate]:* Subject resumes: the opponent 'strikes him these two times'.
> *¶4 #5 [moderate]:* Connects the double striking to the subsequent legal action (going to the judge).
> *¶4 #6 [moderate]:* The judge questions about the measurable evidence: the count/measure of sores left as marks. Fits correspondentially: judgment in ultimates is by 'marks' and 'measure'.
> *¶4 #7 [moderate]:* Copula in 'as he is judged ... according to enmity'.

**¶5** After the end, this enemy is condemned for the retaliation [that he did not] sin against him. Afterwards, the other one is sick [and seeks a cure] the one whom this enemy has struck, and a wound comes out on him [from within] and he becomes a sick person by cause of his illness, and he [endures it] He calls the doctor and shows him his wound. However, that doctor has no [remedy] nor [charm] and he takes the knife to him and cuts this wound. Nevertheless, when he cuts it the blood shall pour out of his wound. That person shall be pained, but he accepts those and these things. This doctor has not retaliated nor was harm imposed on him; because he did this thing to him for healing his wound. All that he did to him was done for the good; and not directly for evil.

> *¶5 #1 [moderate]:* The condemned enemy is judged for retaliation, specifically for retaliating though no prior sin was against him (stated at end of ¶4).
> *¶5 #2 [moderate]:* Transitions from legal punishment to the other case: sickness leading to medical treatment.
> *¶5 #3 [moderate]:* The wound 'comes out' as a manifestation; correspondentially illness emerges from an interior cause, contrasting with externally inflicted wounds.
> *¶5 #4 [moderate]:* He becomes sick by illness and '...'—a verb of undergoing/bearing fits before calling the doctor.
> *¶5 #5 [moderate]:* Doctor has no '...' nor '...': standard pairing is remedy/means (drug) and incantation or charm. In the oldest technical register: practical means.
> *¶5 #6 [tentative]:* Contrasts with knife/surgery: no charm/spell, but incision. Keeps pre-institutional, non-devotional tone while reflecting common ancient medical pairings.

**¶6** After the completion, he cuts this wound and lets blood from it and heals it. He shall even make [to him] gifts and compliments. That illness and the healing become a pride for this doctor, and he is given honour.

> *¶6 #1 [strong]:* After healing, the patient rewards the physician: 'He shall even make ... gifts and compliments'—the indirect object is the doctor.

**¶7** This is also how this matter of [the healing] before this living soul. [For] At the time ⟨p.238⟩ [when it is cut] it [is not as though] you have struck [it,] it is well and [afterwards] ease shall exist for it. [great] A glory comes about for this [doctor] healing [from this] it exists [of the] it is painful [at first]

> *¶7 #1 [moderate]:* The whole exemplum is about distinguishing retaliation (evil) from corrective cutting (healing). This line applies it to the soul: 'matter of healing before this living soul'.
> *¶7 #2 [moderate]:* Introduces the application/explanation after the analogies.
> *¶7 #3 [moderate]:* Matches the medical figure: the soul is 'cut' (disciplined/purged) at a certain time; prepares for 'it is painful' later.
> *¶7 #4 [moderate]:* Needed to negate the appearance of hostile striking: the corrective blow is not enemy-violence.
> *¶7 #5 [moderate]:* Object of 'you have struck ...' referring back to the living soul.
> *¶7 #6 [moderate]:* Sequence: pain first, then well-being/ease. Fits 'it is well and ... ease shall exist'.
> *¶7 #7 [tentative]:* Intensifies 'ease shall exist for it' and aligns with the later 'glory' and 'honour' motif from the doctor analogy.
> *¶7 #8 [moderate]:* Directly parallels ¶6: glory comes about for the physician/healer; in application, the agent of corrective healing receives honour.
> *¶7 #9 [moderate]:* Glory comes about 'for this doctor/healing'—source/occasion marker.
> *¶7 #10 [moderate]:* Bridges 'healing' to its object: healing of the wound/illness of the soul.
> *¶7 #11 [moderate]:* Resolves the final contrast: 'it exists ... it is painful ...'—pain precedes the beneficial state, like incision before cure.

**¶8** Also, [this] is the other person [who] struck the comrade of the [enemy] sick with his [living soul] he struck in [cruelty] he being cruel according to [enmity] living soul [his wrath] upon it [and lays sin] that [the wounds] accusation of [enmity]

> *¶8 #3 [moderate]:* Sets the opposite case: not the healer but the hostile striker, matching ¶4-5.
> *¶8 #4 [tentative]:* The comrade of the 'living soul' (its companion/kin) is what is struck in the retaliatory/hostile mode; keeps to the chapter’s key term.
> *¶8 #5 [moderate]:* He struck the sick with his '...'—correspondentially the opposite of healing is cruelty/violence, matching 'he being cruel'.
> *¶8 #6 [moderate]:* He struck 'in ...'—the motive-force in ¶4 is enmity; the judge sentence is 'according to enmity'.
> *¶8 #7 [tentative]:* He is cruel according to something; in the oldest register this is wrath/heat of the lower powers, opposed to ordered correction.
> *¶8 #8 [tentative]:* Violent striking upon the soul produces guilt/accusation; the text ends with 'accusation of ...'.
> *¶8 #9 [moderate]:* Accusation is grounded in the marks/wounds, paralleling ¶4 where the judge inspects the marks as evidence.
> *¶8 #10 [moderate]:* Closes the antithesis: accusation belongs to enmity (retaliation), not to healing (benefit).

**Assessment:** Most gaps can be constrained by the chapter’s tight forensic/medical antithesis: hostile retaliation (enmity, accusation, punishment) versus corrective cutting (pain → healing → honour). Exact particles and some nouns (e.g., ‘charm’, ‘wrath’) remain uncertain because the surviving Coptic idiom is not visible; these are restored by correspondential parallelism rather than philology.

---

## Chapter 94: Concerning the Purification of these Four Elements [that have been place]d in the Flesh.

**¶2** The four elements [are those that are] purified. From the beginning they are holy ones, living ones, and enlighteners. However, when they undertood [the mingling with the enemy] in that first struggle, the waste was [mixed] in with them, and the Darkness of the enemy was mixed with them. And when they were come into the body, stripping off these polluted garments, they set them in the flesh. Their pollutions, which they have stripped off, shall be displayed in a [visible form] and become a danger to the body.

> *¶2 #1 [moderate]:* Balances the impersonal, definitional style of the substrate: the elements themselves are the purifying agents/subjects, not merely objects of purification.
> *¶2 #2 [moderate]:* The correspondential pivot is the first crisis: perception/consent to mixture. The oldest layer typically speaks of “mingling” (mixture) as the operative event that introduces Darkness into the elements’ vessels.
> *¶2 #4 [moderate]:* What is stripped off becomes manifest in an outward plane and then threatens the body; “displayed in a visible form” matches correspondential logic (pollution becomes externalized in ultimates).

**¶3** The discharge of this pollution, that [fir]e shall disgorge and place in the body, is [blo]od. The discharge of the blood is anger. The discharge of anger is the humours.

**¶4** ⟨p.240⟩
Also, the emissions that the waters discharge from them are lust. The discharge of lust is bitterness. The emission of bitterness is f[e]ver.

**¶5** The emission that light shall disgorge is flesh. The emission of flesh is gloom. [The] emission of gloom is obstinacy.

**¶6** The emissions that the tree shall disgorge, they are the voices of the winds of shame. The emission of winds of shame [is trembling]

> *¶6 #1 [tentative]:* After “voices of the winds of shame,” the next discharge is the bodily/spiritual effect of shame: an agitated vibration in ultimates. “Trembling” fits the emission-chain pattern (substance → affect → bodily symptom).

**¶7** The emission of the [tear] is the [crown] of these twelve emissions.

> *¶7 #2 [tentative]:* The tear (moisture of grief/shame) culminates the chain as a “crown,” i.e., the completion/encirclement of the twelve emissions into a single total effect. This is a substrate way of marking the last as the summation of the series.

**¶8** [By the management] these four elements shall [purify] what are gathered in are [f]ound by the management of this soul food [that] enters the body. When they enter the body, they are cleansed and purified and established [i]n their living image, which is the new man. They shall live [in their aeons] and receive the Light Mind and be purified in their image; and they come forth, being cleansed and holy. They attain their first rest.

> *¶8 #1 [moderate]:* Matches the technical, impersonal register already present (“management of this soul food”), introducing the doctrine of ordered administration rather than exhortation.
> *¶8 #2 [moderate]:* The four elements are said earlier to be purifiers; here they “shall purify” what is gathered inside. Keeps the inside→outside correspondential motion (gathered particles are worked upon).
> *¶8 #6 [moderate]:* After establishment in the living image (new man), the purified elements ‘live’ in their proper discrete degrees/regions. “Aeons” preserves the cosmological/topological idiom of the oldest layer (proper realms), without later pastoral coloring.

**Assessment:** Most gaps are recoverable by the emission-chain logic (each element disgorges a corresponding pollution leading to a bodily/affective ultimate) and by the administrative/purificatory role of the four elements. Two restorations remain tentative: ¶6’s final emission (the bodily effect of shame) and ¶7’s summarizing term for the twelfth (rendered as “crown” to mark completion of the series).

---

## Chapter 95: The Apostle asks his Disciples: What is Cloud?

**¶2** Happen you know that it shall be released from five places and ascend over this great earth and [to the atmosphere between heaven and earth] and it is revealed and seen in the atmosphere between.

> *¶2 #1 [moderate]:* The ascent is described as over the earth and then visible ‘in the atmosphere between’; the oldest-layer cosmology regularly names this as the mid-region between heaven and earth.

**¶3** So, the cloud shall ascend from fire to the heavens towards the likeness of the Virgin of Light that she shall display to it. Its sign is the flashes that occur with lightning storms [and in]ts exchange [shall be in it, between the lives and the rulers]. And they shall be purified by her towards [the place of her purification] the light that she shall reveal [to them from it]. The rulers shall be released by the lightning storms and they are freed and sent.

> *¶3 #1 [moderate]:* Lightning-flash sign leads into a statement about the cloud’s internal ‘exchange’/separation; the preserved tail ‘... i’ fits ‘and in’.
> *¶3 #2 [tentative]:* The cloud’s function is double: purification of ‘lives’ (light-souls) and stripping/release of ‘rulers’. The ‘exchange’ is the separation within the cloud between these two populations.
> *¶3 #3 [moderate]:* After ‘purified by her towards …’ the expected correspondential destination is the Virgin’s purifying station/measure, not a moral exhortation.
> *¶3 #4 [tentative]:* Balances: she ‘reveals’ light; that light is revealed ‘to them from it’ (from the cloud/sign). Keeps impersonal cosmological register.

**¶4** However, [these] angels shall immediately be summoned to them, so that they will seize them, because they know that they shall never do good. Every place they will touch they shall make dead and a loss. Whenever the angels will make towards them and come beside [them], they shall flee in their constraint before them. They go into whatever they will meet upon and assume it, like a robber being abandoned and fleeing before one stronger than him, as he runs after him to arrest him.

> *¶4 #2 [strong]:* The angels come beside ‘them’ (the rulers); immediate antecedent is the rulers freed by storms.

**¶5** Now, those rulers that shall be freed from these clouds and assume whatever they will meet upon [and they enter] into the [tree / flesh] that they did not [until the hour when they will be stripped] will seize this thing, whether indeed a tree to which they have found the way, or animals, or people. Simply, every place that they will assume they shall burn and destroy by the fire of their body. Namely, the rulers, [the] children of fire, they that shall be stripped of the fire.

> *¶5 #1 [moderate]:* After ‘assume whatever they will meet upon’ the narrative turns to their entering into a receptacle/form (tree/animal/person).
> *¶5 #2 [strong]:* The paragraph immediately specifies tree, animals, people and ends with stripping ‘of the fire’; later (§16) explicitly uses ‘tree and flesh’ as the two main receptacles. Oldest-layer binary is vegetal vs animal/human embodiment.
> *¶5 #3 [moderate]:* Matches the later repeated formula: rulers assume tree/flesh until the time of stripping; keeps the deterministic cosmological cadence.

**¶6** In contrast, [the] cloud that shall be raised up from water, and comes above towards the Virgin of Light; its sign is the thunder storms and the booms that shall come in the atmosphere in various forms and are heard. [Now, it is necessary also to know this:] Again, there are times if the rulers that belong to them, which exist in the cloud, shall on occasion be constrained [in a] water [clou]d. And they flee from it and abandon it.

> *¶6 #2 [moderate]:* Introduces the variant case (‘Again, there are times…’) in an instructional, impersonal tone typical of the substrate.

**¶7** Now, [they] too are so, in that every place that they will [reach], they shall make frost and hail and snow. [They rui]n the seed-corn and the fruits and the plants; and they [burn] every place [that] they will reach.

> *¶7 #4 [tentative]:* Even water-cloud rulers ‘destroy’ by their contrary power: they ‘make frost/hail/snow’ and thereby ‘burn’ in the opposite sense (devastate/kill fertility). The next clause already says ‘ruin’ seed and fruits; this fill keeps parallelism with §5’s ‘burn and destroy’.

**¶8** Again, [ang]els shall be summoned to them who seize them. Still, while the angels [ru]sh after them in order to seize them, they shall make this destruction.

**¶9** Also, [the cl]oud that will be raised up from wind ascends to the atmosphere between, towards the likeness of the Virgin that she shall display to it. Its sign is [the s]torm wind that shall blow with bitterness.

**¶10** So, in those winds and tempests shall the lives be purified. Again, the rulers will be stripped of the cloud of wind and they descend from it, safe in their image. All the rivers and the seas that they will reach and arrive at, they shall make waves in them and raise [waves upon them] and they [smash] the ships. They wreak destruction [upon the seas] and they seize and bring them [to the shore].

> *¶10 #1 [moderate]:* The winds agitate waters: ‘make waves in them and raise …’ naturally completes as ‘waves upon them’ (or ‘great waves’). The construction ‘raise X and they smash the ships’ prefers a direct object like ‘waves’.
> *¶10 #2 [moderate]:* Storm-winds’ effect on ships: standard destructive verb in this sequence (make waves → raise waves → smash ships).
> *¶10 #3 [tentative]:* Keeps the impersonal geographic scope: their destruction is ‘upon’ seas/rivers already mentioned.
> *¶10 #4 [tentative]:* After seizing/bringing, the natural terminus for ship-wreck imagery is driving them ‘to the shore’ (or ‘upon the rocks’). Oldest-layer tends to simple cosmographic nouns; ‘shore’ fits.

**¶11** Also the cloud, the one of light, immediately they will be stripped of it [from it / by] the Virgin. She shall display its sign too [in the firmament above]. And its sign is this, in that you shall find it sailing in stillness and calm. The lives in it shall be cleansed in stillness and calm. Again, the rulers that will be stripped of it [like a ship] that will come forth [safe in their form] they shall put [from it] and they take [into tree / flesh] there [until] the angels seize them.

> *¶11 #1 [moderate]:* Idiom: ‘they will be stripped of it … the Virgin’ = stripped ‘from it’ ‘by’ the Virgin (agency).
> *¶11 #2 [tentative]:* The light-cloud’s sign is displayed in the higher, calmer region; ‘sailing in stillness’ is a firmament-phenomenon.
> *¶11 #3 [moderate]:* Explains ‘sailing in stillness and calm’; substrate uses straightforward natural correspondences (cloud as vessel).
> *¶11 #4 [strong]:* Parallels §10: rulers descend ‘safe in their image’. Here same formula after stripping from light-cloud.
> *¶11 #5 [strong]:* They are stripped of the cloud; then ‘come forth from it’.
> *¶11 #6 [strong]:* Same receptacle-pair as §5 and §16; rulers after stripping seek embodiment.
> *¶11 #7 [moderate]:* Sets up ‘until there … the angels seize them’: the rulers remain in the assumed receptacle until seizure/stripping.

**¶12** Again, the cloud that shall be raised up from the mingled air comes above towards [the image] of the Virgin. Its sign is this, in that [the lives] are purified in it in stillness and [rest; again, these rulers] that will be stripped of that cloud and come from it; every place that they will find [a dwelling] they shall make it [shaken] one of [the signs] until [at last] they are seized and taken to their midst.

> *¶12 #2 [strong]:* The clause ‘… are purified in it in stillness…’ requires subject ‘the lives’.
> *¶12 #3 [moderate]:* Restores the transition from purification-rest to renewed action of rulers; keeps the didactic rhythm (‘again’ introducing rulers’ stripping).
> *¶12 #4 [tentative]:* After stripping and coming forth, they seek a ‘place/dwelling’ to assume; leads into ‘they shall make it …’.
> *¶12 #5 [tentative]:* Mingled-air cloud corresponds to unstable, turbulent effects; ‘make it shaken’ fits the air-mixture’s disordering power.
> *¶12 #6 [tentative]:* ‘one of …’ naturally reads ‘one of the signs’ (i.e., phenomena) until seizure; the teaching catalogs signs for each cloud-type.
> *¶12 #7 [moderate]:* Completes ‘until … they are seized’: ‘until at last’ marks the inevitable arrest by angels.

**¶13** Their strippings, that they shall leave behind them in various places they will have reached, whether the produce of the trees or the offspring of the fleshes, they shall be taken midst [the earth; and they] wither away. They shall inflict great dangers, but again those rulers also the angels shall send to their midst. They bring the light from them and cast the demons down to these seven outer ditches of the great sea; which is the vessel of the waters that are outside the world.

> *¶13 #1 [tentative]:* The ‘strippings’ left in places (produce/offspring) are taken ‘midst …’ and ‘wither away’—the missing link is their being taken into/onto the earth as residue, then perishing.

**¶15** Those ditches and the [channels of the abyss] until they reach the earths below [to the] feet of the Porter.

> *¶15 #1 [tentative]:* The ‘seven outer ditches of the great sea’ in this cosmology extend as abyssal channels downward; ‘channels of the abyss’ matches the older impersonal cosmogonic vocabulary.
> *¶15 #2 [strong]:* Connects: ‘until they reach the earths below … feet of the Porter.’ The fixed phrase is ‘to the feet’.

**¶16** Hear also [this also] for sometimes these angels shall be sent to them, to seize the rulers. Whenever the rulers [flee / hide] for the angels rush after them. They beseech them [and they] they that are given the command. However, [t]hose [rul]ers shall be freed from them alone and assume [tree] and [the fle]sh. They are consumed by this offspring [of / of] tree and that of flesh. The angels shall set a [watch] over them, that they might guard them until the hour when [those r]ulers will be stripped of tree and [flesh; a]nd they go up above. They shall be gathered [together] above, and they are poured out [as smoke] in their first for[ms and im]ages in which they first were. They take the light from them, which had remained behind in them. And they too are cast down to these seven outer [path]s that are in the great sea; the place to where these former ones [were cast]t. They shall reach their midst [also] and the light that is in them is taken.

> *¶16 #1 [strong]:* ‘Hear also … for sometimes’ calls for a brief pointer: ‘this also’.
> *¶16 #2 [tentative]:* Rulers attempt evasion while angels pursue; oldest-layer uses paired verbs for their evasive motion.
> *¶16 #3 [strong]:* ‘They beseech them … they that are given the command’ needs ‘and they’ to introduce the beseeching.
> *¶16 #6 [strong]:* ‘assume … and the flesh’ completes as ‘tree and the flesh’.
> *¶16 #8 [strong]:* ‘consumed by this offspring … tree and that of flesh’ requires a double genitive: ‘offspring of tree and that of flesh’.
> *¶16 #9 [moderate]:* ‘set a … over them, that they might guard them’—the natural noun is ‘watch’ (or ‘guard’). ‘watch’ keeps impersonal register.
> *¶16 #12 [strong]:* After stripping, they ‘go up above’ and ‘shall be gathered … above’: ‘gathered together’ is the standard collocation.
> *¶16 #13 [tentative]:* After being gathered, ‘poured out … in their first forms’ suggests dissolution and re-formation; ‘as smoke’ matches ascent residues from tree/flesh being rendered into subtle form before reappearing in original images.
> *¶16 #14 [strong]:* Completes ‘first for[ms ... im]ages’: ‘forms and images’.
> *¶16 #16 [strong]:* Restores ‘the place to where these former ones were cast’.
> *¶16 #17 [moderate]:* Concludes: they reach the midst ‘also’, linking this second casting-down with the earlier one in §13.

**¶17** Behold now, I have instructed you about this cloud: How it shall ascend above, and the lives that are in it are purified. I have also taught you of the rulers that are in it: how they shall make these {rebellions} and how they shall be {caught}. And they are cast to the outer prison by the angels, when the command is given to them through the power of the Virgin of Light. She has authority over the entire zone, and cleanses the life that is in it.

**Assessment:** Most gaps can be restored at the level of fixed cosmological formulas and the repeated correspondential sequence (cloud ascent → purification of lives → stripping of rulers → assumption into tree/flesh → angelic guarding → stripping again → casting into seven outer ditches/paths). The tightest constraints occur where the text itself repeats the same binary (‘tree / flesh’) and the same pursuit-and-seizure pattern; those restorations are strong. Several descriptive fillers (wave/ship imagery; mingled-air effects; abyssal terminology) remain tentative because the surviving Coptic does not preserve the exact idiom, though the correspondential logic fixes the role and direction of the missing phrases.

---

## Chapter 96: The Three Earths that Exist, they bear Fruit.

**¶1** Three earths exist in the universe, and are being toiled over. At first they [receive] the farmer in them. Afterwards they bear [good] fruit to they who toil over them.

> *¶1 #1 [moderate]:* The sequence is: first the earth is worked, then it ‘receives the farmer’ (i.e., admits the cultivating power into itself); afterwards it ‘bears fruit’ to the laborers. This matches the correspondential order of receptacle → implantation → production.

**¶2** One of them is this great earth that people are living upon. They are ploughing in it [and breaking], it with iron hoes, they cultivate, [making] the furrows, turning it over [from it, from its soil.] Afterwards they harvest fruit from [it ...

> *¶2 #1 [moderate]:* The description lists successive agricultural acts: ploughing, then breaking/loosening the ground with iron hoes, then cultivating and forming furrows, then turning it over, then harvesting. The missing phrase should be another verb coordinated with ‘ploughing’, governing ‘it with iron hoes’.
> *¶2 #3 [tentative]:* The sentence anticipates ‘Afterwards they harvest fruit from it …’. The oldest-layer register tends to close the natural example with a complete, concrete phrase (‘from it / from its soil’) before transitioning to the second, interior ‘field’.

**¶3** ⟨p.245⟩
The second field is the living soul, the holy one. It is mixed in the entire zone, above and below. The farmers who toil over it are these two great light-givers, which journey through the heavens. They are toiling and cultivating it, so that they will draw it towards their aspect. And it leaps up and comes to the heights. It goes upwards, out from every place wherein it is, and it adds to them.

**Assessment:** Most gaps are syntactically constrained by the agricultural parallelism (receive → bear; plough/break/cultivate → make furrows → turn → harvest). Exact wording is less tightly fixed in ¶2 #3, where only the closure of the example is demanded, not a specific technical term.

---

## Chapter 97: Concerning the Three Creations of the Flesh: the ones that were brought forth [...]; and the ones that were begotten by the Abortions; and the ones that were formed of themselves.

**¶2** “All these animals that you see, and also the rest of the [crea]tures [products] of the earth, confirm them in three likeness[es]: some from [their begettings and] abortions. Others, however, form their own selves in their shape [from the womb].

> *¶2 #2 [moderate]:* The clause pairs ‘animals … and the rest of the creatures … of the earth’ with a general term for what the earth brings forth; ‘products’/‘produce’ fits the substrate’s impersonal cosmological register better than a moralized term.
> *¶2 #4 [moderate]:* The teaching is classifying earthly forms by three image-sources; the first is generation (‘begetting’) before the second (‘abortions’).
> *¶2 #5 [moderate]:* Balances the next sentence ‘Others … form their own selves…’: some come by gestation and failed gestation; others are self-formed/auto-generated.

**¶3** “Also, their nourishment is thre[e] light in them, if they shall wish [and they draw]. [For those that are of the earth] they eat see[ds and] fr[uit]; they eat seeds and fruit. The [soil receives] it entirely, and they [separate] the three archetyp[es] from their nature and the [mixing] the time when they are on the ea[rth]; they are seized [and bound] in their [members]. The Ambassador [then] all powers [has authority over].

> *¶3 #2 [moderate]:* After ‘their nourishment is three…’ the verb should describe intake of the triple element (light) through eating; ‘draw’/‘receive’ keeps the correspondential sense of influx.
> *¶3 #3 [tentative]:* Introduces the concrete dietary example (seeds/fruit) as the natural plane expression of a more general rule about nourishment.
> *¶3 #5 [strong]:* The readable repetition ‘they eat seeds and fruit’ constrains this to ‘fruit’.
> *¶3 #6 [tentative]:* The earth/soil as receptacle corresponds to the natural plane taking in what falls into it; prepares for ‘entirely’ and a subsequent action by the animals on what was taken in.
> *¶3 #7 [moderate]:* Core correspondential move: digestion as separation—extracting the three archetypes/light from mixed nature.
> *¶3 #9 [tentative]:* Fits the triadic logic: archetypes (pure forms) are ‘mixed’ in nature and must be separated out; ‘mixing’ also anticipates seizure/binding below.
> *¶3 #11 [moderate]:* ‘Seized … in their …’ calls for captivity language typical of the substrate: the powers bind the forms in bodies while on earth.
> *¶3 #12 [moderate]:* What beings are ‘seized … in’ during earthly time is their bodily members/limbs—natural ultimates that hold the mixed principles.
> *¶3 #13 [tentative]:* Bridges from the condition of seizure to the next agentive clause about the Ambassador.
> *¶3 #14 [moderate]:* The Ambassador (a hypostatic envoy-function) is characterized by dominion/order over the powers; ‘has authority over all powers’ matches the cosmological hierarchy without institutional overlay.

**¶4** “[N] it [ow it is necessary to say of the plants], first [that they are threefold in kind]. ⟨p.247⟩ Plant them generally to this name: shoots and grass. It formed the bad, bitter trees, the reeds and the thorns and the [and their first is this] and these others that are like them.

> *¶4 #2 [moderate]:* The discourse shifts from animals to vegetation; an impersonal transition (‘it is necessary to say’) matches the oldest-teaching tone.
> *¶4 #3 [moderate]:* Paragraph 2–3 established ‘three likenesses’ for animals; the plant section mirrors the same triplicity.
> *¶4 #4 [moderate]:* Prepares for ‘Plant them generally to this name: shoots and grass’ as the first class.

**¶5** “For that thought belongs to the flesh-eating rulers. Still, these [that were] planted (belong to) grass and gourds, since they are in their essence. Nevertheless, [this t]ho[ugh]t that came down from the rulers, as they are in fruit and grass first and shall not be planted in flesh and blood, it formed the trees that shall give nourishing fru[it], as their gourds are sweet.

**¶6** “Again, this thou[ght] that came down from the powers that eat m[ea]t and also eat grass and gourds, it has formed the trees that shall not produce any nourishment. Their fruits belong neither to good nor evil.”

**Assessment:** Most gaps are locally constrained by the chapter’s triadic correspondential schema (three likenesses/kinds; digestion as separation of archetypes; captivity in bodily ultimates; shift from animals to plants). Several larger lacunae remain only moderately/ tentatively recoverable because the Coptic Vorlage wording is unknown, but the restored phrases keep to the oldest impersonal cosmological register and preserve the required structural correspondences.

---

## Chapter 98: What is Virginal; or, otherwise, what is Continent?

**¶3** What is called ‘virginal’ is assigned to the mystery of the aeons of greatness. And they called it ‘virginal’ by the category of all the light that did not [taste] of death, nor come to the enemy’s war.

**¶4** However, what is called ‘continent’ occurs to the mystery of the light that came and was mixed with the darkness. It was defiled in bodies [of the bodies of the creatures] and went up to the light. [They] called [it ‘co]ntinent’.

> *¶4 #1 [moderate]:* The ‘continent’ light is the portion that entered mixture and thus is “defiled” in formed bodies (creaturely compounds), then ascends through purification. The substrate idiom regularly pairs ‘mixed with the darkness’ with being ‘defiled in bodies’ (i.e., in creaturely formations) before rising.

**¶5** Furthermore, the holy vir[gin]als [if they remain in their purity] should they come out against the en[emy’s war ... they do not] taste death, nor [be defiled].

> *¶5 #2 [moderate]:* Virginals correspond to the light that never entered mixture; their defining predicate is remaining untouched/unstained. The clause sets the condition for the subsequent ‘even if they go out against the enemy… they do not taste death’.
> *¶5 #3 [moderate]:* ¶3 already defined virginal as light that did not ‘taste death’ and did not ‘come to the enemy’s war’. Here the structure reverses: even if they go out against the enemy’s war, they still do not taste death—because their state is not mixture but guarded purity.
> *¶5 #4 [moderate]:* Parallelism requires a second negated predicate after ‘nor’: virginals neither taste death nor are defiled/tainted by contact. This matches the correspondence: untouched light is not stained by the contrary even when in its presence.

**¶6** ⟨p.249⟩ On the other hand, what is called ‘continent’ [is this li]ght that mixed with the darkness, was purif[ie]d, came [up f]rom all the creatures, and was confirmed in the great[ness]. They called it ‘continent’ since it tasted [of the l]ust; but afterwards was cleansed from it and [become an] holy one.

**¶7** Furthermore, what is called [‘vi]rginal’ is this light that was purified by the ima[ge] of the [A]mbassador. It wen[t up and] attained [the] he[ights. It was] confirmed in the image of the gods.

**¶8** Again, what is [called] ‘continent’ is the remainder of this light th[at] remained behind, from what was cleansed. It goes up and comes down in transmigration. Al[so], there is a part [of] it that shall come and attain the form of [hum]an [fl]esh. Again, that one too shall be chosen in the flesh, [accept] the hope, and become an holy one. And they name it ‘continent’.

**¶9** Furthermore, what is named ‘virginal’ is the Light Mind, should he come and assume the images [of] the ele[ct]. However, on the other hand, what is called ‘contin[ent]’ is the new man, should it be purified from this old man. And it is cleansed and strips off the Sin that is compounded with it, and it becomes a continent one.

**¶10** And also, what is called ‘virgi[n]al’ in the flesh, is a man if he has [ne]ver joined himself to woman, has not been defiled by intercourse. However, [i]n contrast, what is called ‘continent’, is the man who has a wom[a]n in the world. Afterwards, he cleanses himself from her [and] renounces her. And because of this he [turns back from the desire] and he becomes an holy contin[en]t one.

> *¶10 #6 [moderate]:* In the flesh-example, ‘continent’ means a man who had conjunction but later renounces it. The substrate logic ties continence to having ‘tasted’ desire/lust (cf. ¶6) and then withdrawing/being cleansed from it; thus the missing predicate is his turning back from desire, not a moral exhortation.

**¶11** Behold, I have taught you the categories [of] the v[ir]ginal and the continent in a great many [as]pects. For what is virginal, or otherwise what is [con]tinent, is in many forms.

**Assessment:** Most lacunae are trivial letter-restorations tightly constrained by word-forms. The three substantive gaps (¶4#1, ¶5#2–#4, ¶10#6) are constrained by the chapter’s governing correspondence: ‘virginal’ = light never entering mixture; ‘continent’ = light that tasted mixture/desire and is later purified. Restorations therefore emphasize defilement-in-formed-bodies, immunity of virginal light even when confronting the enemy, and the post-conjunction turning-back from desire.

---

## Chapter 99: Concerning Transmigration

**¶5** For [he has set] ⟨p.251⟩ his [thro]ne in the light, which [and the word]hich he had also uttered [from his mouth] When that one will be muzzled, they shall [be bound and] cut limb [and] there is [s]till a [root remaining]

> *¶5 #1 [moderate]:* The clause needs a governing verb before “his throne in the light.” In the oldest cosmological register, establishing/setting a throne in Light is the fixed image of order and dominion, not a personal narrative.
> *¶5 #3 [moderate]:* After “which” the text calls for the counterpart of the throne: the uttered ordering principle. “and the word” fits both the grammar (“which … which …”) and the substrate logic (throne = rule in ultimates; word = formative decree).
> *¶5 #4 [moderate]:* Completes “which he had also uttered …” in a non-institutional, impersonal way. The pairing throne/utterance is correspondential: fixed order below, proceeding breath/word above.
> *¶5 #5 [moderate]:* “When that one will be muzzled, they shall …” demands a parallel restraint. In the substrate idiom, binding + silencing are paired acts of constraint on a hostile power before dismemberment.
> *¶5 #6 [tentative]:* Joins “cut limb … there is …” into a coordinated sequence (bind/silence → cut → still remains). Minimal restoration consistent with the syntax.
> *¶5 #8 [moderate]:* After dismemberment, the teaching commonly notes that something persists: a “root/remnant” of the nature. This matches the correspondential logic (forms can be cut up, but the underlying nature/root can still remain).

**Assessment:** Most gaps are syntactically constrained but not textually anchored by distinctive names or fixed formulae, so restorations are correspondentially guided (throne/word, binding/silencing, dismemberment/root). Exact wording remains somewhat uncertain in the longer lacunae.

---

## Chapter 100: Concerning the Dragon with Fourteen Heads; what it is and [...]

**¶2** There is never a dragon in the flesh with fourteen heads, as the Magi say! Rather, this is a spiritual lesson. It was uttered in virginity [in a mystery, and in purity] the Magi did not understand [because it was spoken in parable] all their words that are written [are spiritual]tual (?).

> *¶2 #1 [moderate]:* The substrate contrasts literal monstrous imagery with an interior (spiritual) teaching. “Virginity” here is the correspondential state of unmingled reception (unadulterated truth). The phrase needed is a fixed pairing: mystery/purity rather than exhortation.
> *¶2 #2 [moderate]:* The Magi are said to miss the inner sense; the natural segue is that the utterance was in a veiled form (parable/riddle), hence their literalism.
> *¶2 #3 [strong]:* The surviving letters “spiri” constrain the completion. The clause’s function is to reclassify the written words as belonging to an inner register.

**¶3** Again, this is how this earth [is interpreted] to it, as it is accounted a corporeal dragon [and the teaching is likened] I will instruct you [in the mystery of the body] the dragon is this doctrine [of the thought] of the flesh [which is in] also the fourteen heads that are distributed over the dragon are these.

> *¶3 #1 [moderate]:* The sentence requires a verb of correspondential explication: the earth/dragon image “is interpreted” (not physically exists).
> *¶3 #2 [tentative]:* Bridges: “this earth … to it” and then “as it is accounted a corporeal dragon”. The point is likeness/correspondence between earth/body and dragon-image.
> *¶3 #3 [tentative]:* The speaker promises instruction; the ‘dragon’ is immediately identified as doctrine/thought bound to flesh. A phrase marking the interior ‘mystery’ of embodiment fits the oldest-layer register.
> *¶3 #4 [moderate]:* Next clause: “the dragon is this doctrine … of the flesh”. The dragon is not ‘flesh’ itself but the mental/impulsive doctrine of the body; “thought” is later explicit in ¶7, so it fits as a forward echo.
> *¶3 #5 [moderate]:* Needed as a small connective to yield: “the dragon is this doctrine of the thought which is in the flesh …”.

**¶4** The seven sense organs on the head of the body are the two of sight by which one sees; the two of sound by which one hears; the two of scent by which one smells; also the tongue [which is speech] by which one selects, receiving there the different tastes that occur in every form.

> *¶4 #1 [moderate]:* After listing sight/sound/scent, the tongue is singled out; the missing phrase must identify its function in ultimates: tongue → speech (and then taste-selection).

**¶5** These are the seven heads of the dragon, the seven sense organs that are on the upper part of the person; [and the seven are also] below. The seven of the torso are these: two [hands]; the two [feet] ruler [of the belly as] the seven heads that are above; so with the ones below they total and make fourteen heads of the dragon.

> *¶5 #1 [moderate]:* The text totals fourteen by pairing seven ‘above’ with seven ‘below’. This lacuna must introduce the second heptad without shifting into moralizing.
> *¶5 #2 [tentative]:* “The seven of the torso are these: two …” The natural first pair on the torso are the hands/arms as instruments of action (effects).
> *¶5 #3 [tentative]:* To make paired ‘below’ members corresponding to action and locomotion; hands/feet are the most stable correspondential pair for outer powers.
> *¶5 #4 [tentative]:* A ‘ruler’ in the torso section points to the belly/appetite governing the outer members in the opposite sense (concupiscence). This yields: “the two … ruler … the seven heads that are above” → the lower appetitive rule over senses.

**¶6** ⟨p.253⟩
Conversely, the five dens about which they have spoken, where the dragon is congregated and protrudes, are these: the first is the tongue; the second is the lungs; the third is the heart; the fourth is the spleen; the fifth is the blood that dwells in them. Again, if it shall look out from them [and reveal its likeness and its observation above and below, it wages war by] its fourteen heads.

> *¶6 #1 [strong]:* Accepting the provided reconstruction: it matches the substrate’s mechanistic description—through the ‘dens’ the dragon manifests and operates via the fourteen heads (faculties). No later devotional overlay.

**¶7** Whoever will recognise them with the dragon, which is the thought of the body, and struggles with it and is victorious and kills it in them [in his members] He receives the victory without suffering on the day of his coming forth.

> *¶7 #1 [moderate]:* “kills it in them …” requires a locative: the conflict is internal, within one’s own organs/powers (members), matching ¶6’s dens and ¶8’s ‘through them’.

**¶8** So, behold, I have taught you of the dragon that exists with its fourteen heads and five dens, wherein it is concealed and hidden. It does through them [according to its will and]ire all the time.

> *¶8 #1 [tentative]:* The closing: “It does through them … / its des]ire all the time.” The intended sense is continuous operation through organs according to will/desire. Restoring a balanced pair “will … desire” fits the oldest-layer psychology.

**Assessment:** Most gaps are structurally constrained by the chapter’s fixed correspondential scheme: dragon = thought/doctrine of the body; fourteen heads = upper seven senses plus lower seven bodily powers; five dens = inner organs. Exact wording in ¶2–¶3 and ¶5 is less tightly constrained (multiple equivalent formulae exist in this register), so those restorations remain tentative/moderate, while the ‘spiri…’ completion and ¶6’s supplied clause are strong.

---

## Chapter 101: [Concer]ning why, if the Person shall look down into Water, [ ... ]

**¶1** instruct me about this lesson: When the person is above water and looking down [ers]ers, if he shall see his face reflected why shall you find his head turned upside down and also his feet upwards? Yet, when he stares down into the waters, his face shall not be visible, nor can he see it there?

**¶3** [And] this: The face of people and the shape of trees shall appear turned upside down in the water as this is signified in [the] mystery of the summons, when it was sent down to the worlds of darkness towards the First Man. Sin[aused] in this way it cast itself down [from the height into the depth] like a person [who] plunges headlong into water; so it is also with the summons. It cast itself headlong to the worlds below, with strength and diligence.

> *¶3 #3 [moderate]:* The sentence needs a causal bridge: “Sin[ce] … in this way it cast itself down …”. The oldest-layer register is impersonal explanation: “Since it caused (this), in this way it cast itself down…”. Restoring a verb of causation keeps the correspondence tight: the inversion in water is an effect caused by the Summons’ descent.
> *¶3 #4 [moderate]:* The image requires a discrete-degree descent (above→below) to match the correspondence: the Summons ‘sent down’ to the worlds of Darkness, therefore it ‘casts itself down’ from height to depth, like a diver. This also prepares ¶4’s answering ascent (obedience from below upward).

**¶4** And this too: Shall the person lift his head up from the water, his face shall not be visible to him, being bent below. This characterises the mystery of the obedience that ascended from below, from with the First Man his father to the Living Spirit. This is a spiritual mystery that I have [led]led to you so that you would understand it.

**¶5** If you wish to understand another corporeal mystery on this subject, listen and I may teach you to understand it. The face of people and of animals and of trees is visible in the water, hanging upside down. This occurs to the mystery of the stars and the zodiac, which hang upside down and are visible in the great sea. For the face of people and beasts and all trees hang on the root of the stars and the zodiac, being begotten from them. Just as their fathers, who are spread out above, hang upside down; so it is also that the mystery of their fathers is being revealed to them whenever they stare down into the water. This is, that you shall find their shapes inverted.

**¶6** ⟨p.255⟩
Not only this, but [the] child who is born; as the ho[b] approaches for him to be born, he hangs upside down and is born head first. Indeed, due to this, his mystery shall be revealed in these waters people stare down into.

> *¶6 #2 [strong]:* Completes “hour”.

**¶7** Furthermore, I reveal something else to you: Behold, you will find the shadow of the person who walks along is joined to the earth all the time. If the person is troubled [and raises] his shadow up upon the earth [it is stretched out] above. It is impossible for him to do [his shadow to stand] on the earth. See, this mystery is [a] great sign, signifying that the entire body came from the earth and had ascended from the abyss. Again, due to this, its shadow is joined on the earth, turned downwards, and hanging from above.

> *¶7 #1 [tentative]:* Balances the clause structure: when disturbed, the person ‘raises’ the shadow’s appearance upward (i.e., it stands out/lengthens on the earth), which the following line contrasts with what is impossible to do in actuality.
> *¶7 #2 [tentative]:* Continues the observable phenomenon: the shadow appears ‘stretched out’/projected upon the earth ‘above’ (i.e., outward/forward from the feet). This keeps the focus on the shadow’s dependence and inversion relative to the body.
> *¶7 #3 [tentative]:* The next line reads: “It is impossible for him to do [X] on the earth.” In the correspondence, the body cannot make its own shadow ‘stand’ independently; the shadow is an effect tied to the earth/light. Hence: impossible for him ‘to do his shadow to stand’ (i.e., to set it upright/free).

**Assessment:** Most bracketed items are straightforward letter-completions. The main recoveries are ¶3 (causal verb + descent phrase) and ¶7 (shadow-behavior clauses). Those are constrained by the chapter’s inside→outside correspondence: descent (Summons) produces inversion in the reflective ‘sea’; ascent (Obedience) removes the face from visibility; the shadow section must express the same dependence of effects on their lower plane (earth/abyss) and the impossibility of reversing that dependence by one’s own power.

---

## Chapter 102: Concerning the Light Mind, why it does not exercise Foreknowledge for the Saint as for the Apostle?

**¶2** Happen you know that the Light Mind, who dwells in the elect, has the power to perform all these wonders among the faithful, all these signs that you have recounted to me; but the elect are not able to persist in the greatness that may be unveiled to them.

---

## Chapter 103: Concerning the Five Wonders that the Light Mind shall display in the Elect

**¶1** The Light Mind shall enact five light signs in the elect.

**¶7** The Light Mind puts these five signs in the new man, [whom he has] purified, cleansed, supported and chosen [in order that they may appear as five witnesses in him]

> *¶7 #1 [strong]:* Accepting the editor’s restoration: it fits the impersonal, operative agency of the Light Mind and governs the following series of perfectives (“purified, cleansed, supported and chosen”) as effects in the recipient.
> *¶7 #2 [moderate]:* The correspondential logic is: the Light Mind “puts” the five signs within the new man (an interior vessel), after purification/cleansing/support/choice (successive ordering of degrees), so that the signs become manifest as attestations. “Witnesses” matches the function of “signs” as self-evidencing forms of Light in ultimates, without shifting into later devotional/register language.

**Assessment:** ¶7 #1 is fixed by syntax and register. ¶7 #2 is recoverable in sense: the missing clause should state the purpose/effect of implanting the five signs—namely their manifestation/attestation in the regenerated (new) man. Exact wording remains somewhat open, but the function is tightly constrained by the correspondential sequence.

---

## Chapter 104: Concerning Food: It shall be allocated to Five Products of the human Body.

**¶2** This sustenance of various kinds that people gather in and eat, and enters the body; it shall be distributed to five productions.

**¶3** The first product is this that shall issue from the person in rapture and rises up in the Mind, and it comes out from all his limbs, and is immeasurable.

**¶4** The second is this that shall issue from the person in voice and word.

**¶5** The third is this that shall spring from them in strength and activity.

**¶6** The fourth is what is engendered by the pleasure of lu[s]t in men and women.

**¶7** The fifth is this that shall be formed and sculpted in the flesh, and be engendered and come from them. It is this corporeal creature; only its parents can recognise this creature that they beget. They are attentive to its desire and its thought and its [lo]ve every day, all the time. However, these other four forms they neither notice nor are concerned about; because they are not displayed.

**Assessment:** Both lacunae are trivial mid-word restorations, fully constrained by the surrounding words: lu[s]t and [lo]ve.

---

## Chapter 105: Concerning the Three Things that are great with Mankind, as they are running all the time, in that they [ ... ]

---

## Chapter 106: There is no Joy that shall remain in the World till the End.

**¶1** There is not [o]ne person [existing in] the world, standing firm [i]n mixture, for whom joy remains to the end.

> *¶1 #2 [strong]:* The clause needs a participial phrase qualifying “person” in the general cosmological statement about existence-in-mixture; this is already the correct substrate sense (no stable joy within mixture).

**¶2** Not only people, even the sun and the moon, the light-givers of the heavens, [are not in joy to the end] nor are they oppressed, they know not [that are]et firm in a summons and an [obe]dience. Nevertheless, they too, they do not rejoice completely.

> *¶2 #1 [moderate]:* The logic requires that even the heavenly light-bearers are not exempt from the law stated in ¶1: nothing standing in mixture retains joy to the end. The following clause (“nor are they oppressed…”) contrasts their non-oppression with their non-complete joy, so the missing phrase must assert their lack of final, complete joy rather than introduce oppression.
> *¶2 #2 [moderate]:* The syntax “they know not … set firm in a summons and an obedience” requires a relative clause: they do not know those (states/beings) that are fixed in pure obedience—i.e., beyond mixture. This keeps the impersonal correspondential contrast (mixture vs. firm summons/obedience).

**¶3** So, one: Their eyes stare at their enemy, they [perceiv]e him and also look at his ugly shapes. They [s]ee him, that he is alive and established and is king above and below, in secret and manifest. He does every thing that he wants according to his wil[l].

> *¶3 #3 [strong]:* Completes “wil[1]” → “will”.

**¶4** [The s]econd: They perceive the living soul, they see [i]t and that it is ensnared and set in a great [fetter] above and below, in the tree and in the flesh, [bound in every form] with every oppression. It is being pressed, drawn near to [and] sliced and eaten as it comes up and down; from above below and from below above. It [is] despoiled and moved from body to body.

> *¶4 #4 [tentative]:* After “in the tree and in the flesh,” the substrate catalogues the soul’s captivity across all embodied ultimates (vegetable/animal/human), before concluding “with every oppression.” A general totalizing phrase best fits the correspondential sweep (soul bound in all forms of mixture).

**¶5** Now, because of their living soul that is loved by them and honoured of them, when they see it in oppression th[ey can] not [re]joice completely.

**¶6** So, one about the soul; and one about their enemy whom they see ruling [in] what is not his own. He is proud and satisfied with a wealth that is not his own. Indeed, due to this, they do not rejoice in total; but bear up and swallow their heart until they cleanse the living soul and take it from the enemy's hands. Then they obliterate the death that is their enemy and they gather it in [and fetter it in the lump] for ever and ever.

> *¶6 #2 [strong]:* Retained: it matches the old cosmogonic closure-motif—Death/Darkness gathered into a single mass (‘lump’) and bound, i.e., confinement of the enemy’s substance into its ultimate compaction.

**¶7** When they will chain their enemy in the bond at the [las]t, then they will rejoice and no longer grieve from this t[ime]. Also, they become the very same as they were from the [start] for ever!

**Assessment:** Most lacunae are trivial letter/word-completions and are recoverable with high certainty. The main uncertainty is ¶2 #1–#2 and ¶4 #4, where the damaged phrases must be inferred from the correspondential structure (mixture vs. firm obedience; captivity of the soul across embodied forms). The proposed restorations keep the impersonal, cosmological register of the oldest substrate and preserve the argument’s parallelism.

---

## Chapter 107: Concerning the Form of the Word, that [ ... 30 ... ]

**¶1** [The morsel is taken by the hand and brought to the mouth; the jaw receives it,] and the throat draws it up and the tongue spreads it out and the teeth cut it and the lips g[at]her it! The word shall come forth through the power of th[ese five] members and be heard outside.

> *¶1 #1 [moderate]:* The sequence in ¶1 enumerates five “members” that process food into speech: throat, tongue, teeth, lips plus an initial receiver. The missing opening must introduce the edible “morsel” and the first member (jaw/mouth) so that the following clauses (“and the throat… and the tongue… and the teeth… and the lips…”) total five. This keeps the impersonal, mechanistic correspondential style (natural organs illustrating spiritual formation).

**¶2** Simila[rly] the coin: One shall pour it out and an [othe]r beat it and another trim it as it is turned, and a[nother] put the stamp on it and another wipe it in the sieve (?). [Behold], these five craftsmen shall shape and beautify [their] coin, and it comes amongst mankind. It becomes a posse[ssion] to be received and given.

**¶3** This is also the case with the [wor]d, as it is formed and embellished by five [member]s. It comes forth and is heard by the ears [of] others.

**Assessment:** All minor lacunae are mechanically certain (word-fragments). The only substantial gap is ¶1 #1; it is constrained by the required fivefold correspondential structure (a first organ/member preceding throat–tongue–teeth–lips) and by the food→speech analogy. Exact wording remains uncertain, but the restored sense and member-count are tight.

---

## Chapter 108: Concerning the Seed Grain that shall be formed by the Elements, and also be destroyed by them

**¶2** “This seed grain and this barley grain that you see shall be formed and [be raised up] and beautified by the five elements. Now, the warmth and the cold nourish the seed grain and the entire tree. So, just as these things nourish it [and increase, so] it is destroyed [when they are in excess] through [their] hunger.

> *¶2 #1 [moderate]:* The sequence “formed … and … and beautified by the five elements” expects a three-step natural process: shaping → growing/being raised → coming to beauty (ripening). In the oldest cosmological register, the middle term is the elevation/growth of the form through the elements’ work.
> *¶2 #2 [tentative]:* “Just as these things nourish it … it is destroyed … through … hunger” requires a parallel: the same pairs (warmth/cold) that nourish in due measure also, when imbalanced, bring diminution. A brief clause like “and increase, so” completes the analogy and sets up the contrary effect.
> *¶2 #3 [moderate]:* The destruction is attributed to the same agencies named just before (warmth and cold). The correspondential logic is measure: elements in order give growth; elements in excess (or defect) give corruption. Hence a condition of excess fits the pivot to “it is destroyed.”
> *¶2 #4 [moderate]:* The final phrase “through … hunger” most tightly constrains to “through their hunger,” i.e., hunger as the effect proceeding from the disordered elements’ action upon the seed/tree. This keeps the impersonal causal register (not moral exhortation).

**¶4** “In this way too, a lack and a shortfall shall occur from place to place through them; and they who have chosen it have gathered it in and beautified and attained it, while an affliction shall arise for it through those.

**¶5** “People also shall be caused to stumble on their account, through the energy of Matter that dwells in them. It has poured its shadow over them, just as it shall also pour its shadow over the elements and destroy the fruits of all the trees.”

**Assessment:** All four gaps in ¶2 can be restored only by internal parallelism (nourish/destroy; due measure/excess) rather than by fixed technical names. The reconstructions are correspondentially coherent (order of elements produces growth; imbalance produces lack/hunger), but exact wording remains somewhat underdetermined.

---

## Chapter 109: Concerning the Fifty Lord's Days; to what Mysteries do they correspond? Or the Second (Fifty); to whose Sign (are they)?

**¶2** Happen you know that [in each]h one of these five garments that the [First] Man summoned there are five powers. They shall be counted to the [from] other, these five, and five times [make] their number. However, at the time [when] the summons went down. The answer wished to ascend with it. And the summons with the obedience [constitute] twenty-five characteristics in their twenty-five limbs, [which] are the sons of the Man. The summons and [the obed]ience were raised to the heights through them.

> *¶2 #2 [strong]:* Fixed technical title in this stratum: the Primal/First Man who summons (i.e., evokes) the fivefold garment-powers as his own armature.
> *¶2 #3 [moderate]:* The sentence is enumerative: the five powers are “counted from each other” (i.e., by mutual correspondence/derivation) before the multiplication to twenty-five.
> *¶2 #4 [moderate]:* Needed to complete the arithmetic statement: “five times make their number.”
> *¶2 #5 [moderate]:* Temporal hinge: “at the time when the summons went down.”

**¶3** ⟨p.263⟩ Just as their fa[ther] the living Man raised himself up through them, by these these other ones have set up all things a[ov]e and below. Indeed, these are what are called pentecost, the fifty great days that are the [archetypes of the] holy [d]ays. They fasted from their [od]d, [h]hich is their own.

> *¶3 #3 [strong]:* Matches the oldest-layer correspondential logic: the “fifty days” above are prototypes/patterns for the holy days below, not an ecclesial feast-history.

**¶4** So, from that time until [the time] of [adv]ent of Jesus the Splendour, the glorious one who came [to show / their foo]heir food which is the [living portion] of sou[l], which is the summons and the obedience [and they were nourished by] that spiritual food. They rele[ased from] all these fifty days, which [by this rule] this living food suffices for all of them. It filled them all with power and richness and life. Light [since] this living food that [from] became rich.

> *¶4 #3 [tentative]:* The syntax requires an infinitive of disclosure followed by resumption of the theme “their food”: the advent is mentioned as the epoch when the teaching about their proper nourishment was manifested. Kept impersonal and instructional rather than devotional.
> *¶4 #4 [tentative]:* This register prefers substance/portion language for spiritual nutriment: “their food which is the living portion of soul,” i.e., what actually sustains soul as such (the descending/ascending call-and-response).
> *¶4 #6 [moderate]:* Connects smoothly: the food “which is the summons and the obedience, and they were nourished by that spiritual food.” Keeps the oldest-layer mechanism: nourishment = participation in call/obedience as living influx.
> *¶4 #7 [moderate]:* Best fit for “They rele[ased ... ] all these fifty days”: the fasting is a release/abstention “from” their own food (their proper, internal nourishment), consistent with ¶3.
> *¶4 #8 [tentative]:* Signals a law-like pattern rather than community practice: the fifty-day cycle operates “by this rule,” i.e., by correspondential order.
> *¶4 #9 [moderate]:* Causal link: “since this living food suffices for all of them.”
> *¶4 #10 [moderate]:* Completes: “Light from this living food … became rich,” i.e., light is derived from and enriched by that same influx.

**Assessment:** Most lacunae here are short and structurally constrained by the five→twenty‑five→fifty correspondential arithmetic and by the recurring theme that ‘food’ = the living call-and-response (summons/obedience). The longest gaps in ¶4 remain only moderately constrained; restorations there are guided by the oldest-layer mechanism (manifestation of the teaching; nourishment as influx) and by avoiding later ecclesial/paraenetic phrasing.

---

## Chapter 110: Concerning the Nourishment of the Person, for there are Powers in it [ ... / ... ]

**¶1** exists [a power that] daily to the body of the person [and] there are two hundred and fifty thousand seals in it [and] there are another two hundred and fifty thousand rulers [of it; and when] they are cleansed and purified and enter [into the living storehouse] ⟨p.265⟩ by the voice, by the word, and by the [three measures] quiet with the silence at the time when his heart [is made still and rests] of God the Father.

> *¶1 #1 [moderate]:* Opening clause needs a subject for “exists … daily to the body.” In the substrate register, the constant influx/operation of a cosmic “power” (not a personal savior) fits the correspondential logic of a daily action upon the body.
> *¶1 #2 [tentative]:* Connects the daily operation “to the body of the person” with the ensuing anatomical/cosmic enumeration of “seals” and “rulers.”
> *¶1 #3 [tentative]:* Parallelism: “there are two hundred and fifty thousand seals in it … there are another two hundred and fifty thousand rulers …” requires a simple connective at each step.
> *¶1 #4 [tentative]:* After listing the two sets, the text shifts to their action (“they are cleansed…”). A transition like “of it; and when” matches the move from static constitution to process.
> *¶1 #5 [tentative]:* Cleansed powers entering a receptacle corresponds to purified influxes being received/collected in a higher vessel. “Storehouse/treasury” is substrate-cosmological (impersonal), avoiding later ecclesial framing.
> *¶1 #6 [tentative]:* The triad “voice, word, and …” demands a third term that is not explicitly Christian-personal but denotes ordered degrees/operations. “Measures” fits correspondentially (distinct degrees) and can be named as a set of three.
> *¶1 #7 [moderate]:* “quiet with the silence” needs a governing verb phrase describing the heart’s state-change. Stillness/rest is the correspondential effect of ordered influx (voice/word/measures) when the interior is set in equilibrium.

**¶2** Also, the twelve [great archontic] rulers that belong to the Darkness shall be swept out outside in wandering and in transmigration [in the circuits] some of them [shall pass through] this transmigration, and others among them shall be brought back.

> *¶2 #1 [tentative]:* The phrase modifies “rulers that belong to the Darkness.” The oldest layer tends to classify by function (archontic/ruling) rather than moral exhortation; “great archontic rulers” matches the cosmological taxonomy.
> *¶2 #2 [tentative]:* “swept out outside in wandering and in transmigration …” is followed naturally by a spatial-cosmological qualifier: wandering occurs “in the circuits” (revolutions/rounds), aligning with transmigratory motion.
> *¶2 #3 [strong]:* Syntax requires a verb governing “this transmigration.” “Pass through” is the minimal, structurally correct reading and preserves the impersonal, process-based register.

**Assessment:** Only a small amount of contiguous wording survives around each gap, so most restorations are constrained mainly by parallelism (enumerations, triads), required syntax, and the substrate’s impersonal cosmological register. The most secure is ¶2 #3 (“shall pass through”), which is syntactically forced. The triadic slot after “voice” and “word” is structurally constrained but lexically uncertain; other connectives and transition phrases are plausible but remain tentative.

---

## Chapter 111: Concerning the Four Archetypes that occur in the Eye, and the Fifth that is hidden in them; to whom do they belong?

**¶1** There are four aspects among mankind that perceive, as they perceive [the four elements] without these four that are hidden in them [and the four powers that are hidden in them] they belong [to the four roots]. So, [from them the body is woven].

> *¶1 #1 [moderate]:* The sentence defines four hidden aspects in humans that enable perception; in the oldest cosmological register these are the elemental degrees (fire, water, wind/air, earth) present as an interior structure of the body and senses.
> *¶1 #2 [moderate]:* The text distinguishes (a) perceivable aspects and (b) the hidden enabling forces within; the parallelism calls for a second tetrad: powers/forces within the elements/senses rather than external doctrines.
> *¶1 #3 [moderate]:* The phrase “they belong …” requires an antecedent to which the four aspects correspond; “roots” matches Iranian/elemental cosmology and the correspondential idiom (roots as first natural receptacles).
> *¶1 #4 [moderate]:* Concludes the correspondential claim: without the hidden tetrad, the human form cannot function; “woven” fits the substrate’s impersonal craft-language for composition from elements.

**¶2** [The heat] that is in the [blood] belongs to the fire. The darkness belongs to the abyss; but the light that perceives what is hidden in all these things, being clear, is the living air.

> *¶2 #1 [strong]:* The clause “... that is in the ... belongs to the fire” needs a quality assigned to fire; heat is the proper correspondential property (fire as active ardor/energy in ultimates).
> *¶2 #2 [moderate]:* In this substrate, blood corresponds to the fiery element as life-heat in the body; it also contrasts well with “darkness belongs to the abyss” and “living air” as the clarifying perceiver.

**¶3** How shall the pupil of the eye perceive? While it perceives by day, yet come the night it shall not perceive. What mystery does this matter signify? The pupil of the eye shall not [perceive in the night] the mystery of the [night] is the door [of the abyss] below [and it is set] the other four cardinal points. For in the [the West] above. He has [the East] great [a] they came down [glory] living [powers]. The First Man came forth from it and knew all things.

> *¶3 #1 [strong]:* Direct continuation of the day/night contrast: the pupil cannot perceive at night. The point then turns to the ‘mystery’ (correspondence) of that limitation.
> *¶3 #2 [strong]:* The mystery being expounded is precisely the alternation of day/night as states of light and obscuration; naming “night” keeps the register concrete before it is unfolded cosmologically.
> *¶3 #3 [moderate]:* The ‘door’ below aligns with the abyss in ¶2 (darkness belongs to the abyss). The eye’s incapacity at night corresponds to the lower gate by which obscuration rises.
> *¶3 #4 [moderate]:* The syntax needs a verbal link: the ‘door … below’ is positioned with respect to the cardinal points; “set/placed” is the expected structural verb in the impersonal cosmological voice.
> *¶3 #5 [moderate]:* In correspondential cosmology, the ‘door below’ associated with night and descent aligns with the West (setting, obscuration), contrasted with the upper region of light.
> *¶3 #6 [moderate]:* Balances the previous: the ‘mystery … is the door’ in the lower/western quarter, while the opposite quarter is ‘above’ (rising/light) in the east.
> *¶3 #8 [moderate]:* “He has … great …” calls for an attribute of the upper region/source that sends down living powers; “glory” fits the oldest layer’s impersonal radiance-language without importing devotional titles.
> *¶3 #9 [moderate]:* What “came down … living …” are not persons but dynamic agencies; “living powers” is the natural phrase in the substrate for the descending forces by which First Man ‘knew all things’.

**Assessment:** Most gaps are restorable by the chapter’s correspondential structure: a tetradic anthropology (elements/powers), elemental attributions (fire/heat/blood), and the day–night/above–below mapping onto East–West and the abyssal ‘door’. Exact original lexemes (e.g., whether the first tetrad is explicitly ‘elements’ or ‘members/senses’) remain somewhat open, but the required cosmological relations tightly constrain the fills.

---

## Chapter 112: The Human is less than all the Things of the Universe, and he is rebellious beyond them all.

**¶2** ⟨p.267⟩
After he had stood [in] that place in the midst of the river, he shook his head. He [sa]ys: If one of all these five wo[r]ds of the f]lesh [that are in the] today on the surfaces of the earth [they could not bring them all] out from [the abyss] which is deep. They will make it like [a single heap] and they set them in order [and they set them in order together, with o]ne another, one [wi]th one upon the earth. They all [be able to reach] up to the heights against the elevation of this mountain [in measure corresponding to] its height; they will neither come to breadth corresponding to [its] breadth, nor to width corresponding to its width, [nor to] depth corresponding to its depth.

> *¶2 #4 [moderate]:* The sentence is enumerating the “five words of the flesh” as dispersed and present “today on the surfaces of the earth”; the substrate idiom regularly locates these as principles/members *in* the body/mixture. “that are in the” links “five words of the flesh” to their present manifestation without introducing later soteriological language.
> *¶2 #5 [tentative]:* The comparison that follows (“They will make it like … and they set them in order … one with one upon the earth … up to the heights …”) is a hyperbolic measuring trope: even if they gathered/assembled them, they cannot match the mountain’s measures. Hence a constraint: a failed attempt to collect and marshal the fivefold elements.
> *¶2 #6 [moderate]:* The phrase “which is deep” calls for a cosmological depth-term; the oldest layer contrasts surface earth with a deep “abyss”/deep-place from which things are drawn out.
> *¶2 #7 [tentative]:* They “make it like … and set them in order … one with one upon the earth”: the natural image is piling/stacking into one mass before arranging layers. “heap” fits the building-measure comparison.
> *¶2 #8 [moderate]:* Keeps the given tail “with o-” and matches the parallelism “one with one”; “together” is demanded by the subsequent “with one another.”
> *¶2 #10 [moderate]:* The sequence requires a verb of ability/attainment before “up to the heights against the elevation of this mountain.”
> *¶2 #11 [moderate]:* The passage is a fourfold measurement formula (height/breadth/width/depth). “in measure corresponding to” preserves the technical, impersonal register of proportional correspondence.

**¶3** Not only this, b[ut each] one, all of them from their smallest to their g[reatest, would fill] a single sea [and make] single [one] but [they would cast themselves into] that [s]ea and they are swallowed an[d and sink down in it] is in it [and whatever is in it] they are in [the midst of] earth [and there are] many [m]ountains [and a] sea in it, they [and though the] are very [gre]at in their fa[shion, yet]hey did not guard against him [in their mouth] the silence and they [kept] blasphemed hi[m] not, neither against his l[i/gh]t that is displayed, no[r] do they utter blasphemies [a]gainst his power that supports the totality, nor against the [e]ssence that is displayed indicating the totality, [nor] against the good works of righteousness. [They re]count no cause of evil after Go[d].

> *¶3 #2 [tentative]:* The syntax demands a result-clause leading into “a single sea …”: from least to greatest, if combined, they would not exceed (or would only make) one sea. “would fill” gives the natural container-image that the next clauses negate by swallowing.
> *¶3 #3 [tentative]:* Bridges to “a single sea” as the produced aggregate.
> *¶3 #4 [moderate]:* Parallelism: “a single sea … single …” requires the numeral/qualifier.
> *¶3 #5 [tentative]:* The following “that sea and they are swallowed …” requires an entry-action preceding the swallowing.
> *¶3 #7 [tentative]:* After “they are swallowed,” the next action is descent/being drawn down into the sea’s depths; “sink down” matches the correspondential direction (into interiors).
> *¶3 #8 [tentative]:* Prepares for “is in it … they are in … earth …”: the contents of the sea are being enumerated as swallowed.
> *¶3 #9 [moderate]:* Fits “they are in … earth …”: “in the midst of the earth” is a standard cosmological location for the enclosed mixture.
> *¶3 #10 [tentative]:* Introduces the next feature: “many mountains … sea in it,” i.e., internal structures within the earth/sea image.
> *¶3 #12 [tentative]:* Needed to connect “many mountains … sea in it,” making the internal inventory: mountains and a sea.
> *¶3 #13 [tentative]:* Transitions from the cosmological image to the moral-judicial point: despite their greatness in form, they did not blaspheme.
> *¶3 #15 [moderate]:* The clause “are very great in their fashion … they did not guard against him” needs a concessive pivot; “yet” supplies it while keeping the given letters.
> *¶3 #16 [tentative]:* What follows is about not blaspheming/uttering blasphemies; this is done “in the mouth/tongue.” Substrate prefers the plain organ term over later moralizing.
> *¶3 #17 [moderate]:* Works with “the silence”: “they kept the silence.”

**¶4** However, [the i]dol of ill-fated human[it]y [invented speech of] the one that will be unlike anything, that is equal [to the] great works; and it was found evil [by] God, the Lord of all. It even blasphemed again[st the] power that supports the totality, against its hol[y] light; [and it] profaned its glorious wisdom, bringing forth the cause of every sin. They say that every disgraceful wickedness and defilement, the rulers and the demons and the fiends and the satans, they say that they have come from God, that he is the one who [es]tablished them [from the beginning; but] they came not from him, and they give false testimony about him.

> *¶4 #3 [tentative]:* The “idol of ill-fated humanity” is a fabricated doctrinal image: a false account about origins. The next clause “the one that will be unlike anything, that is equal … great works” implies an invented assertion about a unique/equal maker. “invented speech” matches the impersonal substrate critique of false cosmology.
> *¶4 #4 [tentative]:* Links “that is equal … great works”: equality is being asserted *to* the great works/powers, i.e., wrongly equating the idol-doctrine with the true ordering powers.
> *¶4 #10 [moderate]:* The accusation is that God “established them …”; the rebuttal follows: “they came not from him.” A temporal-causal addendum (“from the beginning; but”) fits the polemical structure without later theological overlay.
