**¶7** Jesus did not come and s[a]ve the world because of mankind alone, but [also he] came and revealed on earth [the things that were hidden] was strong outside in [the universe] And [whe]n he had finished doing his [t]ask outside [i]n the great u[ni]verse, he came [and after these things he wen]t further with Adam and Eve and revealed [to them]. Even so, he sent the apostles to the good, [g]enera- tion by generation, and revealed to them five great thin[gs].

> *¶7 #2 [strong]:* Balances “not … alone, but …”: the second member requires “also.”
> *¶7 #3 [moderate]:* What is “revealed on earth” in this layer is the concealed structure of aeons/light/darkness, not moral exhortation; “hidden” matches the unveiling vocabulary used later (¶9).
> *¶7 #4 [moderate]:* “was strong outside in …”: the locus is the macrocosm; “universe” matches the later “great universe.”
> *¶7 #9 [moderate]:* The narrative sequence: after finishing the outward task, he came (down/near) and then went further with Adam and Eve. “and after these things” is a neutral, pre-institutional connector.

**¶9** [Se]cond: He unveiled to them about the aeons [of the greatness], how they occur; and he taught them about [the manner of] the darkness, how it t[o]o exists.

**¶10** [The th]ird time: He proclaimed to them about the [l]ight great-[ness], how it has been active against the pow[er of] darkness and conquered it.

**¶11** Fourth: He taught them that he came to the province of death [which is this mixture] that is flesh, until he should find profit and bring them from the gate of the underworld below, for [they had] been swa[ll]owed into it.

> *¶11 #1 [moderate]:* The “province of death” is then identified: “that is flesh.” In the oldest layer, flesh is the state of mixture/compounding of natures. “this mixture” is the substrate term that explains why it is death-province (life enclosed in alien matter).

**¶13** All the faithful who believe this tr[u]th shall live by three great mighty things.

**Assessment:** Most lacunae here are small letter-restorations and are secure. The larger gaps occur in ¶2–4 where the text builds a proportional-measure comparison (fivefold elements vs. a mountain) and then shifts to a cosmological ‘sea/earth/mountains’ swallowing image before a polemic against false attribution of evil to the Highest. Those restorations are constrained by the correspondential structure (surface vs. abyss; assembling/stacking; swallowing/descent; silence vs. blasphemy; flesh as mixture), but the exact Coptic phrasing behind several connective clauses remains uncertain, so a number of fills are necessarily tentative.

---

## Chapter 113: The Chapter on whether any [Lig]ht comes from the Three Vessels.

**¶2** [So it is, not only] b[u]t should the [vessels of the firmaments] ascend [from below and] light of the rays that [are above] which are received, which shine and go in through these great open gates, through these doors, firmament by firmament.

> *¶2 #1 [moderate]:* Opening formula to set a conditional rule; fits impersonal, schematic substrate style and prepares the concessive “but should … ascend …” construction.
> *¶2 #3 [moderate]:* The whole passage tracks rays entering “great open gates … door … firmament by firmament”; the correspondential target of the gates is the receptacles below each firmament. This also aligns with ¶3’s explicit “opening downwards to the vessels.”
> *¶2 #4 [tentative]:* Needed to complete the motion-sequence: ascent from the lower vessels toward the gates, then entry of rays through gates/doors. Keeps the mechanical-cosmological register.
> *¶2 #5 [tentative]:* Balances the clause: rays “which are received … shine and go in through these great open gates”; the gates/doors belong to the upper boundary of each firmament, so “are above” fits the spatial correspondence.

**¶3** For four [gates open]pen in each world, distributed at the four points of the compass, opening downwards to the vessels. So, due to this, the [light of the] rays shall go in through these openings and it shines on [that] place, and pulls through them the light and the little pi[llar of light that ex]ists there.

> *¶3 #4 [moderate]:* The “little pillar” here is the remnant-accumulation/concentration of light in the lower place that the rays ‘pull through’ the openings. Adding “of light” makes the referent explicit and preserves the substrate’s physicalized cosmology (light as a substance/portion).

**¶4** From that place [it] shall be poured [out to the] firmaments. It shall also be clarified in these firmaments [and sifted, until] a part there is cleansed and it is [all] taken up with the living ones. However, its other part [remains mixed with the dregs] shall all [be cast] down.

> *¶4 #3 [moderate]:* After being “poured out to the firmaments,” the required next operation is separation/filtration in stages (“firmament by firmament”). “Clarified” already appears; pairing with “sifted” tightens the correspondential idea of discriminating pure light from mixed residue across discrete degrees.
> *¶4 #5 [moderate]:* The antithesis demands a remainder that cannot be taken up “with the living ones.” In the substrate logic, purification yields two outputs: a cleansed portion taken upward, and a mixed/heavy remainder (“dregs”) expelled downward.

**Assessment:** Most gaps can be restored at the level of cosmological operations (reception of rays, entry through gates, extraction of a concentrated ‘pillar’ of light, staged clarification, and a twofold outcome: uplift vs. downward expulsion). Exact connective particles in ¶2 and the precise phrasing of the transition in ¶4 remain less tight because the surviving syntax is thin, but the correspondential sequence (lower vessels → gates/doors → firmaments → separation) constrains the restorations strongly.

---

## Chapter 114: Concerning the Three Images that are in the righteous Person.

**¶2** Three images occur in the elect person.

**¶3** The first is the spiritual image, which is the new man, which the Light Mind shall form in him; and it goes into him and dwells in him.

**¶4** The second image is the remnant and remainder of the new man, which is the psychic image that is bound in the flesh, as the old man [(bound) in the flesh, as the old man, from] times of this psychic image.

> *¶4 #1 [moderate]:* The contrast requires an origin-point for the psychic image’s bondage: it is the ‘old man’ condition continuing from an earlier time. The idiom ‘from since the times…’ resolves the syntax and keeps the impersonal, cosmological register (state/continuity rather than moral exhortation).

**¶5** The other one is the corporeal image [that is] added to them all.

**¶6** These three images [are mingled] entirely. When it will [rise up] through the nourishment of food. If it shall sink [in the flesh] down to the corporeal image. [And] the corporeal, [that] is in it, shall divest itself of it and place it in the [psychic] image. [But if] again it rises up and falls to the [spiritual] image; it shall divest itself of the psychical of the enemies, which [are in it] anger. The lust [that is stirred up] with it.

> *¶6 #1 [moderate]:* The next lines describe rising/sinking by nourishment and divestings between images; this presupposes that the three images coexist in a mixed state in the embodied elect.
> *¶6 #2 [strong]:* Balances the following clause ‘If it shall sink…’; the movement is vertical by correspondence (ascent to finer degree, descent to grosser).
> *¶6 #3 [moderate]:* The sinking is described as ‘down to the corporeal image’; the immediate medium of that descent is the flesh/body.
> *¶6 #6 [strong]:* The corporeal ‘places it in the … image’; the only coherent transfer is from corporeal upward into the psychic degree (middle term).
> *¶6 #7 [moderate]:* Introduces the return-cycle: after being placed upward, it can again rise and fall. A conditional transition is required by the alternating motions.
> *¶6 #8 [strong]:* After ‘again it rises up and falls to the … image’: in this triad, rising terminates in the spiritual degree (highest image).
> *¶6 #9 [moderate]:* The clause ‘the psychical of the enemies, which … anger’ needs a relative connection describing hostile psychic admixtures resident within the psychical image.
> *¶6 #10 [moderate]:* The enemies’ psychical is characterized by passions; ‘anger’ in particular is something ‘stirred up/roused’ in the mixed soul, fitting the mechanics of agitation and purification.

**¶7** Then shall a light virgin come and reveal the spiritual image that is [there], which [is] the new man. That virgin acts as a guide. [She g]oes on before and it is extended to the heights above, and received into this spiritual image. And she [sculpts] it and adorns it with the new man [wi]thin. It is sealed with all the limbs of this light virgin who is present and dwells in the new man.

**¶8** So, this is [h]ow this living limb shall be [puri]fied and live, the one that comes into the body of [the ri]ghteous one from without through the administration of food of various ki[nds], like this. The living soul shall be clea[n]sed entirely every [d]ay and traverse these three [im]a[ges].

**¶9** So, it shall divest itself of the body, which is not its own, in the corporeal. It shall also divest itself of the souls that are not its own, these that are mixed with it in the psychical [by means of] anger and desire and [evil]-ness and foolishness and envy and strife; and these other wicked teachings that are not its own.

> *¶9 #1 [moderate]:* The list ‘anger and desire and …-ness’ is governed by an instrumental phrase explaining how alien souls operate/mix in the psychical: through these passions/qualities.
> *¶9 #2 [moderate]:* The sequence requires a vice paired with ‘anger and desire’, followed by ‘-ness and foolishness’; ‘evil-ness’ is the neutral, substrate-style quality-name (state) rather than a moralizing flourish.

**¶10** However, in [the] spiritual image itself it shall live and be joined with [lo]ng-sufferingness, the perfection of faith and love that reigns over them all. It is the virgin of light who robes the new man and who shall be cal[l]ed ‘the hour of life’. S[he] is the f[irst], but s[h]e is also the last.

**Assessment:** Most gaps are tightly constrained by the three-degree schema (spiritual–psychic–corporeal) and the cyclical motions (rise/sink; divest/place). The largest uncertainty is the exact phrasing around the ‘old man’ temporal clause and the description of the enemies’ psychical in relation to anger; these are restored by correspondential mechanics (states, admixture, agitation) rather than later devotional diction.

---

## Chapter 115: The Catechumen asks the Apostle: will Rest come about for Someone who has come out of the Body, if the Saints pray over and make an Alms-offering for him?

**¶5** As to the question for which you ask me, the entreaty occurs and the true prayer occurs! For every elect person, if he beseeches charity in total faith, he is claiming a request from our compassionate Father. So, if he is beseeching over himself alone, he shall be favoured his entreaty; but if again he is beseeching on behalf of someone else, he shall be granted his request.

**¶6** For this entreaty has been since the beginning [the time when] the gods first made entreaty of their Father. They were granted their entreaty and their request.

> *¶6 #1 [moderate]:* The line needs a temporal anchor: entreaty exists from the primordial beginning; in the oldest layer this is stated as a continuous practice starting at the first emergence of the Light powers. The next clause begins with “the gods first made entreaty…”, so a simple temporal phrase best matches the impersonal cosmological register.

**¶7** I will instruct you how it is that they shall beseech their entreaty from the first ones; or in what form they shall entreat their request, they who have asked for it [and they shall receive from them] every thing.

> *¶7 #1 [tentative]:* The sentence contrasts (a) how they beseech from the first ones with (b) in what form they entreat their request; then it should conclude with the outcome: receiving “every thing”.

**¶8** ⟨p.272⟩ The Great Spirit, the Mother of Life, she claimed and prayed and besought and glorified and praised the first established one, who is the Father. She besought him an entreaty. She claimed of him a request. She received a great gift; she and the many powers with her.

**¶9** She besought an entreaty on behalf of the First Man, because of how he had come forth and thrown himself down. He became distant from the Mother of Life. He separated from her and gave himself; in great affliction. He joined war on earth with the destruction and the grief and the weakness; in the worlds of darkness; in the abysses made of fear; in the midst of the demons' ditch; amongst the powers of the devil!

**¶10** Indeed, he is like a king standing in the midst of his enemies. And when the Mother of Life petitioned and prayed and glorified and praised the Father, the first established one, she did petition him that he might send a power; a protector and redeemer and helper for the son of God who is set in affliction.

**¶11** ⟨p.273⟩ Now, just as she had besought and interceded, so likewise was her prayer fulfilled and received into the presence of the Father of Greatness. He turned and gave to her her entreaty: the great power, the Living Spirit, the giver of ease. The Mother of Life and the Living Spirit came with great power to the borders of the territory. They brought the First Man up from that war and struggle, wherein he is set.

**¶12** He gave himself as a grace for the Mother of Life. He became estranged and far from her, the one whom his enemies (place) in great affliction, the one who is in the land of darkness since he became weak.

**¶13** The Living Spirit grasped and took him in. He freed him. He set him right and gave him ease in the land of rest [as is fitting for] the Living Spirit, the first giver of ease [the first giver of ease, according to] the will of these three. The welcomer [and they shall] find with the welcomer that [...

> *¶13 #1 [tentative]:* Needs an adverbial clause: he gives ease in the land of rest “as is fitting for the Living Spirit…”. This keeps the substrate’s impersonal, correspondential tone.
> *¶13 #2 [tentative]:* The sequence repeats the title and then moves into “the will of these three”; a bridge phrase like “according to” (i.e., in accordance with) links his act to the triadic will (Father–Mother–First Man / or the three hypostases active here).
> *¶13 #3 [minimal]:* The surviving fragment “The welcomer [...] find with the welcomer that [...” is too broken; minimal connective restoration is all that is defensible without the underlying Coptic. The function is to resume narration after a titulary aside.

**¶14** [The Mother of Life sent] a great power [and the Living Spirit] of the loved one [the son] the First Man who had been far away. He had become distant. She had besought on his behalf. He was set fast in affliction. (The Living Spirit) went to the place of destruction and weakness, the one that freed and redeemed him and gave him ease from the affliction. He set him right with rest and joy. He has himself become rested, with him for ever.

> *¶14 #1 [moderate]:* ¶14 resumes the consequence of the Mother’s entreaty and introduces “a great power … the First Man…”. In the cosmogonic logic, the Mother (by granted entreaty) sends/commissions the Living Spirit to redeem the First Man.
> *¶14 #2 [moderate]:* The clause “a great power [...] of the loved one [...]” expects identification of the power: the Living Spirit is the “great power” given in ¶11 and is the agent of redemption here.
> *¶14 #3 [tentative]:* “of the loved one” in this register is commonly completed as a kinship term indicating derivation from the Father’s love; “son” fits the surrounding son-language already present in ¶10 (“son of God”).

**¶15** See now, I have taught you the first entreaty that the Mother of Life besought by her prayer. They granted her her entreaty.

**¶17** [Then they gathered the elements] and they built the universe and completed it with its firmaments, with its wheels, with the ships of light that are in the heights, with its fastenings, with its earths and its walls, with its vessels. They completed the universe and set it right by the fathers of light.

> *¶17 #1 [moderate]:* Before “and they built the universe…”, a collective act of arranging/assembling is required. “elements” matches the later mention of “elements” (¶27) and the builder-theme of the oldest cosmological layer.

**¶18** The Great Spirit stood with the Beloved of the Lights, the Great Builder with the Living Spirit and the First Man. These five stood to prayer; they glorified and praised the Father, the first established one. They besought of him an entreaty. They claimed of him a request, that he might give them the power [of the Third one,] the leader, a guide to all the things of activity that they had constructed. That he might come and purify the living soul that is set in affliction.

> *¶18 #1 [moderate]:* They request “the power … the leader, a guide…” which, in this system, is the Third Ambassador. A genitival pointer (“of the Third one”) anticipates the explicit naming in ¶19.

**¶19** They prayed and besought their Father, and he received from them their prayer [and he was pleased;] he gave them their entreaty and their request [and] he summoned from him [the power] which is the Third Ambassador. He, he came from the power of his greatness. He saw the entire soul, how it is entangled in a chain and set fast. It sees the things of activity that his brothers have constructed, that have come from the heights. Again, he saw the living soul, that it is entangled and set fast in a great torture. It is oppressed in the stink of the abyss that is joined with it.

> *¶19 #1 [moderate]:* After “he received from them their prayer” the next move is divine assent leading to granting. “he was pleased” is a standard impersonal assent formula consistent with substrate tone.
> *¶19 #3 [moderate]:* “he summoned from him […] which is the Third Ambassador” needs “the power” as what is summoned forth from the Father’s treasury/majesty; fits the cosmological mechanics of emanation.

**¶20** And therefore, he, the Ambassador [had compassion] in his charity. So, he revealed his way, before this underworld that is dug deep down, to free the living soul. He redeemed it from all the fleshes of death wherein it is pained. He gave it ease. He set it right in the place of rest and joy.

> *¶20 #1 [strong]:* “the Ambassador […] in his charity” requires a verb of merciful inclination; “had compassion” preserves the structural (not moralistic) sense: the Light power turns toward what is bound to effect release.

**¶21** And again, he too, the Ambassador, he fulfilled the will of these three; the will of his brothers who had besought the Father for him. They claimed of him a request, together with the [entreaty] of the Father, the one that [he had received;] he gave the entreaty that they had besought of him.

> *¶21 #1 [tentative]:* “together with the […] of the Father” most naturally takes “entreaty”/“request” as the paired terms throughout the chapter; the triad’s will is fulfilled together with the Father’s granting act.
> *¶21 #2 [tentative]:* “the one that […] he gave the entreaty…” requires a prior reception/acceptance of prayer as the condition for giving. This also mirrors ¶19’s sequence (receive → give).

**¶22** The third is the transferral of the living soul that he had freed. He released it and gave it ease from the affliction. He became king and first to the entire body.

**¶23** Behold, I have instructed you as to the second entreaty by which they first besought the Father. They were given the Third Ambassador.

**¶25** Again, let us come to the third entreaty when they besought him the third time [when the Third Ambassador] displayed his image [had] he hid his image from the [before the rulers;] he stood firm [and] they revealed [face of the abyss;] the light [until] the First Man [his form] mock [again;] to him in [and] which is exalted [to them;] garment [and] they were freed [they remembered] to him [and they] their voice [they mocked] the body [in their pride;] prayer [the height] it [a] beseech [robe of light] the body [and] beseech [some] she besought [and returned] in [their cry] be free [from] Ambassador [within] and also [the bonds.]

> *¶25 #1 [moderate]:* ¶25 introduces “the third entreaty… third time” and then immediately narrates image-display/hiding—this is the Third Ambassador’s epiphany tactic to stir separation. So he must be the subject entering the scene.
> *¶25 #3 [moderate]:* “displayed his image” is typically done before the Archons/rulers of the Darkness as a lure/trigger for emission and separation; “rulers” keeps oldest-layer cosmology.
> *¶25 #5 [tentative]:* He “hid his image from the …” and then “stood firm … they revealed … the light …”. Hiding from the abyssal depth (the devouring field) fits the correspondential mechanics: concealment from those who would swallow, manifestation to those who will emit.
> *¶25 #7 [tentative]:* “he stood firm […] they revealed […] the light” implies stability of form/measure while others react. “form” matches the image/figure vocabulary.
> *¶25 #8 [tentative]:* A resumptive marker is expected in this highly compressed line to move from concealment to renewed revelation.
> *¶25 #10 [tentative]:* “they revealed … the light …” needs a dative; the revelation is ‘to them’ (to the opposing powers) as part of the separation process.
> *¶25 #12 [tentative]:* The sudden “the First Man … mock …” suggests a response by the Darkness powers when the archetypal combatant is evoked; “remembered” fits: they recall the First Man and begin derision.
> *¶25 #14 [strong]:* Restores the verb explicitly demanded by “mock … to him…”.
> *¶25 #15 [tentative]:* Mockery issues from the opposite correspondence of height/pride; this also prepares for “which is exalted … garment …”.
> *¶25 #16 [moderate]:* “which is exalted” needs a noun: height/loftiness; correspondentially the false ‘height’ of Darkness opposes true height of Light.
> *¶25 #18 [moderate]:* “garment” here is the luminous investiture/appearance used in the epiphany. “robe of light” is the oldest-layer way to speak of the light-body/form.
> *¶25 #20 [tentative]:* “they were freed …” suggests a partial liberation at this stage (some light-elements released by the reaction).
> *¶25 #21 [tentative]:* Freedom in this mythic mechanism is coupled with return/ascent toward the Light treasury.
> *¶25 #22 [moderate]:* “their voice … the body … prayer …” points to a cry/voice rising from embodied soul as prayer-impulse awakened by the epiphany.
> *¶25 #24 [moderate]:* Voice/prayer arises from within the bound body (interior → exterior correspondence).
> *¶25 #25 [moderate]:* The whole section is about freeing; “bonds” matches “entangled and set fast” motifs repeated in ¶19, ¶31, ¶37.

**¶26** ⟨p.276⟩ [He came and stood] with the living ones and [stood to prayer] for ever [they shall live] the first [entreaty] from the [Father of Greatness;] he gave him the hope [of ascent.]

> *¶26 #1 [tentative]:* ¶26 continues the third entreaty sequence and needs a subject/action introducing a standing with the living ones. “came and stood” fits the theophanic/ordering pattern.
> *¶26 #2 [moderate]:* Matches the chapter’s repeated formula of standing to prayer (cf. ¶18).
> *¶26 #3 [moderate]:* “with the living ones and […] for ever” strongly suggests a promise of enduring life among the Living.
> *¶26 #4 [tentative]:* “the first […] from the […] he gave him the hope” expects “the first entreaty” / “first request” language reused structurally.
> *¶26 #5 [moderate]:* Standard source of granting/hope in this cosmological register; matches earlier “presence of the Father of Greatness.”
> *¶26 #6 [moderate]:* Hope given in this system is hope of ascent/return of the soul/light. Fits the immediate soteriological-cosmological motion without pastoral overlay.

**¶27** Also, Jesus the Splendour perfected the will [of the five;] the will [of the elements;] they found the light [elements,] the will of the [powers] that he had [promised;] he gave them the power that is Jesus the Splendour. He granted the will of the elements. [and they shall stand] for ever. This first [entreaty,] he was humbled, he was made free [by it] the [Splendour]-ness is displayed to his enemies. He received the hope [of release;] reveal [to] Jesus the Splendour, the light power [the bound ones;] and the entreaty of the [and] of light [first entreaty]

> *¶27 #1 [tentative]:* “Jesus the Splendour perfected the will …” likely refers back to the five powers who prayed in ¶18. He perfects/fulfills their will by acting as revelatory power.
> *¶27 #2 [moderate]:* The paragraph later explicitly says “He granted the will of the elements.” Earlier “the will …” should match the same subject.
> *¶27 #3 [tentative]:* “they found the light …” in this myth is the elements/light-particles recognizing their kind through the appearing Splendour; restoring “elements” keeps coherence.
> *¶27 #4 [tentative]:* “the will of the …” most naturally “powers” (Light powers) in this register.
> *¶27 #5 [tentative]:* “that he had … he gave them the power” needs a prior commitment/ordinance—“promised” or “ordained”. “promised” is least intrusive.
> *¶27 #6 [tentative]:* Before “for ever” a stable-state verb is expected: stand/remain. Echoes “stood to prayer” but now as eternal standing in Light.
> *¶27 #7 [tentative]:* “This first […] he was humbled…” likely refers to “this first entreaty/first figure” within the third entreaty complex; “entreaty” best matches chapter vocabulary.
> *¶27 #8 [tentative]:* “he was humbled, he was made free …” needs an instrumental: by it (by this power/appearance) the bound one is humbled/freed.
> *¶27 #9 [moderate]:* “the [...]-ness is displayed to his enemies” points to the quality of splendour/brightness; completing as “Splendour-ness” keeps the proper name’s semantic force.
> *¶27 #10 [tentative]:* “He received the hope […] reveal […]” suggests “hope of release” awakened by revelation.
> *¶27 #12 [moderate]:* Jesus the Splendour reveals to those bound in mixture; this is the substrate’s functional definition of the revealer.
> *¶27 #14 [tentative]:* Ends with “and the entreaty of the […] of light …” The lacuna should supply the genitive: the first entreaty (i.e., the primordial plea) of the powers of light, keeping the entreaty-theme continuous.

**¶28** Now, just as this entreaty [has been fulfilled,] the figure [so also] that they have [of the likeness] this is also how [established,] the figure of [it is,] you are set firm [it is made known] Happen you know this: Every one [the figure of truth;] knows, he has perceived [if] all the things [in your understanding,] out from [who] within him [truly] upon him [from without, and from within.]

> *¶28 #1 [moderate]:* “Now, just as this entreaty […]” requires the same fulfillment formula used earlier (receive/fulfill).
> *¶28 #2 [strong]:* Sets up a correspondence: as the entreaty is, so is the figure/form that results.
> *¶28 #3 [tentative]:* The paragraph is about “figure” repeatedly: figure/l likeness/image as correspondential manifestation.
> *¶28 #4 [tentative]:* “that they have …” most naturally “established/constructed” in a cosmological teaching tone.
> *¶28 #6 [moderate]:* The section turns to epistemology (“Happen you know this: Every one … knows, he has perceived …”). “made known” fits.
> *¶28 #7 [tentative]:* Oldest layer: “figure” corresponds to an interior truth made visible in ultimates; “truth” is the spiritual degree underlying forms.
> *¶28 #9 [tentative]:* “you are set firm …” is firmness in understanding/knowledge rather than faith-exhortation; keeps impersonal didactic tone.
> *¶28 #11 [moderate]:* Strengthens the epistemic claim without turning pastoral.
> *¶28 #12 [moderate]:* The close “out from … within him … upon him …” demands the correspondential axiom: perception joins outer and inner; restoring as a balanced pair fits the doctrine of correspondences (inside→outside, yet read also outside→inside as sign).

**¶31** The first: [the coming of the revealer] of the living soul that is entangled and bound in the entire universe. For it shall be freed and cleansed and purified and redeemed by cause of him.

> *¶31 #1 [moderate]:* ¶31 begins an enumerated list (“The first: …”) and then defines a function “of the living soul … freed… by cause of him.” The agent is the revealer (Jesus Splendour / Third Ambassador in this complex). Oldest-layer functional title: “the revealer.”

**¶37** He redeemed the living person who is entangled [in the universe] entirely. He has [seen] his father, the first [established one;] he made redemption for the soul [also] that he had freed [that] the light has pity [again,] the way also [and the light] a request [opened] affliction. Again, just as he has [and] of the light [for him from] give ease for his [requested] grant the entreaty and the request [powers,] He besought for the soul [brother,] him, that had come forth from the world. They shall make [and they shall] ease. It shall be freed from affliction and come forth [into the Light] for ever.

> *¶37 #1 [moderate]:* “entangled […] entirely” most naturally completes with “in the universe”, a repeated scope-term in this chapter.
> *¶37 #2 [tentative]:* “He has […] his father” likely “seen”/“beheld” (vision of the Father) as a result of redemption; fits the ascent logic.
> *¶37 #3 [strong]:* Pairs with earlier repeated epithet “the first established one” for the Father.
> *¶37 #7 [moderate]:* “the light has pity … the way also …” needs the subject explicitly: the Light (as power/realm) pities and opens a way.
> *¶37 #8 [moderate]:* “the way also …” is best completed as “opened (a way)” in redemption mechanics.
> *¶37 #10 [tentative]:* “a request […] affliction” suggests petitioning ‘for him from affliction’ (deliverance).
> *¶37 #11 [tentative]:* “Again, just as he has […] of the light” needs a verb parallel to “besought/claimed”: requested/asked of the Light powers.
> *¶37 #12 [strong]:* Completes “of the light […]” as “powers” consistent with vocabulary.
> *¶37 #13 [tentative]:* “give ease for his […]” in this mythic register is often ‘brother’ (one soul aiding another of the same stock). This also anticipates ¶43’s co-helper motif.
> *¶37 #14 [moderate]:* Moves from present exemplar to future general rule (they shall grant…).
> *¶37 #15 [moderate]:* Ends with “come forth […] for ever.” The destination is the Light realm; this keeps the cosmological (not ecclesial) endpoint.

**¶43** Indeed, this living soul that was freed by cause of this other soul; it too, that living soul, it shall be redeemed in the name of that person. It is redeemed and purified and attains its first essence. Also, it shall become his co-helper and beseeches for the soul of the one who has been freed from his body. He shall petition charity for it and forgiveness of sins from the powers of light.

**¶44** Just as ease has come about for the living soul by cause of this soul, this is how this soul shall find ease and escape that body. It goes into the land of light [with its portion,] soul that was redeemed because of it [and that] comes about for it; and it goes to the land of life and rest [for ever.]

> *¶44 #1 [tentative]:* After “It goes into the land of light […] soul that was redeemed because of it …” the gap likely specifies that it enters with its own portion/measure of light reclaimed; this matches correspondential ‘measure’ language (portion of essence).
> *¶44 #3 [moderate]:* The cadence “land of life and rest …” closes naturally with the eternal-state marker already used elsewhere in the chapter.

**Assessment:** Only a small subset of lacunae can be tightly restored from the provided English core because most gaps in ¶25–¶28 and ¶37 depend on lost Coptic syntactic cues and fixed Manichaean formulae. Where the correspondential cosmology strongly constrains the agent (Mother of Life → Living Spirit; Five → Third Ambassador; revealer → Jesus Splendour) restorations are moderate/strong. Many minor connectives are necessarily tentative/minimal. If you provide the Coptic line-breaks or any surviving letters for the brackets, the reconstructions can be made much tighter and expanded to all 92 lacunae.

## Chapter 116: Concerning why if a [Nail] is cut the Person shall not be sick; but if a Limb is cut he shall immediately be pained.

**¶5** Happen you know th[at the] body is like the earth, wh[ile these] limbs als[o are] of it are like the trees. There are varieties [kinds] of tree you shall find growing on the earth. They ar[is]e and grow again and stand firm. They have not [no root] Again, there are others that if they are cut shall not [grow again] and they grow, as they are li[ke those] which shall be cut and grow [again] and [some] they too that occur [in the wild] they shall be cut and not grow, resembling [like those that neither] grow nor shall they [bear fruit] it [sh]all please him on [whatever] and grows [and it] in impurity [and separates] from him. This flesh that is corrupt [and is mixed with death] in the liv[in]g flesh and they [are in affliction] are somewhat sick.

> *¶5 #3 [moderate]:* The syntax needs a finite verb: “these limbs also are of it,” matching the earth/body correspondence (earth : trees :: body : limbs).
> *¶5 #4 [moderate]:* The line is classifying “varieties … of tree,” then immediately continues with “There are others…,” so a simple taxonomic term is demanded.
> *¶5 #6 [moderate]:* In correspondential physiology, what “arises and grows again and stands firm” has a root (an interior connection); what “has not …” is what cannot regenerate after cutting. The contrast that follows (“others… cut shall not grow”) points to root/life-principle as the missing term.
> *¶5 #7 [strong]:* Required by the repeated regeneration motif: some cut and regrow; others cut and do not.
> *¶5 #8 [moderate]:* The comparison is to another class already described (“like those which shall be cut and grow…”).
> *¶5 #9 [moderate]:* Completes the cycle: cut → grow → stand; maintains the parallelism with the first class of trees.
> *¶5 #10 [moderate]:* Introduces an additional subset: “and some … they too…,” preparing the next clause about those that occur and are cut.
> *¶5 #11 [tentative]:* “occur …” in this context is best constrained by a locality/source term (trees ‘occurring’ naturally vs cultivated). Correspondentially it marks spontaneous growth from the earth’s own state.
> *¶5 #12 [moderate]:* Sets up the double negation that follows (“neither grow nor shall they …”), maintaining the classification by regenerative capacity.
> *¶5 #13 [moderate]:* The natural sign of a tree’s inner life is fruit; in correspondence, fruit = effect/issue. Those that neither grow nor “bear fruit” fit the ‘dead’ class.
> *¶5 #15 [tentative]:* The phrase “it shall please him on …” needs an indeterminate object of choice/occasion: “on whatever (he chooses).”
> *¶5 #16 [tentative]:* Links the subject back to the tree/body analogue: “and it grows … in impurity … from him.”
> *¶5 #17 [tentative]:* Correspondentially, “impurity” produces disjunction; what grows “in impurity” is something that divides/estranges from its source (“from him”).
> *¶5 #18 [tentative]:* The surrounding sentence turns to “This flesh that is corrupt…,” so the missing portion at the page break should name the condition of corrupt flesh: admixture with death/decay as the opposite-sense life-principle.
> *¶5 #20 [moderate]:* The close reads: “in the living flesh and they … are somewhat sick.” The lacuna must supply a predicate of distress preceding “somewhat sick,” i.e., an intermediate state of suffering/affliction.

**Assessment:** Most gaps can be constrained by the chapter’s fixed correspondence (body↔earth; limbs↔trees) and the repeated taxonomy of trees by regenerative power (cut/regrow vs cut/not regrow), which in the oldest layer maps to inner connection (root/life-principle) and outward effects (fruit). A few clauses near the end (impurity/page break) remain only tentatively recoverable because the immediate manuscript context is missing and multiple substrate-appropriate terms could fit.

---

## Chapter 117: Concerning why Some shall delay to come forth from the Body, and Others die in an instant.

**¶2** The [sec]ond [order is as this] if he shall fall [into the abyss] one [they do not leave him] one hour [for] others who shall [depart] and they remain. They shall delay until they [until they see that] he is freed, and they delay to come toward [the place from which he fell]

> *¶2 #2 [moderate]:* The paragraph is enumerating a rule-like sequence ("The second …"), so the lacuna should supply a neutral structural marker rather than any exhortation: an impersonal statement introducing the second case/order.
> *¶2 #3 [moderate]:* The verb "fall" in this substrate typically takes a spatial/cosmic object (pit/abyss/depth) rather than a moral one. Here it sets the scene for delay and rescue: a fall into a lower region.
> *¶2 #4 [moderate]:* The next clause contrasts with others who depart; thus the first group is defined by remaining with the fallen one. This also matches the closing "and they remain."
> *¶2 #5 [tentative]:* The repetition "one … one hour …" reads as a timed interval: "for one …, one hour …"; the smallest connective that makes the Coptic-like cadence work is the prepositional/causal link "for".
> *¶2 #6 [moderate]:* The sentence contrasts two sets: some remain and delay; "others who shall …" naturally completes as "depart/go away" in this kind of procedural description.
> *¶2 #7 [moderate]:* "They shall delay until they … he is freed" requires a perception/verification verb. In correspondential terms, liberation must be *known/seen* before motion resumes.
> *¶2 #8 [tentative]:* "come toward …" needs a determinate locus. The tightest correspondential closure is a return toward the originating place/level, matching the opening "fall" and the freeing/restoration sequence.

**Assessment:** Only a small amount of continuous wording survives, but the internal correspondences constrain the sense: a second ‘order’ describing a fall into a lower region, a faithful group that stays, a timed delay, others who depart, waiting until liberation is verified, then returning toward the original place/level. Exact connective particles remain uncertain due to the fragmentary syntax.

---

## Chapter 118: [ ... ]

**¶2** ⟨p.284⟩[and a]ll of them [are set in their places,] that they shall [show forth] seven people [of the powers.] Again, he too, this per[person,] the time when [comes the separation] of the light [is in the heights, and fills] the universe [so that the order] is revealed [and the measures] take to the light [ly and with purity;]erly, the way that he exists [in his own form,] from [the one, and not] these other three [natures.]

> *¶2 #1 [strong]:* The preserved tail “...5 a]ll of them” most naturally yields “and all of them”, a common connective in this register, introducing a collective statement.
> *¶2 #2 [moderate]:* The clause needs a predication about the totality (“all of them …”) before the purpose/result (“that they shall …”). In the oldest cosmological substrate, bodies/powers are ‘set/established in their places’ according to measure and order.
> *¶2 #3 [moderate]:* After “that they shall …” the expected correspondential function is manifestation: the ordered totality exists so as to ‘reveal/show’ what is within (light, measure, order).
> *¶2 #4 [moderate]:* “seven people” is almost certainly a Coptic/Greek calque for ‘seven powers/ones’ (heptad as powers), not literal persons. The substrate speaks of powers and measures rather than ecclesial persons.
> *¶2 #5 [strong]:* The word is already mostly given as “per[son ... ]”; completing to “person,” is the minimal restoration.
> *¶2 #6 [moderate]:* “the time when [ ... ] of the light” calls for the cosmogonic act proper to this layer: a separation/distinguishing of Light from mixture, prior to any moralizing. Hence “comes the separation of the light”.
> *¶2 #7 [tentative]:* The sequence “of the light [ ... 19 ... ] the universe” requires a relation between Light and the cosmos: Light above, filling/ordering the universe as its inner form. This matches correspondential inside→outside logic (light as form of understanding).
> *¶2 #8 [moderate]:* Before “is revealed” the text needs the subject of revelation. In this substrate the “order” (taxis/measure) of the universe is what becomes manifest when the Light is distinguished and seen in effects.
> *¶2 #9 [tentative]:* After “is revealed” an appositive extension is expected: order and measures (limits/number/weight) as the revealed structure. This is characteristic of Iranian-cosmological ‘measure’ language.
> *¶2 #10 [moderate]:* The surviving “... utt]erly” strongly constrains to “utterly”; what precedes should complete an adverbial pair: ‘wholly/with purity’ fits the substrate’s emphasis on unmixed Light (purity) rather than moral exhortation.
> *¶2 #11 [moderate]:* After “the way that he exists …” the natural completion is a statement of mode/essence: existence ‘in his own form’ (i.e., according to his proper degree), aligning with discrete degrees.
> *¶2 #12 [tentative]:* “from [ ... ] these other three [ ... ]” points to a contrast between ‘the one’ (single nature/degree) and ‘the other three’ (the triad of degrees or threefold powers). Thus “from the one, and not … these other three …”.
> *¶2 #13 [moderate]:* The phrase “these other three …” most coherently closes as “natures” in the oldest layer, marking discrete kinds/levels rather than offices or persons.

**Assessment:** Most gaps can be restored only to the level of structural sense: order/measure, separation of Light, the heptad as powers, and the contrast of one nature with ‘other three’ natures/degrees. Exact wording remains uncertain in several places because the surviving anchors are few (especially #2, #7–#9, #10–#13), but the correspondential constraints (Light as inner form; revelation as manifest order; powers and measures rather than ‘people’) narrow the restorations to a limited semantic range.

## Chapter 119: Chapter 119: [ ... ]

**¶1** ⟨p.285⟩[He is the Mind,] and the [righte]o[ousness of his thought] is more than [their sound, but] the voice through them.

> *¶1 #1 [moderate]:* Opening needs a subject that can “voice through” the powers; in the oldest substrate the first emissive principle is Mind/Thought (Greek nous; Iranian *menōg*), through which the light-realities speak/act. A personal/hagiographic opener is avoided.
> *¶1 #3 [moderate]:* The sentence contrasts an interior quality with “the voice through them”: righteousness is located in the inmost (thought/mind) and exceeds mere utterance through intermediaries.
> *¶1 #4 [tentative]:* Needs a comparative: righteousness is “more than” the outward voice/sound carried through subordinate beings.

**¶3** This one, whom they called “approved” [in the beginning,] they approved him in the midst of the ae[ons of] light. These numerous multitudes set him a[part], and he came forth against the enemy. They gave this name [upon him,] this matter [because of] who are numerous, that are in the aeons of light and who [are without] measure or number.

> *¶3 #1 [tentative]:* Sets the temporal/cosmic frame for the acclamation “approved” and their enthronement of him among the aeons of light; fits impersonal cosmogonic register.
> *¶3 #4 [moderate]:* After “They gave this name …” the syntax expects the bestowal directed to him.
> *¶3 #5 [moderate]:* Introduces the causal clause explaining the name: it is given “because of this matter … who are numerous…”.

**¶4** This one, [whom they called] “righteous”, because he is the first righteous one who had no sin amongst all of the aeons that belong to the light and the thought [is in him,] they knew him, so that [ ...

> *¶4 #2 [moderate]:* “light and the thought … they knew him” requires the thought/light to be described as indwelling; keeps correspondential inside→outside logic (thought as interior form of light).

**¶5** This one also [whom they called] “only begotten”, because he was established [before the onset of the assault,] pour [himself to] enemy.

> *¶5 #2 [tentative]:* “Only begotten” in this substrate points to the unique emissary established prior to mixture/assault; avoids later Christian phrasing while keeping cosmogonic sequence.
> *¶5 #3 [moderate]:* Yields: “he was established … to pour … enemy.” The subject acts directly, consistent with impersonal cosmological function.

**¶7** Again, just as the only begotten ones from your [only] begotten race and you [are established] according to flesh [carnal, s]nly begot-ten also from gold and silver and all the [elements] of the universe.

> *¶7 #2 [tentative]:* Parallels the prior line about being “established”; applies to the class “only begotten ones” as generated forms within a race/kind.
> *¶7 #3 [tentative]:* Produces: “according to flesh … carnal, s]only begotten…” The preserved final “o” strongly suggests the start of “only”; the intervening adjective contrasts spiritual generation with material (carnal) derivation.
> *¶7 #4 [moderate]:* Gold and silver are species within a wider set; “elements of the universe” matches cosmological register better than ethical or institutional terms.

**¶8** ⟨p.286⟩Just as your father, [the F]irst Man, himself stood firm in the [midst] of all the worlds of darkness. He did not love [the] worlds of darkness, nor did he lust for them! Rather, h[is] thought and love for the household of his people is in h[i]m. Anything else that the enemy [mingles with them] with his violent de[e]ds. Behold, (the First Man) toils and struggles; so that (the enemy) would be separat[e from] them and be cast out from them. And he purifies them and ma[ke]s them f[r]ee, and makes them as they were from the beginning.

> *¶8 #6 [moderate]:* The enemy’s aim in the assault is mixture/appropriation; “mingles with them” fits the ensuing separation/purification motif and the inside→outside correspondential struggle.

**¶10** [If they desire it,] may they discover the remembrance of the household of his peo[ple], as he remains in this [place ni]ght and day.

> *¶10 #1 [tentative]:* Introduces a conditional purpose-clause leading to “may they discover the remembrance… night and day,” matching the impersonal instructional tone (not exhortative preaching).

**Assessment:** Most gaps are short and tightly constrained by syntax and the core mythic logic (approval/naming in the aeons of Light; only-begotten as unique emissary; assault as attempted mixture; separation/purification restoring the first state). A few longer reconstructions (¶1 #1, #4; ¶3 #1; ¶5 #2; ¶7 #2–#4; ¶8 #6; ¶10 #1) remain tentative because the surviving context does not fix a single idiom, though the correspondential structure (interior thought/light vs outward voice; mixture vs separation) constrains the semantic field.

---

## Chapter 120: Concerning the Two Essences

**¶1** [These are] two essences that are present at the beginning [before all things:] the light and the darkness, that which is good and that which is evi[l], life and] death.

> *¶1 #1 [moderate]:* Opening of the substrate typically states the primordial duality in an impersonal, demonstrative way before listing paired contraries; “These are” fits the catalogue style that follows.
> *¶1 #2 [moderate]:* The correspondential logic requires a temporal/ontological priority formula: the two roots/essences are posited as prior to the formed world. A colon naturally introduces the ensuing pair-list (light/darkness, good/evil, life/death).

**¶5** For if God has hi[mself] created them, the one who does them [h]as [no] sin therein! If they did not come [about] from him, nor did he command them to be d[on]e, [y]ou are the one who will speak a lie against God, saying [that] all [these e]vil things come about from him [but rather from the Darkness and its root]

> *¶5 #9 [moderate]:* The argument is the substrate’s anti-monist constraint: if evils are not from God and not commanded by him, their origin must be assigned to the contrary essence. The ending therefore points to the evil root (Darkness) as the generative source of “these evil things,” maintaining the two-essence correspondential structure of ¶1.

**Assessment:** Most gaps are trivial letter/word completions. The two substantive restorations are constrained by the chapter’s dual-principle cosmology: ¶1 requires a formal primordial-duality heading and ¶5 must ascribe the origin of evils to the contrary root (Darkness), not to God.

---

## Chapter 121: Concerning the Sect of the Basket

**¶3** So, [verse]verse in which you stand is like a tree; while you are like the fruit that hangs from the tree. Now, if you yourself had been plucked from the universe with the basket, then well could you call yourself the son of the basket! However, if up till now you are entangled in the universe; how then do you call yourself the son of the basket, before you are plucked from the universe like a fruit? For as long as it hangs from the tree, it shall not be called ‘alms’. And you too, as long as you are entangled in [the] universe you [shall not be] well called the son of the basket.

**¶5** The [great Soul] and the great [great Light] and the great Mind that is [revealed] this Youth that is with the [Living Spirit] thirty-six angels [twelve] twenty-two angels [that are placed under him] but [that are placed under them] of the twenty-two [seven] ⟨p.290⟩ the seven pillars that stan[stand] the sea that surrounds the universe [round about it] four angels that are distributed to the [four corners of the] universe, supporting the mightiness [of it] below, which is the earth of all the heavens [that is set] these other five gods, the sons of the Man, they that [that are over] the universe is stood firm [built up] the summons and the obedience [by them]

> *¶5 #1 [moderate]:* The series here enumerates hypostases in descending order; after the tree/fruit correspondence the text pivots to the constitution of the cosmos. In the oldest Iranian substrate the immediate cosmic principle paired with Mind is Soul (life as the recipient-form of Mind).
> *¶5 #2 [moderate]:* A second ‘great’ preceding ‘great Mind’ is best read as the primal element/realm out of which Mind is manifest. ‘Light’ fits the substrate register (impersonal cosmological element) better than later ecclesial terms.
> *¶5 #3 [moderate]:* The clause ‘the great Mind that is … this Youth’ demands a participle linking Mind to its epiphanic form as ‘Youth’. ‘Revealed/manifest’ is the expected correspondential move: Mind in itself, then Mind in appearance.
> *¶5 #4 [moderate]:* The ‘Youth’ in Manichaean-cognate strata is the active demiurgic agent; in the oldest layer this corresponds to the Life-Spirit that orders forms. The surrounding angelic enumerations match the Living Spirit’s administration of the constructed cosmos.
> *¶5 #5 [tentative]:* ‘This Youth … with the … thirty-six angels’ implies an intermediate cardinal group that, with 36, yields the common 12×3 structuring of powers/images elsewhere in the chapter. Twelve coheres with the later mention of ‘twelve powers’ and ‘twelve images’ as discrete degrees.
> *¶5 #6 [tentative]:* The syntax needs a verb/relative clause completing the first angelic cohort before introducing the next. The register is administrative/structural, not devotional.
> *¶5 #7 [tentative]:* Parallelism with the 36 suggests a second ordered cohort. The text is listing ranks supporting the cosmic architecture.
> *¶5 #8 [strong]:* The phrase ‘but … of the twenty-two … the seven pillars’ strongly constrains the missing number to ‘seven’ as the extracted subset from the 22 that function architectonically as pillars.
> *¶5 #9 [strong]:* Only the completion to ‘stand’ is required by the preserved letters and grammar (‘pillars that stan[d …]’).
> *¶5 #10 [moderate]:* ‘the sea that surrounds the universe …’ expects an adverbial locator tied to the pillars and sea as boundary-supports. Old-layer cosmology treats the cosmic sea as the encircling limit.
> *¶5 #11 [moderate]:* The four angels ‘distributed to the … universe’ are typically at the four corners/quarters as stabilizers. This is correspondentially the extension of order into spatial ultimates.
> *¶5 #12 [tentative]:* Completes ‘supporting the mightiness … below’ with a pronominal object referring to the universe/disk just mentioned.
> *¶5 #13 [moderate]:* ‘below, which is the earth of all the heavens’ reads as the foundational stratum. ‘Set/placed’ is the expected ontological verb in this register.
> *¶5 #14 [tentative]:* Introduces the ‘other five gods’ as a further tier in a stacked cosmography (over/above/beyond the lower supports).
> *¶5 #15 [moderate]:* ‘they that … the universe is stood firm’ requires a constructive verb. The substrate describes stabilizing as building/establishing forms in ultimates.
> *¶5 #16 [moderate]:* Completes the passive sense: the universe ‘is stood firm’ by these agents/powers.

**¶6** The third also, [in the depth] below, there are twelve powers standing firm there. However, the number of the twelve images [there] the three great pillars of mightiness that [is in three times four] pillars supporting the disk [are] the other three [are three] they are the garments that the Living Spirit [of it] he put them at that time [pillars of glory;] that threw them, together with [took] which is displayed therein [in the firmament;] this [the darkness,] perfect before [the mixture] great Porter [order] in his power [the] these are the three great [the] against [that is called the] are in the midst of people [who stands] knows [gates] entrusts his soul to them [pillars of mightiness].

> *¶6 #1 [moderate]:* ‘The third also, … below’ points to the third region/degree in a tripartite cosmography; ‘in the depth’ fits the descent into the lowest discrete degree.
> *¶6 #2 [strong]:* Small locator needed to anchor ‘below, there are twelve powers standing firm there’.
> *¶6 #3 [tentative]:* The text immediately contrasts ‘twelve powers’ with ‘twelve images’ and then with ‘three great pillars’. Explaining twelve as 3×4 matches the chapter’s recurring triadic structuring into quarters (discrete degrees expressed in ultimates).
> *¶6 #4 [strong]:* Grammatical completion: ‘the three great pillars of mightiness that … pillars supporting the disk’.
> *¶6 #5 [moderate]:* The passage distinguishes ‘three great pillars’ and then ‘the other three’; supplying ‘are three’ prepares the explicit parallel set.
> *¶6 #6 [moderate]:* Completes ‘pillars supporting the disk …’ with reference back to the disk/universe.
> *¶6 #7 [tentative]:* The ‘other three … they are the garments that the Living Spirit …’ requires naming the second triad before identifying them correspondentially as garments (outermost forms). ‘Glory’ keeps the impersonal radiance register of the oldest layer.
> *¶6 #8 [moderate]:* ‘the garments that the Living Spirit … he put them at that time’ needs a verb of assumption before the act of putting/placing.
> *¶6 #9 [moderate]:* Where garments are ‘put’ in cosmological architecture is the visible expanse (firmament), i.e., ultimates where inner powers appear as outer form.
> *¶6 #10 [moderate]:* ‘that threw them, together with … which is displayed therein’ implies the adversarial counterforce that ‘throws’ (projects) forms into mixture. In the substrate this is Darkness as active principle.
> *¶6 #11 [moderate]:* What is ‘displayed therein’ (in the firmament/world) is the mixed condition of light and darkness. This is the core cosmological datum of the oldest stratum.
> *¶6 #12 [tentative]:* After mixture the text points to ‘this … perfect before … great Porter’: the missing term is the ordering principle by which the mixture is held in form.
> *¶6 #13 [strong]:* Small article before ‘great Porter’.
> *¶6 #14 [strong]:* Article before the title phrase.
> *¶6 #15 [moderate]:* The line ‘perfect before … great Porter’ needs an identificatory clause introducing the title as a known cosmic function/name.
> *¶6 #16 [moderate]:* ‘… great Porter … in his power’ requires an active description: the Porter stands/keeps watch at the boundary between degrees.
> *¶6 #17 [moderate]:* A ‘Porter’ implies gates. In correspondential cosmology gates are discrete-degree boundaries; the Porter is the function that regulates influx/efflux.
> *¶6 #18 [moderate]:* The concluding ‘these are the three great … against …’ loops back to identify the triad as defensive supports. Restoring ‘pillars of mightiness’ preserves the chapter’s internal terminology and ties to the ‘against’ clause (standing against dissolution/assault).

**Assessment:** Only ¶3 is mechanically recoverable. ¶5–¶6 are heavily lacunose and preserve mainly the scaffold of an enumeration: ranks (Youth/Living Spirit), angelic cohorts, seven pillars, cosmic sea, four quarter-guardians, five gods/sons, then a third lower region with twelve powers/images and two triads of pillars (one structural, one as ‘garments’). Many fills are therefore constrained by correspondential structure (triads/quaternities; boundary/sea; pillars/garments; porter/gates) but exact wording remains uncertain in multiple places; several reconstructions are best-effort substrate-register restorations rather than tight restorations from surviving letters.

## Chapter 122: Concerning the 'Assent' and the 'Amen'

**¶2** The assent and the amen have come about from these great ones of the powers. They were given the name as the assent belongs to the summons and the amen belongs to the answer.

**¶3** Just as the summons and the obedience are the ones that opened the portals of mercy and entreaty for the First Man, so they also became portals for the Mother of Life and the Father of the living. The First Man sought and found his redeemer. And also the Mother of Life and the Father of the living sought [and found] this runner, who came forth from them, who is the First Man; but the summons and the obedience became the portals for them.

> *¶3 #1 [strong]:* The triadic parallelism is explicit: First Man “sought and found his redeemer”; likewise Mother of Life and Father of the living “sought … this runner” who proceeds from them. The verb-pair ‘sought and found’ maintains the correspondential symmetry (quest → discovery) and keeps the impersonal cosmological register without importing later devotional language.

**¶4** ⟨p.293⟩
Now, in this way they have become portals for the aeons of greatness towards the First Man, and the soul that was conjoined with death. They became helpers [and powers] of the First Man during his ascent. They gave him [remembrance and strength] the Father of Life and the Mother of Life [to meet]. They are purifiers of the living soul, being helpers and bestowers of remembrance for it, be it either in [whether in the waters] in the tree or in the creation of flesh [and in every body].

> *¶4 #1 [moderate]:* The sentence enumerates functions of Summons and Obedience: as ‘portals’ they also become ‘helpers … of the First Man’. In the oldest cosmological register, these are not moral acts but operative forces; pairing ‘helpers and powers’ is a common correspondential collocation (aid as effect; power as potency).
> *¶4 #2 [moderate]:* What they ‘gave him’ during ascent should be interior provisions corresponding to restoration of the Living Soul: memory (remembrance of origin) and force (strength for separation from Death). This matches the next lines where they are ‘bestowers of remembrance’ and ‘purifiers’.
> *¶4 #3 [tentative]:* They “gave him … the Father of Life and the Mother of Life …” fits an infinitival purpose: enabling encounter/approach to the higher sources. ‘To meet’ preserves the structural, non-hagiographic tone (not ‘to worship’, ‘to pray’).
> *¶4 #4 [moderate]:* The clause contrasts locations/states where the living soul is embedded: ‘in … in the tree or in the creation of flesh …’. In the substrate cosmology the three common natural repositories are waters, vegetation (tree), and flesh (animal/human). Thus ‘waters’ completes a triad of ultimates (fluid/vegetative/animal) for purification and recollection.
> *¶4 #5 [tentative]:* After ‘creation of flesh’ the correspondential extension is to embodiment generally: flesh as the general natural formation, ‘every body’ as its varied ultimates. This keeps the teaching impersonal and comprehensive (no later ethical/pastoral turn).

**Assessment:** Most gaps can be constrained by the text’s internal correspondential parallelism: summons/obedience function as operative ‘portals’, giving remembrance and strength, enabling ascent and reunion with the higher living sources, and acting upon the soul across its natural repositories (waters/tree/flesh/body). Exact wording is tightest where the chapter itself provides a model phrase (‘sought and found’); looser where the lacuna likely held a short purpose-clause or a concluding generalizer.
